# Decision log

**Status:** Append-only log, started 2026-10-02 at the implementation walkthrough. It lives in `docs/` of the `dwarf-harness` repository, created on 2026-10-02 at the owner's request; step C0 in `ROADMAP-CORE.md` completes the repository. Since D145 it is a private record: the published repository does not hold it, and the private `dwarf-harness-records` repository tracks it at the same path.

**How to use it.** Each entry records the question, the decision, who made it and the documents it changes. Entries are never edited after they are recorded. A later entry that changes an earlier one says so ("Amends D17", "Supersedes D28"), and the earlier entry gains only a one-line pointer to it. Owner decisions are numbered D19 onward, continuing core §2. Build decisions are numbered B1 onward. A build decision may revise anything a document marks **[PROPOSAL]** or **[INFERENCE]**; anything a document marks as awaiting an owner decision goes back to the owner.

## Entries

### D19. Separate roadmaps that name their dependencies on one another

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner (walkthrough Q1 and Q18).
- **Question:** The single implementation roadmap had no step that builds the agent itself, and several agents are to work on the project at the same time.
- **Decision:**
  - Three roadmaps: core (`ROADMAP-CORE.md`), router (`ROADMAP-ROUTER.md`) and Undercurrent (`ROADMAP-UNDERCURRENT.md`, formerly `ROADMAP-IMPLEMENTATION.md`). A GUI roadmap follows toolkit selection.
  - The core roadmap includes a runtime step: one agent with its execution session and bound contexts; hand-written Anthropic and OpenAI adapters on a static configuration; host tools that run only inside a context's scratch; a plain terminal; credentials from environment variables or the OS keychain; a ledger event for every action.
  - Every step records Goal, Delivers, Waits for, Stop point, Unblocks, Exit criterion and Status, so an agent knows where to stop when the next piece belongs to another roadmap. `ROADMAPS.md` holds the cross-roadmap graph and status board.
- **Changes:** new `ROADMAPS.md`, `ROADMAP-CORE.md`, `ROADMAP-ROUTER.md`; `ROADMAP-IMPLEMENTATION.md` becomes `ROADMAP-UNDERCURRENT.md`.

### D20. Core first; Undercurrent only against frozen contracts

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner (Q2).
- **Decision:** Build one roadmap step after another, core first. Undercurrent work is limited to foundations that need no real data (law loading, the engine, synthetic ledgers, simulation), and starts only once the contracts it consumes are frozen (D40). A change to a frozen contract names every Undercurrent step it affects. The core design is still being fleshed out, and that is a known constraint on Undercurrent.
- **Changes:** refines the old roadmap §2 statement that the engine runs "in parallel with steps 2 to 6"; `ROADMAPS.md`, `ROADMAP-UNDERCURRENT.md`.

### D21. One execution session per agent, many private contexts

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner (Q3).
- **Decision:** Adopt core §7.3. Each agent has one directory holding `Agent.md`, its private memory and documentation, and one execution session. Its private contexts are keyed by `(agent_id, context_id)`: general work, tasks, relationships and boards. Changing counterpart renders a new turn bound to the new context; provider continuations, approvals, router state and cache ledgers never carry over. This resolves D9's open question. The broker in core §7.4 remains a proposal.
- **Changes:** core §1, §7.3, §10; routing header and §5.1.

### D22. Simultaneous turns, with four safeguards

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner (Q3).
- **Decision:** An agent may run turns in several of its contexts at once, up to a per-agent limit that is a setting (start near 3; a limit of 1 gives one turn at a time). Orchestrators and Diplomats hold many relationships and would otherwise become the colony's bottleneck. Safeguards:
  1. Agent-wide files (`Agent.md`, general `Memory.md`, skill edits) are written by the core with a version check. A write based on an out-of-date copy is refused, and the turn re-reads.
  2. Anything that must not be handed out twice, such as task assignments and reservations, is a core record changed atomically, never something the model remembers.
  3. At most one active turn per context. Only a task's own turn writes to its scratch; another context's write follows D23.
  4. Before a call, its maximum cost is reserved against every applicable budget scope; afterwards the reservation is settled against reported usage.
- **Costs accepted:** a higher peak spend rate, peak memory and peak provider requests; an interleaved audit trail; race testing from the first concurrent code (D28).
- **Changes:** core §4.1, §7.3; routing §5.1, §11.

### D23. Temporary scratch for a second writer to one task (amends D17)

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner (Q3a, option B1).
- **Decision:**
  - If a second of the same agent's turns writes to a task while that task's scratch is in use, the core gives it a temporary worktree on its own branch, cut from the task scratch's last commit. It is created on that turn's first write, not at turn start. Work by other agents reaches a task only through canonical.
  - At the end of every turn, the core commits the changes it observed in that turn's scratch.
  - A temporary scratch is merged back as soon as its turn ends and nothing else is writing to the task scratch. If the task scratch has not moved, the core fast-forwards it. Otherwise the core computes a three-way merge with `git merge-tree --write-tree`, commits it if clean, and turns a conflict into work for the agent.
  - The task's next turn receives a note naming the files changed and the context that changed them.
  - Two turns in the same context never run at once (D22).
- **Amends D17:** a promotion may carry changes written from several of one agent's contexts. The ledger records the authoring context of each change; scratch events gain parent-scratch and authoring-context fields. D18 still governs canonical, and promotion checks still test the merged result.
- **Evidence:** core Appendix A.7.
- **Changes:** core §2, §3.5; Undercurrent §15.1.

### D24. Accept prompt-cache double payments

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner (Q3b, option a).
- **Decision:** When several of one agent's turns start together while the shared prompt prefix (tools and system instructions) is cold, each may pay the cache-write price. This is accepted; there are no staggered starts or pre-warm requests. Estimated extra cost per collision on Sonnet 5.5, assuming a 4,000-token shared prefix [INFERENCE]: $0.0092 with the 5-minute cache, $0.0152 with the 1-hour cache.
- **Not decided:** a separate cache record for the shared prefix, keyed by provider, model, cache scope and prefix hash, so that routing §9.2 stops predicting a prefix as cold that other contexts keep warm. Resolved by D51.
- **Changes:** routing §9.2, §15.

### D25. A turn sees a list of its agent's other active contexts

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner (Q3c, option ii).
- **Decision:** The prompt tail lists the agent's other active contexts by counterpart name or task title only, never their content. It sits after the cached prefix (routing R3), roughly 10–30 tokens per active context [INFERENCE].
- **Changes:** core §7.3; routing §5.1.

### D26. One core and one ledger per colony

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner (Q4).
- **Decision:** One core host process per active colony, the sole writer of that colony's ledger. Every event carries the colony's globally unique ID, so ledgers from different colonies can be combined for the owner's cross-colony experiments. In v1 a workspace root belongs to one colony. Cross-colony messaging needs its own design; core §7.2's "no cross-colony route by implication" stands.
- **Changes:** core §3, §3.2.

### D27. Windows first; portable to macOS and Linux

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner (Q5).
- **Decision:** Windows is the first target; the goal is portability across Windows, macOS and Linux. WSL may be used later only if it adds no slowdown. Step C10 (isolation boundary) therefore needs Windows mechanisms, not only the Linux ones the old step 15 listed.
- **Changes:** core §8; `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C10.
- **See also:** D87 and B11 specify the adopted one-slot macOS guest coverage and its host/guest limits; they do not claim measured A2.

### D28. Toolchain and cgo

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner (Q5, Q16 and the cgo question).
- **Decision:**
  - go1.27.1, installed permanently with the official installer; `go 1.27` in `go.mod`.
  - The core and terminal program never uses cgo. Every build states `CGO_ENABLED` explicitly, because Go's default switches cgo on when a C compiler is on `PATH`.
  - C compiler: llvm-mingw 20260922 (clang 23.1.2, UCRT, x86_64 host), pinned and installed **outside `PATH`**. Builds point Go at it through `CC` only for race-detector test builds and the GUI build.
  - Not TDM-GCC (last release May 2021) or Microsoft's compiler (cgo support is open as golang/go#20982).
  - Before the first GUI release, check the licences of toolchain libraries linked into a cgo build.
- **Reasons:** the race detector needs cgo and a C compiler; a native GUI on macOS and Linux very probably needs cgo [INFERENCE from the toolkit documentation]; cgo stops cross-compiling from one machine.
- **Evidence:** core Appendix A.9.
- **Changes:** core §8.1, §8.5; supersedes §8.1's "cgo-free where practical" for the core.
- **See also:** B11 preserves the cgo-free Core/terminal while selecting isolated GUI and signed macOS execution-helper build boundaries.
- **Amended by D106:** go1.27.2 replaces go1.27.1 as the pinned toolchain.
- **Amended by D147:** go1.27.3 replaces go1.27.2 as the pinned toolchain.

### D29. Dependency policy

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner (Q6, Q6b).
- **Decision:**
  - **Tiers.** The standard library is free. `golang.org/x/*` needs a decision-log entry. Any other module needs a record: function, licence, modules it brings in, change in binary size, maintenance status, minimum Go version, and how it would be removed. Test-only and tool-only dependencies (go.mod `tool` lines) need the same record but ship in no program. The core never uses cgo.
  - **Allowlist per layer**, enforced by the import-boundary test: Undercurrent packages use the standard library only; core and router packages use the standard library, `golang.org/x/*` and recorded exceptions; GUI packages use the chosen toolkit, recorded; separate helper programs (for example parsers) may use cgo.
  - **Write it or use a library.** Write it ourselves only when the needed part is small, fully specified and fully testable. Use a recorded library for anything security-sensitive, anything that differs across operating systems, and protocols or formats with many edge cases. Never write our own cryptography.
  - **Vendoring** is allowed when there is a reason, such as release or offline builds; vendored modules keep their records.
  - **Copied code** lives only in `third_party/<name>/`, with its licence, its source (URL and commit) and a record. Copied code is never mixed into our own packages.
- **Changes:** core §8.2.
- **See also:** B3 and B11 record scoped x/sys, x/term, go-winio and Fyne use plus the separate macOS helper boundary; these are narrow layer exceptions, not a general dependency expansion.
- **See also:** D132 records `golang.org/x/tools` v0.50.0, admitted only for `golang.org/x/tools/go/packages` in the Go analyzer helper program.

### D32. `Agent.md` spelling

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner.
- **Decision:** The identity file is `Agent.md`, exactly, everywhere. Linux file names are case-sensitive.

### D33. What the colony log holds

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner (Q9; recorded on the owner's instruction to write the described resolutions into the documents).
- **Decision:** One colony log, with one sequence, holds actions; workspace and validation events; turn starts and ends; approvals and their outcomes; budget reservations and settlements; messages and inbox processing (core phase 2); and router measurements (R11). Text streaming from a model during a turn is held in memory only, for the views watching that turn; the finished message is recorded. An agent's private context content stays in its directory, and each change is recorded in the log by hash, never by content. Message bodies, diffs and run logs go in the body store. The router's per-call decision record (routing §11) stays a separate JSON-lines file.
- **Resolves:** core §10 item 3's question about the action ledger and the session/view log.
- **Changes:** core §3.2, §4.1, §10.

### D34. Ledger storage: our own append-only log

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner (Q10).
- **Decision:** A log written with the standard library only. Segment files are named by the sequence number of their first record. Each commit, one record or several that belong together, is one write followed by a flush. Recovery truncates a half-written final commit. Lookup indexes are rebuilt in memory at startup; snapshots come later if startup replay is too slow. A SQLite index may be added later only on a measured trigger (startup replay time or index memory over budget), and it must be rebuildable from the log, which stays the only source of truth.
- **Evidence:** core Appendix A.8.
- **Changes:** core §3.2, §8.2; `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C1.
- **Amended by B3:** permit `golang.org/x/sys` only in the narrow Core OS durability/locking adapter; record framing, hashing, ledger contracts and replay stay standard-library-only. This does not claim platform crash testing has passed.

### D35. Record format and evolution

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner (Q11).
- **Decision:**
  - One JSON object per line, prefixed by its SHA-256; the last record of each commit is marked.
  - Envelope: format version; sequence; namespaced event type (for example `workspace.promotion_accepted`) and type version; event time; colony, agent, execution-session, context and turn IDs; actor kind (owner, agent, core, runner); measurement path; operation ID; previous record's hash; payload; body references.
  - Rules: a written record is never rewritten. New fields are optional, and a reader treats a missing field as absent, never as a default. A breaking change gets a new type version, with upgraders applied on read and tested against stored samples of every past version. Each reader declares the types it consumes and skips the rest. A consumed type that a reader cannot decode stops it with a clear error.
- **Changes:** core §3.2; `CONTRACTS.md` (ledger-format); `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C1.

### D36. Integrity and retention

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner (Q12).
- **Decision:** SHA-256 for bodies and for the record chain; git object IDs stay in each repository's own format (git 2.55 still creates SHA-1 repositories by default). Each record carries the previous record's hash from the start; external anchoring of the latest hash waits for assurance level A3. The log is never deleted or compacted; old segments may be compressed. Bodies may be garbage-collected under an operator policy, so everything replay needs lives in the record itself: changed paths and their hashes, check digests, outcomes, measurements.
- **Resolves:** Undercurrent Q16.
- **Changes:** core §3.2; Undercurrent §17.

### D37. IDs and time

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner (Q13).
- **Decision:** Every ID (colony, agent, session, context, turn, operation, message) is a UUIDv7 from Go 1.27's standard-library `uuid.NewV7()`. Names are separate from IDs and never grant authority. Each colony has a gapless integer sequence, the only order replay uses. Event time is UTC Unix nanoseconds at observation, never decreasing within a colony; a time held back because the system clock moved backwards is flagged. Durations come from the monotonic clock. In-flight work is a start and an end sharing an operation ID; after a crash the core writes an `interrupted` end for every unmatched start and never assumes success.
- **Changes:** core §3.2; `CONTRACTS.md` (ledger-format).

### D38. Where colony data lives

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner (Q14).
- **Decision:** Default root `%LOCALAPPDATA%\dwarf-harness\colonies\<colony>` (Local, not Roaming), `~/.local/share/dwarf-harness` on Linux and `~/Library/Application Support/dwarf-harness` on macOS, with a per-colony override. Layout: `colony.json`, `lock`, `log/`, `bodies/`, `agents/<name>-<short id>/` (`Agent.md`, `Memory.md`, `contexts/<id>/`, `audit/`), `workspaces/`, `scratch/<short id>/`, `runs/<short id>/`. Scratch and run directories use short IDs because git's `core.longpaths` is unset by default. For private memory, the agent directory holds the content and the log record is the commit point; on restart a mismatch is reported, never guessed.
- **Changes:** core §3.2.

### D39. Two programs

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner (Q15, option a; recorded on the owner's instruction).
- **Decision:** `dwarf-harness` is the colony core host plus the terminal, always built without cgo. `dwarf-harness-GUI` is a display client only: if no core runs for its colony, it starts `dwarf-harness` as that colony's host, then attaches over the local connection (core §3.4). It holds no colony logic and no API keys. The two check protocol versions when they connect. A GUI crash cannot stop a turn.
- **Settles:** core §6.1's packaging question.
- **Changes:** core §3, §3.1, §6.1, §8.5.
- **See also:** B11's signed macOS execution helper is a bounded platform executor, not another public launcher, GUI dependency or Core.

### D40. Contracts between roadmaps

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner (Q17; recorded on the owner's instruction).
- **Decision:** A contract is a standard-library-only Go package, golden sample files for every type version, and an entry in `CONTRACTS.md` (owning step, version, status, dependent steps). The initial contracts are the ledger record format and event catalogue, the ledger reader interface, the launcher command and event protocol, the context-module interface, and the law file format. A draft changes freely. The owner freezes a contract; other roadmaps build against it only once frozen. A frozen contract changes only by addition; a breaking change needs a decision entry listing the steps it affects, and the golden tests catch one made without it.
- **Changes:** new `CONTRACTS.md`.
- **See also:** B2 stages event-catalogue freezes by producer readiness; D40's owner-controlled freeze and compatibility rules remain.
- **Amended by D125:** the project manager freezes a completed contract slice once the build advisor has approved it, without the owner's approval; a breaking change to a frozen contract still goes to him.

### D41. Several agents in one repository

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner (Q19; recorded on the owner's instruction).
- **Decision:** The owner assigns one agent per step, or per part of a step where a roadmap splits it. Each agent works in its own git worktree and branch. To integrate, it rebases onto `main`, runs the full check, and fast-forwards `main`; `main` only moves forward. An agent may fast-forward `main` itself, except for changes that touch a frozen contract, an owner decision, or a new dependency, which wait for the owner. A blocked agent stops at its step's stop point and sets the step's status to blocked. It never writes a stub or a fake of what it is waiting for; tests may use stand-in data only against a frozen contract.
- **Changes:** `ROADMAPS.md`; `CLAUDE.md` at C0.

### D42. Check command and CI

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner (Q20; recorded on the owner's instruction).
- **Decision:**
  - One command, `go run ./tools/check`, which runs: gofmt, `go vet`, staticcheck and govulncheck; the import-boundary test; tests with `CGO_ENABLED=0`; tests again with `-race`, using the pinned compiler (D28); and Linux and macOS builds with `CGO_ENABLED=0`.
  - Tools are pinned with go.mod `tool` lines (Go 1.24 and later). staticcheck 2026.2.1 (MIT) and govulncheck are recorded as tool dependencies under D29.
  - Test tooling: `testing/synctest`; Go's native fuzzing (for example truncating ledger segments); rapid v1.3.0 (MPL-2.0) as a recorded test-only dependency; golden samples for contracts.
  - The check gates fast-forwarding `main`, not every commit.
  - A private GitHub remote with CI on Windows, Linux and macOS exists no later than step C3, because job objects, process groups and `Pdeathsig` behave differently per OS and cross-compiling only proves that code compiles.
- **Changes:** `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C0, C3.
- **Amended by D122:** the tests and race steps run with a 20-minute limit per package.
- **Amended by D129:** slice merges run the check without its race step (`-race=false`); each ticket's last code slice runs it in full.
- **See also:** D133 records the CI this entry requires: its workflow, its pinned downloads, its budget and when C3 may start.
- **Amended by D137:** the vet, staticcheck and govulncheck steps run for every supported target, not only the host.
- **Amended by D145:** the GitHub remote becomes public once the owner makes it so; CI stays paused under D143.

### B1. Line endings

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** build, at repository creation.
- **Question:** This machine's system git configuration sets `core.autocrlf=true`, so files on disk can differ byte-for-byte from what git stores. Host-computed content hashes (D23, D35, D36) and gofmt output need one line-ending convention from the first commit.
- **Decision:** `.gitattributes` contains `* text=auto eol=lf`: text files are stored and checked out with LF on every platform. Repositories that agents work on are not changed by this; how C2 handles their line endings is a C2 question.
- **Changes:** `.gitattributes`.
- **See also:** D74 selects Git-compatible canonical candidate contents under recorded workspace normalization settings for repositories agents work on; it does not extend B1's LF-only rule to them.

### D43. Module path

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner.
- **Decision:** The module path is `github.com/Darius-PJ/dwarf-harness`. The owner's GitHub account is `Darius-PJ`, already set up over SSH; the remote itself is created per D42. This closes D30's open item.
- **Changes:** `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C0.

### D44. A nested module for the GUI

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner (walkthrough Q21, option b).
- **Decision:** The root module holds the core, router, Undercurrent, the contracts and `dwarf-harness`. The GUI is a nested module at `gui/` with its own `go.mod`, module path `github.com/Darius-PJ/dwarf-harness/gui`, holding `dwarf-harness-GUI`; it depends on the root module and may import only its `contract/...` packages (D45). A committed `go.work` joins the two modules for local development; Go's modules reference allows this when the modules "are developed exclusively with each other". The check also tests the GUI module with `GOWORK=off`, so the workspace file can never mask a missing version requirement. The toolkit and its cgo therefore never enter the root module, and `go build ./...` in the root never touches the GUI.
- **When:** the `gui/` module and `go.work` are created when the GUI roadmap starts; until then the root module is all there is.
- **Changes:** `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C0; the GUI roadmap when written.

### D45. Package layout and import rules

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner (Q22, Q23).
- **Layout of the root module:**

  ```
  go.mod  go.sum  go.work (once gui/ exists)
  .gitattributes  CLAUDE.md  README.md
  cmd/dwarf-harness/        main package: colony host plus terminal (D39)
  contract/                 the contracts (D40); standard library only
    ledger/                 record format, event catalogue and reader (C1)
      appender/              restricted per-colony append interface (C1; D50)
    launcher/               command and event protocol (C7)
    module/                 context-module interface (R1)
    law/                    law-file format (U1)
  internal/core/            ledger store, workspace, runtime, providers, tools, validation, broker, host
  internal/router/
  internal/undercurrent/    law, graph, engine, perceive, render, sim (Undercurrent §13.1)
  tools/check/              go run ./tools/check (D42), including the import-boundary test
  third_party/              only if D29's copy rule is ever used
  docs/
  gui/                      nested module (D44)
  ```

  - The three layers live under `internal/`, so nothing outside the module can import them. Their public faces are the contracts and the `main` packages.
  - Package names inside a layer are chosen by the step that creates them, following Go convention; no decision entry is needed to add a package inside a layer.
  - OS-specific code uses Go's file-name suffixes. Tests live beside the code; each contract's golden samples live under `contract/<name>/testdata/`.
  - `go.mod` declares `go 1.27` and `toolchain go1.27.1`; the check runs with `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`.
- **Import rules, enforced by the import-boundary test in `tools/check`:**

  | Package tree | May import, besides the standard library |
  |---|---|
  | `contract/...` | Nothing else |
  | `internal/undercurrent/...` | `contract/...` only; appender contract only in `internal/undercurrent/graph/...` (D50) |
  | `internal/router/...` | `contract/...`, `golang.org/x/...`, recorded exceptions; may use the appender contract (D50) |
  | `internal/core/...` | `contract/...`, `internal/router/...`, `internal/undercurrent/...`, `golang.org/x/...`, recorded exceptions; may use the appender contract (D50) |
  | `cmd/dwarf-harness` | `internal/core/...`, `contract/...` |
  | `tools/...` | `golang.org/x/...`, recorded tool dependencies |
  | `gui/...` | `contract/...`, the toolkit, recorded exceptions; never `internal/...` |
  | Test files | The above plus recorded test-only dependencies |

  - The dependency direction is one way: `cmd` → `core` → `router` and `undercurrent` → `contract`. The router and the graph service may request appends through the restricted interface in `contract/ledger/appender`; the single per-colony appender and concrete ledger writer remain core-owned (D50).
  - This refines D29: Undercurrent may import the in-module contract packages, which are themselves standard-library only.
  - Go's `internal` rule is checked by import path, so it would not stop the nested GUI module; the import-boundary test is what enforces the `gui/...` row.
  - The check runs `go list -deps` per package and compares every import against this table, which lives in `tools/check` as code. A new row or exception needs a decision entry (D29).
  - **Amended by D50:** the restricted appender contract may be used only by core, router and `undercurrent/graph`.
- **Changes:** `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C0; core §8.2. Amended by D50 for the restricted appender contract.
- **Amended by D106:** `go.mod` declares `toolchain go1.27.2`.
- **Amended by D132:** a new top-level tree `helpers/` for separate helper programs, and a row for the first: `helpers/go-analyzer` may import, besides the standard library, `golang.org/x/tools/go/packages` only.
- **Amended by D147:** `go.mod` declares `toolchain go1.27.3`.

### D46. When the full check must pass (amends D31)

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner (Q24, option b).
- **Decision:** An agent commits on its own branch whenever the tests it touched pass. The full check (`go run ./tools/check`) must pass before `main` moves, and `main` moves only by fast-forward (D41). D31's "commits when the check passes" is read this way; D42's gate is unchanged.
- **Changes:** `CLAUDE.md` at C0; `ROADMAPS.md` working rules.
- **Amended by D128:** a commit that changes only Markdown files since a commit that passed the full check moves `main` after the document check instead (links resolve, LF).
- **Amended by D129:** a slice merge's check skips the race step; each ticket's last code slice runs the full check.

### D47. Validation parameters belong to the versioned law (Q25a)

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner.
- **Decision:** The flake-rate prior, its confidence and the quarantine threshold are versioned validation parameters in Undercurrent's `law-format`, owned by U1. C4 consumes the frozen parameters rather than defining a second policy source; C4 starts only after C3 and U1 are done and the owner has frozen `law-format`.
- **Changes:** `DESIGN-UNDERCURRENT.md` §13.4; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C4; `ROADMAP-UNDERCURRENT.md` U1; `ROADMAPS.md`.
- **See also:** D52 retains this order and separates testing-only runtime completion from the validated-delivery gate.
- **Amended by B2:** C4 waits for completed U1.L law-format, its owner freeze and C3—not full U1.E engine completion. U1 remains the sole source of the validation parameters.

### D48. The workspace's `main` ref is canonical (Q26b)

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner.
- **Decision:** In each workspace registered with a running colony, `main` is the canonical ref. It is never checked out in a working tree; people and agents work on branches. The core advances `main` by compare-and-swap through the promotion path, with promotion authority belonging to the promoting context subject to the validation gate. Human changes use the same branch-and-promotion path. A direct Git move of `main` can bypass the gate and is outside the supported workflow; C10 must establish the enforceable boundary before the system claims protection against child processes. This does not relax D46: while developing DwarfHarness itself, this repository's `main` remains governed by the full-check integration rule.
- **Changes:** core §2, §3.5, §10; `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C2, C10; `ROADMAPS.md`.
- **See also:** D52 keeps promotion unavailable in C2/C3 builds and defers real colony work until C4's end-to-end gate passes.
- **See also:** B5 clarifies that a canonical Git ref movement becomes accepted work only through the durable Core ledger acceptance fact.

### D49. Durable cursors and live text use separate streams (Q27)

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner.
- **Decision:** Durable view cursors use the colony-wide ledger sequence. Streamed model text remains ephemeral and is delivered on a separate in-memory channel keyed by turn ID and delta index. A view attach atomically subscribes and returns the current text snapshot with its included delta high-water index; live deltas then continue above it without a gap. Streamed text is not replayable after the core process exits.
- **Changes:** core §4; `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C1, C7; `CONTRACTS.md`.

### D50. One core-owned appender serializes colony writes (Q28)

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner.
- **Decision:** One in-process appender per colony serializes appends and assigns the colony sequence. The core owns the concrete writer; components request appends through its contract. Only `internal/core/...`, `internal/router/...` and `internal/undercurrent/graph/...` may import/use that appender contract. The graph service emits graph deltas through the core-owned appender; other Undercurrent packages remain ledger readers.
- **Changes:** `DESIGN-CORE-AND-LAUNCHERS.md` §§3.2, 8.2; `DESIGN-UNDERCURRENT.md` §§4, 15.1; `CONTRACTS.md`; `DECISIONS.md` D45 import rules; `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C1–C3; `ROADMAP-ROUTER.md` R1; `ROADMAP-UNDERCURRENT.md` U3; `ROADMAPS.md`.

### D51. Shared prefix cache state is one router record (Q29; resolves D24)

- **Date:** 2026-10-02. **Decided by:** owner.
- **Decision:** R0 keeps one shared-prefix cache record per provider, model, cache-isolation scope and prefix hash, separate from each context's private history/cache record. The router may use it to price the tools-plus-system prefix; it never grants access to another context's history or claims a cross-context history hit. D24's accepted cache-write double-payment behavior remains unchanged.
- **Changes:** routing §9.2, §15; `ROADMAP-ROUTER.md` R0; `DECISIONS.md` open items.

### D52. Path A: testing-only agents until validated promotion is proven

- **Date:** 2026-10-03. **Decided by:** owner, after reviewing the sequencing tradeoffs.
- **Decision:** Adopt Path A with its refinements: C2 builds workspace and promotion mechanics; C3 builds the agent runtime; C4 implements and proves real validation-gated promotion. D47 stands: C4 still waits for C3, U1 and the owner-frozen `law-format`. There is no earlier, weaker validator or duplicate law policy.
- **Use before the gate:** Harness agents are solely for testing until C4's end-to-end promotion gate passes and C4 is Done. Real colony assignments and delivery wait until then; private test output is not colony delivery. Authorized live-provider tests and otherwise-unblocked router, broker and launcher development may proceed on test workloads. This does not restrict engineers developing DwarfHarness itself under D41/D46.
- **Fail closed:** C2/C3 builds report promotion as unavailable, change no canonical ref through that request and record no accepted promotion. No permissive validator, temporary bypass or direct-ref workaround is allowed. Mechanical compare-and-swap tests use disposable repositories and are not accepted promotions; synthetic event fixtures remain subject to D41. C4 uses its real validator on test workspaces to prove both accepted and refused promotions before real work begins.
- **Decide the boundary before building it:** Before C2 or C3 implementation, the owner approves the promotion-boundary specification in Core C2. C2 owns workspace/promotion orchestration and the final ref mutation; C3 binds agent/owner requests and presents results; C4 owns validation evidence and the end-to-end integration gate. The specification must settle request/result lifecycle, exact candidate and evidence bindings, canonical movement during validation, cancellation and crash recovery including Git/ledger ordering, and initial check selection/no-check behavior without a permissive fallback. These details are not silently delegated to builders; this decision approves the requirement to settle them, not invented answers. C2 is *Not ready: design open* until that specification is approved, and still waits for C1's frozen contracts.
- **Separate acceptance gates:** C2 proves workspace mechanics and rejection when validation is unavailable; it does not claim successful validated delivery. C4 proves the exact validated tree is promoted, the recorded change set matches it, failing or otherwise non-passing required checks and stale canonical prevent acceptance, and the approved cancellation/recovery rules cannot duplicate a promotion.
- **Downstream handoffs:** U3's graph foundation may start after C2 and the frozen appender; its integration gate against real accepted promotions waits for C4. U3 is Done only after both stages. U4 consumes full U3; U5 consumes the completed graph foundation, so that promotion-specific integration does not unnecessarily block resource-pressure work. Existing R0, C6 and C7 start dependencies stay unchanged; their pre-C4 use remains testing-only.
- **Limits:** This is a build/use gate, not an OS security boundary; C10 still owns A2 isolation. C4 completion does not waive later governance, routing, field-release or isolation gates.
- **Changes:** `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C2–C4; `ROADMAP-ROUTER.md`; `ROADMAP-UNDERCURRENT.md` U3/U5 and dependencies; `ROADMAPS.md`; `CONTRACTS.md`; core design §3.5 and §9; Undercurrent cross-layer dependencies; validation research's current rollout notes.
- **See also:** D53–D59 settle lifecycle/recovery, snapshot, cancellation and request-identity policies; D77 amends the concurrency and integration model, and D78 requires performance feasibility. These do not complete the pre-build specification.
- **See also:** B2, B4 and B5 record the staged C1/U1.L prerequisites and selected C2 boundary mechanisms; owner approval remains pending as shown above.
- **See also:** D112 records the owner's approval of this boundary.

### D53. Promotion requests belong to the core, not the requesting turn (Q30)

- **Date:** 2026-10-04. **Decided by:** owner.
- **Decision:** The core durably records a promotion request and returns its request ID with a pending result. The requesting turn may finish while validation and promotion continue; results belong to the original context, never the view's current selection or another context. A caller may wait for convenience, but that wait does not own the request's lifetime. Closing a terminal or GUI is not cancellation.
- **Limits:** D52's unavailable-validator path still returns unavailable without changing canonical or recording acceptance; it does not queue work behind a nonexistent validator. Request identity/retry details, cancellation, candidate changes and result-delivery mechanics remain in the owner-gated specification.
- **Changes:** Core C1–C4 and source command/workspace boundary; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`; validation and Undercurrent lifecycle handoffs.
- **See also:** D56 fixes the submitted snapshot while scratch work continues; D57 separates promotion cancellation from turn cancellation and sets its authority; D58 settles request deduplication; D63 selects passive result delivery.

### D54. Serialize promotion pipelines within each workspace (Q31)

- **Date:** 2026-10-04. **Decided by:** owner.
- **Decision:** Each workspace has at most one active promotion validation-and-finalization pipeline. Requests queue in durable arrival order. Before spending validation effort on a queued request, recheck its expected canonical tip; refuse a stale request without running its promotion checks. The final compare-and-swap still guards movement during validation. Different workspaces can progress concurrently within the runner's limits.
- **Limits:** No automatic rebase or silent replacement of the candidate. Queue limits, admission details and the precise finalization/slot-release boundary remain open. The workspace promotion queue is distinct from C4's validation-run queue; this decision does not add an Undercurrent resource kind or set runner concurrency.
- **Changes:** Core C2/C4 and source workspace boundary; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`; validation and Undercurrent queue handoffs.
- **See also:** D59 holds the workspace promotion slot through durable finalization; D62 limits each context/workspace to one outstanding request. D77 supersedes whole-pipeline serialization and stale-original-base refusal with bounded parallel cumulative validation and one ordered durable finalizer; D78 records the performance-feasibility requirement.

### D55. After a crash, reconcile but do not restart promotion execution (Q32)

- **Date:** 2026-10-04. **Decided by:** owner.
- **Decision:** Recovery automatically reconciles facts and completes missing bookkeeping, never automatically restarts promotion execution. If the Git update is proven to have happened, record the actual result without applying it again. Requests proven not to have promoted end as interrupted, including queued requests; an agent or owner explicitly submits a new request to try again. This adds no human-approval requirement where the context already has authority. An unresolved effect blocks further promotions to that workspace rather than being guessed.
- **D37 remains:** Every unmatched operation start receives an interrupted end. An interrupted operation is not proof that Git was unchanged; the request's actual effect must be reconciled without rewriting history or duplicating promotion.
- **Still open:** Exact evidence sufficient to prove the update, Git/ledger ordering, request-versus-operation identity and state transitions, recovery records and downstream duplicate prevention. This decision selects restart policy, not a recovery implementation.
- **Changes:** Core C1–C4 and source recovery/workspace boundary; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`; validation and Undercurrent reconciliation handoffs.
- **See also:** D59 selects Git/ledger ordering and the conditional recovery mapping; D77 binds that mapping to the exact derived integration candidate and predecessor without authorizing post-crash execution retries.

### D56. Promote the submitted snapshot while scratch work continues (Q33)

- **Date:** 2026-10-04. **Decided by:** owner (option A).
- **Decision:** A promotion request names an immutable submitted candidate snapshot. Scratch work may continue under the existing D22/D23 writer safeguards while that request is pending; subsequent edits neither replace nor invalidate its candidate. Validation and possible promotion apply only to the submitted snapshot. A later correction in scratch does not prevent the older submitted snapshot from being promoted. Withdrawing that snapshot requires explicit promotion cancellation; replacing it requires a new request.
- **Consequences:** The accepted delta is the change actually promoted, not the scratch's later contents. Accepting the submitted snapshot does not deliver or authorize deletion of later scratch edits.
- **Still open:** Exact snapshot capture and identity, evidence bindings, object retention, and post-promotion scratch base/closure handling. This decision does not choose those mechanisms or change D23's commit/merge rules.
- **Changes:** Core C2–C4 and source workspace boundary; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`; validation and Undercurrent candidate/observer handoffs.
- **See also:** D77 preserves immutable original submissions but amends submitted-snapshot-only validation/promotion: each derived integration candidate is separately identified, validated and attributed.
- **See also:** B4 defines bounded checkpoint ancestry without mutating the immutable original submission.
- **See also:** B23 settles object retention for ticket 05's sealed trees and scratch anchors: refs under `refs/dwarf-harness/`, each released by its owner.

### D57. Promotion cancellation is separate and request-specific (Q34)

- **Date:** 2026-10-04. **Decided by:** owner (option A, with the proposed cancellation authority).
- **Decision:** Cancelling a promotion requires a separate request-specific command. Neither normal completion nor explicit cancellation of the originating turn cancels its submitted promotion requests; closing or disconnecting a view does not cancel them either. The submitting context and colony owner may cancel a promotion. A supervisory role gains no cancellation authority merely from its position.
- **Limits:** Cancellation cannot undo a Git update already applied. The exact irreversible cutoff, the race with finalization, command/result shape and cleanup semantics remain in the owner-gated specification; this decision does not promise cancellation at every pending stage.
- **Changes:** Core C2–C4/C7 and source command/workspace boundary; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`; validation and Undercurrent lifecycle handoffs.
- **See also:** D59 closes cancellation when the finalization intent commits durably and orders the race against that transition.

### D58. Deduplicate promotion submissions by request ID, not content (Q35)

- **Date:** 2026-10-04. **Decided by:** owner (recommendation A).
- **Decision:** The same request ID and same immutable submission retrieve the original request's current or terminal state; they never enqueue or execute another request. Reusing an ID with different submission content, candidate or context is a mismatch error. Different IDs remain separate requests even when their candidates match; there is no automatic content-based merging or aliasing.
- **Retries and authority:** An interrupted or cancelled request is not reopened by repeating its ID. An explicit new attempt uses a new request ID, preserving D55's no-automatic-retry rule. Knowing an ID grants no access or cancellation authority; D53's context binding and D57's authority still apply. Validation-evidence reuse is a separate policy, not a consequence of request deduplication.
- **Still open:** Exact immutable submission fields and request/operation schema, ID issuance and retention, and result-delivery mechanics. These do not reopen the identity behavior selected here.
- **Changes:** Core C1–C4/C7 and source command/workspace boundary; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`; validation and Undercurrent request/replay handoffs.
- **See also:** D77 distinguishes the immutable submission/request identity from derived integration candidates and their attempt generations; it preserves deduplication, terminal-request and access rules.
- **See also:** B4 preserves D58 request identity while distinguishing local checkpoint ancestry from the cumulative train predecessor.
- **See also:** B21 settles C1's part of D58's open items: the `ledger.command` identity tombstone, colony-wide UUIDv7 command IDs that never expire, and a 5-minute future bound that is Core policy, not frozen; submission bytes, the request schema and result delivery stay with C3, and D58's identity behaviour remains.

### D59. Durable finalization intent closes cancellation and orders recovery (Q36)

- **Date:** 2026-10-04. **Decided by:** owner (recommendation A, after the expanded comparison).
- **Finalization order:** After required validation succeeds, durably record the finalization intent identifying the submitted candidate, expected old canonical tip and bound validation evidence. Then perform the guarded Git update and establish its durability; then durably record acceptance; only then release the workspace promotion slot. The intent is not the earlier receipt of a queued request. No later promotion passes an unfinished finalization: a failed finalization records its outcome before release, and an unresolved effect keeps further promotions blocked.
- **Cancellation race:** Core orders cancellation against the durable intent transition. If cancellation wins, that request cannot subsequently update canonical. Once the intent commits, cancellation reports too late, even if Git has not changed yet; this is not an acceptance or success result. There is no post-intent cancellation abort path. D57's authority is unchanged.
- **Recovery prerequisites:** Reconcile an unfinished intent only with an intact durable intent and matching validation evidence, exclusive workspace mutation ownership, any previous Git process stopped or fenced out, and assurance that no later promotion passed the unfinished one. The expected-old and candidate tips must differ; no-op handling remains a separate open case. Merely finding a candidate commit in Git, or reading `main` without these prerequisites, is not proof of a request's effect.
- **Recovery mapping:** With those prerequisites, the expected old tip still canonical means interruption without retry; the submitted candidate canonical means complete missing acceptance bookkeeping without another Git update. Inconsistent state or insufficient proof blocks further promotions. Existing durable acceptance is not appended or applied again. Preserve D37's interrupted operation ends and reconcile the actual request effect separately, without rewriting history.
- **Durability and threat limits:** Git and the ledger are not atomic together. Candidate objects, references and ledger records require explicit durability handling and platform verification; process-crash reasoning alone is not a power-loss or damaged-storage guarantee. This supported-workflow protocol is not protection against same-user ref manipulation before C10.
- **Still open:** Exact schemas and request/operation representation, bound-evidence fields and invalidation policy, no-op handling, platform durability/fencing mechanisms, cancellation cleanup, and downstream replay/checkpoint mechanics. The ordering, cancellation cutoff, slot-release rule and conditional recovery mapping above are settled, not builder choices.
- **Changes:** Core C1–C4/C7 and source recovery/workspace/command boundary; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`; validation and Undercurrent acceptance/reconciliation handoffs.
- **See also:** D71 removes identical file trees from ref-changing finalization. D77 applies that comparison to the proven accepted predecessor and binds the one serialized durable finalizer to the exact derived integration target, not necessarily the original submitted tree.

### D60. Candidate eligibility includes tracked changes and non-ignored new files (Q37)

- **Date:** 2026-10-04. **Decided by:** owner (option A).
- **Decision:** The host-derived candidate includes tracked-file modifications and deletions, plus new files not excluded by the workspace's ignore rules. Already-tracked files remain included even if an ignore rule matches them; ignored untracked files are excluded. The mutable agent-controlled Git index is not the authority for eligibility or content. Later scratch edits do not change the admitted snapshot (D56).
- **Still open:** Exact capture sequencing, trusted tracked-file baseline and ignore-rule input representation, normalization and observation mechanisms. File eligibility is settled here, not those mechanisms.
- **Changes:** Core C2/C4 and source workspace boundary; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`; validation and Undercurrent candidate handoffs.
- **See also:** D70 selects immediate capture-busy/protected capture. D74–D76 settle canonical content representation, pinned local/global exclusions and captured scratch `.gitignore` rules; the trusted tracked baseline and exact input/capture/evidence mechanisms still require specification.

### D61. Policy changes justify fresh validation; activation mechanics remain open (Q38 direction)

- **Date:** 2026-10-04. **Decided by:** owner, as guidance while requesting further explanation of Q38.
- **Direction:** The owner considers extra validation work worthwhile when validation policy changes and expects such changes to be improvements. The remaining design should honor changed requirements rather than preserve superseded validation merely to save time. This is an owner expectation and cost tradeoff, not proof that every policy change is correct.
- **Not yet selected:** Overnight/designated-time activation was suggested for discussion, not approved as a complete mechanism. Activation timing, draining versus switching with revalidation, treatment of queued/running requests, binding timing, evidence reuse, and whether fresh validation stays within the same request still await the owner. No option A/B/C from the original Q38 is recorded as selected.
- **Limits:** D47's versioned law ownership, D55's no-automatic-retry rule after crashes and D59's finalization boundary remain unchanged; neither the law-activation emitter nor its authority is assigned by this guidance.
- **Changes:** Core C2/C4 and source policy-binding open questions; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`; validation and Undercurrent law/lifecycle handoffs.
- **See also:** D64 selects controlled activation followed by fresh validation of affected pre-intent requests using the same candidate and request ID; exact scheduling, authority and implementation mechanisms remain open.

### D62. One outstanding promotion per context and workspace (Q39)

- **Date:** 2026-10-04. **Decided by:** owner (option A), to avoid unnecessary snapshots.
- **Decision:** Each work context may have at most one outstanding promotion request per workspace. A different new request receives busy until the existing request has a final outcome or is successfully cancelled; it neither replaces nor merges with the existing request. Same-ID/same-submission retries still retrieve the existing state under D58. Serialize this admission check before creating an additional snapshot, so refused extra submissions do not capture unnecessary snapshots, including when submissions compete.
- **Scope:** Other contexts can each submit; D54 still serializes the workspace's promotion pipelines. Work on the scratch may continue under D56. Workspace-wide capacity, busy-response schema/ID persistence and the admission reservation/recovery mechanism remain open.
- **Changes:** Core C2–C4/C7 and source request/queue boundary; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`; validation and Undercurrent admission handoffs.
- **See also:** D77 supersedes D54's serialization cross-reference, not this admission limit. Reconcile bounded admission with every-change burst demand before the C2 boundary is ready; busy responses are not completed work (D78).
- **Amended by B4:** admit up to four independently identified pending checkpoints per declared code-working context/workspace in a bounded FIFO; keep admission-before-capture and D70 busy-without-capture. Four is a calibration envelope, not a colony-size limit.

### D63. Promotion results are passive by default (Q40)

- **Date:** 2026-10-04. **Decided by:** owner (option A).
- **Decision:** Make durable terminal results immediately available through status/events and surface them in the original context's next naturally scheduled turn. Completion alone does not create a follow-up model turn. An explicitly waiting caller may continue normally, and the context may query its request sooner. Results never migrate to another context.
- **Limits:** Exact notification, rendering and acknowledgment mechanics remain open. This does not prohibit independently authorized scheduling triggers, relax their budget/authority gates, or authorize automatic promotion retries after a crash.
- **Changes:** Core C3/C4/C7 and source command/context boundary; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`; validation and Undercurrent result/wakeup handoffs.

### D64. Activate changed policy, then revalidate the existing request (Q38a)

- **Date:** 2026-10-04. **Decided by:** owner (refined option B).
- **Decision:** At a planned applicable validation-policy change, prevent new finalization intents in affected workspaces. Allow finalizations already past D59's durable-intent cutoff to reach proven durable outcomes; unresolved effects delay safe activation. Activate the new policy version, then freshly validate affected pre-intent requests under it before finalization. Requests not yet validated start under the new policy. Activation need not wait for every old validation run to finish; draining all active pipelines first was the alternative, not the selected policy.
- **Identity and evidence:** Revalidation uses the same immutable candidate and request ID, without changing the original submission or capturing another snapshot. Keep earlier attempts and their evidence as history; do not overwrite them as failures or use superseded policy evidence to create a new finalization intent. The fresh validation binds the applicable policy. Existing accepted history is not retroactively invalidated.
- **Limits:** This is validation within an existing live request, not changed-input resubmission or a second promotion pipeline. D54 serialization, D58 retry semantics, D62 admission and D63 passive results remain intact. It does not rebase a stale candidate, include later scratch edits, reopen terminal requests or authorize automatic promotion execution after a crash (D55).
- **Still open:** Exact maintenance schedule/recurrence/timezone, policy scope and version-binding fields, activation authority/emitter/schema, durable hold/activation coordination, old validation-child lifetime and rerun sequencing, and freshness/reuse rules for separately changed check definitions or environments. No automatic nightly scheduler or guaranteed exact-time activation is approved here.
- **Changes:** Core C1–C4 and source law/evidence/request boundary; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`; validation and Undercurrent policy activation and attempt-history handoffs.
- **See also:** D65–D69 settle activation authority, scheduling, shared version, child exit and check/environment refresh. D77 applies refresh across parallel lanes while preserving original submissions and distinguishing requirement refresh from integration-candidate rebuilding.

### D65. Only the colony owner authorizes policy activation (Q41)

- **Date:** 2026-10-04. **Decided by:** owner (option A).
- **Decision:** The colony owner approves each exact validation-policy version for activation. Agents may propose changes, but authorship, supervisory position or a proposed edit grants no activation authority. Core executes and records the authorized transition; Undercurrent does not authorize its own law changes.
- **Limits:** U1 remains the sole source of D47's versioned validation policy. Approval representation, command/event schemas and concrete Core implementation placement remain open; this selects authority and the executing/recording component, not a frozen API or new dependency.
- **Changes:** Core C1–C4 and command/workspace boundary; U1 law activation and lifecycle catalogue; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`.

### D66. Activate immediately or at a one-off scheduled time (Q42)

- **Date:** 2026-10-04. **Decided by:** owner (option B).
- **Decision:** Support an authorized activation now or at one specified future time. The authorization and schedule identify the exact approved version, not mutable file contents read later. A scheduled time is not-before eligibility for the safe transition, never a guarantee of completion at that time or permission to bypass D64's finalization barrier.
- **Limits:** No recurring maintenance window, automatic nightly scheduler or activation merely because a law file changed. Timestamp/timezone representation, missed-time behavior, schedule replacement/cancellation and durable activation recovery remain to be specified. D55 still forbids automatic promotion execution after a crash.
- **Changes:** Core activation command/orchestration and ledger boundary; U1 law activation handoff; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`.

### D67. Keep one active validation-policy version colony-wide (Q43)

- **Date:** 2026-10-04. **Decided by:** owner (option A).
- **Decision:** The colony uses one active validation-policy version. Affected workspaces switch together after already-intended finalizations reach proven durable outcomes under D64; an unresolved finalization in one affected workspace delays the shared activation. Do not introduce a per-workspace rollout of different active policy versions.
- **Limits:** Workspaces may still have different required checks and execution environments. This does not settle the exact applicability/version-binding fields or durable cross-workspace coordination, and it does not require old validation children to finish before activation.
- **Changes:** Core C2/C4 activation coordination; U1 configuration, activation/replay and lifecycle catalogue; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`.

### D68. Stop superseded validation children before replacement runs (Q44)

- **Date:** 2026-10-04. **Decided by:** owner (option A).
- **Decision:** After activation, stop superseded old-policy validation children and confirm their exit before starting replacement validation for that request. If cleanup cannot be established, the affected request waits rather than overlapping old and replacement runs. Activation itself does not wait for old checks to finish merely because they are running.
- **History and identity:** Preserve old attempts, evidence and actual observed outcomes. Supersession or stopping an obsolete attempt is not a failed test, cancellation of the promotion request or permission to reopen a terminal request. The live request keeps its immutable candidate, original submission and ID.
- **Still open:** Exact stop/escalation, descendant-exit confirmation, cleanup and attempt-state mechanisms. D54 workspace serialization and runner resource accounting remain intact.
- **Changes:** Core C2–C4 process/validation sequencing and acceptance scenarios; validation research; U1/U4/U5 attempt and resource handoffs; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`.
- **See also:** D77 supersedes the D54 serialization reference, preserves child-exit confirmation and extends supersession handling to dependent integration generations.

### D69. Refresh pending work after changed checks or environments (Q45)

- **Date:** 2026-10-04. **Decided by:** owner (option A).
- **Decision:** Deliberately adopted host-side changes to required checks or execution environments use controlled cutover even when the validation law is unchanged: hold new finalization intents in affected workspaces, resolve already-intended finalizations to proven durable outcomes, activate the new requirements, then freshly validate affected pre-intent requests before finalization. Unresolved finalization effects delay that cutover. Not-yet-validated requests use the new requirements; superseded children follow D68.
- **Identity and evidence:** Keep the same immutable candidate, original submission and request ID; capture no new snapshot. Retain earlier version-bound evidence and outcomes as history, not sufficient evidence for a new intent under changed requirements. Binding and attempt records must distinguish the changed checks/environment even when the law version is identical.
- **Limits:** This concerns host-adopted requirements, not later scratch edits, merely available package releases or permission to substitute a different candidate. Stale canonical still causes refusal; D55 crash recovery and terminal-request rules are unchanged. It grants no new check-edit/retirement authority, erases no pinned historical failure check and requires no colony-wide uniform check set or toolchain. Exact bindings, change coordination and evidence reuse outside these mandatory refreshes remain open.
- **Changes:** Core C2/C4 evidence and activation boundary; validation research's reuse/governance distinction; U1/U3–U5 evidence, replay and runner-work handoffs; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`.
- **See also:** D77 permits explicitly derived integration candidates under the original request, replacing stale-original-base refusal and submitted-tree-only binding while retaining controlled requirement cutover and fresh evidence.

### D70. Return busy when exclusive capture cannot begin immediately (Q46)

- **Date:** 2026-10-04. **Decided by:** owner (option B).
- **Decision:** If safe exclusive capture against host-managed scratch writes and merge-back operations cannot begin now, return busy without capturing a snapshot, waiting for the writer or queuing capture. The caller explicitly retries later; do not stop a writer or automatically retry on its behalf. When capture can begin, exclude host-managed writes and merge-back during capture, then release that window so scratch work may continue before validation finishes.
- **Limits:** Preserve D62's admission-before-extra-snapshot rule, D56's immutable submitted candidate and D22/D23 writer rules. This is coordination, not C10 isolation against hostile processes. Exact exclusion/descendant handling, capture mechanics, busy schema/ID persistence and admission-reservation recovery remain open.
- **Changes:** Core C2/C3 capture/admission and C4 integration scenarios; source workspace/command boundary; validation and Undercurrent lifecycle distinctions; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`.

### D71. Identical file trees end as no-change, not promotion (Q47)

- **Date:** 2026-10-04. **Decided by:** owner (option A).
- **Decision:** With validation available and the expected canonical tip current, a host-derived candidate Git file tree identical to canonical ends with a durable terminal no-change result. This includes the exact same commit tip and different eligible commit tips whose histories differ but file trees match. Skip promotion checks and finalization: no finalization intent, canonical ref movement or accepted-promotion record. No-change is neither passing validation nor delivered work.
- **Limits:** Preserve unavailable/stale refusal, original-context results and D58 request identity: separate IDs are not coalesced. Retain scratch work and history; this authorizes no scratch cleanup or removal of later edits. D59's distinct-tip recovery remains for actual ref-changing finalizations, not a no-op update path. Exact no-change state/event representation and ordering mechanisms remain to be specified.
- **Changes:** Core C1–C4 result/finalization boundary and acceptance scenarios; source workspace/command boundary; validation and Undercurrent evidence/graph/field handoffs; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`.
- **See also:** D77 compares a derived integration tree with its actual durably accepted predecessor; redundancy with a speculative predecessor alone cannot produce terminal no-change.

### D72. C4 requires real automated checks (Q49)

- **Date:** 2026-10-04. **Decided by:** owner (option A).
- **Decision:** C4 requires a valid nonempty configuration of real declared automated checks and passing required evidence before promotion. Empty, unconfigured or invalid gates return unavailable, never vacuous success; a required check that did not pass cannot be waived by human approval. Do not add an owner-review fallback or human-oracle promotion mode to C4.
- **Scope and remaining choice:** Human-oracle verdict work stays in C5, where it is already planned; this does not itself approve owner-verdict-only promotion at C5. Q48 remains undecided: run every declared required check or use explicit host-controlled path applicability. The owner requested downstream analysis before choosing; neither option is adopted here. C4 still uses the per-workspace declared list without depending on U3, and no selection may turn absent validation into passing evidence.
- **Changes:** Core C2's pre-build gate, C4 configuration/acceptance and C5 placement clarification; validation and Undercurrent handoffs; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`.
- **See also:** D73 resolves Q48 after downstream analysis: the initial gate runs every declared required check, without path-based exclusion.

### D73. Run the full declared required-check set at the initial gate (Q48)

- **Date:** 2026-10-04. **Decided by:** owner (option A, after downstream analysis).
- **Decision:** For initial C4 non-no-op promotion, run every check declared required for that workspace, regardless of which paths changed. Acceptance requires actual passing, bound evidence for every required check. This means the deliberately declared required set, not every test that exists in the repository. Do not add path-based applicability or graph-based exclusion to the initial gate.
- **Scope:** Keep selection simple and conservative; conditional selection may be reconsidered only through separate approval justified by real check inventories and measured costs, with compatible evidence records. No future selector is approved here. C4 still does not depend on U3.
- **Limits:** D72's unavailable empty/invalid/unconfigured gate and no-human-fallback rule remain; D69 still requires fresh validation after changed required checks. This neither retires pinned failures nor relaxes repair confirmation, assurance or lineage requirements, and passing the declared set is not proof of general correctness. Exact check declaration/configuration and evidence bindings, execution mechanisms and run-time budget remain open; no statuses or contracts are advanced.
- **Changes:** Core C2 pre-build specification and C4 gate/acceptance scenarios; source workspace and validation planning; Undercurrent evidence/resource handoffs; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`.

### D74. Use Git-compatible canonical candidate contents (Q50)

- **Date:** 2026-10-04. **Decided by:** owner (option A).
- **Decision:** Capture candidate contents in Git-compatible canonical representation using recorded workspace normalization settings and applicable repository attributes. Honor repository conversion conventions rather than imposing this repository's LF-only B1 on other repositories. Raw on-disk observation hashes are distinct from Git candidate identity; a normal CRLF checkout need not become a Git content change solely because its stored representation is LF.
- **Limits:** Host-derived identity, immutable capture and the full required gate remain D56/D60/D70/D73. Exact attribute/configuration inputs, normalization and materialization mechanisms, and content/evidence bindings remain open. This does not authorize arbitrary clean/smudge programs, settle external filter support, accept silent passthrough as proof of correct conversion, or establish C10 isolation. The proposed tracked baseline is not approved by this choice.
- **Changes:** Core C2 capture specification and C4 acceptance cases; source workspace/validation and Undercurrent identity handoffs; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`.

### D75. Pin explicit workspace local/global ignore inputs (Q51)

- **Date:** 2026-10-04. **Decided by:** owner (option A).
- **Decision:** Use explicit, host-managed workspace inputs for local/global ignore exclusions. Import/review existing local and global exclusions during workspace setup, preserving private-file exclusions rather than silently dropping them. Keep those inputs fixed until deliberately updated; unrelated personal Git configuration edits do not automatically change promotion capture.
- **Limits:** This does not authorize rewriting personal Git configuration, introduce a new validation-law source or alter D60's inclusion of tracked changes. Repository `.gitignore` files are a separate source governed by D76. Record the inputs used for a capture; later updates cannot rewrite an admitted candidate (D56). Exact input representation, import/update authority and mechanisms, and evidence bindings remain in the pre-build specification.
- **Changes:** Core C2 workspace setup/capture and C4 acceptance cases; source validation and downstream candidate handoffs; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`.
- **See also:** D112 settles the import/update authority: the colony owner alone.

### D76. Apply captured scratch ignore rules to the same submission (Q52)

- **Date:** 2026-10-04. **Decided by:** owner (option A).
- **Decision:** Repository `.gitignore` rules from the captured scratch, including edits in that submission, govern its new-file eligibility immediately; do not wait for those rule edits to reach canonical. New ignore rules can exclude new files in that capture, but cannot hide already-tracked modifications or deletions (D60).
- **Limits:** Local/global exclusions remain the pinned workspace inputs from D75. Preserve the actual rule inputs used with the immutable capture; later scratch/rule edits cannot mutate that submitted candidate (D56). D70's capture coordination remains. Trusted tracked baseline, exact rule/input representation, capture mechanics and evidence schemas are still open; no status, contract or dependency changes.
- **Changes:** Core C2 candidate capture and C4 acceptance cases; source workspace/validation and Undercurrent candidate handoffs; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`.

### D77. Bounded parallel validation with ordered durable finalization

- **Date:** 2026-10-04. **Decided by:** owner, after revisiting the single-pipeline capacity analysis.
- **Decision — supersedes D54:** Allow bounded concurrent validation within a workspace using an ordered cumulative integration train. For requests A, B and C, validate `M+A`, `M+A+B` and `M+A+B+C` concurrently, subject to workspace and colony runner/resource limits. Publish only the successful prefix in durable request order. Independent passes against old `M` do not validate the combined tree.
- **Submission and integration identity — amends D56/D58:** Keep the original submission, captured base/content/inputs and requesting context immutable. Record each explicitly derived integration candidate, its expected predecessor and attempt/generation separately; bind validation to that exact subject, required checks, environment and law. Advancing an authorized predecessor is not itself grounds to refuse the original request as stale: derive and validate the combined candidate instead. Surface integration conflicts without automatic semantic resolution. This is Core-owned integration for validation, not mutation of the submitted snapshot, inclusion of later scratch edits or early delivery into another agent's task scratch.
- **Finalization — amends D59:** Retain one serialized complete durable finalization sequence per workspace: bound passing evidence → durable intent identifying the derived target and expected predecessor → guarded fast-forward canonical update with durability established → durable acceptance → finalization-slot release. No downstream intent may precede its predecessor's proven durable outcome. Guard the actual integrated predecessor, never bypass a mismatched canonical ref or reuse evidence for an untested merge.
- **Invalidation and refresh — amends D64/D68/D69:** A failed, removed or cancelled predecessor invalidates affected descendant candidates and evidence. Rebuild from the surviving order and freshly run the full required gate; never carry withdrawn changes forward or publish a passing aggregate over a failed prefix. Confirm superseded child exit before replacement runs or materialization reuse. Preserve attempts and actual outcomes; supersession is not test failure. Requirement cutover still holds new intents, resolves already-intended finalizations and uses owner-authorized activation under D65–D67. The original submission/request remains fixed; refreshing a still-current integration candidate and rebuilding it after a predecessor change are distinct recorded causes for fresh validation.
- **No-change — amends D71:** Compare the derived integration tree with its actual durably accepted predecessor. Speculative redundancy may avoid unnecessary checks but cannot become terminal no-change until that predecessor is proven; if it disappears, rebuild the candidate. A proven no-change outcome still has no promotion checks, finalization intent, canonical update or acceptance, and preserves scratch work and request identity.
- **Recovery, cancellation and attribution:** Preserve D55's no automatic post-crash execution, D57's cancellation authority and D59's durable-intent cutoff, exclusive-writer/fencing prerequisites and exactly-once reconciliation. Recovery maps the expected predecessor and exact derived target, not merely the original submission; unresolved effects block further publication. Attribute B's accepted delta to `(M+A) → (M+A+B)`, not `M → (M+A+B)`, retaining D23 authoring provenance. A cumulative failure alone does not prove the newest request caused it; speculative passes are not delivered work.
- **Bounds and remaining specification:** Four lanes is an illustrative planning target, not a fixed/default count or measured capacity. Exact limits, scheduling, integration/conflict mechanics, identity/evidence schemas, object retention and durable coordination remain in the owner-gated C2 specification. D62's one-outstanding-request admission remains until explicitly amended; reconcile it and bounded queue/backpressure choices with every-change burst demand before claiming readiness. Do not silently coalesce or drop changes, introduce unbounded queues, or count busy refusals as throughput. D73's full declared gate, U1 law ownership, C4's independence from U3, testing-only boundary, statuses and contracts are unchanged.
- **Changes:** Core C1–C4/C7 and source workspace/validation design; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`; validation research; Undercurrent evidence, graph-attribution, replay and runner-resource handoffs.
- **See also:** B4/B5 distinguish hard local-parent ancestry from a foreign train predecessor and seal all speculative generation output until its exact predecessor is durably accepted.
- **See also:** B23 settles object retention for ticket 05's sealed trees and scratch anchors: refs under `refs/dwarf-harness/`, each released by its owner.

### D78. Performance feasibility is part of architecture decisions

- **Date:** 2026-10-04. **Decided by:** owner.
- **Decision:** Evaluate performance and resource feasibility when selecting architecture and concurrency policies, not as a later optimization. D54 had a strong correctness rationale but whole-validation serialization was not feasible for the intended every-change promotion workload; safe ordering must not unnecessarily serialize the expensive validation stage. A foundational safety argument alone is insufficient to establish that a design can complete its intended work.
- **Required reasoning:** State the workload, actual change/promotion arrival rate, steady and burst behavior, accepted throughput and latency, CPU/RAM/disk/process costs, shared-resource contention, speculative invalidation/revalidation and failure/recovery costs. Separate validation-attempt capacity from accepted-promotion capacity. Busy/stale refusals and work left with agents are not completed work; do not make the model feasible by silently assuming fewer changes need promotion. If capacity cannot sustain the intended load with headroom, revisit the choice before claiming feasibility.
- **Evidence and gates:** Label assumptions, calculated scenarios, historical measurements and current runtime measurements distinctly. Before freezing an affected design boundary, include capacity reasoning and a concrete measurement/acceptance plan; at the relevant implementation gate, exercise the actual concurrent path and report accepted throughput, backlog/latency and resource costs under representative steady, burst and rework conditions. Reassess limits and architecture from those results. This does not invent a numerical service-level target, claim an unimplemented benchmark passed or weaken validation, durability and recovery guarantees.
- **Current example:** The modeled 12/20-agent scenario assumes one change per agent per ten minutes, or 72/120 promotions/hour. At an unchanged 50/60-second run duration, one lane offers only 72/60 validation attempts/hour; four offer 288/240. These are ideal attempt ceilings, not measured or guaranteed accepted throughput; integration, finalization, contention and repeated validation reduce delivery. Core §3.5 retains the assumptions and sensitivity analysis.
- **Changes:** `ROADMAPS.md` decision-working rules; Core pre-build specification, C4 verification and source engineering/workspace guidance; related contract and Undercurrent performance/evidence handoffs. No readiness, dependency or contract status advances.

### D79. Literature-grounded authority guidance and modest-laptop efficiency

- **Date:** 2026-10-04. **Decided by:** owner, approving the authority research as starting implementation guidance.
- **Direction:** Preserve the full social/environmental vision while running efficiently on modest laptops with as few model calls as necessary. Record the literature-grounded authority recommendations in the roadmap; their proposed mechanisms are starting guidance, not frozen APIs or evidence of measured performance.
- **Guidance:** Separate proposing, authorizing, executing, observing and disclosing an action. Prefer small deterministic Core checks over per-action model deliberation or a remote authorization service; bind access to actor, context, operation and target, not rank alone. Where delegation is authorized, narrow rather than enlarge grants. Preserve current authority at effect/admission boundaries, including revocation and queued access; keep authorization revisions distinct from validation-law versions. Reuse existing ledger and execution boundaries rather than adding duplicate services or serializing whole activities.
- **Efficiency and evidence:** Count necessary model calls, tokens, money, latency and whole-process resource costs, including social, compaction and helper work.
