Prefer zero-model mechanical administration, passive status and bounded local work; do not remove intended social behavior or weaken safety merely to lower call counts. Apply D78's workload and runtime-evidence discipline on representative modest hardware; no numerical performance claim or shipping default is selected here.
- **Limits:** D22 reservations, D33/D50 ledger ownership, D57/D59 cancellation, D65–D69 policy activation and D77 parallel validation remain intact. Delegation authority, grant/revocation schemas and treatment of already-running work, private-to-field disclosure and C10 containment still need their applicable design decisions. No new contract, dependency, resource kind, readiness advance or implementation is authorized.
- **Next investigation:** Apply the same evidence-first approach to accounting uncertainty using other harnesses, operating systems, academic papers, industry practice and cybersecurity. The research itself does not select an uncertainty-release, retry, reconciliation or cross-colony budget policy.
- **Changes:** `ROADMAP-CORE.md` §1.1 owns the authority guidance and primary sources; `ROADMAPS.md` carries the cross-roadmap efficiency rule; `ROADMAP-ROUTER.md` applies it to local controls and model-overhead evaluation.

### D80. Private pressure and permission-scoped overlap hints

- **Date:** 2026-10-05. **Decided by:** adopted (the owner selected an assistant-written option; this is a paraphrase, not owner-originated wording).
- **Question:** Whether private work and shared-resource pressure may have any outward field effect.
- **Decision:** Permit a law-defined coarse pressure band and freshness for explicitly shared resources, and a permission-scoped content-free hint that work may overlap the recipient's own currently authorized region. Filter by current audience and scope before projecting or ranking. Do not disclose actor, task, private path, content, usage amount, credential/account identity or source handle.
- **Limits:** This intentionally reveals some existence, relevance and timing. Coarsening, aggregation and delayed sampling do not promise anonymity or noninterference; hostile same-user tools remain C10's concern. The field layer does not gain routing, budget, access or managerial authority.
- **Changes:** `FIELD-PHILOSOPHY.md` disclosure policy; `DESIGN-UNDERCURRENT.md` §§9–11; `ROADMAP-UNDERCURRENT.md` U4–U6/U9; `DESIGN-ROUTING-LAYER.md` §5.5. See B10, B13 and B14 for measurement/projection ownership.

### D81. Federated universes remain locally sovereign

- **Date:** 2026-10-05. **Decided by:** adopted (the owner selected an assistant-written option; this is a paraphrase, not owner-originated wording).
- **Question:** Whether Undercurrent spans independently administered colonies or only a trusted single operator.
- **Decision:** Include federation across independent operators through explicit enrollment, exact agreement on shared field laws and restricted source projections. Each colony keeps its Core, ledger, validation policy, budgets and authority. There is no implicit trust, transitive enrollment, global coordinator or shared ledger.
- **Limits:** Federation authenticates origin and agreed semantics, not the truth or assurance of a foreign observation. An operator controls its own exports/import grants; a recipient cannot erase information already disclosed to another operator. See B14 for the bounded transport and recovery mechanism.
- **Changes:** `FIELD-PHILOSOPHY.md`; `DESIGN-UNDERCURRENT.md` federation and law sections; `ROADMAP-UNDERCURRENT.md` U9; `ROADMAPS.md`.

### D82. Owner ceilings bound scoped task delegation

- **Date:** 2026-10-05. **Decided by:** adopted (the owner selected an assistant-written option; this is a paraphrase, not owner-originated wording).
- **Question:** Whether each delegated operation requires repeated owner consent or may use an expressly authorized narrower grant.
- **Decision:** The owner sets capability and budget ceilings. An explicitly authorized Orchestrator may issue a narrower, context/task-scoped grant within those ceilings; operations within a valid grant need no repeated consent. An expansion of action, target, budget, duration or delegation scope returns to the owner.
- **Limits:** A grant does not reserve spend, defeat current Core admission or give access to another context's private material. Revocation affects future admissions/disclosures under the existing operation/finalization rules; it does not rewrite completed effects.
- **Changes:** Core authority/grant design §§4, 7 and 11; `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C3/C6; `DESIGN-ROUTING-LAYER.md` §§5.3, 11. See B7 and B9.

### D83. Retired participants retain only delivered history

- **Date:** 2026-10-05. **Decided by:** adopted (the owner selected an assistant-written option; this is a paraphrase, not owner-originated wording).
- **Question:** What a participant may retain or receive after a relationship or role is retired.
- **Decision:** Preserve read-only messages already delivered to that participant and its private memories. Retirement stops future delivery, posting and successor access; unseen backlog stays inaccessible. Preserve holes: the retained record is not a grant to retrieve an entire historical prefix.
- **Limits:** No retroactive erasure of material already delivered into a participant's memory is promised. Ledger retention is not permission for broad history browsing.
- **Changes:** Core relationship/receipt handling §§7.3–7.4; `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C3/C6; `FIELD-PHILOSOPHY.md` privacy rules.

### D84. Grand roles coordinate their corresponding chains; the owner is apex

- **Date:** 2026-10-05. **Decided by:** adopted (the owner selected an assistant-written option; this is a paraphrase, not owner-originated wording).
- **Decision:** Each Grand coordinates only its corresponding chain and receives no blanket descendant-context or conversation access. Grand-initiated births require owner approval. A Grand mood notifies the owner through the durable operational route; notification is not ordinary action permission and does not await approval.
- **Limits:** Grand title, mood, hierarchy and notification do not create private-memory, tool, budget, clone or cross-context authority. Birth approval remains separate from a mood's self-initiation.
- **Changes:** Core hierarchy/birth/mood handling §§7.5–7.6; `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C6/C8; `FIELD-PHILOSOPHY.md`. See B8 and B13.

### D85. Memory inheritance selects only already-shareable lessons

- **Date:** 2026-10-05. **Decided by:** adopted (the owner selected an assistant-written option; this is a paraphrase, not owner-originated wording).
- **Decision:** A new agent starts with fresh private memory. An explicit birth manifest may select lessons or artifacts already shareable with that recipient; private relationship memory is never inherited by implication.
- **Limits:** Manifest inclusion does not itself authorize disclosure; validate every item against its existing audience grant. Identity/instruction composition and independently writable memory remain distinct from inherited authority.
- **Changes:** Core birth and manifest design §7.6; `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C8; `FIELD-PHILOSOPHY.md`. See B8.

### D86. Retirement exclusion is scoped to the repairing context and its clones

- **Date:** 2026-10-05. **Decided by:** adopted (the owner selected an assistant-written option; this is a paraphrase, not owner-originated wording).
- **Decision:** The C5 retirement-review exclusion covers the repairing context and its clone lineage, not every context belonging to that agent. An unrelated context of the same agent remains eligible unless it is independently within the excluded lineage.
- **Limits:** Context/clone identity is not proof of statistical or cognitive independence; all other evidence-admissibility rules remain. Do not recreate whole-agent exclusion through another field.
- **Changes:** `RESEARCH-ADMISSIBLE-VALIDATION.md` §4.6.1; `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C5/C6; `ROADMAPS.md` Corps-authority gate. See B6.

### D87. One demand-started disposable native macOS guest slot

- **Date:** 2026-10-05. **Decided by:** adopted (the owner selected an assistant-written option; this is a paraphrase, not owner-originated wording).
- **Decision:** Apple-silicon hosts provide one demand-started disposable macOS VM slot for native-macOS A2 checks; a separately budgeted Linux guest serves work that declares a Linux environment. Intel Macs provide the Core/GUI and Linux-guest path only; native macOS work is unsupported there.
- **Limits:** Native-macOS work queues at the one-slot capacity. A disposable VM is reset between private executions; it is not a shared dirty worker. This coverage choice is subject to the full workload and assurance gates, and does not establish that they pass. Preserve actual guest/host/toolchain fidelity and applicable Apple licensing; memory, disk and throughput remain unmeasured.
- **Changes:** Core C10; `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C10 and the native GUI/packaging roadmap; `ROADMAPS.md`. See B11 and B15.
- **See also:** D138 defers this slot and the Linux guest until after the first release.

### B2. Stage producer-ready contracts and split U1 capabilities

- **Date:** 2026-10-05. **Decided by:** assistant, within the owner's delegated technical roadmap research.
- **Decision:** C1 initially freezes the shared envelope/identities, framing, commit/hash rules and only fully specified C1-produced records. It adds producer-owned event families through versioned catalogue freezes with type/version, payload owner, producer, permitted appender callers, consumers, privacy class, replay-essential fields and golden sample. C1 does not freeze every future event or use empty placeholder payloads. C2 owns promotion/scratch families; C3 contexts/tasks/reservations; C4 check evidence; C5 governance; C6 broker/role records; R1 router measurements; U3 graph changes; Core C2 owns the activation transition while U1.L supplies policy meaning and C4 supplies check evidence. The existing six contracts, one Core-owned sequencer and D40 owner freeze remain.
- **Decision:** Split U1 into **U1.L** (strict law-format/loading capability) and **U1.E** (engine/replay capability). C4 requires completed U1.L, C3 and the owner-frozen `law-format`, not full U1.E. U1.E, U2's field simulation and U4/U5 engine-dependent work still require the engine capability and consumed ledger families. C3 must prove private-context isolation with test-only workloads before broad runtime use.
- **Limits:** Catalogue/spec readiness is not an operational producer, contract freeze, C4 gate or D52 approval. No seventh ledger/schema system is added. This amends D47/D52 only as to the full-U1 dependency; sole law ownership, D40 owner freeze and D52's testing-only boundary remain.
- **Changes:** `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`; Core C1/C3/C4 and Undercurrent U1/U2/U4/U5 capability handoffs.
- **Evidence:** Parnas, [On the Criteria To Be Used in Decomposing Systems into Modules](https://www.cs.colostate.edu/~france/CS314/Readings/Parnas-decomposition.pdf); Go [Backward Compatibility](https://go.dev/doc/go1compat).
- **See also:** D113 stages C2's F2 freeze in parts, each part before the code that writes it.

### B3. One authoritative durable ledger and bounded platform storage

- **Date:** 2026-10-05. **Decided by:** assistant, within the owner's delegated technical roadmap research.
- **Decision:** Preserve one Core and one append-only per-colony action ledger as the sole durable source for operations, accepted effects, reservations and recoverable state. One sequencer commits related records together; content-addressed bodies remain separate from the log. Rebuildable indexes/snapshots accelerate lookup/replay but are discardable and introduced only on measured triggers. Preserve exact record bytes, hash-chain validation, half-written-tail recovery, D36 retention and D58 deduplication from the authoritative segment catalogue on a hot-cache miss.
- **Amendment to D34:** Permit `golang.org/x/sys` only inside the narrow Core OS durability/locking adapter; ledger framing, hashing, contracts, parsing and replay remain standard-library-only. Keep Windows Defender scanning enabled: no `scratch/` or `runs/` exclusion is selected. Measure normal configurations; do not attribute historical latency to Defender without a controlled comparison.
- **Limits:** This does not promise power-loss durability, constant memory/disk or a passed platform gate. A cache miss is not proof of absence, snapshots do not remove genuinely live state, and indefinite history retention entails disk growth. D29 still requires scoped dependency records and removal paths.
- **Changes:** Core §3.2/§8.2 and C1; `CONTRACTS.md`; `DECISIONS.md` open-item index. D34 is amended narrowly; the Defender item is resolved as an assistant engineering selection, not an owner-originated instruction.
- **Evidence:** [Go `x/sys` v0.48.0 module metadata](https://proxy.golang.org/golang.org/x/sys/@v/v0.48.0.mod); [Git object and durability documentation](https://git-scm.com/docs).

### B4. Bound checkpoint admission and make ancestry explicit

- **Date:** 2026-10-05. **Decided by:** assistant, within the owner's delegated technical roadmap research.
- **Decision:** Amend D62 to a FIFO of at most four independently identified immutable checkpoints per declared code-working context/workspace. Each checkpoint keeps its own request ID, snapshot, checks and terminal result; admission remains before extra capture, and exhausted capacity returns busy. Four is a finite overlap/burst calibration for each enrolled pair, not a cap on agents or colony size; workspace slots scale with four times its actually declared code-working context/workspace pairs.
- **Ancestry:** Keep immutable original submissions and local checkpoint ancestry distinct from the cumulative workspace train. A root uses its Core-recorded scratch anchor. A successor's immutable latest admitted local parent's sealed tree is its trusted tracked-path eligibility baseline; its delta base is a separate binding even when both tree IDs coincide. A foreign speculative train predecessor never defines tracked eligibility. A failed, cancelled or withdrawn local parent terminates its dependent local suffix as `dependency_failed`; do not rebase a child in a way that silently reintroduces the parent's change. Removing a foreign train predecessor instead rebuilds surviving local submissions against the new train and reruns the complete gate. A new lineage root requires explicit resolution and a newly captured request.
- **Limits:** D70 remains immediate capture-or-busy: no queued capture, writer interruption or capture wait. Local-parent failure and foreign train-predecessor removal are not interchangeable. Four-checkpoint admission does not prove D78 throughput; see B15.
- **Changes:** Core C2/C4; `CONTRACTS.md` promotion-boundary readiness; `ROADMAPS.md`. Amends D62 and clarifies D56/D58/D77.
- **Evidence:** Git's [ignore rules](https://git-scm.com/docs/gitignore), [attributes](https://git-scm.com/docs/gitattributes) and [merge-tree](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-merge-tree).

### B5. Order cumulative validation, durable acceptance and disclosure

- **Date:** 2026-10-05. **Decided by:** assistant, within the owner's delegated technical roadmap research.
- **Decision:** Preserve D77's bounded parallel cumulative train (`M+A`, `M+A+B`, …) and one complete ordered durable finalizer per workspace; publish only the successful request-order prefix. Keep original submissions, derived integration generations, expected predecessors and validation attempts separately bound. Rebuild and rerun the full declared gate for surviving requests when a foreign predecessor changes; invalidate a local dependent suffix when its hard parent terminates. Acceptance is the durable Core ledger fact after the guarded Git update and its durability—not a ref movement, validation pass, finalization intent or UI state.
- **Disclosure barrier:** Every speculative generation's stdout, stderr, artifacts, receipts, diagnostics, verdicts and hashes stays host-private until that generation's exact predecessor is durably accepted. No model, agent, ordinary view, field projection or shared cache receives speculative output. A visible UI cannot substitute for an isolated execution/output compartment.
- **Limits:** Preserve D55's no automatic post-crash execution restart, D58 same-ID/same-submission lookup and mismatch error, D63 passive results/no completion-only model turn, D65 exact owner policy authorization, D68 confirmed child exit, D73's full required-check set and D77 every-change semantics. A durable acceptance record does not claim Git and the ledger are one atomic medium or prove power-loss safety.
- **Activation and reuse:** Core §13.2 selects one durable pending owner-approved request with exact UTC not-before and target/expected-manifest bindings, owner CAS cancellation/replacement, an atomic hold/cutover, and overdue restart that reconciles finalizations without restarting execution. Historical replay never invents activation. New validation attempts cannot reuse old evidence; same-ID receipt recovery is not a new run. D64–D69 policy is unchanged; C1 freeze and crash/race/clock/child-exit proof remain unrun.
- **Changes:** Core C2–C5/C10; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`; validation and Undercurrent evidence/disclosure handoffs. See D48 and D55–D78.
- **Evidence:** [Git `update-ref`](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-update-ref); [GitLab merge trains](https://docs.gitlab.com/ee/ci/pipelines/merge_trains/), used as a workflow pattern only. The GitLab project is canonically hosted at [gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab), is open-core, and is not a dependency.

### B6. Run the full declared check set under scoped governance

- **Date:** 2026-10-05. **Decided by:** assistant, within the owner's delegated technical roadmap research.
- **Decision:** C4 runs every valid, declared, per-workspace required check for each non-no-op candidate, regardless of changed paths; missing, empty or invalid configuration is unavailable, never vacuous success. Human review cannot replace passing automated evidence. C5 owns typed check registration, review and retirement records; the owner remains the authority, with the existing Orchestrator overturn path. Retirement evidence excludes exactly the repairing context and its clone lineage under adopted D86, not all contexts of that agent.
- **Limits:** A passing declared set is not proof of universal correctness; semantic review is fallible and does not become a required adversary for every task. C4 remains independent of U3 and waits only on U1.L law-format, not the full engine.
- **Changes:** Core C4/C5; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAP-CORE.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`. See D72/D73/D86 and B2.
- **See also (parent-selected P-V2 F1 boundary):** Undercurrent §9/§14.2 and U4 preserve authorized private diagnostics and explicit requests for an existing C4 reproduction; F1 seeds no local or federated field source, F2 is the minimum failure-field evidence, and this rule schedules no autonomous validation job or model/provider turn.

### B7. Use bound Core transitions for contexts, tasks, grants and broker state

- **Date:** 2026-10-05. **Decided by:** assistant, within the owner's delegated technical roadmap research.
- **Decision:** Keep one execution session per agent and private `(agent, context)` relationships, turns, memories and provider continuations. Core's short typed transition checks actor/context/operation/target, expected version and current grant, durably appends the bounded event group, then updates projections; expensive model, Git and check work runs outside it and returns with operation/generation identity. Task claims, assignments, transitions and evidence-bound completion are Core records with atomic version checks, not Markdown, a model-memory convention, queue/database or lease service. A task grant is an owner-ceiling-bounded, context/task-scoped capability issued only by an explicitly authorized Orchestrator; valid in-scope actions need no repeated owner prompt, while expansion returns to the owner (D82).
- **Delivery:** Retired participants may inspect only their already-delivered messages and private memories (D83). No unseen backlog, future delivery/posting, successor access or whole-prefix retrieval follows from a relationship, role or durable cursor. Core rechecks current authority at command, delivery and effect boundaries.
- **Limits:** Typed bindings and Core checks are not OS containment; C10 remains necessary for same-user children. No new broker/task/approval store, remote policy service, generic scripting engine or automatic uncertain-effect replay is introduced.
- **Changes:** Core §§4, 7 and 11; C3/C6; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`. See D21–D23, D82–D83.

### B8. Keep roles, births, stories and workflows typed and explicitly authorized

- **Date:** 2026-10-05. **Decided by:** assistant, within the owner's delegated technical roadmap research.
- **Decision:** C6 supplies explicit role/parent/relationship records and typed broker operations. C8 uses a durable birth proposal/approval/creation transition and independent child identity, session, directories and memory. A Grand coordinates only its corresponding chain; the owner approves Grand births; a mood notifies the owner without ordinary permission (D84). New agents start with fresh memory; a validated manifest may select only lessons/artifacts already shareable to the recipient, never private relationship memory by implication (D85). Griot publication uses authorized source references and bounded cadence; it does not turn private relationship content into a public feed. C9 recipes/workflows use versioned JSON schemas compiled to existing typed Core commands and acceptance rows, not scripts, hooks, callbacks or a second execution engine.
- **Limits:** A workflow is not authority, a budget grant, automatic agent creation or a hidden shell fragment. Griot/story limits remain calibration hypotheses; no call cadence or usefulness result is measured. C8/C9 design choices are documented, but dependencies and acceptance remain.
- **Changes:** Core C6/C8/C9; `ROADMAP-CORE.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`; `ROADMAP-GUI.md` C9 parity handoff.
- **Evidence:** Coordination schemas use existing D21/D22 and one Core; no Orleans, Temporal, GitLab or other workflow runtime is adopted.

### B9. Keep provider accounting hard-bounded and Core-owned

- **Date:** 2026-10-05. **Decided by:** assistant, within the owner's delegated technical roadmap research.
- **Decision:** Only Core's existing per-colony `BudgetBook` and durable ledger reserve, settle and correct exact money. Each attempt uses checked integer USD microdollars and an immutable `CostBound` derived from a versioned supported endpoint, mutually exclusive billable classes, documented finite maxima and exact rational tariff. The Core hold binds a sealed provider request, request/quote hashes, capability/tariff versions, `TurnBinding`, period and sorted de-duplicated colony, agent, credential/account-within-colony, task/grant and attenuated ancestor scopes. Every attempt reserves its full maximum before dispatch intent; a sent attempt lacking final usage stays fully held through restart/period rollover. One physical POST per attempt; no hidden retry or parallel accounting store.
- **Limits:** Provider credential scopes are local to one colony, not global invoice/provider caps. Paid dispatch requires explicit owner-selected finite ceiling(s) or explicit unlimited authorization plus a finite endpoint bound. Unknown or unpriced features fail closed; reported usage is not invoice proof.
- **Changes:** `DESIGN-ROUTING-LAYER.md` §§5.3, 10–11; `ROADMAP-ROUTER.md` R0/R1; `ROADMAPS.md`. See D22 and D79.
- **See also (router §§5.3/9.4):** Input and output maxima are independent and inclusive: never subtract requested output from the input bound, and count provider tool/system tokens once in input. The sealed request identity includes semantic application headers and the pinned non-secret credential-scope/version binding; the durable hold binds request/quote digests and scopes.

### B10. Use explicit bounded routes and adapter-versioned headroom

- **Date:** 2026-10-05. **Decided by:** assistant, within the owner's delegated technical roadmap research.
- **Decision:** Require an explicitly configured provider/model and supported capability; choose the neutral `balanced` profile without implying a vendor or price. Initially bound only direct standard Anthropic Messages and OpenAI Responses text requests; unsupported/unpriced modalities, hosted tools, extra iterations and tiers are unavailable. Provider handover is explicit and context-bound; no implicit failover. Embedding and paid LLM classifiers are off by default and any enabled classifier is a separately admitted Core attempt. R1 emits typed R11 observations at their true provider/account/model/region/context scope. R1 owns capability-versioned `router-headroom/v1` (`requests`, `tokens`, `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, saturated limits/remaining, band and freshness), not `law-format`; field applies its own authorized projection and normalization.
- **Limits:** Prediction/cache estimates are optimization inputs, never a spending bound. Field does not become budget, provider or permission authority. The R1 module and R11 catalogue remain unfrozen until their D40/C1 dependencies.
- **Changes:** `DESIGN-ROUTING-LAYER.md` §§5.3–5.5, 9, 11; `ROADMAP-ROUTER.md` R1; `CONTRACTS.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`. Resolves contract source items Q28 and routing question 7 without an R1→U1.L dependency.
- **See also (router §§5.4–5.5):** R1 publishes capability-versioned `router-headroom/v1` at the true provider/account/model/region/metric scope, with band and validity/renewal times; field receives only authorized coarse band/freshness (expired/unknown remains unknown), never raw counts/headers/private identifiers, and applies its own law projection.

### B11. Isolate native clients and operating-system adapters

- **Date:** 2026-10-05. **Decided by:** assistant, within the owner's delegated technical roadmap research.
- **Boundaries:** Keep `dwarf-harness`/Core and terminal cgo-free. `gui/` is a native display-client module behind only the launcher contract, selected Fyne v2.8.1 (BSD-3-Clause; module Go floor 1.22.0); no web renderer or internal Core imports. A separate small signed Swift/Objective-C macOS virtualization helper uses Apple's Virtualization framework; it is not a third launcher, GUI dependency, Core or ledger.
- **Recorded Go modules:** `golang.org/x/sys` v0.48.0 (BSD-3-Clause, Go 1.26.0, no module dependencies) is limited to Core OS adapters including B3's durability/locking. `golang.org/x/term` v0.46.0 (BSD-3-Clause, Go 1.26.0, requires x/sys v0.48.0) is limited to terminal mode/detection. `github.com/Microsoft/go-winio` v0.6.2 (MIT, Go 1.21) is limited to Windows local named-pipe IPC; its module metadata requires logrus, x/sys, x/tools and indirect x/mod. D28's Go 1.27.1 remains the project toolchain.
- **D29 records/removal:** As of 2026-10-05, Fyne and go-winio are not archived and their inspected project READMEs have no deprecation notice; x/sys and x/term are official Go supplemental modules with no observed deprecation. Record exact module graph, layer import boundary and binary-size delta under D29; Fyne's graphics/text/platform graph and size/cost are not yet measured. Remove an exception only after its named adapter is retired or replaced; Fyne removal requires an owner-approved replacement native launcher client and does not drop GUI functionality or the cgo/IPC/privacy boundaries.
- **Coverage:** Adopt D87's one demand-started disposable macOS guest slot on Apple silicon, with a separately budgeted Linux guest for Linux-declared work. Intel Macs have Core/GUI and Linux-guest coverage only. Preserve Windows AppContainer/Job Object and Linux per-run isolation proposals; each guest/run returns bounded receipts to the sole host Core. MacOS guest checks retain explicit host/guest/toolchain fidelity, pristine per-run disk/auxiliary state, positive VM-stop-before-slot-release, and no host home, credentials, provider keys, shared Git or writable cross-run cache.
- **External router helpers:** R5 reuses a qualified C10 compartment for external plugins/native helpers, with authorized immutable inputs, a bounded result channel, no Core private roots/credentials/general host IPC or provider egress, and positive descendant stop. A complete finite wall/CPU/memory/process/input/output limit profile is required; absent or unenforceable limits keep the extension disabled. First-party in-process Go stages remain in Core's trusted code. This adds no second sandbox or service and claims no completed C10 qualification.
- **Limits:** Do not claim A2 implementation, host-native equivalence, guest capacity or accessibility/performance parity. The Fyne choice does not prove screen-reader support or resource budgets; the GUI roadmap must verify keyboard use, non-color-only status, independent cursors, reconnect/live text and bounded rendering. Apple guest memory/disk and workload costs are unmeasured, and the current Apple SLA permits only stated purposes/hardware/VM counts; verify the applicable agreement. D34's `x/sys` durability adapter remains narrow (B3).
- **Changes:** D27–D29, D39 and D44 implementation boundaries; Core C7/C10; Router R5; `ROADMAP-GUI.md`; `ROADMAPS.md`. No third host, second Core or new OS helper service is selected.
- **Evidence:** [Fyne v2.8.1](https://github.com/fyne-io/fyne/tree/v2.8.1), [module metadata](https://proxy.golang.org/fyne.io/fyne/v2/@v/v2.8.1.mod), [BSD-3-Clause license](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/fyne-io/fyne/v2.8.1/LICENSE); [go-winio v0.6.2](https://github.com/microsoft/go-winio/tree/v0.6.2), [module metadata](https://proxy.golang.org/github.com/%21microsoft/go-winio/@v/v0.6.2.mod), [MIT license](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/microsoft/go-winio/v0.6.2/LICENSE).
- Go supplemental module sources: [x/sys v0.48.0 metadata](https://proxy.golang.org/golang.org/x/sys/@v/v0.48.0.mod) and [BSD-3-Clause license](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/golang/sys/v0.48.0/LICENSE); [x/term v0.46.0 metadata](https://proxy.golang.org/golang.org/x/term/@v/v0.46.0.mod) and [BSD-3-Clause license](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/golang/term/v0.46.0/LICENSE). Apple's [Virtualization framework](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/virtualization), [macOS-on-Apple-silicon requirements](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/virtualization/running-macos-in-a-virtual-machine-on-apple-silicon) and [macOS 27 SLA](https://www.apple.com/legal/sla/docs/macOS27.pdf).
- **See also:** D89 adds the owner's fortress/RPG art direction; B16 retains Fyne and selects an offline asset pipeline plus durable cosmetic presentation. Neither changes this entry's native-client or Core authority boundaries.

### B12. Separate shared field law, local validation and replay

- **Date:** 2026-10-05. **Decided by:** assistant, within the owner's delegated technical roadmap research.
- **Decision:** U1.L owns a strict typed JSON v1 `law-format`, finite parser profiles (`compact-v1` and `standard-v1`), validation and versioned law semantics. Hash the exact immutable Git-blob bytes with SHA-256: whitespace, line endings or key-order changes change identity; do not call ordinary JSON serialization canonical or add a canonicalization library. The shared field-law hash excludes private workspace, check, environment, grant and credential data. Core's existing `PolicyActivationManifest` binds the shared law identity with local C4 validation/check hashes in the one authoritative activation chain; the activation event is a Core/C2 fact, U1.L owns law meaning, C4 owns check evidence and C1 owns its record format. U1.E provides deterministic causal engine/replay against the frozen ledger families; snapshots remain discardable caches, never truth. U2/U4/U5 wait for the actual engine capability; C4 needs only U1.L.
- **Limits:** Law bytes and schema are specified in `DESIGN-UNDERCURRENT.md` §13.4; this decision does not freeze the contract, choose local check sets, activate a law or claim numeric cross-platform identity. Alchemist and Protelis are GPL-3.0-or-later with linking exceptions; their ideas are references only, not permissive dependencies or selected runtimes.
- **Changes:** `CONTRACTS.md` `law-format`; `ROADMAPS.md` capability edges; Undercurrent U1/U2/U4/U5 and Core C4. See D47/D52 and B2.
- **Evidence:** [RFC 8785](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8785) distinguishes deterministic JSON serialization from JSON as such; the selected law identity is instead the exact stored Git-blob bytes. [Alchemist license](https://github.com/AlchemistSimulator/Alchemist/blob/master/LICENSE.md) and [Protelis license](https://github.com/Protelis/Protelis/blob/master/LICENSE.txt) are GPL-3.0-or-later with project linking exceptions; neither runtime is adopted.

### B13. Project field impressions only after authorization filtering

- **Date:** 2026-10-05. **Decided by:** assistant, within the owner's delegated technical roadmap research.
- **Decision:** U6 consumes a binding-scoped `ProjectedImpressionIndex` built only after audience authorization and projection. Its deterministic JSON block carries at most six complete items: four situated items, one strength-biased ambient item and one reserved exploration item. The provisional module estimate is at most 384 tokens (`ceil(UTF-8 bytes/4)`) with a 4,096-byte hard block limit; exact JSON grammar/field order, deterministic selection/omission and revocation are specified in Undercurrent §§10.3/11.1. The R1 `context-module` contract supplies the authorized snapshot, turn binding, bounded output and omission/continuation rules; C3's D25 active-context note is injected once through that contract, not duplicated as a fixed tail.
- **Policy:** D80's coarse pressure/freshness and permission-scoped overlap hints are allowed; no identities, private paths/content, amounts, credentials or rank-only access. Moods may notify the owner under D84 but confer no ordinary authority. The field layer projects true-scope measurements and does not own money, provider or permission decisions.
- **Limits:** Tokenizer/provider cost, privacy/fairness/coverage, revocation and actual context-module freeze remain U6 acceptance work. `ceil(bytes/4)` and 384 are a declared estimate, not exact provider billing, measured fit or a passed gate. Coarse hints intentionally disclose some activity and are not anonymity/noninterference guarantees.
- **Changes:** `DESIGN-UNDERCURRENT.md` §§10.3/11.1; `ROADMAP-UNDERCURRENT.md` U6/U9; `CONTRACTS.md` `context-module`; `ROADMAP-ROUTER.md` R1. See D80/D84 and B10.

### B14. Implement federation as explicit peer agreements and restricted exchange

- **Date:** 2026-10-05. **Decided by:** assistant, within the owner's delegated technical roadmap research.
- **Decision:** Implement D81 as a required U9 capability using owner-imported peer descriptors, pinned colony identity, bilateral local enrollment/grants and exact agreed field-law/profile hashes. Exchange only authorized typed projections over bounded mutually authenticated HTTPS/TLS 1.3 requests; check current grants at each request/delivery, deduplicate by origin identity/revision and retain sender-observed versus receiver-received time. Each operator keeps its own ledger, law activation, C4 policy, budget and disclosure authority; there is no global sequencer/coordinator, shared ledger, trust-on-first-use, transitive trust or automatic discovery-to-enrollment.
- **Limits:** A signature or TLS session authenticates an origin/peer, not truth or independent evidence. Partitions delay exchange; revocation stops future delivery but cannot erase facts already disclosed. Explicit limits, stale-time handling, replay and per-peer resource costs remain in U9's actual gate; no consensus or Byzantine guarantee is claimed.
- **Changes:** `DESIGN-UNDERCURRENT.md` federation section; `ROADMAP-UNDERCURRENT.md` U9; `ROADMAPS.md`; `DECISIONS.md` D81. See B12 for shared-law versus private local-policy identity.
- **Evidence:** [TLS 1.3, RFC 9846](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9846); Go [`crypto/tls`](https://pkg.go.dev/crypto/tls) and [`net/http`](https://pkg.go.dev/net/http). Alchemist/Protelis runtimes are not used.

### B15. Gate every-change capacity and whole-workload cost

- **Date:** 2026-10-05. **Decided by:** assistant, within the owner's delegated technical roadmap research.
- **Decision:** Apply D78 to every required change and every admitted code-working context, measuring accepted throughput, backlog/latency, CPU/RAM/disk/process/VM/network cost, observation, full declared checks, contention, invalidated generations, rework, replay and recovery. Retain the 12/20-agent example at one required change per ten minutes (72/120 changes per hour) as a stated workload, not observed arrival history. The four-checkpoint FIFO scales with declared code-working context/workspace pairs; the four validation lanes remain a planning calibration, not a fixed shipping cap or proven accepted throughput. One native macOS slot queues work and its cost is measured alongside independent model/context concurrency. Infeasible measured limits fail the declared workload gate; they do not silently reduce intended scope or count busy refusals as completed work.
- **Limits:** Source/profile, queue, VM and token values remain provisional until their named runtime gates. Snapshots reduce startup work only; genuinely live memory/indexes stay bounded by admission, while immutable D36 history grows on disk. No benchmark, privacy, performance or usefulness result is claimed.
- **Changes:** D78 implementation gates; Core C1–C4/C10; `ROADMAPS.md`; Undercurrent U2/U10 and native GUI/guest gates. See B4/B11/D87.

### D88. Make the ordinary runtime path carry correctness and model-facing usability

- **Date:** 2026-10-06. **Decided by:** modified (the owner accepted assistant-proposed nudges, then narrowed the scope to exclude GUI changes).
- **Direction:** Build on D79 and B3/B7–B10: preserve the existing guarantees while making correct state handling and efficient model use the ordinary path, not extra plumbing every feature author must remember.
  1. Make producer-owned typed Core transitions compulsory for authoritative state changes, carrying durable publication, replay, command receipts and authorized projections together.
  2. Normalize only declared, unambiguous equivalent model-tool argument forms before canonical validation and authorization; return useful local errors without guessing intent or weakening strict stored/protocol formats.
  3. Design a small, stable, versioned model-facing tool surface; reuse permission-aware local catalogue discovery for infrequent operations rather than exposing every internal operation or adopting a catch-all execution gateway.
  4. Schedule bounded, owner-agreed context-local compaction against an immutable snapshot while useful work continues; publish only still-applicable results, preserve later progress and canonical history, and charge every paid attempt through Core.
  5. Give the existing Core runtime one explicit owner of continuation, waiting, yielding and finishing, with bounded persisted state and clear precedence for cancellation, authority, limits and pending work. Do not add an extensible Director stack or a second workflow engine.
- **Limits:** Keep one active live turn per context. A background summary job has immutable input, no tool loop or independent context-writing authority, and consumes the existing concurrency/resource budget; its result is applied by a Core transition. Preserve passive promotion results, no automatic interrupted-effect replay, explicit provider handover and one-shot paid-attempt accounting. Compaction remains lossy and requires owner agreement. This selects direction, not exact schemas, trigger thresholds, measured improvements, D52 approval, a contract freeze or implementation readiness.
- **Scope:** The owner's subsequent restriction was: "No GUI section modifications are authorized at this time". GUI expansion and native-inspection changes are deferred. Existing implementation tickets stay untouched for later rework.
- **Changes:** [System specification](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/spec.md), non-GUI implementation and testing requirements only. This entry supplements the existing decisions; it does not supersede their guarantees or change roadmap/contract status. Producer documents and tickets are not rewritten in this spec-edit task.
- **Reference:** [The Harness Playbook](https://stencil.so/blog/harness-playbook), especially state ownership, model-facing tool schemas, tool surface, scheduled compaction and continuation ownership. The adopted direction is a project choice, not evidence that these mechanisms have been implemented or measured here.
- **See also:** D89 is the owner's subsequent, explicit GUI-development request; it ends this prior task's GUI-edit deferral without changing D88's non-GUI requirements.

### D89. A fortress overworld and RPG agent screens

- **Date:** 2026-10-06. **Decided by:** owner.
- **Owner's words:**
  > I want to develop the GUI for this project. I want it inspired by Dwarf Fortress as mentioned in the foundational documents. I do want to have tilesets supported. Every dwarf should represent an agent. Leadership dwarfs (Orchestrator & Griot & Diplomat) should have their own offices while the Grand Dwarfs have Grand offices. I want agents that are using jev or another classifier to assist them to have dogs in their offices. The workers sit at their workshops, which are within siderooms of a greater Corp common area. The should have stockpiles nearby their stations. The stockpiles have rails which link to the agents that they communicate with. Diplomats should have offices but congregate in a shared space. Clicking on a dwarf or inside of their room/office will transition to an RPG style screen with stats about the agent and an option to go into its CLI. Clicking on a common area will bring go to another, similarly RPG-party screen which shows the all of the corp agents and their stats and telemetry. These RPG screens will have sprites which represent the agents. The agents that they have relationships with should be included as a dedicated screen/menu as well. Clicking on these agents should take me to their relationship screen. The relationships should show one word summing up the sentiment (within defined parameters so that a classifier can handle it). A memory screen should be included as well. Assets like clothing, hair, shoes, etc. will start off randomized but will be selectable and customizable. Choose an engine or asset maker which will support this customizable sprite system. The 'overworld' that is strictly Dwarf Fortress will maintain dwarf classes visually distiguishable by their color. Offices, rooms and common areas will be customizable to make the easier to dishinguish. Statues, colors, & furniture can all be placed and changed. These assets will be strictly cosmetic.
  >
  > As a user, I want to experience building my agent teams similarly to building my fortress. I want digging out and planning the rooms to be a setting that I can toggle, rather than allowing the harness GUI to decide for me. I want to be able to adjust the layout on the fly without changing agent properties and relationships. If I set up a new corps in the terminal then the next time I load the GUI I want to be prompted that this change has occured. I want the option to 'autocomplete' the build or to manually do it with a TODO list included. This TODO should persist through shutdowns so that if i only get halfway through placing the team I can pick up upon the next GUI restart.
- **Decision:** Adopt the requested tile-based fortress overworld, one dwarf per agent, role-colored dwarves, offices/Grand offices, helper dogs, workshop siderooms/common areas, stockpiles/communication rails and shared diplomat gathering space. Add navigable agent/Corps RPG screens, real CLI attachment, relationship/sentiment and memory screens, randomized customizable sprite parts and strictly cosmetic rooms/furniture/statues. Manual digging/planning is toggleable; live rearrangement never changes agents or their relationships. Terminal-created teams are announced on GUI load, with autocomplete/manual placement and a durable resumable build TODO.
- **Amends:** the current Core/GUI/system-spec restriction to operational layout without visual art direction. Supersedes D88's GUI-edit deferral for this subsequent request. B11's native Fyne selection, D39/D44/D45 display-only separation, private contexts, messaging rules, plain terminal and accessibility requirements remain intact.
- **Limits:** The owner delegated engine or asset-maker selection but did not name an engine, editor, sentiment vocabulary, palette, storage schema or default toggle value. Those assistant-authored baselines are identified in B16 and the feature spec. This request is not a contract freeze, D52 approval or evidence of a runnable GUI.
- **Changes:** [Fortress GUI specification](../.scratch/fortress-gui/spec.md), [system specification](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/spec.md), Core design/producer handoffs, GUI roadmap, roadmap index and contract register. Existing implementation tickets are not regenerated.
- **See also:** D90 records the owner's first GUI interview answers, including amendments to stockpile meaning, relationship classification, administrator inspection and cosmetic history. Full design reconciliation remains pending the interview.

### B16. Native sprite authoring and durable cosmetic presentation

- **Date:** 2026-10-06. **Decided by:** assistant, within the owner's GUI-development request and explicit delegation to choose an engine or asset maker.
- **Tooling:** Retain B11's Fyne v2.8.1 native client. Select Aseprite for offline authoring of dwarf parts and tilesets; ship passive PNG art and a versioned project manifest, not the editor or its native document parser. Go/Fyne composition supplies runtime clothing/hair/shoe selection, stable randomized appearance, tile rendering and hit testing. Neither tool already supplies a complete dress-up GUI. Aseprite is source-available/commercially licensed under its EULA, not an OSS runtime dependency; authored-art rights are separate. No purchase, installation, new runtime engine or Core graphics dependency is authorized by this selection.
- **Cosmetic seam:** Stable Core IDs bind dwarves, rooms, helpers and rail endpoints. The GUI computes presentation geometry but never identity, membership, communication rights, agent memory or execution. A dog reflects a real configured helper association; it does not instantiate a helper. Layout and room completion never gate agent work. Asset packs are bounded inert local content with provenance, not scripts, remote resources or copied Dwarf Fortress art.
- **Durability:** Persist owner-scoped presentation, appearance, unfinished blueprints, topology-review disposition and build TODO progress through Core-owned typed C7 commands into the existing ledger. Commit related presentation/progress changes atomically before acknowledging; use expected revisions and command idempotency. Keep the reviewed topology baseline separate from pending placement and the GUI event cursor. Cosmetic records do not become prompt, scheduler or field inputs. No second GUI database, direct colony-file access or independent appender.
- **Interaction baselines:** Manual digging/planning defaults on but is toggleable; external terminal changes always prompt. Autocomplete preserves completed/customized work and fills only the accepted remaining plan. Relationship navigation names the current viewpoint; `relationship-sentiment/v1` uses `unknown`, `hostile`, `wary`, `neutral`, `warm`, `trusted` with the directional evidence rubric in the feature spec. Labels never grant authority; invalid or unavailable annotations are unknown. Classification uses an eligible Core-owned helper path, never a GUI/model call on inspection. Actual optional helper use keeps its existing accounting/isolation gates.
- **Gates and ownership:** C6 owns relationship/topology annotation bindings; configured producers own classification/helper validity. C7/C1 stage the actual presentation/projection/attach families for owner freeze; C7 base does not wait for social producers or GUI. The separate fortress gate consumes actual C6/C8 and the corresponding C7 additions. Preserve `C9 backend capability → GUI parity → whole C9 completion`. No contract or roadmap status advances; numerical art, classifier and rendering limits are provisional calibration inputs, not measured capacity.
- **Evidence:** [Fyne image/pixel scaling](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/fyne-io/fyne/v2.8.1/canvas/image.go), [custom widgets](https://docs.fyne.io/extend/custom-widget/), [Aseprite layers](https://www.aseprite.org/docs/layers/), [tilemaps](https://www.aseprite.org/docs/tilemap/), [PNG/JSON and layer exports](https://www.aseprite.org/docs/cli/), [art rights](https://www.aseprite.org/faq/), [editor EULA](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/aseprite/aseprite/main/EULA.txt). These establish documented capabilities/licensing, not a built compositor, real art export, native accessibility or performance.
- **Changes:** the D89 feature specification and its Core/C7, GUI, system-spec and contract-register handoffs. No code or asset pack is delivered by this design entry.
- **See also:** D90 supersedes the single-axis sentiment baseline and the requirement to retain every cosmetic edit in the permanent operational ledger. The replacement persistence mechanism and remaining GUI questions are not yet settled.
- **Amended by D103:** a Go generator kept in the repository replaces Aseprite as the offline asset maker; the runtime design and passive packs stand.

### D90. Fortress GUI interview: downward building, task stockpiles and administrator inspection

- **Date:** 2026-10-06. **Decided by:** modified. The owner adopted the animated-fortress, real-statistics and original-art options, and changed the proposed level model, creation journey, relationship dimensions, stockpile meaning, inspection scope and cosmetic-history lifetime. Terminal embedding remains a research request, not a selected component.
- **Owner's words:**
  > 1B. 2B (z-levels should be included but z+3 and above will be reserved for other information. Fortresses are meant to be build downwards). 3B (this will enrich the gameplay aspect of the GUI and needs discrete, reversible and transparent steps with an approval box before finalization of 'one-way door' actions such as defining a new Corp, spawning a new agent (actions such as extending a Corps boudary will not have his approval box) ; define the space, set it as a Corps office/common room/workroom, create and define the Agent through an RPG-like character creation, and place them). 4A (actual agent statistics represented as though they were stats in a video game but not misrepresenting activity for achievement). 5- define 2 orthoginal axies: trust and sentiment; within that framework we can define at least 9 square areas to correspond to labels). 6A. 7B-(incoming messages should be represented through the rails but the stockpile represents tasks that need to be done, self-assigned and delegated. a Corps with large stockpiles translates to an understaffed team. These tasks should be handed off by ephemeral 'runner' horse-drawn with wheelbarrows. Every office retains one at all times, they fade out upon delivery. The resident horse fades in after the running horse leaves but only after a short cooldown timer). 8A - (Admins will encounter no secrets about their colony). 9 - This deserves further research. Embedding an existing terminal project would be ideal, creating a native one would not be. 10- B (undo/history will be bounded to a sustainable number of steps. This history is lost on GUI close.)
- **Resolved interview choices:**
  1. Animated workshops, helper dogs and message traffic are part of the cosmetic fortress experience, rather than only a static status map. Animation does not control actual work or delivery.
  2. Include stacked top-down z-levels and build the fortress downward. Reserve z+3 and above for other information; their contents and the use of z0 through z+2 are not selected here.
  3. Integrate space-first team construction: define space, designate its Corps office/common room/workroom purpose, create and define an agent through an RPG-like character creator, then place it. Steps must be discrete, transparent and reversible until a one-way-door action is finalized. GUI finalization of a new Corps or agent requires an approval box; cosmetic extension of a Corps boundary does not. This does not silently amend the existing policies for births initiated by agents outside that GUI workflow.
  4. Render actual agent statistics like video-game statistics without presenting activity as achievement or inventing competence/progression scores.
  5. Use orthogonal trust and sentiment axes, with at least nine regions corresponding to relationship labels. This replaces B16's mixed single-axis `unknown`/`hostile`/`wary`/`neutral`/`warm`/`trusted` baseline; exact bands, labels and classification criteria remain to be resolved.
  6. Produce original modular dwarf artwork rather than adopting a pre-existing character kit. B16's offline Aseprite/native Fyne tooling remains selected, not implemented.
  7. Stockpiles represent unfinished self-assigned and delegated tasks, not incoming messages. The owner intends large Corps stockpiles to communicate understaffing; exact task inclusion and the distinction between backlog and a staffing diagnosis remain interview questions. Incoming messages are represented on rails. Task handoffs use ephemeral horse-drawn runners with wheelbarrows: runners fade out on delivery, and an office's resident horse fades in after departure and a short cooldown. The simultaneous requirement to retain a horse at every office, plus burst/concurrent-handoff behavior, needs clarification rather than an invented lifecycle.
  8. Authenticated colony administrators may inspect all colony information, including agent-private memories and both sides of relationship histories. Inspection remains the administrator's action, not impersonation of the viewed agent or broader agent-to-agent access. The existing prohibition on provider keys in the GUI needs an explicit reconciliation with the owner's no-secrets wording; no credential-display mechanism is selected here.
  9. Research reuse of an existing embedded terminal project; do not build a bespoke native terminal. External-only attachment is not accepted as the final interaction choice. No terminal library, web renderer or replacement toolkit is adopted by this research request.
  10. Cosmetic undo/history is bounded to a sustainable number of steps and discarded on GUI close. Saved fortress state and unfinished construction still survive under D89. This selects bounded cosmetic history instead of keeping every cosmetic edit forever; it does not weaken retention of operational history or select the replacement storage mechanism.
- **Amends and limits:** Amend D89/B16's interpretation where specified above. Core remains authority for agents, tasks, permissions and execution; room geometry, animals, clothes and animation do not change those facts. Full-spec reconciliation waits for confirmed shared understanding. This records resolved interview choices, not approval of the whole design, implementation, a contract freeze, a measured limit or a passed gate.
- **Documentation:** Resolved vocabulary is captured in [the glossary](../GLOSSARY.md); discussion status is recorded in [the fortress specification](../.scratch/fortress-gui/spec.md#comments). Existing producer/roadmap/system-spec handoffs remain awaiting the consolidated interview revision and must not override these newer choices.
- **See also:** D91 records the second interview round: unified draft creation, mandatory cross-launcher Purpose alignment, the accepted relationship vocabulary, representative handoff animation and a conditional terminal policy.

### D91. Unified creation drafts and mandatory Purpose alignment

- **Date:** 2026-10-06. **Decided by:** modified. The owner accepted the relationship vocabulary, resting-horse lifecycle, Core-held credentials and graphical pixel-art defaults; expanded creation into a unified scratch/clone workflow with compulsory leadership alignment; and constrained levels, work representation and terminal integration.
- **Owner's words:**
  > Q11- This is correct. Buildings can be established up to 2 levels above the surface. Users may want to put the Grand suite above groud for example. Z-levels will need to be bound in the positive and negative direction. The upper layers are to be inaccessible until their utility is decided. Q12- B, this applies to the creation of a corps. Their Purpose is established, their leadership traid is completed and then workers can be added. All through the RPG-like screens. Until the final decision this is all a draft. The same is true of establishing an agent or multiple agents in an existing Corps. There must be one starting screen for the two situations with options to batch one or multiple clones of a selected agent as well as the ability to start from scratch. Q13 - Offer both options, Create and activate will produce a screen with the Orchestrator using the grill-with-docs skill to gain alignment. Then a similar one with the Griot. They will seek alignment in their domains: what are we expected to do and what are our values, respectively. Simply creating leaves this process (accessable via the terminal, open). Purposes are mutable. The first thing that a new Orchestrator or Griot does is run the grill-with-docs skill against the Purpose no matter if they are activated through the GUI or the terminal. Q14- this vocablulary is acceptable. Q15 - A, for now, the GUI should only represent stalled work. Once more data is gathered it can perhaps be trusted to make judgement calls and diagnoses. Q16- A, correct one horse and wheelbarrow is the resting state. Q17- Batch handoffs and gate visual sendoffs to not produce clutter. Every single handoff does not need to be shown. The goal is represent not to simulate. Q18- A. Q19- B. Q20- A, a native terminal can be used and the examples given are good guidance but this is the preferred route if and only if (1) it requires a marginal (5-10%) amount of work compared to the overall project and (2) there is no off-the-shelf option for a native terminal. Should either of these conditions fail then all terminal spawning will be a through a separate user-selected default terminal window
- **Resolved interview choices:**
  1. Buildings may occupy the surface and up to z+2, including Grand offices. Bound the level range in both directions; the negative bound remains to be selected. Reserved higher layers are inaccessible until their utility is decided, not empty navigable information screens.
  2. Provide one RPG-style creation entry for a new Corps or one/multiple additions to an existing Corps. For a new Corps, establish its Purpose, complete its Orchestrator/Griot/Diplomat leadership triad, then allow workers to be added. Support starting from scratch and batches of one or multiple clones of a selected agent. All proposed creations remain drafts until the final exact batch approval; cosmetic room edits are not births.
  3. Offer both **Create** and **Create and activate**. The latter presents the Orchestrator's `grill-with-docs` interview about expected work, then the Griot's interview about values. Create-only leaves this alignment process open and accessible through the terminal. The first thing every new Orchestrator or Griot does is run this skill against the Purpose, regardless of GUI or terminal activation. Purposes are mutable. This is a Core/terminal requirement as well as a GUI interaction, not merely a suggested first prompt.
  4. Adopt the proposed nine-region trust/sentiment vocabulary and positions shown below. The words are interpretive relationship labels, not authority or objective performance scores; exact evidence criteria and update policy remain open.
  5. The GUI represents observable stalled work rather than making staffing judgments or diagnoses. Diagnosis is not a current feature. The exact task inclusion implied by “only represent stalled work,” versus the earlier unfinished-task stockpile requirement, needs clarification; do not silently invent a stall-age threshold.
  6. One horse and wheelbarrow is an office's resting state. The resident departs, the home is briefly empty during cooldown, and a resident fades back in; the travelling runner fades out upon delivery. The replacement need not wait for delivery.
  7. Batch handoffs and gate visual departures to avoid clutter. Not every handoff needs a visible trip: this is representative animation, not a physical simulation. Actual assignment/delivery and stockpile facts remain independent of animation and cooldown.
  8. Preserve administrator-wide inspection of colony records while keeping provider credentials Core-held, not revealed/exported through the GUI. Viewing an agent still does not impersonate it or expand agent-to-agent access.
  9. Use original graphical pixel dwarves and terrain for the default top-down tileset, with larger layered RPG sprites. Tileset replacement remains supported.
  10. Record the owner's conditional terminal rule verbatim: the proposed native route must require only a marginal 5–10% of overall-project work and have no off-the-shelf native-terminal option; failure of either condition means external spawning through a separate user-selected default terminal. The meaning of “off-the-shelf” and whether an available embeddable component should instead be reused remain a clarification, not permission to reverse the written condition. No effort fraction, dependency adoption or native-runtime result is established.

| Sentiment / Trust | Low | Medium | High |
|---|---|---|---|
| Negative | Hostile | Strained | Grudging |
| Neutral | Wary | Neutral | Trusted |
| Positive | Hopeful | Friendly | Bonded |

- **Relationship display baseline:** Keep `unknown` outside the nine regions rather than treating absent evidence as medium trust/neutral sentiment; retain explicit directional perspective and axis information. These are display semantics, not a frozen classifier schema.
- **Required integration:** The consolidated revision must cover C3 startup/continuation and paid-turn eligibility, C8 birth/clone/Purpose handling, C9 skill/workflow capabilities, C7 shared interview progress and terminal/GUI parity, plus the system/fortress specifications. Reuse typed Core authority, exact birth approvals, fresh clone-private memory and explicit budget/grant admission. A GUI-only wizard, an unpinned slash-command string or a prompt request alone does not satisfy the first-action requirement. Stage actual producer capabilities without making Core startup depend on GUI completion.
- **Open boundaries:** Purpose scope and edit/approval lifecycle; leadership cardinality; who answers/accepts alignment; Create-only model-call timing and work eligibility while alignment waits; durable interview/artifact ownership; exact stalled-work inclusion; terminal condition interpretation; numerical z limits and classifier policy. Earlier decisions do not settle these merely by having generic ledgers, skills or workflows.
- **Limits:** Amend D90 where specified; preserve all unchanged Core privacy, validation, accounting and display-only boundaries. Full-spec reconciliation still waits for confirmed shared understanding. No contract freeze, implementation approval, readiness advancement or passed gate is claimed.
- **Documentation:** [Glossary](../GLOSSARY.md), [fortress interview comments](../.scratch/fortress-gui/spec.md#comments) and the existing [terminal source research](../.scratch/fortress-gui/terminal-research.md). Producer/roadmap/system-spec bodies remain awaiting the consolidated interview revision.
- **See also:** D92 resolves the third interview round, including shared Purpose ownership, unique Corps leadership, human alignment approval, warned Create-only interview spending, level bounds, perspective-bound relationship labels and meaningful construction completion.

### D92. Shared Purpose, bounded construction and perspective-bound relationships

- **Date:** 2026-10-06. **Decided by:** modified. The owner adopted shared Purpose, unique Corps leadership, human alignment approval, waiting-work stockpiles, reuse-first terminal embedding and automatic perspective-bound classification. He chose immediate Create-only interviewing with an advance warning, changed the proposed lower z bound from -64 to -15, specified navigation from either side of a relationship pair, and affirmed GUI-enforced construction standards.
- **Owner's words:**
  > Q21-A. 22- A. 23- Human approval is required. 24- B, list a warning beforehand. 25- A, tasks ready to be worked. 26- A. 27- set lower bound to -15. 28- A, represent the agent's perspective, which should only be aware of failed tasks that it is privy to. 29-A. 30- A, selecting either from that screen will take you to their relationship roster. 31- A, the GUI will enforce small standards like that. Meaningful fortress construction
- **Resolved interview choices:**
  1. Use one shared Purpose per Corps, with role-specific mandates beneath it; Grand leadership uses a Colony Purpose. Do not add independently editable per-agent Purposes. Purpose remains mutable; its change and re-alignment lifecycle is still an interview branch.
  2. A Corps has exactly one Orchestrator, one Griot and one Diplomat. A leadership clone cannot silently become a second holder of an occupied role. Proposed roles/destinations remain part of the reviewed creation draft; replacements and their operational transition still need a defined lifecycle.
  3. Human approval of Purpose alignment is required, including for leaders born through agent-initiated operations. A superior agent cannot accept alignment on the human's behalf. This does not replace the existing birth-approval rules or turn administrator inspection into operation authority.
  4. **Create-only runs the first alignment interview turn immediately and leaves its questions waiting.** Warn beforehand that creation will start this model work and incur usage. Existing explicit budget/capability admission still applies; a creation label or warning alone is not a spend grant. Ordinary-work eligibility, activation after approval and the completed alignment record remain separate questions. No permission is given to run unattended interview rounds while waiting for the human.
  5. Stockpiles show self-assigned and delegated tasks waiting to be worked, including ready-but-queued work, with ready and blocked work distinguished; active work is represented at workshops. This resolves D91's ambiguous “stalled work” wording without inventing a stall-age threshold or an understaffing diagnosis.
  6. Prefer embedding a suitable complete off-the-shelf native terminal, subject to the marginal integration/adaptation-work allowance, rather than rejecting reuse because such a component exists. This clarifies and supersedes the literal “no off-the-shelf” condition in D91. Retain the owner's approximately 5–10% of overall-project work constraint and user-selected external-terminal fallback; no particular library, effort estimate, bespoke emulator or unverified native capability is approved.
  7. Set the fortress build/navigation range to **z-15 through z+2**, including the surface. Higher reserved levels remain inaccessible until their utility is decided. These are chosen product bounds, not measured renderer capacity.
  8. A relationship label represents the named agent's perspective, not an independent judgment of the counterpart's competence. Failed tasks may inform that perspective only when the agent is privy to them; administrator-wide inspection must not inject otherwise-hidden outcomes or private information. Keep A-about-B and B-about-A separate and objective performance in telemetry.
  9. Update classifications automatically when relevant eligible evidence changes, with coalescing and bounded spending through existing Core admission/accounting. Opening, hovering or repainting a screen does not initiate a classification call. The exact rubric and handling of partial/stale evidence still need specification.
  10. Clicking B from A's relationship roster opens the focused A/B pair, showing both separately scoped directional labels and memories. Selecting either agent from that pair opens that agent's relationship roster. Viewpoint, private memories and delivered shared history remain distinct.
  11. Connectivity and comparable basic construction standards are enforced by the GUI when deciding whether construction is complete. A disconnected room remains on the persistent Build TODO until its corridor/stair connection is built. These are meaningful fortress-building requirements, never prerequisites for agent work, actual membership or message delivery.
- **Amends and limits:** Amend D90/D91 and incompatible B16 interaction baselines only where stated. Preserve existing Core authority, private contexts, clone safeguards, operational durability, budgets and native/display-client boundaries. Record resolved interview choices now; full feature/system/producer/roadmap reconciliation still waits for confirmed shared understanding. No implementation, contract freeze, readiness advancement or passed runtime gate is claimed.
- **Documentation:** [Glossary](../GLOSSARY.md), [fortress interview comments](../.scratch/fortress-gui/spec.md#comments) and the existing [terminal source research](../.scratch/fortress-gui/terminal-research.md). The terminal report remains historical source evidence, not component adoption or runtime qualification.
- **See also:** D93 records human-controlled interview completion, optional re-alignment, the Corp Creed and Griot mythology, Aspirant succession, and configurable construction requirements with mandatory functional furniture.

### D93. Human-ended alignment, Corp Creeds and Aspirant succession

- **Date:** 2026-10-06. **Decided by:** modified. The owner adopted whole-Corps initial alignment gating, recorded activation intent, staged Purpose updates and marked stale relationship labels. He made interview completion human-determined and re-alignment optional, named the Corp Creed and added Griot mythology, expanded succession into an Incumbent/Aspirant handoff, and required configurable room/furnishing standards, previewed rearrangement and non-vacant officer succession.
- **Owner's words:**
  > 32 - A; 33 - A; 34 - A, one note: these interviews are endless in nature so providing guidance that the user should stop when questions no longer feel like they matter is important. The user is ultimately the one who chooses when alignment has been reached. 35 - B, these re-alignment interviews can be skipped, cut short, or disabled at user's disgression. 36 - A, with some caveats. The Griot interview should also produce a reviewable mythology or origin story. The Corp Creed (referenced as Corps Colony agreement) falls out of the orchestrator interview. 37 - Prepare and align the Aspirant. There should be a handoff communication as well between the Incumbent and the Aspirant to highilght ongoing processes, lessons learned and the relationship roster (which the Aspirant will discount, not adapting the exact values). 38 - A, 39 - Room size requirements are enforced, offices of Corp and Grand Officers must meet a minimum furniture threshold ('x' number of statues, cabinets, tables, chairs, etc.) which be autocompleted at user disgression. The requirements likewise can be disabled, though functional furniture (desks for Officers, stations/desks/labs for workers depending on Corps) cannot be disabled. 40 - B, though room size requirements should make this a nonfactor. Every room size will be able to accomadate the necessary furniture. If a filled room is missing specific *types* of furniture and the user tries to proceed through the Office/Room TODO with autocomplete then they will be told that redundant furniture will be replaced with the necessary pieces. 41- A, This empty Worker Room (Officer positions will not be allowed to be vacant without an aspirant ready) can be reused, relocated or reclaimed/demolished (filling in with dirt if it is underground)
- **Resolved interview choices:**
  1. A new Corps waits before ordinary work begins until both its Orchestrator and Griot have human-approved initial alignment. Alignment and inspection remain available; adding workers to an already-aligned Corps does not restart those interviews. Construction completion remains unrelated to operational eligibility.
  2. Preserve creation intent after alignment: **Create** leaves the team ready but inactive; **Create and activate** proceeds once alignment and all existing permission, budget and operational gates permit. Do not add another mandatory Activate action or interpret closing/reopening a view as activation.
  3. Stage live Purpose revisions while the current approved Purpose continues governing work; switch after the required human decision. The human decides when alignment has been reached. Explain that interviews can generate endless questions and encourage stopping when the questions no longer feel consequential. Neither a model nor an empty question frontier determines completion, and remaining questions alone cannot prevent the human from accepting the current understanding.
  4. Re-alignment targets meaningful changes in the affected leadership domain: work changes concern the Orchestrator, values changes concern the Griot, and corrections alone need no new interview. The human may skip, cut short or disable these **re-alignment** interviews. This does not silently disable the previously required initial alignment/human acceptance for new Orchestrators or Griots, nor waive ordinary grants, spending controls or validation gates. A model cannot grant itself an exemption.
  5. The Orchestrator interview produces the **Corp Creed**, replacing the generic Corps/Colony agreement label: a versioned agreement containing Purpose, role mandates and resolved vocabulary/decisions for its affected agents. The Griot interview additionally produces a **reviewable mythology or origin story**. These are distinct artifacts. Preserve the adopted shared-agreement audience, private raw interviews with administrator inspection, and separately reviewed project-document exports; neither interview automatically edits working repositories. Initial mythology publication/review timing and the boundary between invented lore and historical claims remain consequential clarification points.
  6. Prepare and align the **Aspirant** before it succeeds the **Incumbent**. Include a handoff communication covering ongoing processes, lessons learned and the relationship roster; the Aspirant discounts the reported relationships rather than adopting the exact values. The Aspirant does not occupy the Incumbent's officer slot before handover. This requests an explicit recipient-scoped handoff, not wholesale private-memory, transcript or grant inheritance. Existing D83/D85 boundaries require the actual authorized delivery/shareability contract; the discount policy and exact readiness/cutover criteria still need resolution.
  7. While a relationship classification refresh is pending, retain the last still-authorized label with an explicit outdated/updating indication. Do not pretend it is current, substitute Neutral for missing evidence, or retain inaccessible evidence for display.
  8. Enforce room-size requirements and minimum furniture thresholds for Corps and Grand officer offices, covering the prescribed types/counts of statues, cabinets, tables, chairs and similar furnishings. The user may request autocomplete and may disable the size/decorative furnishing requirements. **Functional furniture cannot be waived:** officer desks and Corps-appropriate worker stations, desks or labs remain construction requirements. These requirements concern Build TODO completion only, never agent competence, permissions or the ability to work. Exact dimensions, counts and footprint-qualified presets are still design/asset work, not measured or frozen values.
  9. Autocomplete may offer a preview that rearranges completed rooms, rather than being limited to unused space. Every room-size preset must accommodate its required furniture. If a filled room lacks required furniture types, Office/Room TODO autocomplete warns beforehand that redundant furniture will be replaced with the necessary pieces. This is user-directed completion, not permission for silent demolition, silent replacement or incomplete placement marked complete.
  10. Preserve an emptied worker room for explicit reuse, relocation or reclamation/demolition; reclamation of an underground room fills it with dirt. Officer positions may not be vacated without a ready Aspirant. This is a Core succession/role-transition constraint as well as a GUI workflow, not a cosmetic office-occupancy check. Readiness and unexpected Incumbent-unavailability handling must be specified without pretending that process failure is a completed handover.
- **Amends and limits:** Amend D90-D92 and incompatible B16 baselines where stated, including formerly optional decoration and preserve-only autocomplete. Human-controlled completion supersedes an exhaustive-question-tree stopping rule for the requested alignment workflow and this interview; it does not by itself confirm the whole design or authorize implementation. Preserve Core authority, exactly one holder per Corps leadership role, private-context separation, explicit recipient authorization, clone safeguards, operational durability and the cosmetic/operational boundary. No contract freeze, implementation, readiness advancement or passed runtime gate is claimed.
- **Documentation:** [Glossary](../GLOSSARY.md) and [fortress interview comments](../.scratch/fortress-gui/spec.md#comments). Full feature/system/producer/roadmap reconciliation waits for the owner's confirmation of shared understanding, which does not require exhausting every possible question.
- **See also:** D94 resolves deterministic relationship attenuation, mandatory predecessor retirement, deferral to ordinary officer recovery, and the Grand/Corps prehistory model with human-approved initial publication.

### D94. Attenuated succession relationships and shared colony prehistory

- **Date:** 2026-10-06. **Decided by:** modified. The owner replaced qualitative relationship discounting with deterministic normalized attenuation, required retirement after successful handover, rejected a separate emergency-succession exception in favor of ordinary officer recovery, and specified Grand-authored prehistory with Purpose-shaped Corps reinterpretations. He adopted human approval before initial mythology publication without another activation gate.
- **Owner's words:**
  > 42- There should be some deterministic, normalized function which brings sentiment and trust towards 0. Trust values should be maintained more strongly than sentiment.
