This will allow the Aspirant to develop its own opinions while incorperating this second-hand evidence. 43- The predecessor should be retired after the successful handover. 44- If the succession process is in progress when the Incumbent is lost then there are larger problems. This will be handled in other documentaion such as what the protocol is for an Officer losing connection during normal operations. The succession process will default to that procedure and continue after service is restored. 45- The Grand Griot shall  tell of a 'prehistory' era that is allowed to be fictional and will be the founding story of the colony. Part of their interview process will involve asking and suggesting Coprs to include in this 'prehistory' era, even if they aren't included in the colony at the very beginning. The Grand Griot can produce a reason that they will join later. Corps Griots will take this mythology and Purpose to inform their founding story. This means that they will be allowed to *reinterpret* the events of 'prehistory', choosing characters or events that are aligned with their Purpose and being given creative liscense in that way. The Grand Griot should construct the story in a loose fashion to allow for reinterpretation of the Heros and characters of 'prehistory'. 46- a
- **Resolved interview choices:**
  1. At succession, use a **deterministic, normalized function** to bring inherited relationship sentiment and trust toward zero. Retain trust more strongly than sentiment, preserving attenuated second-hand evidence while leaving room for the Aspirant's own opinions. Do not replace this with an unconstrained model instruction to discount advice or simply copy the Incumbent's values. Preserve directional source/provenance and the existing distinction between a known value and `unknown`; missing evidence is not manufactured as zero. This authorizes the specified transformed relationship evidence through the explicit handoff, not wholesale private-context, counterpart-memory or grant inheritance.
  2. Retire the predecessor **after successful handover**. Do not silently keep an active adviser, select a replacement role or retire it merely because preparation/alignment has finished. Preserve D83's read-only delivered history and private memories after retirement; an apparent GUI transition or interrupted connection does not establish successful handover.
  3. An Incumbent connection/service loss during succession is handled by the **ordinary officer connection-loss/recovery procedure**, whose policy belongs in the appropriate operational documentation. Continue succession after service is restored. Do not create an emergency exception that bypasses the Incumbent, fabricates a completed handoff or promotes a successor solely because the connection failed. This records the required dependency; it neither specifies that broader procedure here nor claims it has been implemented.
  4. The **Grand Griot** creates a **prehistory** era that may be fictional and serves as the Colony's founding story. Its interview asks about and suggests Corps to include, including Corps that will not be present at the Colony's beginning, and may supply narrative reasons for their later arrival. Mentioning a Corps or character in this story is not an actual birth, membership change or creation approval; the existing explicit creation workflow remains authoritative.
  5. **Corps Griots** use that shared mythology and their Corps' **Purpose** to produce their own founding stories. They may creatively reinterpret prehistory, selecting Purpose-aligned events, heroes and characters rather than copying a single interpretation. The Grand Griot deliberately leaves its story loose enough to support these differing interpretations. Shared narrative does not merge private interviews or confer blanket access to other Corps' memories and boards.
  6. Require human approval before initial publication of the founding mythology/origin story, including the Grand source and the Corps founding stories. Alignment may finish with a story still an unpublished draft; publication approval is not an additional ordinary-work activation gate. This does not add mandatory human approval of every subsequent Griot story. Approved narrative, private raw interview material and separately reviewed project exports remain distinct.
  7. The permission to invent prehistory and reinterpret it does not turn fiction into actual operational history, completed work, authority or agent statistics. Keep the narrative's provenance and approved source/version distinguishable from factual colony records.
- **Remaining specification boundaries:** The owner selected deterministic attenuation and stronger retention of trust, not a numerical domain, the semantic meaning of zero, exact curve, coefficients or classification cutpoints. These must be distinguished from the already adopted nine-region display vocabulary; an explicit normalization proposal still needs resolution. The ordinary officer recovery policy is a separate operational prerequisite, not another GUI-interview branch. Story delivery/versioning must use the existing authorized publication and Griot-channel boundaries.
- **Amends and limits:** Amend D93's unresolved relationship discount, predecessor disposition, loss handling and fiction/publication questions as stated. The predecessor's retirement is mandatory on successful handover, not merely a default with an automatic advisory/reassignment alternative. Preserve human-controlled alignment completion, the no-vacant-officer succession requirement, exactly one holder of each Corps leadership role, explicit grants, fresh clone-private contexts, spending controls and the cosmetic/operational separation. No implementation, contract freeze, readiness advancement or passed runtime gate is claimed.
- **Documentation:** [Glossary](../GLOSSARY.md) and [fortress interview comments](../.scratch/fortress-gui/spec.md#comments). Full feature/system/producer/roadmap reconciliation still waits for the owner's confirmation of shared understanding.
- **See also:** D95 settles the signed trust domain and neutral-zero meaning while explicitly leaving the exact attenuation implementation open pending testing.
- **Amended by D98:** the owner may declare a lost officer unrecoverable, after which a prepared, aligned Aspirant can succeed it without the live handoff.

### D95. Signed trust semantics with attenuation implementation left open

- **Date:** 2026-10-07. **Decided by:** modified. The owner accepted option Q47A and explicitly reserved the exact implementation pending further testing.
- **Owner's words:**
  > 47A, with the exact implimentation open pending further testing
- **Decision:** Trust uses a signed normalized scale from **-1 (distrust)** through **0 (neutral)** to **+1 (trust)**. Attenuation toward zero therefore softens inherited distrust as well as inherited trust. Preserve `unknown` as distinct from a known neutral value.
- **Implementation remains open:** D94 still requires deterministic normalized attenuation of sentiment and trust, with stronger retention of trust. The exact function, coefficients, numerical representation and classification cutpoints remain unresolved pending further testing. The suggested independent linear formula and illustrative retention factors are candidates, not adopted implementation defaults; the earlier arithmetic exercise does not settle them.
- **Amends and limits:** Resolve D94's open trust-domain/zero-semantics question only. This is not implementation authorization, a contract freeze, an empirical qualification or a completed runtime gate. No further owner-semantic interview question is identified; full specification and producer/roadmap reconciliation still waits for the owner's confirmation of shared understanding.
- **Documentation:** [Glossary](../GLOSSARY.md) and [fortress interview comments](../.scratch/fortress-gui/spec.md#comments).
- **See also:** D96 authorizes consolidation as a pre-alpha stopping point, not exhaustive design closure; the exact attenuation implementation remains open.

### D96. Pre-alpha fortress alignment and consolidation boundary

- **Date:** 2026-10-07. **Decided by:** modified. The owner accepted consolidation and explicitly framed the alignment as a pre-alpha stopping point with more still available for discussion, rather than an exhausted design space.
- **Owner's words:**
  > There's more that can be discussed but this is a good stopping point for a pre-alpha design. Consolidate this alignment
- **Decision:** Consolidate D89-D95 into the fortress feature specification, affected system specification, Core/terminal producer design, GUI/Core roadmaps and contract/coordination handoffs. Replace incompatible current baseline clauses rather than relying on an interview-in-progress warning. Preserve the chronological interview comments and decision provenance.
- **Open work remains explicit:** The exact relationship attenuation implementation remains open pending further testing (D95), as do producer schemas and the separately owned ordinary officer connection-loss/recovery policy. Native terminal qualification, art/compositor proof, numerical/resource calibration, accessibility, durability and runtime acceptance remain their actual engineering gates. The pre-alpha agreement is not a claim that no further product/design discussion is possible.
- **Limits:** This is authorization to consolidate the agreed design, not to implement the product, freeze contracts, approve the separate D52 promotion boundary, advance roadmap readiness or claim successful runtime gates. Keep the earlier decisions' unchanged authority/privacy/accounting guarantees and preserve historical evidence with its stated limits. Existing implementation tickets are not regenerated by this consolidation.
- **Documentation:** [Fortress specification](../.scratch/fortress-gui/spec.md), [system specification](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/spec.md), [Core design](DESIGN-CORE-AND-LAUNCHERS.md), [Core roadmap](ROADMAP-CORE.md), [GUI roadmap](ROADMAP-GUI.md), [roadmap index](ROADMAPS.md), [contract register](CONTRACTS.md) and [glossary](../GLOSSARY.md).
- **See also:** D97 records the first round of the ordinary officer service-loss and restoration interview that D94 deferred and this entry left open.

### D97. Officer service loss: causes, onset, held questions and restoration evidence

- **Date:** 2026-10-08. **Decided by:** modified. The owner adopted the proposed service-loss causes (provider failure, execution failure and lost contact with an out-of-process agent host), one service-loss state for every agent, outage notices and completed-call restoration evidence. He shortened the proposed 15-minute grace period to 2 minutes; added that Corps members of a lost Orchestrator hold further questions until a Service Restoration message and pause their own work at an allotted number of questions; and stated that pending officer decisions wait until the office is filled or service is restored, because no member has the officer's context.
- **Owner's words:**
  > For general context: the most likely service loss will come from provider budget overruns. The other
  >
  > 48-A,B,C
  >
  > 49-A
  >
  > 50-B, set the grace period to 2 minutes
  >
  > 51-A, the Corp keeps working on current tasks and decisions that require the Orchestrator are paused. Once they receive that the Orchestrator is down they will shall reserve further questions until they receive a Service Restoration message from the recovered Orchestrator. Once *they* have reached an alotted number of queries then they will pause work
  >
  > 52- No member will have the context that the Orchestrator or other Officers have. The decisions wait until their office is filled or service is restored.
  >
  > 53- A
  >
  > 54- a
- **Resolved interview choices:**
  1. **Service loss** is a recorded period in which Core cannot run an agent's required work, together with its cause: a provider failure (rate-limited past the router's silent wait, overloaded, unreachable, denied, or out of quota); an execution failure (processes not confirmed stopped, so their resources stay locked off, or private state failing its restart integrity check); or lost contact with a future agent host outside the Core process. Being idle, busy or awaiting a reply, a turn marked interrupted after restart, a detached view and a stopped colony host are not service loss. Agents hold no connection of their own; the cause is what Core can no longer do for them.
  2. One service-loss state covers every agent and belongs with C3's runtime guards. Officer-specific consequences, such as Corps notices, held questions and succession, belong to C8.
  3. Service loss begins once the agent's required work has stayed paused for a **2-minute grace period**, counted from the router's pause after its 30-second silent wait. Causes only the owner can clear count at once: refused credentials, quota exhausted without a stated reset, locked-off processes and a failed integrity check. Detection uses existing records only: no heartbeat or probe call is added, and an idle agent counts as lost only when work it cannot run arrives.
  4. While an officer is in service loss its Corps keeps working on current tasks, and decisions that require that officer pause. Messages to the officer are still accepted and queued within existing limits, and senders see that it is unavailable. For a lost Orchestrator, members who learn it is down reserve further questions until they receive a **Service Restoration message** from the recovered Orchestrator, and each member that reaches an allotted number of reserved questions pauses its own work. Held spending and interrupted work keep their existing rules: nothing is released, replayed or retried automatically.
  5. No agent decides in a lost officer's place, because no member has that officer's context. No superior, deputy or temporary authority acts for it; its pending decisions wait until the office is filled or service is restored.
  6. The owner receives one durable notice when an outage begins and one when it ends, grouped by cause, so one provider outage lists every affected officer. Agents learn passively: a requester sees why its request waits, and the officer's superior finds a notice at its next naturally scheduled turn. No notice starts a model turn.
  7. **Service restoration** is established by the agent's first successfully completed call after its cause clears. A stated reset passing, the owner fixing credentials or topping up, an explicit retry, confirmed process shutdown or a repaired state only permits another attempt. After restoration, queued work is scheduled as usual; paused turns follow the owner's chosen option (wait, switch provider or stop); paid calls with unknown outcomes stay held and are never replayed; and a succession in progress continues from its last saved step.
- **Remaining interview branches:** what "provider budget overruns" covers, given that owner-set money limits were not selected as a cause and the owner's context note breaks off; how members learn an officer is down; where reserved questions live and how they are counted; the allotment and what a paused member stops; which lost roles and which senders the held-question rule covers; whether members may self-assign new work meanwhile; who issues the Service Restoration message; whether the owner's own commands act meanwhile; how an office is filled while its officer is lost; and what happens if an Aspirant loses service during succession. Exact records and encodings remain C3/C6/C8 producer work.
- **Amends and limits:** Begins the ordinary procedure D94 deferred to operational documentation. Preserves D94's prohibitions on emergency successor promotion, timeout takeover, waived handoff, GUI bypass and treating a connection failure as handover; D22/D63/D79's one-shot paid attempts, passive results and absence of bookkeeping model calls; D82 grants; D83 retirement history; and exactly one holder per officer role. No implementation, contract freeze, readiness advancement or passed runtime gate is claimed.
- **Documentation:** [Glossary](../GLOSSARY.md) and [ticket 87](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/issues/87-specify-the-ordinary-officer-connection-loss-and-service-restoration-policy.md). Producer, roadmap and specification reconciliation and ticket 91's criteria wait for the owner's confirmation of shared understanding.
- **See also:** D98 records round 2 and amends item 1 to add owner-set money limits as a cause.
- **Amended by D102:** service loss also ends once the owner moves all of the agent's work off the failed provider, and before the router exists the grace counts from the first refused call.

### D98. Officer service loss: Corps operation, owner limits, owner stand-in and unrecoverable officers

- **Date:** 2026-10-08. **Decided by:** modified. The owner adopted the round's proposals: his own money limits count alongside provider limits, outage notices reach the whole Corps, reserved questions wait in the lost officer's queue against a default allotment of three, a member at its allotment pauses all ordinary work, the rule covers every officer, Core sends the Service Restoration message, he may declare an officer unrecoverable, and an Aspirant's service loss pauses its succession. He added that members' self-assigned work allows no scope creep, warned that releasing everyone at restoration could overload the restored officer, and extended his own powers so that in an emergency he can answer questions and act as the officer.
- **Owner's words:**
  > 55- Both count, what was meant was the provider limit
  >
  > 56-A
  >
  > 57-A
  >
  > 58-A
  >
  > 59-A
  >
  > 60-A
  >
  > 61-A, but no scope creep will be allowed
  >
  > 62-A, though this could overload the restored officer
  >
  > 63-A, in an emergency the user can answer questions and act as the officer
  >
  > 64- A
  >
  > 65- A
- **Resolved interview choices:**
  1. **Money limits.** An exhausted owner-set money limit is a service-loss cause when it stops all of an agent's paid calls: the agent's own limit, its provider account's or the colony's. A task or grant budget, spending permission not yet set and revoked permissions stay refusals, not service loss. The owner's "provider budget overruns", which he expects to be the commonest cause, means the provider's own account limit, already a cause under D97.
  2. **Outage notices.** Everyone the reserved-question rule covers (item 5) finds an outage notice at its next naturally scheduled turn, and a message it sends to the lost officer is answered at once as unavailable. No notice starts a model turn. This widens D97's passive notices from members with a waiting request to the whole Corps.
  3. **Reserved questions** wait in the lost officer's queue: Core accepts and holds each message as usual and counts it against its sender's question allotment, and after restoration the officer answers them in order.
  4. **Question allotment.** A member's count is its questions waiting on officers currently in service loss. The default allotment is three, configurable per colony and to be tuned. At the allotment the member pauses all ordinary work under the hold Core already uses while a new Corps' leaders await alignment: running calls finish, nothing is cancelled or retried, held spending stays held, and the member still receives messages and can be inspected.
  5. **Coverage.** The rule covers every officer, Grand counterparts included. The allotment applies to members of the lost officer's own Corps and, for a Grand officer, to the officers it directly leads. Other senders, such as a parent officer or another Corps' Diplomat, see the officer as unavailable; their messages queue and never pause them. Messages to a lost worker simply queue.
  6. **Self-assigned work.** During an outage members may self-assign work within their existing grants, which D82 does not treat as an officer decision, but no scope creep is allowed. New assignments, grants and approvals still wait.
  7. **Service Restoration message.** Core sends it in the restored officer's name as soon as restoration is proven, marked as sent by Core, to everyone told of the outage, at no model cost; the officer can still write its own messages. The owner warned that releasing everyone at once could overload the restored officer.
  8. **The owner during an outage.** The owner's existing commands stay available, used as himself. In an emergency he may also answer questions and act as the officer.
  9. **Unrecoverable officer.** The owner may declare an officer in service loss unrecoverable. It stays the recorded holder until a prepared, aligned Aspirant takes over through the normal reviewed cutover without the live handoff; it then retires with its records kept. There is never a vacancy, second holder or timer.
  10. **Aspirant service loss.** The Incumbent stays the sole holder and keeps working. The succession pauses at its last saved step and resumes after the Aspirant's restoration, with cutover waiting until then. The owner may cancel the succession at any time and keep the Incumbent.
- **Remaining interview branches:** the concrete boundary of scope creep; whether a paused officer still serves those who depend on it; pacing the restored officer's backlog; restarting many agents after one shared cause; when, how visibly and how far the owner acts as an officer; the Aspirant's handoff from an unrecoverable officer; unanswered messages and pending decisions when an office changes hands; and an unrecoverable officer restored before cutover.
- **Amends and limits:** Amends D97 item 1 by adding owner-set money limits, and D94 by letting an officer the owner declares unrecoverable be succeeded without its live handoff. D94's prohibitions on automatic or timeout promotion, emergency successor promotion by agents and GUI bypass stand, as do D82 grants, D83 retirement history, D85 private-memory limits and exactly one holder per officer role. No implementation, contract freeze, readiness advancement or passed runtime gate is claimed.
- **Documentation:** [Glossary](../GLOSSARY.md) and [ticket 87](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/issues/87-specify-the-ordinary-officer-connection-loss-and-service-restoration-policy.md). Producer, roadmap and specification reconciliation and ticket 91's criteria wait for the owner's confirmation of shared understanding.
- **See also:** D99 records round 3 and amends item 7 so that paused members are released in step with the restored officer's backlog.

### D99. Officer service loss: outage work rules, owner stand-in and office handover

- **Date:** 2026-10-08. **Decided by:** adopted. The owner picked an assistant-written option for every question, choosing the non-recommended option for Q74 with his own reason, and gave reasons for Q69 and Q75.
- **Owner's words:**
  > 66-C, 67-A, 68-B, 69-A: I'm not aware of the rate limits, this will be tried out and fixed with testing if it proves to be an issue, 70-A, 71-A, 72-A, 73-A, 74-B, this will allow for the most smooth transition the questions were for the office as much as they are for the agent, 75-C, in the unlikely event that the user was wrong and the office was recoverable then the user should be given the final call
- **Resolved interview choices:**
  1. **No scope creep.** While an officer is in service loss, members may claim tasks already waiting in their Corps' queue and split their own current tasks into subtasks that serve the parent task's acceptance criteria. They may draft new tasks only as **task proposals**, which cannot start until the officer or the owner accepts them. They may not create new top-level tasks, change any task's goal or acceptance criteria, or start work outside the Corps' existing tasks.
  2. **Paused officers keep serving.** An officer that reaches its own allotment still answers messages from its own Corps and from those it directly leads, and still makes the decisions they await; only its other work pauses. Core identifies these senders from Corps membership and parent links, so an outage does not spread down the hierarchy.
  3. **In-step release.** The Service Restoration message still goes out at once, but each paused member stays paused until the restored officer has handled its reserved questions, so no new questions arrive while the backlog is largest. This amends D98 item 7's immediate release.
  4. **Restart bursts.** No colony-wide pacing is added for agents restored together after a shared cause; existing rate-limit handling applies. The workload gate measures that burst, and pacing is added only if testing shows repeat outages.
  5. **Owner stand-in.** Whenever an officer is in service loss, the owner may answer questions and act for its office, with no separate declaration. His answers and decisions appear in the office's conversations and records labelled "the owner, acting for" that office, never as the officer's own words. A restored officer finds them in its history and receives a list of the decisions made for its office at its next naturally scheduled turn. He acts through the office's permitted channels and within its authority; anything wider uses his ordinary owner commands.
  6. **Handoff from an unrecoverable officer.** Core assembles it from the officer's Core records only: its open tasks, the assignments it made and its pending decisions, plus its relationship roster softened as in an ordinary handoff. It carries no lessons and nothing from private memory.
  7. **Office handover.** At every succession, the outgoing officer's unanswered messages and pending decisions pass to the new holder and are delivered as if sent to it, because they were addressed to the office as much as to the agent.
  8. **Restoration before cutover.** If an officer declared unrecoverable is restored before cutover, Core pauses the succession and asks the owner to cancel it, switch to an ordinary succession with a live handoff, or proceed as declared. The restored officer works as the holder meanwhile; the owner has the final call.
- **Assistant-derived consequences (no owner objection):** An exhausted owner money limit starts service loss as provider quota does: at once without a stated reset, otherwise after the grace period. It clears when the owner raises the limit or its period resets. A reserved question the owner answers stops counting toward that member's allotment, and the restored officer does not answer it again.
- **Remaining interview branches:** whether task proposals count toward the allotment; whether the owner hears when members begin pausing; whether one cause that pauses many turns produces one grouped prompt; and whether the outgoing officer's own open tasks pass with the office.
- **Amends and limits:** Amends D98 item 7 (in-step release). The owner stand-in never makes the owner the office's holder, always labels him as the sender, and leaves D90's non-impersonating administrator inspection unchanged. Office handover passes only unanswered messages and pending decisions, not the previous holder's conversation history or private memory (D83, D85). D94's prohibitions on automatic or timeout promotion and on emergency promotion by agents stand. No implementation, contract freeze, readiness advancement or passed runtime gate is claimed.
- **Documentation:** [Glossary](../GLOSSARY.md) and [ticket 87](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/issues/87-specify-the-ordinary-officer-connection-loss-and-service-restoration-policy.md). Producer, roadmap and specification reconciliation and ticket 91's criteria wait for the owner's confirmation of shared understanding.
- **See also:** D100 records round 4, after which no interview branches remain.

### D100. Officer service loss: proposal cap, Corps status, grouped prompts and open tasks

- **Date:** 2026-10-08. **Decided by:** modified. The owner picked the recommended options for task proposals (a separate cap), grouped outage prompts and open tasks at handover. For pause notices he chose the non-recommended option of no extra notice and changed it: the Corps status page shows how many members are paused and how many, and which, questions are waiting.
- **Owner's words:**
  > 76-C, 77-A: the Coprs status page can show the number that are paused, the number and specific questions that are waiting, 78-A, 79-A
- **Resolved interview choices:**
  1. **Task proposal cap.** Task proposals have their own cap, separate from the question allotment: a member may have up to three waiting, further drafts are refused as busy, and the member keeps working. Proposals never pause a member.
  2. **Corps status instead of a pause notice.** The owner receives no extra notice when members begin pausing. Instead the Corps status page shows how many members are paused and how many, and which, questions are waiting.
  3. **Grouped outage prompts.** When one cause pauses several turns, the owner gets one prompt for that cause instead of one per turn: he chooses wait or stop once for all of them and can still choose differently for any single turn. Switching provider and compacting stay per-turn choices, each with its own cost and report of what gets dropped.
  4. **Open tasks at handover.** The outgoing officer's open tasks pass to the new holder at cutover and are listed in the cutover review. Unsubmitted changes in the old officer's private workspace do not carry over; in an ordinary succession the officer can submit them first.
- **Assistant-derived consequences (no owner objection):** Messages passed to a new holder keep their original sender and are marked as passed on from the previous holder; each sender is told at its next naturally scheduled turn, and the new holder receives only the unanswered messages, not the earlier conversation. When an office changes hands instead of being restored, members receive a handover notice in place of the Service Restoration message, and paused members are released in step as the new holder handles their questions. Decisions the owner makes while acting for an office are part of its records and are included in the handoff to a new holder. Task proposals are pending decisions for the office: they wait for the officer or the owner and pass to a new holder at handover.
- **Remaining interview branches:** none. The design tree of the ordinary procedure is exhausted; the owner's confirmation of shared understanding is pending.
- **Readings awaiting confirmation:** "Corps status page" means the Corps' status in both launchers (the Corps party sheet in the GUI and the Corps status in the terminal) and, for a Grand officer, the colony's status; specific questions appear only to principals allowed to read them, so the owner as administrator sees all of them. The task-proposal cap, like the allotment, defaults to three and is configurable per colony. Turns that a cause pauses after the owner has answered its grouped prompt join that prompt: an earlier "wait" covers them, while "stop" applies only to the turns the owner saw. The new holder takes each open task with a fresh private context, not the previous holder's task context. The owner's outage notices list every affected agent, workers included. Nothing submits an outgoing officer's unsubmitted changes automatically.
- **Amends and limits:** Replaces the routing design's one prompt per paused turn (§9.8) with one prompt per cause for the wait-or-stop choice; D4's owner-selected provider changes stand, with no automatic switching, retry or switch-back. Office handover still passes no conversation history or private memory (D83, D85). Completes the ordinary officer service-loss procedure D94 deferred, subject to the owner's confirmation. No implementation, contract freeze, readiness advancement or passed runtime gate is claimed.
- **Documentation:** [Glossary](../GLOSSARY.md) and [ticket 87](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/issues/87-specify-the-ordinary-officer-connection-loss-and-service-restoration-policy.md). Producer, roadmap and specification reconciliation and ticket 91's criteria wait for the owner's confirmation of shared understanding.
- **See also:** D101 records the owner's confirmation of shared understanding.

### D101. Officer service-loss procedure confirmed for consolidation

- **Date:** 2026-10-08. **Decided by:** adopted. The owner accepted the assistant's summary of the whole procedure and its listed readings as written.
- **Owner's words:**
  > Approved, we are aligned
- **Decision:** The procedure summarized at the end of the interview is the shared understanding: D97–D100 as amended, with D100's readings confirmed. "Corps status page" means the Corps party sheet in the GUI and the Corps status in the terminal, and the colony's status for a Grand officer; specific questions appear only to principals allowed to read them, so the owner as administrator sees them all. The task-proposal cap defaults to three and is configurable per colony. Turns that a cause pauses after the owner has answered its grouped prompt join that prompt: an earlier "wait" covers them, while "stop" applies only to the turns the owner saw. A new holder takes each open task with a fresh private context. Outage notices list every affected agent, workers included. Nothing submits an outgoing officer's unsubmitted changes automatically.
- **Consolidation:** Reconcile the procedure into the Core design, Core and Router roadmaps, routing design, GUI roadmap, roadmap index, contract register and both specifications, replacing every statement that the ordinary officer recovery policy is open. Changes to tickets need the owner's separate approval of the landing breakdown.
- **Open work remains explicit:** Exact service-loss, outage, reserved-question, stand-in and office-handover records and encodings remain C3/C6/C7/C8/R1 producer work under D40. The question allotment, the task-proposal cap and restart bursts after a shared cause need calibration at the workload gate.
- **Limits:** This confirms the design. It does not implement it, freeze a contract, approve the D52 promotion boundary, advance roadmap readiness or claim a passed runtime gate.
- **Documentation:** [Core design](DESIGN-CORE-AND-LAUNCHERS.md), [Core roadmap](ROADMAP-CORE.md), [Router roadmap](ROADMAP-ROUTER.md), [routing design](DESIGN-ROUTING-LAYER.md), [GUI roadmap](ROADMAP-GUI.md), [roadmap index](ROADMAPS.md), [contract register](CONTRACTS.md), [system specification](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/spec.md), [fortress specification](../.scratch/fortress-gui/spec.md), [glossary](../GLOSSARY.md) and [ticket 87](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/issues/87-specify-the-ordinary-officer-connection-loss-and-service-restoration-policy.md).
- **See also:** D102 records Q80 and the approved ticket landing.

### D102. Officer service loss: moved work, pre-router onset and ticket landing

- **Date:** 2026-10-08. **Decided by:** adopted. In the question tool the owner selected the recommended option for Q80 and approved the landing breakdown, including its staging note, as written.
- **Owner's words** (his selections in the question tool):
  > Landing: Approve and publish
  >
  > Q80: A: Moving all its work ends it
- **Resolved choices:**
  1. **Moved work.** Service loss also ends when its cause no longer applies to the agent: once the owner moves all of the agent's work off the failed provider, for example by changing its default provider, its first completed call there restores it. A single turn switched to another provider runs there, and reserved questions it handles count as answered, but the agent stays in service loss until its cause clears or no longer applies.
  2. **Onset before the router.** Until the router is built, the Core's test-only static provider path has no router pause, so the 2-minute grace counts from the first refused call. Once the router lands, the grace counts from the router's pause as D97 decided.
  3. **Landing.** Ticket 91 becomes the general Core service-loss ticket, blocked by 16 and 87 instead of 27 and 87. Tickets 39 and 106 gain the grouped prompt, office handover and Aspirant pause. New tickets 130–136 carry the router integration, Corps operation during an outage, outage work rules, the owner stand-in, office handover after an outage, view projections and the GUI. The system specification gains user stories 165–168, implementation decision 64 and testing decision 23; the fortress specification gains acceptance scenario FG22.
- **Amends and limits:** Amends D97 item 7's restoration evidence and, on the pre-router path only, item 3's onset timing. Only ticket 91, the needs-info placeholder for this policy, changes its blockers. D94's prohibitions, exactly one holder per office and D82/D83/D85 limits stand. No implementation, contract freeze, readiness advancement or passed runtime gate is claimed.
- **Documentation:** [Core design §13.11](DESIGN-CORE-AND-LAUNCHERS.md#1311-ordinary-service-loss-and-officer-recovery-d97d102), [glossary](../GLOSSARY.md), [ticket 87](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/issues/87-specify-the-ordinary-officer-connection-loss-and-service-restoration-policy.md) and tickets 39, 91, 106 and 130–136.

### D103. Default fortress art: a Go generator replaces Aseprite

- **Date:** 2026-10-08. **Decided by:** adopted. Before buying an Aseprite licence, the owner asked for alternatives. He then stated that neither he nor users will edit sprites by hand, approved the assistant's recommended Go generator as written, and chose to keep replacement packs, one of two assistant-written options.
- **Owner's words:**
  > Before aquiring an Asperite key I want to double check if there are any other options
  >
  > I don't plan to edit the sprites by hand neither do I plan to let users.
  >
  > Approved to implement the Go Generator. I want to keep replacement packs an option.
- **Resolved choices:**
  1. **Generator.** A Go program kept in the repository writes the default asset pack: separate transparent, untrimmed PNG layers for dwarf parts, plus frames and tiles, on the 16-pixel and 64-pixel grids, with the DwarfHarness manifest and a contact sheet for review. Its shape data, palettes and seeds are kept in the repository. It uses only the Go standard library and the pinned toolchain. The same inputs reproduce the same files, and any difference is recorded and explained.
  2. **Development tool only.** The generator lives with the development tools in the root module (D45), outside Core, the terminal and the GUI module. It never ships in the GUI, never runs on a user's computer and makes no model call. No image editor, image-generation model or third-party art is used, and no Aseprite licence is bought.
  3. **No hand editing.** Neither the owner nor users draw or edit sprite pixels; users choose parts, colors and packs. The default art changes only through the generator's code and data, and the owner's review of its contact sheet gates the default pack.
  4. **Replacement packs stay.** A user may still import a local replacement pack made with any tool, under the same passive manifest and loader rules, if its licence permits the use (D91 item 9).
- **Consequences:** Ticket 88 is retitled "Generate a minimal original layered art sample with the Go art generator" and now waits for ticket 01, because the generator is project Go code that must build with the pinned toolchain and pass the project check. Tickets 107 and 108 change only their references to the art tool.
- **Amends and limits:** Amends B16's selection of Aseprite as the offline asset maker. B16's runtime design stands: Go/Fyne composition of passive PNG and manifest packs, with no runtime editor, editor-document parser, script or plug-in. No generator or art exists yet, so whether the generated art meets the owner's quality bar stays unproven until tickets 88 and 108 run and he reviews the output. The importer format still freezes only after ticket 88's real output is inspected. No new dependency, contract freeze, readiness advancement or passed runtime gate is claimed.
- **Documentation:** [Sprite-tool research](../.scratch/fortress-gui/sprite-tool-research.md), [fortress specification §6](../.scratch/fortress-gui/spec.md#6-chosen-sprite-and-tileset-pipeline), [system specification](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/spec.md), [GUI roadmap](ROADMAP-GUI.md), [Core design](DESIGN-CORE-AND-LAUNCHERS.md), [Core roadmap](ROADMAP-CORE.md) and tickets 88, 107 and 108.
- **Amended by D104:** agents write sprite shapes as pixel maps, and generator code is approved only after the owner approves contact sheets of both the map and the character sprites.
- **Amended by D105:** Go modules from outside the standard library may assist sprite generation, each with its D29 record.

### D104. Default fortress art: pixel maps and owner review of generated sprites

- **Date:** 2026-10-08. **Decided by:** modified. The owner adopted the assistant's two proposed controls for ticket 88: pixel maps for sprite shapes and an early review of the generated sample. He changed the review so that it covers both the map sprites and the character sprites and gates approval of the generator code. He added that the sprite generation will be improved after the first build, learning from open-source projects.
- **Owner's words:**
  > Please add the controls. We can iterate on the code generation of the map and character sprites once this is built, learning from open source projects. I want to review both sprites before the code is approved
- **Resolved choices:**
  1. **Pixel maps.** Agents write the shape of every part, variant and frame as a pixel map kept in the repository: a text grid in which each character is one pixel. Code rules supply only colors, palette masks, combinations of variants and small seeded texture in rock and floor tiles, so a review comment changes only the parts it names.
  2. **Contact sheets.** The generator writes two contact sheets, each at 1x and at an integer zoom. The map sheet shows the 16-pixel tiles, props, animals and overworld dwarves in every role, plus a small sample room built from the tiles. The character sheet shows every 64-pixel part, variant and frame, plus assembled example dwarves.
  3. **Approval gate.** The owner reviews both sheets, and agents turn his comments into changes to pixel maps or rules before they run the generator again. Generator code is approved only after he approves both sheets, and his approval is recorded in the ticket's comments. Ticket 88's sample is the first review, so he sees the art before the importer format freezes (ticket 107) and before the full pack is built (ticket 108).
  4. **Iteration.** After ticket 88's first build, the generation of map and character sprites improves through further review rounds, learning from open-source projects.
- **Readings awaiting confirmation:** "Both sprites" means the 16-pixel map sprites and the 64-pixel character sprites. "The code" is the generator code, so the gate applies to ticket 88 and to every later change to the generator, including ticket 108's full pack. Open-source projects inform technique and style only: none of their art is copied into the default pack (D90 item 6), reused code follows D29 and its source licence, and adding a module beyond the Go standard library would amend D103 item 1. Iteration happens in ticket 88 until the owner approves, and then in ticket 108; no separate iteration or research ticket was created.
- **Amends and limits:** Amends D103 item 1, because shape data are now pixel maps, and item 3, because the owner's review now covers both sprite kinds and gates every approval of generator code, not only the default pack. Ticket numbers and blockers are unchanged. No generator or art exists yet, and no new dependency, contract freeze, readiness advancement or passed runtime gate is claimed.
- **Documentation:** [Fortress specification §6](../.scratch/fortress-gui/spec.md#6-chosen-sprite-and-tileset-pipeline), [system specification](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/spec.md) and tickets 88 and 108.
- **Amended by D105:** the owner confirmed these readings, allowed Go modules from outside the standard library for sprite generation, and added ticket 137 for review rounds before the native GUI exists.

### D105. Default fortress art: Go modules for sprite generation and art rounds before the GUI

- **Date:** 2026-10-08. **Decided by:** modified. The owner changed the assistant's standard-library-only generator design (D103 item 1): Go modules from outside the standard library may now assist sprite generation. He approved D104's other readings as written, and he asked for the ticket the assistant offered, so that the art can improve before the native GUI exists.
- **Owner's words:**
  > A Go module from outside the standard library that can assist with sprite generation is worth the concession. Approve and note this decision. The remaining confirmation is approved, add the ticked to allow for art improvement before the GUI is complete
- **Resolved choices:**
  1. **Go modules.** The generator may use Go modules from outside the standard library when they assist sprite generation. D29 still applies to each one: it needs its record (function, licence, the modules it brings in, change in binary size, maintenance status, minimum Go version and how it would be removed) before use, and a `golang.org/x/*` module needs a decision-log entry. The import-boundary test admits only recorded modules to the generator and keeps them out of Core, router, Undercurrent and GUI packages. Module versions are pinned, so the same inputs still reproduce the same files, and neither the generator nor its modules ship in any program.
  2. **Readings confirmed.** "Both sprites" are the 16-pixel map sprites and the 64-pixel character sprites. The owner's approval of both contact sheets gates every change to the generator code, including ticket 108's full pack. Open-source projects inform technique and style only: none of their art is copied into the default pack, and reused code follows D29 and its source licence.
  3. **Art improvement before the GUI.** New ticket 137 continues the review rounds on the map and character sprites after ticket 88. It waits only for ticket 88, not for the native GUI or its asset loader (tickets 54 and 107). Each round follows D104's approval gate, and the ticket ends when the owner records that the art is ready to serve as the base for the full pack. Ticket 108 does not wait for ticket 137; it starts from the generator as last approved.
- **Amends and limits:** Amends D103 item 1, which kept the generator to the standard library, and D104's readings on modules and on where iteration happens. Pixel maps (D104 item 1), original art, no image-generation model, no model call and reproducible output stand. A module does not replace pixel maps: a technique that generates shapes by rule needs the owner's decision first. Where the generator declares its modules, in the root module or in a nested module as D44 did for the GUI, is settled with the first module's D29 record. No module is chosen yet, and no dependency, contract freeze, readiness advancement or passed runtime gate is claimed.
- **Documentation:** [Fortress specification §6](../.scratch/fortress-gui/spec.md#6-chosen-sprite-and-tileset-pipeline), [system specification](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/spec.md), [sprite-tool research](../.scratch/fortress-gui/sprite-tool-research.md) and tickets 88, 107, 108 and 137.

### D106. Toolchain: go1.27.2

- **Date:** 2026-10-09. **Decided by:** adopted. C0's vulnerability scan found 13 known standard-library vulnerabilities in go1.27.1, all fixed in go1.27.2 (released 2026-10-08). No project code calls them, so the scan passed. The assistant proposed moving the pin, and the owner approved.
- **Owner's words:**
  > move to go1.27.2 approved
- **Decision:** go1.27.2, installed permanently with the official installer, replaces go1.27.1. `go.mod` keeps `go 1.27` and declares `toolchain go1.27.2`; the check reads that pin and runs with `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`. The rest of D28 stands.
- **Changes:** core §8.1; `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C0; system specification implementation decision 5; fortress terminal research step 1. Amends D28 and D45. Measurements taken with go1.27.1 (core Appendix A, `DECISION-RUST-VS-GO.md`, `RESEARCH-IMPLEMENTATION-LANGUAGE.md`) stay as recorded.
- **Amended by D147:** go1.27.3 replaces go1.27.2.

### B17. C0: tool pins, check steps and import-table encoding

- **Date:** 2026-10-09. **Decided by:** assistant, within the owner's delegated build authority.
- **Question:** C0 implements D42's check and D45's import table under D41 and D50. Which tool versions does `go.mod` pin, how does the check encode the table, and which packages and agent files does C0 create?
- **Decision:**
  1. **Tool pins.** `go.mod` declares staticcheck 2026.2.1 (module `honnef.co/go/tools` v0.8.1) and govulncheck v1.8.0 (module `golang.org/x/vuln`) as `tool` lines, and the check runs them with `go tool`. Core §8.2 holds their D29 records; this entry admits the `golang.org/x/...` modules they bring in as tool-only dependencies. rapid v1.3.0 joins `go.mod` with the first test that imports it, because `go mod tidy` removes an unused requirement.
  2. **Check steps.** `go run ./tools/check` uses the standard library only and runs nine steps in order; every step runs even after an earlier failure, and the check exits non-zero if any step failed. The steps: the toolchain must match `go.mod`'s `toolchain` line; the pinned toolchain's gofmt; `go vet`; staticcheck; govulncheck against the official online database, named explicitly with `-db https://vuln.go.dev` (C0 has no snapshot mode, and an unreachable database fails); the import-boundary test; tests with `CGO_ENABLED=0`; the race tests; and `CGO_ENABLED=0` builds for windows/amd64, linux/amd64, linux/arm64, darwin/amd64 and darwin/arm64, written to the null device so that nothing lands in the worktree; Go still compiles every matched package in that form, as a planted type error in a library package showed. Every child Go command sets `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`, an explicit `CGO_ENABLED` and an empty `GOFLAGS`, and step 0 fails if a `GOFLAGS` value saved with `go env -w` would still apply.
  3. **Race compiler.** On Windows, `DWARF_CC` must be an absolute path whose `--version` reports `clang version 23.1.2` and `Target: x86_64-w64-windows-gnu`; otherwise the race step fails without searching or installing. Linux uses `/usr/bin/gcc` and macOS `xcrun --find clang`; both stay unproven until C3's CI.
  4. **Import-table encoding.** D45's table lives in `tools/check` as code. A package may import its own tree. D50's appender rule overrides that: only `internal/core/...`, `internal/router/...`, `internal/undercurrent/graph/...` and the appender's own tests may import `contract/ledger/appender`. Any cgo file in the root module fails, and a package in no row fails as unknown. Exception lists start empty, and every entry must cite its decision. Test variants map to their package's tree, may import the package under test and follow the test-files row. The listing runs once per build target with cgo enabled for metadata, so files selected by any of the five supported targets are checked, not only the host's.
  5. **Packages.** C0 creates only `tools/check`. `cmd/dwarf-harness` arrives with the first step that gives it real behavior, because an empty program would be a placeholder (D41).
  6. **Agent instructions.** `AGENTS.md` is the canonical agent file and `CLAUDE.md` imports it, so C0's agent instructions are a section of `AGENTS.md` rather than a separate `CLAUDE.md`.
- **Reasons:** each choice follows D28, D41, D42, D45 and D50, or closes a gap they leave open: a listing for the host alone misses files for other operating systems, and `go build ./...` writes a binary into the worktree when it matches a single main package.
- **Changes:** `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C0; core §8.2; `AGENTS.md`.

The licences of the current dependencies (MIT, BSD-3-Clause and Apache-2.0) are compatible with it.

### D108. Character sprites: a still front view, an animated three-quarter view, and Corps emblems and colours

- **Date:** 2026-10-09. **Decided by:** owner. Reviewing ticket 88's first contact sheets, the owner approved the map sheet and set new requirements for the 64-pixel character sprites. He then settled three details by picking from assistant-written options.
- **Owner's words:**
  > Map sheet approved, ArtGen coder should be aware that the RPG character sheet should have animation ready Sprites. They will be displayed straight on in a roster screen but in three-quarters profile for their page, animated to display their Corps and role
- **Clarifications (question tool):**
  > Emblem and colour
  >
  > Idle plus role action
  >
  > Still frame
- **Decision:**
  1. **Two views.** Every 64-pixel character is drawn in two views: straight on, shown as a still frame wherever agents are listed (roster screens such as the Corps party sheet), and in three-quarter profile on the agent's own page.
  2. **Animation on the agent's page.** The three-quarter sprite plays an idle loop while the agent is idle and a role action loop while it is working, for example the worker at work with a tool, the orchestrator directing, the griot reciting and the diplomat bowing. Core's actual work status decides which loop plays; the animation reports it and never decides it.
  3. **Corps identity.** Each Corps has a colour and an emblem, shown together as the emblem on a field of the Corps colour, for example on a pennant or shield, in both views. They are presentation data like other appearance: they grant no authority and never enter prompts, routing or Core state. The role keeps its trim colour and non-colour badge, and Grand roles keep their crest.
  4. **Animation-ready parts.** Every 64-pixel part is drawn for both views and registers across every frame, and every shape and frame stays a hand-written pixel map (D104).
- **Consequences:** Ticket 88's sample adds both views, the idle loop, representative role actions and example Corps markers to the character sheet, whose approval waits for that round; the approved 16-pixel map sprites stay unchanged. Ticket 108's full pack covers every part in both views, the idle loop and a role action for every role, and a default set of Corps emblems and colours. Tickets 107, 113 and 117 gain cross-links for the manifest, the agent and party sheets, and the appearance preview.
- **Amends and limits:** Extends fortress spec §4 and §6 and the art requirements of D103–D105. It changes neither the overworld's one dwarf per agent nor the separation of presentation from Core facts. Frame counts, the emblem set, Corps colours and how a Corps's emblem and colour are chosen are presentation and engineering work that the owner reviews through the contact sheets and the GUI tickets. No implementation, contract freeze, readiness advancement or passed runtime gate is claimed.
- **Documentation:** [Fortress specification §4 and §6](../.scratch/fortress-gui/spec.md#6-chosen-sprite-and-tileset-pipeline), [system specification](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/spec.md) and tickets 88, 107, 108, 113 and 117.
- **Amended by D109:** the 64-pixel sprites carry no role marker; item 3's last sentence applies to the 16-pixel map sprites only.
- **See also D110:** a new Corps's colour and emblem are chosen on ticket 125's creation screen.

### D109. Character sprites carry no role marker

- **Date:** 2026-10-09. **Decided by:** adopted. While drafting the second round of ticket 88's sample, the art designer asked whether the 64-pixel character sprites also carry a role marker. The owner picked one of three assistant-written options.
- **Owner's words (question tool):**
  > No role marker
- **Decision:** The 64-pixel sprites carry no role trim, badge or crest in either view; they show only the Corps emblem and colour. On the agent's page the three-quarter view's role action loop shows the role, and roster screens show it as text beside the straight-on still. The 16-pixel map sprites keep the role-coloured trim, the non-colour badge and the Grand crest unchanged (fortress spec §2).
- **Amends:** D108 item 3's last sentence, which now applies to the 16-pixel map sprites only.
- **Documentation:** [Fortress specification §6](../.scratch/fortress-gui/spec.md#6-chosen-sprite-and-tileset-pipeline) and ticket 88.
- **Amended by D120:** Grand officers show a colony banner instead of a Corps marker.

### B18. C1: ledger format v1 encoding, colony store and OS adapter

- **Date:** 2026-10-09. **Decided by:** assistant, within the owner's delegated build authority.
- **Question:** C1's ticket 02 builds D34's log, D35–D37's record rules, D38's colony directory, D50's appender and B3's OS adapter. Those decisions and Core §13.1 name the envelope fields, caps and recovery duties, but not the JSON keys, byte rules, error contract or platform calls. How does ledger format version 1 encode and verify records, and how does Core's store write, lock and recover a colony on each operating system?
- **Decision:**
  1. **Format and envelope** (`contract/ledger`, `FormatVersion = 1`; D35, D37).
     - A record is one line: `lowercase_hex(SHA-256(json))`, TAB, `json`, LF. `json` is one compact UTF-8 object that `json.Compact` leaves unchanged. The hash covers exactly those bytes, and readers never re-marshal.
     - Keys, in the order the writer emits them: `format`, `seq`, `commit_id`, `commit_index`, `commit_count`, `prev_hash`, `event_time_ns`, `clock_held`, `colony_id`, `type`, `type_version`, `actor`, `agent_id`, `execution_session_id`, `context_id`, `turn_id`, `operation_id`, `measurement_path`, `payload`. `clock_held` (written only as `true`), the five IDs after `actor` and `measurement_path` are optional; the rest are required. `format` must be the first key.
     - IDs are canonical lowercase dashed UUIDv7 strings. Integers have no sign, fraction or exponent; `seq` and `event_time_ns` can exceed 2^53 and are exact only for decoders that keep 64-bit integers. `type` matches `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*(\.[a-z][a-z0-9_]*)+$` and `type_version` is at least 1. `actor` is one of `owner`, `agent`, `core` and `runner`; adding one is a format change. `measurement_path` matches `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*(\.[a-z][a-z0-9_]*)*$`, and each record family owns its vocabulary. `payload` is an object, `{}` when empty, with no repeated key at any depth; `EncodeCommit` refuses one that breaks this, and readers do not re-check payload interiors.
     - A repeated key, a `null` known key, a missing required key, a present but empty optional value or `"clock_held":false` makes a record invalid. Unknown keys are ignored, so optional keys can be added later. An absent optional key decodes to its zero value, which no present value can equal, so a missing field is never read as a default. A correctly hashed record whose `format` is an integer other than 1 returns `*UnsupportedFormatError{Segment, Offset, Format}`, matching `ErrUnsupportedFormat`; every other invalid record fails closed with `*CorruptError{Segment, Offset, Reason}`, matching `ErrCorrupt`.
     - Body references are not in this envelope. Ticket 03 adds them with the body store, as an optional key with a fixed position, and regenerates the goldens.
  2. **Commits, chain and caps** (D34, D36, D37).
     - A commit's records have consecutive `seq`, one `commit_id`, a `commit_count` of at least 1, `commit_index` from 0 to `commit_count-1`, and the same `event_time_ns` and `clock_held`. The record with `commit_index == commit_count-1` ends the commit (D35's mark). A log's first record has `seq` 1. `prev_hash` is the previous record's hash across commits and segments; `seq` 1 carries `ZeroHash`, 64 zeros.
     - The writer stamps one time per commit. If the clock reads earlier than the previous commit's time, the writer reuses that time and sets `clock_held` on every record of the commit. Event time is above 0 and never decreases, a `clock_held` time equals the previous record's, and `clock_held` is never on `seq` 1. Producers keep their own observed times in their payloads.
     - `EncodeCommit(after Chain, h CommitHeader, drafts []Draft)` is the only encoder, a pure function used by the writer and the golden generator. It assigns `seq`, `prev_hash` and the commit fields; it refuses with `ErrInvalid` any commit a reader would reject, and with `ErrTooLarge` a line over `MaxRecordBytes` (1 << 20, counting hash, TAB and LF) or a commit over `MaxCommitBytes` (16 << 20). A line or commit of exactly the cap is accepted.
     - `Check` and `Reader` fail closed on any longer line or larger complete commit on disk, which bounds a reader's memory to one commit. Changing either cap breaks the format, because older readers reject larger records, and needs a D40 decision entry.
  3. **Segments and recovery** (D34, Core §13.1).
     - Segments in `log/` are named by their first record's `seq` as 20 zero-padded digits plus `.jsonl` (`SegmentName`). The first is `00000000000000000001.jsonl`; each later one is named by the previous segment's last `seq` plus 1. A segment holds whole commits, and only the newest may be empty or end in a torn commit. Names beginning with a dot are ignored; any other entry that is not a regular file with a segment name fails closed. Compressed segments (D36) need a name rule before they are recognised.
     - `Check(logDir, colony)` verifies the whole log read-only and returns `CheckResult{Last, Segment, Size, TornBytes}` (`Genesis()` and an empty `Segment` for a log with no segment). The bytes after the last complete commit of the final segment are a torn final commit only if they total at most `MaxCommitBytes` and every LF-terminated chunk that is, or ends in, a correctly hashed line decodes and fits the one commit that could have been in flight: it carries the colony's ID; all such lines share `commit_id`, `commit_count`, `event_time_ns` and `clock_held`; `seq` equals the last complete `seq` + 1 + `commit_index`; `commit_index` strictly increases with file position; the index-0 line starts exactly where the last complete commit ends and links to its hash; a line whose predecessor in the commit is present links to it; and the time is not before the last complete commit's, and equals it when `clock_held` is set. Partial lines, zeros and garbage are permitted. Anything else, and any damage before the final commit, fails closed.
  4. **Reader** (D35, D49).
     - `OpenReader(logDir, ReadOptions{Colony, After, Through, Consume})` opens files read-only and never writes, truncates, renames or locks, so replay has no effects and may run while a writer holds the colony. `Next` returns whole commits holding only consumed records and skips commits without one. `Cursor()` advances past every commit fully processed, skipped or returned, and stays at the last one on any error; `After` is the last consumed `seq`, 0 for none. An unconsumed type is skipped; a consumed type at an undeclared version stops the reader with `*UnsupportedVersionError{Seq, Type, Version, Supported}`. Every record read is verified, consumed or not; a resumed reader first verifies the link from the previous segment's last line; memory is never sized from `commit_count`.
     - `Through` is explicit. Inside a running Core it is the writer's `Durable()`. `ToEnd` (`^uint64(0)`) reads every complete commit and applies the torn-tail rule; it is for offline reading, because beside a live writer it can expose an unacknowledged commit. `After` or `Through` inside a commit, `After` above `Through`, or `After` beyond the end of a log read with `ToEnd` returns `ErrCursor`; a log that ends before a numeric `Through` returns `ErrCorrupt`. `OpenReader` and `Check` return `ErrInvalid` for a colony ID that is not a UUIDv7. After `Close`, `Next` fails with an error wrapping `os.ErrClosed` unless the reader had already failed or ended.
  5. **Catalogue and goldens** (B2, D40).
     - `Defined()` returns the record type versions F0 defines: none in ticket 02, because no source yet fully specifies a C1-produced payload and B2 forbids placeholders. `NewCatalogue` refuses a type that breaks the grammar, a version below 1 and a repeated entry. The store refuses every type version outside the catalogue it is given, and production callers must pass `ledger.Defined()`; `tools/check` cannot express this rule, so C3's review verifies it. The namespace `fixture.` is reserved for contract fixtures and tests: no family may use it, and `Defined()` never contains it.
     - The golden F0 log is `contract/ledger/testdata/f0/log/00000000000000000001.jsonl` (seq 1–4) and `00000000000000000005.jsonl` (seq 5–6): four commits of `fixture.sample` v1 and v2 and `fixture.other` v1, covering one- and two-record commits, absent and present optional fields, `clock_held`, two versions of one type and rotation. `TestGoldenF0` builds them with `EncodeCommit` and compares them byte for byte; its `-update` flag rewrites them. They are LF-only (B1).
  6. **Colony store and lock** (`internal/core/ledgerstore`; D38, D50, Core §13.1).
     - A colony directory holds `colony.json`, `lock` and `log/`, with mode 0o700 for directories and 0o600 for files (Windows ignores the modes; the inherited ACL applies). `Create(dir, cat)` and `Open(dir, cat)` take an explicit directory, D38's per-colony override, and make it absolute; D38's default root and `<colony>` naming are left to the first caller that selects colonies, C3 or C7. `colony.json` is Core-internal, not a contract: exactly `{"format":1,"colony_id":"<UUIDv7>"}` and LF, written to `colony.json.tmp`, flushed and published with `durable.Rename`. Reading it requires `format` 1 and a canonical UUIDv7 `colony_id` and ignores other keys; a bad file returns a plain error naming the file and what is wrong.
     - The store holds an exclusive OS lock on the empty `lock` file from `Create` or `Open` until `Close` or the holder's death, through recovery; nothing steals it by age or PID, and the store never deletes a lock file. A held lock returns `ErrLocked`, which also matches `durable.ErrLocked`, and every `Create` or `Open` error after locking releases the lock. `Create` returns `ErrExists` if `colony.json` exists, checked before locking and again under the lock. It creates a missing directory and flushes its parent, creates `log/` or refuses an existing one holding any entry that does not begin with a dot (`*ledger.CorruptError`), flushes the directory, then publishes `colony.json` with a new colony ID; a crash leaves no colony or a complete one. The first `Append` creates the first segment.
     - `Open` looks for `colony.json` before taking the lock. For a missing directory, or one without `colony.json`, it returns `ErrNotColony`, wrapping the not-exist error, and creates nothing; it creates the lock file only inside a colony, then reads `colony.json` under the lock. A colony without `log/` fails closed, and `Open` never recreates it. `Open` runs `Check` and, on any error, changes no byte of the log. It then truncates and flushes a torn final commit, reported as `Recovery{Segment, TruncatedBytes}`, and flushes `log/` and the colony directory.
     - `Append` implements `appender.Appender` and returns `Committed{First, Last}` only after the commit is written and flushed. `ctx` bounds only the wait for the writer turn: an ended `ctx` is checked before the turn, and a cancelled wait returns `ctx.Err()`. The turn is a 1-slot semaphore with no fairness promise; AC06 control priority (ticket 28) belongs to Core's writer as a separate method or interface, so the contract does not change for it. Under the turn, a closed store returns `ErrClosed` and a poisoned one `ErrPoisoned`. A type version outside the catalogue, or an `EncodeCommit` refusal, returns `ErrRefused`, and size refusals also match `ledger.ErrTooLarge`. `ErrRefused`, `ErrPoisoned`, `ErrClosed` and a `ctx` error mean that no commit bytes were written and no sequence was used; `ErrUncertain` means the outcome is unknown.
     - A new segment starts when there is none, or before a commit that would take a non-empty segment past the 64 MiB segment target; the target is writer policy, not a format rule, and tests override it through an unexported seam. The writer closes the old segment's file, creates the new one under its final name with `O_CREATE|O_EXCL|O_WRONLY` and flushes `log/` before any commit bytes go into it; segments are never renamed. Any failure there, including closing the old file, poisons the store and returns `ErrPoisoned`. The commit is one `WriteAt` at the acknowledged size and one `os.File.Sync`; a write error, short write or flush error poisons the store and returns `ErrUncertain`. Only after the flush succeeds do the chain, size and `Durable()` advance.
     - `Recover` truncates the current segment to the end of its last acknowledged commit, or empties the new segment whose start failed (keeping the previous segment if that file was never created), flushes it and `log/` under the held lock, and clears the poison only on success. It may be retried, ignores the close error of the file that saw the failure, does nothing to a store that is not poisoned and returns `ErrClosed` after `Close`. An unacknowledged commit is then absent even if it was complete; after a crash before `Recover`, `Open` keeps a complete valid commit, which is why `ErrUncertain` means unknown. `Close` waits for the turn, closes the segment and releases the lock; `Append` then returns `ErrClosed`, and a second `Close` returns nil.
  7. **OS adapter** (`internal/core/durable`; B3, D29).
     - Its surface is `ErrLocked`, `Lock`, `LockFile`, `(*Lock).Close`, `SyncDir` and `Rename`; file data is flushed with `os.File.Sync`. Every error is returned, and nothing falls back. Windows: `LockFileEx` with `LOCKFILE_EXCLUSIVE_LOCK|LOCKFILE_FAIL_IMMEDIATELY` on the first byte (`ERROR_LOCK_VIOLATION` gives `ErrLocked`) and `UnlockFileEx`; `SyncDir` opens the directory with `CreateFile` (`GENERIC_READ|GENERIC_WRITE`, all three share modes, `OPEN_EXISTING`, `FILE_FLAG_BACKUP_SEMANTICS`) and calls `FlushFileBuffers`; `Rename` is `MoveFileEx` with `MOVEFILE_REPLACE_EXISTING|MOVEFILE_WRITE_THROUGH`, then `SyncDir` of the target's directory. Linux and macOS share `durable_posix.go` (`//go:build linux || darwin`): `flock` with `LOCK_EX|LOCK_NB` (`EWOULDBLOCK` gives `ErrLocked`; `EINTR` is retried), `fsync` of the directory (`F_FULLFSYNC` on macOS), and `os.Rename` followed by `SyncDir`.
     - On Windows, `SyncDir` and `Rename` always pass paths made absolute by `filepath.Abs` in the extended-length form: `\\?\` before a drive path, `\\?\UNC\` for a UNC path, and `\\?\` or `\\.\` paths unchanged. `LockFile` opens its file through package `os`, which handles long paths itself.
     - `go.mod` requires `golang.org/x/sys` v0.48.0 directly, and only this package imports it; Core §8.2 holds the D29 record. `tools/check` admits `golang.org/x/...` for all of `internal/core/...`, so B3's narrower rule is held by review, which lists the x/sys importers for each build target. A check rule, an allowlist of x/sys importers with its own decision entry, is due no later than the arrival of a second importer or C3's CI step, whichever comes first.
  8. **Tests and verification** (D42).
     - `contract/ledger`: `TestGoldenF0`, `TestGoldenDecodes`, `TestReaderConsumption`, `TestReaderCursorAndBound`, `TestCheckFailsClosed` (each mutation located by `Segment` and `Offset`), `TestEncodeCommitRefuses` (each cap accepted exactly and refused one byte over), `TestReadingHasNoEffects`, `TestNewCatalogue`, and the fuzz targets `FuzzCheckTruncation`, `FuzzCheckTornTail` and `FuzzCheckCorruption`. `internal/core/durable`: `TestLockIsExclusive`, `TestSyncDirAndRenameBeyondMaxPath` (paths over 260 characters, absolute and relative to a short working directory) and the Windows-only `TestExtendedPath` (a table of path forms). The long-path test can detect a missing prefix only where Windows long paths are disabled; `TestExtendedPath` checks the form on every Windows machine.
     - `internal/core/ledgerstore`: `TestCreateAndOpenLayout` (including `Open` of a non-colony, which leaves its directory empty), `TestWriterReproducesGolden` (Core's writer emits the golden bytes), `TestConcurrentAppendsAreGapless`, `TestDurableAdvancesOnlyAfterSync`, `TestAppendFailurePoisonsUntilRecover` (short write, write error, flush error, and a directory-flush or segment-creation error while rotating), `TestRefusedAppendConsumesNothing`, `TestRotationKeepsCommitsWhole`, `TestOpenTruncatesTornFinalCommit` (a torn three-record commit cut at 16 structural points: the commit boundary as the control, `boundary+1`, and on each line the hash end, after the TAB, the JSON midpoint, before the LF and after the LF, except after the last line; and a fully torn later segment), `TestOpenFailsClosedOnCorruption`, `TestEventTimeNeverDecreases`, `TestCancelledAppendWritesNothing` and `TestKilledWriterReleasesLockAndRecovers` (the parent sees `ErrLocked` while a child process writes, kills the child, waits for its exit and trusts only its complete output lines, then opens the colony with no stale-lock handling and finds every acknowledged commit). The unexported seams (`deps`: clock, the source of the colony and commit IDs, files, segment target) serve deterministic regressions only and prove no platform behaviour.
     - Verification on 2026-10-09 at the evidence commits: `go run ./tools/check` passed all nine steps; regenerating the goldens with `-update` left `testdata` unchanged; the concurrency, durability and killed-writer tests passed with `-count=20`; `go list` showed `contract/...` importing only the standard library and `contract/ledger`, and only `internal/core/durable` importing x/sys; `go.mod`'s only change was x/sys becoming a direct requirement, and `go.sum` was unchanged. Each fuzz target ran for 120 s, without a failure or a new corpus file, at the evidence commit for `contract/ledger`; the `contract/ledger` code they exercise has not changed since, and only tests were added. Before `main` moves, `go run ./tools/check` (all nine steps) and the `-count=20` runs are repeated on the commit that reaches it.
- **Owner sign-off:** OG1 (D41, D29, B3), given in the question tool on 2026-10-09, for `golang.org/x/sys` v0.48.0 as a direct production dependency imported only by `internal/core/durable`. It is the owner's D41 sign-off, not part of this assistant decision, and with it the commits that add x/sys may reach `main`. Owner's words:
  > Sign off x/sys
- **Reasons:**
  - Each item applies D29, D34–D38, D40, D42, D49, D50, B2 and B3, or settles a detail they leave open.
  - `format` comes first so that a record of a later format stops a reader with a clear error (D35) instead of failing as corruption on an earlier key. Marking the commit end by `commit_index` and `commit_count` leaves no separate flag that could disagree with the count.
  - The torn-tail rule permits what one interrupted write can leave (partial, zeroed or reordered blocks), yet fails closed on any correctly hashed line that does not fit the one commit in flight, so corruption followed by later commits is never truncated (Core §13.1). Its time conditions reject no genuine interrupted write, because `EncodeCommit` never emits a commit that breaks them.
  - After a failed flush the page cache may hold bytes that never reach the disk, so only truncation followed by a successful flush makes an uncertain commit's outcome definite, and the lock stays held throughout (Core §13.1). The writer stamps time because D37 flags a system clock that moved backwards; flagging producer-supplied times would mislabel late but legitimate observations. An explicit `Through` exposes only flushed commits without tying the contract to Core.
  - `Create` refuses every entry of an existing `log/` that does not begin with a dot, not only segments, because `Check` would fail closed on the others once the colony exists. `Open` looks for `colony.json` before locking because a lock file is never deleted, so a mistaken path must not receive one. `TestOpenTruncatesTornFinalCommit` samples structural cut points because the offset-sensitive rule is `Check`'s, which `FuzzCheckTruncation` seeds at every golden commit boundary and its neighbours and then fuzzes.
  - The standard `syscall` package lacks `LockFileEx`, `UnlockFileEx` and `MoveFileEx` with write-through, D29 asks for a recorded library for code that differs across operating systems, and B3 admits x/sys for exactly this adapter. The extended-length form is safe unconditionally because `filepath.Abs` has already normalised the path, and it works whatever a machine's long-path setting.
- **Limits:**
  - Nothing is frozen: format version 1, the reader and the appender stay Draft. OG2, the D40 freeze request, belongs to ticket 03 at C1's end. It will ask the owner about the empty ticket-02 catalogue and the `fixture.` reservation, the keys with `format` first and integers above 2^53, the commit marker, the caps and that changing them breaks the format, the closed actor set, the appender's error taxonomy and the `Through`/`ToEnd` rule. Nothing here grants D52 approval, freezes a contract or moves any status beyond C1 In progress.
  - Platform status: Windows behaviour is verified on NTFS on this machine; other file systems and network shares are unverified. Linux and macOS are only compiled until C3's CI exists (D42).
  - This is not a power-loss guarantee (B3): it relies on the file system and the device honouring their flushes. The torn-tail rule cannot tell damage confined to the final commit, up to `MaxCommitBytes`, from an interrupted write until the latest hash is anchored externally (D36, assurance A3).
  - Ticket 03 adds body references and the body store, unavailable-body records, D58 identity lookups, rebuilt indexes and workload measurements; ticket 28 adds AC06 priority. C3 wires the store into the host and decides when to call `Recover`.
- **Changes:** new packages `contract/ledger`, `contract/ledger/appender`, `internal/core/durable` and `internal/core/ledgerstore`; `go.mod` (`golang.org/x/sys` v0.48.0 becomes a direct requirement); `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C1; `CONTRACTS.md` (`ledger-format`, `ledger-reader` and `ledger-appender` Draft); Core §8.2 (`golang.org/x/sys` row and D29 record).
- **See also:** D111 records the owner's freeze of `ledger-format`, `ledger-reader` and `ledger-appender` at version 1, which this entry's Limits left Draft.
- **See also:** D151 implements item 7's x/sys importer allowlist in `tools/check`.

### B19. Art generator round 2: character views, page loops and Corps pennants

- **Date:** 2026-10-09. **Decided by:** assistant, within the owner's delegated build authority.
- **Question:** The second round of ticket 88 gives the 64-pixel character sprites what D108 and D109 ask for: a still front view, an animated three-quarter view with an idle loop and representative role actions, and each Corps's emblem on a field of its colour, with no role marker and every frame a hand-written pixel map (D104). Those decisions say what the sprites show, but not how the generator declares views, loops and Corps markers, which rules keep layers registered and colours apart, what the draft metadata records, or which roles and Corps the sample draws. How does round 2 settle them?
- **Decision:**
  1. **Views.** A table in the generator code defines three views. Each has a directory under `art/` and `sample/`, a grid, a facing, and the canvas and anchor that all its layers share. Facing `front` means the dwarf faces the viewer; `three_quarter_right` turns it 45 degrees toward screen right, so its right hand, which holds the front accessory, is the near hand.
