Undercurrent §13.4 selects the field law's schema and registry, its two parser profiles and its exact-byte identity; D47 puts the flake-rate prior, its confidence and the quarantine threshold in `law-format`; and B12 keeps local validation, check and environment data out of the shared law. They leave open how those parameters are encoded and identified, how the profiles count, how a refusal is reported, how versions are told apart, and which Go API and golden vectors the contract has. How does ticket 04 settle them?
- **Decision:**
  1. **Package** (D29, D40, D45).
     - `contract/law` holds `law-format` and uses the standard library only. It imports neither `contract/ledger` nor anything under `internal/`, which `tools/check`'s `contract/...` row enforces; the check gains no row.
     - Two pure loaders, `Load` and `LoadValidationPolicy`, take an artifact's bytes and the host's `LoadLimits`. They read no file, clock or environment, start no goroutine and keep no package state, and a result shares no memory with its input or with another result.
     - The JSON reader is `encoding/json`'s `Decoder.Token` with `UseNumber`, as in `contract/ledger`, not `encoding/json/v2`. There is no encoder, no `io.Reader` variant and no `internal/undercurrent/law` package.
     - `Digest` is the package's own SHA-256 type, written as 64 lowercase hex digits like `ledger.Digest`, so that `law-format` can be frozen and delivered without `ledger-format` (Undercurrent §13.4).
  2. **The field law, version 1** (Undercurrent §13.4).
     - One strict UTF-8 JSON object without a BOM. Its members: `format`, `dwarf-harness/undercurrent-law`; `schema_version`, 1; `evaluator_version`, 1; `kernel`, `bounded_best_arrival/v1`; `law_id`, matching `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9._-]{0,63}$`; `law_version`, 1 to 2,147,483,647; `weight_scale_ppm`, exactly 1,000,000; and `kinds`, exactly six kind objects in ascending `id` order. `law_id` and `law_version` are labels, never identity.
     - A kind object has `id`; `quantity_class`, `extensive` or `evidential`; `unit`, the kind's own; `sources`, 1 to 8 in ascending `rule_id` order; `hop_latency_ms`, 0 to 86,400,000; `attenuation_ppm`, 1 to 999,999; and `cutoff_units` and `saturation_units`, each 1 to 1,000,000,000,000, with saturation above cutoff. A source object has `rule_id`, a rule registered for the kind; `mode`, the rule's; `unit`, the kind's; `half_life_seconds`, 1 to 31,536,000 for `impulse` and 0 otherwise; `age_rule_id`, `linear_per_day/v1` for `rising` and `none` otherwise; `rise_units_per_day`, 1 to 1,000,000,000,000 for `rising` and 0 otherwise; and `coupling`, whose `profile`, `direction` and `edge_types` (0 to 2, ascending) must be the rule's registered profile. Every member is required, no other member is allowed, nothing has a default, and member order is free.
     - The evaluator-v1 registry is §13.4's: six kinds, each with its unit; 22 rules, each with a fixed kind, mode and profile; four profiles, each fixing a direction and the whole edge list; and three edge types, `declared_dataflow`, `registered_resource_use` and `static_dependency`. A law may omit rules, but each kind keeps at least one source. `validation_f1_unconfirmed/v1` is not a rule, and R1's `router-headroom/v1` scheme is not the `router_headroom/v1` rule. The package documentation tables the registry, and exported constants name every closed value.
     - `quantity_class` is checked only against its two values, for any kind, because §9 calls `surprise` derived. There is no amplitude member and no amplitude check: §13.4's rule that amplitudes stay within saturation belongs to the engine, which caps them, and §9's amplitudes are provisional candidates.
     - The Go types keep §13.2's numeric field types, with the kinds as an ascending slice instead of a map and the vocabulary as string types whose empty value is never valid.
  3. **Load limits** (Undercurrent §13.4).
     - The host passes `LoadLimits` explicitly; neither artifact supplies them. `CompactV1()` returns 65,536 bytes, depth 12, 64 members, 128 elements, 128 string bytes and 2,048 tokens, and `StandardV1()` 262,144, 16, 128, 256, 256 and 8,192, each as a fresh value. Every bound is inclusive. A field below 1 or above its standard-v1 value is refused with `ErrInvalidLimits` before the input is read, so limits tighten but never widen, and zero is not a default. Both artifacts use the same limits.
     - `Bytes` counts the whole input. The root container is depth 1, and each nested object or array adds one. `Members` counts an object's name/value pairs, a repeated key included; `Elements` counts an array's values; `StringBytes` counts a string's decoded UTF-8 bytes, keys included. `Tokens` counts every value `json.Decoder.Token` returns, keys and delimiters included, but not commas, colons or whitespace.
     - A valid version-1 law needs at most depth 7, 8 members, 6 elements, 64-byte strings and 681 tokens, so only its size, through whitespace or escaped spellings, can exceed `compact-v1`. A validation policy needs depth 2, 5 members, 31-byte strings and 27 tokens.
  4. **Refusal: phases and errors.**
     - Refusal is atomic: a loader returns its zero value and one error, never part of an artifact. The phases run in a fixed order. Phase 0 checks the limits. Phase 1 checks strict JSON over the whole input: `Bytes`, then a BOM, then UTF-8, then a token walk that checks, at each token, syntax, `Tokens`, `Depth`, `Members`, `Elements`, `StringBytes`, repeated decoded keys and integer spelling, then anything but whitespace after the root. Phase 2 checks the head: the root must be an object, then `format`, `schema_version`, `evaluator_version` and `kernel` for a law, or `format` and `schema_version` for a policy, each refused as missing, of the wrong type, or unsupported. Phase 3 decodes the closed version-1 objects depth first: in each object, unknown members, then missing ones, then each member's type and value, then the rules between members; in each array, its length, then its elements, then their order.
     - Each error wraps exactly one sentinel: `ErrInvalidLimits`; `ErrLimit`, with `*LimitError` (bound, maximum, offset); `ErrMalformed`, with `*MalformedError` (`bom`, `invalid_utf8`, `syntax`, `duplicate_key`, `not_unsigned_integer` or `trailing_data`); `ErrUnsupported`, with `*UnsupportedError` (field, value, supported value); and `ErrInvalid`, with `*FieldError`, an RFC 6901 JSON Pointer and one of `unknown_field`, `missing_field`, `wrong_type`, `unknown_value`, `out_of_range`, `pattern`, `count`, `unsorted`, `duplicate` and `mismatch`. `null` is always `wrong_type`.
     - Integers are unsigned decimal digits: a sign, fraction or exponent is `not_unsigned_integer`, leading zeros are a syntax error, and a phase-3 integer outside its member's range, including one beyond 64 bits, is `out_of_range`; a head member's other value is unsupported. Strings and keys are decoded before they are validated, so an escaped spelling is accepted with a different identity, and an escaped lone surrogate decodes to U+FFFD and fails as `pattern`, `unknown_value` or `unknown_field`.
     - **Phase 1 belongs to the format family.** Its rules hold for every schema and evaluator version: no version relaxes them or reorders the phases.
     - **What is promised.** Error types, codes, paths and the order of the phases are the contract. The order among several violations within a phase follows the package documentation's tables and is pinned by tests, but it is not a compatibility promise, and a consumer must not branch on which of several violations is reported. Neither `Error()` text nor offsets are promised; in this version a BOM's offset is 0 and the size limit's is its maximum.
  5. **Identity** (B12).
     - An accepted artifact's `SHA256` is the SHA-256 of its content bytes exactly as given: not Git's object ID, with no `blob <size>` header, and with nothing normalized, neither whitespace, line endings, key order, escapes nor fraction terms. Only an accepted artifact has an identity, and neither artifact may hold a hash member. The caller supplies the immutable blob bytes, not a converted checkout.
     - `Law.SHA256` is the `field_law_sha256` that Core's activation manifest binds, and `ValidationPolicy.SHA256` is its `validation_policy_hash`.
  6. **The validation policy, version 1** (D47; Undercurrent §13.4).
     - D47's parameters form a separate local artifact, never members of the shared law: `format`, `dwarf-harness/validation-policy`; `schema_version`, 1; and exactly `flake_rate_prior`, `failure_repair_confidence` and `quarantine_threshold`. Each is a `{numerator, denominator}` object of integers from 1 to 18,446,744,073,709,551,615, the `uint64` range, with the numerator below the denominator. Fractions are not reduced, so 1/20 and 2/40 are different artifacts. There is no label or policy ID: the digest is the identity.
     - Following OG-B, the policy holds these three only, and a root that carries `max_fresh_executions_per_incident` is refused as `unknown_field`. The per-incident execution cap, which Core §13.4 and the validation research's V1 list among the provisional law parameters, needs its own decision before C4 relies on it.
  7. **Binding and exclusions** (Undercurrent §13.4; Core §13.2).
     - A consumer reads the policy only as the bytes Core retains and binds by `validation_policy_hash`, never from a working-tree settings file. It checks that `ValidationPolicy.SHA256` equals that hash, and, when it reads the law, that `Law.SHA256` equals `field_law_sha256`; then it uses the typed values. So C4 reads its parameters without parsing the field law or starting U1.E, as `ExampleLoadValidationPolicy` shows.
     - Core alone supplies `activation_id`, `epoch`, `effective_sequence`, the sorted workspace, check and environment bindings, the owner's authorization, the activation-request and expected-manifest hashes and `not_before_utc_ns`. Neither artifact may carry these, grants, credentials or its own hash, and the law also refuses D47's values and `validation_policy_hash`: each is `unknown_field`, at the root or nested.
     - Loading executes no reducer and activates, approves, schedules, authorizes, retains or retrieves nothing. A loaded artifact is neither engine readiness nor activation approval.
  8. **Versions and compatibility** (Undercurrent §13.4; D40).
     - Version 1 reads law schema 1 with evaluator 1 and kernel `bounded_best_arrival/v1`, and policy schema 1. Any other head value is unsupported, and is found before unknown members, so a newer artifact fails clearly and is never reinterpreted.
     - Version 1 is closed and never gains optional members. Additions come as new schema or evaluator versions read beside it, and a new kind, rule or kernel needs a new evaluator version. A later contract version must load every version-1 golden to the same typed value and digest, and no upgrader converts an old artifact to new semantics.
  9. **Golden vectors** (`CONTRACTS.md`; B1).
     - Ten files under `contract/law/testdata/`, authored without a committed encoder or an `-update` flag and pinned by SHA-256 in `golden_test.go`:
       - `law/v1/full.json`, every registered rule: 3b148a28c5cd88445c3cd43b53e25cb99b8e9009e57ab7416e7293469134895c;
       - `law/v1/full-compact.json`, the same law compacted: f1382c84a68f79ea258040f160fdd4d8a55decf4bed93d3a20eb861c81965583;
       - `law/v1/full-reordered.json`, every object's members reversed: 3617c086c1cd5d2479175cbdbeb6d6fbcec0a48ca7333cae86fea730ec49b7a4;
       - `law/v1/minimal.json`, one source per kind and every range at its minimum: b6341867c260f09f666ead95102d405ad67ed2e7f38595a5c254a9fd68daf280;
       - `law/v1/bounds.json`, every range at its maximum: be2d34fa8e9c7fd58a133c1c9768cef4947988559ab65049fc8516b3ddb3fc43;
       - `law/unsupported/schema-version-2.json`, with an extra member: fa30e702051ef0695ca0501c55965a3b2beea32a9cafb6adf846d31f748232a6;
       - `law/unsupported/evaluator-version-2.json`: ecc378673dd0830be3841023cfcc47560003165a1b6bbc8b7a6cd6354eca0337;
       - `law/unsupported/kernel-v2.json`: ba5d5a61a8b0ca6b65aac1943294c4d63bc5bca866ebc97b75471d52766a6450;
       - `validation-policy/v1/policy.json`: 1f3c66a9eca49f82e0e224c8f7a5edaee890757b6281a035f2e9a1f543f4f447;
       - `validation-policy/unsupported/schema-version-2.json`, with an extra member: 5f95d48c1dc8ac933d79d156e289e8ee0931e887d28f1e1dd2ba90e1a739402a.
     - The line-ending vector is `full.json` with each LF replaced by CRLF, built in the test because B1 stores text files with LF. Its digest is pinned too, 83cad664b5eb46a5ef8453f4d57cfce5c9023d24bd54e8d9a463c0f2b6eaf9bf, and the test first checks that `full.json` has no CR and ends with exactly one LF. `full.json` and its compact, reordered and CRLF forms load to the same law with four different digests.
     - The values are Undercurrent §13.4's preregistration candidates and the validation research's provisional V1 values, 1/20, 999/1000 and 1/5: test data, not the owner's law or validation policy.
  10. **Tests** (D42).
      - `TestGoldenFilesPinned`, `TestGoldenLawsLoad`, `TestGoldenUnsupported`, `TestIdentity` and `TestEscapedSpellings`; `TestLawRejects`, `TestLawRefusesLocalData`, `TestPhaseOnePrecedence`, `TestFieldViolationOrder` and `TestLoadOwnsResult`; `TestLoadLimitsBounds`, `TestLoadLimitsTightened` and `TestLoadLimitsRefused`; `TestValidationPolicyLoads`, `TestValidationPolicyRejects` and `TestValidationPolicyExactRationals`; `FuzzLoad` and `FuzzLoadValidationPolicy`; `ExampleLoad`, and `ExampleLoadValidationPolicy`, a C4-facing consumer that imports only the standard library and `contract/law`; and `BenchmarkLoad`, which sets no threshold. Every refusal case runs under both profiles.
      - Seven deliberate rule breaks each failed named tests, and the restored code passed: accepting repeated keys, accepting signed integers, allowing limits above standard-v1, trimming a final newline before hashing, accepting unknown policy members, accepting a numerator equal to its denominator, and reporting a newer schema as invalid instead of unsupported.
  11. **Landing and the freeze** (D40, D41, D46).
      - S1, its documentation fix-up and the documents reach `main` together, after the owner approves the merge as his instruction for the staged build requires (ticket 04's comments), so that `main` never holds a loadable contract that the register calls Not started. No frozen contract, dependency or owner decision is touched (D41).
      - Nothing is frozen: `law-format` is Draft, version —. The D40 freeze request follows once these commits are on `main` and is not awaited. U1.L is Done only when the owner freezes the format (the Undercurrent roadmap's U1 exit criterion).
- **Reasons:**
  - Each item applies D29, D40, D45, D47, B1, B2, B12 and Undercurrent §13.4, or settles a detail they leave open. The brief's choices P1–P26 and the build advisor's ruling, under `.scratch/dwarf-harness/u1-evidence/`, give each choice's options.
  - **A policy artifact.** D47 puts the three parameters in `law-format` and allows C4 no second source. §13.4 keeps them out of the shared law, and Core's manifest binds a `validation_policy_hash` over approved bytes that Core retains before referencing them (Core §13.2). A hash over retained bytes needs an artifact; a hash over values would need the canonical serialization that B12 rules out.
  - **Strictness.** On go1.27.2, `encoding/json` returns a repeated key without an error and silently replaces invalid UTF-8 with U+FFFD, so the walker checks UTF-8 first and compares decoded keys itself. Every version-1 range is non-negative, so a sign carries no meaning, and `-0` would be a second spelling of 0.
  - **Versions first, phase 1 fixed.** Checking the head before unknown members lets a newer artifact fail clearly rather than be misreported or reinterpreted (§13.4). Keeping phase 1 the same for every version keeps that order meaningful.
  - **Error order.** Promising the order among several violations would make any later reordering a breaking change (D40) for no consumer's benefit; consumers need the codes and the phases.
  - **Limits.** Profiles returned as fresh values, and the refusal of zero or wider limits, make §13.4's "never silently widen" a property of the contract rather than a convention.
  - **Line endings.** B1 would store a committed CRLF golden as LF. Deriving the vector in the test and pinning its digest keeps it a real golden without an exception to B1.
  - **One package, no encoder.** An internal package with no behaviour yet would be a placeholder, and an encoder would invite treating serialization as canonical (B12). Either can be added later by addition (D40).
- **Evidence:**
  - S1 was reviewed at 62e2c6b, on evidence collected in a separate worktree of that commit:
     - `go test -count=1 -v ./contract/law`: 595 tests and subtests, examples and fuzz seeds included, ran and passed, and none failed.
     - Each fuzz target ran for 120 s, `FuzzLoad` for 752,723 executions and `FuzzLoadValidationPolicy` for 2,385,988, with no failure and no new file under `testdata`.
     - `go run ./tools/check` passed all nine steps with `DWARF_CC` set; without it, only the race step failed, with D28's message.
     - `go list -deps` named no package outside the standard library but `contract/law` itself; `go mod tidy` changed neither `go.mod` nor `go.sum`, and `go mod verify` passed; gofmt listed no file.
     - `git ls-files --eol` showed `i/lf w/lf` for every file of the package except `full-compact.json`, which has no line ending (`i/none w/none`).
     - PowerShell's `Get-FileHash` gave exactly the ten pinned digests of item 9. The CRLF digest was computed with `Get-FileHash` too, independently of the loader, over `full.json`'s bytes with each LF replaced by CRLF in memory, and a runner recomputed it on 9bba28f; `TestIdentity` checks the derived bytes against it.
     - An import of `internal/core/durable` planted in `contract/law` failed the check's import-boundary step on windows/amd64, linux/amd64, linux/arm64, darwin/amd64 and darwin/arm64.
  - On 9bba28f, which corrects one figure in the package documentation, a policy's longest string, from 33 to 31 bytes, `go test -count=1 ./contract/law` passed and `gofmt -l contract/law` listed no file.
  - `BenchmarkLoad`, five runs on an Intel Core i7-10610U under Windows with go1.27.2, a measurement and not a threshold: `full` took 226–255 µs a load and allocated about 176 kB in 1,734 allocations; `standard-max`, `full.json` padded with spaces to 262,144 bytes, took 1.41–1.51 ms and allocated about 1.22 MB in 1,741 allocations.
  - D78, labelled: *assumption*, the artifacts are loaded at approval, at activation and when a consumer starts, and C4 may load the policy for each attempt; *calculation*, each load is bounded by 262,144 bytes and 8,192 tokens; *measurement*, the figures above; *plan*, C2 and C4 measure their actual call rates at their own gates.
  - The full check runs again on the commit that reaches `main` (D46).
- **Limits:**
  - Nothing is frozen. `law-format` stays Draft, version —, until the owner freezes it under D40, and consumers wait for that freeze. U1.L is Done only then (the Undercurrent roadmap's U1 exit criterion); U1 is In progress, and U1.E has not started.
  - There is no engine, activation, C4 or Core dependency, and none is built: Core's activation manifest and its retention of artifact bytes, and C4's use of the parameters, remain C2's and C4's work.
  - The golden values are test data, not the owner's law or validation policy, which he approves and Core activates later (D65).
  - The per-incident execution cap is not in the policy (OG-B) and needs its own decision.
  - Platform status: verified on Windows on this machine; Linux and macOS are compiled only, by the check's cross builds.
- **Changes:**
  - `contract/law`, new: `doc.go`, `law.go`, `registry.go`, `limits.go`, `walk.go`, `decode.go`, `policy.go` and `errors.go`; the tests `golden_test.go`, `identity_test.go`, `reject_test.go`, `limits_test.go`, `policy_test.go`, `fuzz_test.go`, `bench_test.go` and `example_test.go`; and the ten golden files under `testdata/law/` and `testdata/validation-policy/`.
  - Commits on `main`: S1 6f82da1 and its documentation fix-up 9bba28f, with the documents commit that carries this entry, by fast-forward on 2026-10-12.
  - Not changed: `contract/ledger`, `tools/check`, `go.mod`, `go.sum`, `.gitattributes`, `internal/` and `cmd/`. No module is added (D29).
  - Documents: in `CONTRACTS.md`, the status line and the `law-format` row, now Draft; in `ROADMAP-UNDERCURRENT.md`, its status line and U1's status, and in `ROADMAPS.md`, its status line and U1's status-board row, in one commit; one sentence on the validation policy in `DESIGN-UNDERCURRENT.md` §13.4; the contract-status sentences in Core (Start here, §3.5 and §13's opening), `ROADMAP-GUI.md`'s status paragraph and the system specification's status sentence; and ticket 04's comment; its status, criteria and Answer change with the commit that sends the freeze request (P24).
  - Evidence under `.scratch/dwarf-harness/u1-evidence/`: the design brief and the build advisor's ruling.
- **See also:** D114 records the owner's freeze of `law-format` at version 1, which item 11 left to him; the golden values stay test data.

### D113. C2's F2 freeze: in parts, each part before the code that writes it

- **Date:** 2026-10-12. **Decided by:** adopted. In OG-B, which ticket 05's design request asked after OG-A, the design approval, the owner picked "In parts, records first" from the assistant's options. It was the option that the design brief and the build advisor's ruling recommended (both in `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/`). It settles the F2 staging that D112 left undecided.
- **Owner's words (question tool):**
  > In parts, records first
- **Question:** C2's records form F2, a catalogue stage that joins the frozen `ledger-format` and that the owner freezes under D40 (B2; Core §13.1). The sources tie that freeze both to "C2 implementation" (Core §13.1 and `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C1's deliverables, which D112's "Not decided here" repeats) and to "producer implementation" (`ROADMAP-CORE.md` C1's event-catalogue row; ticket 05). They say neither whether F2 is frozen once or in parts, as tickets 05–09 need it, nor which C2 work may come before a freeze (the D52 approval packet, §5.3).
- **Decision:** As the option read:
  - Freeze F2 in parts, ticket by ticket, each part before the code that writes it.
  - Ticket 05's record-free S1 may reach `main` after review, before any F2 freeze.
  - Core line 844 and `ROADMAP-CORE.md` line 220 get a one-line clarification to "before C2 producer implementation".
- **Not decided here:** No part of F2 is frozen, and no event shape is approved. Freezing ticket 05's part, F2-05, which holds its four `workspace.*` record types, is a later and separate owner question. Ticket 05's S2, the draft of those types in `contract/ledger`, reaches `main` only with that freeze (D41), and S3, the code that writes them, waits for it. D112's approval and its limits are unchanged.
- **Consequences:** Each part joins the frozen `ledger-format` by addition (D40, D111). Each later C2 ticket that writes records proposes its own part with its design. No step's or contract's status changes here.
- **Changes:** Core §13.1's F2 sentence; `ROADMAP-CORE.md` (C1's F2 deliverable, Start here and C2's Status); `ROADMAPS.md` (the Path A paragraph); "See also" lines under D112 and B2.
- **See also:** D115 records the owner's freeze of F2-05, which this entry left as a later question.
- **See also:** D118 records the owner's freeze of F2-06, ticket 06's part, proposed with its design as this entry foresaw.
- **See also:** D119 records the owner's freeze of F2-07, ticket 07's part, proposed with its design as this entry foresaw.
- **See also:** D123 records the owner's freeze of F2-08, ticket 08's part, proposed with its design as this entry foresaw.
- **See also:** D124 records the owner's freeze of F2-09, ticket 09's part, proposed with its design as this entry foresaw.

### D114. law-format frozen at version 1

- **Date:** 2026-10-12. **Decided by:** adopted. In OG4, the `law-format` freeze request that ticket 04 sent on 2026-10-12, the owner picked "Freeze as presented" from the assistant's options.
- **Owner's words (question tool):**
  > Freeze as presented
- **Decision:** `law-format` is frozen at version 1, at commit 35ca559 (D40), which reached `main` with the record of the request, as B22 items 1–9 record it and the package documentation of `contract/law` states it:
  - the field law: format `dwarf-harness/undercurrent-law`, schema version 1, evaluator version 1 and kernel `bounded_best_arrival/v1`, with its members and ranges and the evaluator-v1 registry of six kinds, 22 rules, four profiles and three edge types; `quantity_class` takes either value for any kind;
  - the validation policy: format `dwarf-harness/validation-policy`, schema version 1, with exactly `flake_rate_prior`, `failure_repair_confidence` and `quarantine_threshold`, each an unreduced fraction of `uint64` terms with the numerator below the denominator;
  - the profiles `compact-v1` and `standard-v1` and their counting rules; a host may tighten them, never widen them;
  - refusal: atomic, in phases 0 to 3 in that order; phase 1's rules, unsigned-integer spelling among them, hold for every schema and evaluator version; the five sentinels with their error types, and the closed bound, problem and violation codes, with RFC 6901 paths;
  - identity: the SHA-256 of an accepted artifact's exact bytes, as the package's own `Digest`; and the exclusions of B22 item 7;
  - the Go API of `contract/law`, in which an empty edge list loads as an empty, non-nil slice;
  - version 1 is closed: additions come only as new schema or evaluator versions read beside it, with no optional members and no upgrader, and later contract versions load every version-1 golden to the same typed value and digest;
  - the golden samples are the ten files under `contract/law/testdata/` with the SHA-256 digests of B22 item 9, and the line-ending vector derived from `law/v1/full.json`, 83cad664b5eb46a5ef8453f4d57cfce5c9023d24bd54e8d9a463c0f2b6eaf9bf.
- **Not frozen:** the golden values, which are test data, not the owner's law or validation policy; the exact law and policy, which the owner approves and Core activates (D65); the per-incident execution cap, which needs its own decision before C4 relies on it (B22); the order among several violations within a phase, `Error()` wording and diagnostic offsets, which the documentation does not promise; Core's activation manifest and its retention of artifact bytes (C2), and C4's use of the parameters.
- **Consequences:** `law-format` now changes only by addition, which for this format means new versions read beside version 1. A breaking change, such as a change to a profile's bounds, a counting rule, a phase-1 rule or a code, needs an owner decision entry listing every step it affects (D40). U1.L is Done (the Undercurrent roadmap's U1 exit criterion); U1 stays In progress until U1.E is Done. C4 may build against the format once C3 is Done; its D52 approval is given (D112). U1.E may build against it, on synthetic ledgers until its producer contracts are frozen. Nothing is approved or activated.
- **Changes:** `CONTRACTS.md` (status line; the `law-format` row's version and status); `ROADMAP-UNDERCURRENT.md` (status line; U1's stop point, Q24 row and status; the U1 and U5 rows of remaining gates) and `ROADMAPS.md` (status line), in one commit; Core (the contract-status sentences in Start here, §3.5 and §13's opening; §10's item 3; §13.10's list of gates); `ROADMAP-CORE.md` (C4's row of remaining gates); `ROADMAP-GUI.md`'s status paragraph; the system specification's status sentence; Undercurrent design §13.4, its U1.L dependency row, its Q24 row and its list of unrun gates; the validation research's V1 row; the open item in this log, which this entry closes, and a "See also" line under B22; the freeze packet's answer line; ticket 04's comments.

### D115. F2-05 workspace and scratch records frozen

- **Date:** 2026-10-12. **Decided by:** adopted. In OG5, the F2-05 freeze request that ticket 05 sent on 2026-10-12, the owner picked "Freeze as presented" from the assistant's options.
- **Owner's words (question tool):**
  > Freeze as presented
- **Decision:** F2-05, ticket 05's part of C2's F2 family, is frozen by addition to `ledger-format` version 1 (D40, D111, D113), at commit d4e3032, whose `contract/ledger` reached `main` unchanged in bce48a0:
  - four record types, each at version 1: `workspace.registered`, `workspace.capture_inputs_pinned`, `workspace.scratch_created` and `workspace.scratch_closed`, with their payloads and record rules as the `contract/ledger` package documentation states them; `Defined()` lists the eight type versions of F0 and F2-05, and the namespace `workspace.` is reserved for C2's record families;
  - their value rules, identical on every host: the `common_dir` and `branch` grammars with their 4,096-byte caps; Git object IDs of 40 or 64 lowercase hex digits; a `dir` of 8 lowercase hex digits; revisions from 1; and `reason`, a token matching `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$` of at most 64 bytes, open to later reasons, with version 1 defining `abandoned`. Unknown payload keys are ignored;
  - their record rules, which Core's workspace service checks when it appends and when it opens a log;
  - the golden sample `contract/ledger/testdata/f2/05/log/00000000000000000001.jsonl` (SHA-256 843663a168e472adf4656fdba4ffa6db770e588feec0020bb10e20bf14def437); the three F0 golden samples keep D111's digests;
  - with them, the *Scratch closed* event that `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C2 proposed is adopted; *Scratch base advanced* stays a proposal.
- **Not frozen:** where the record rules are enforced, now Core's workspace service, and Core's naming of scratch branches and directories, both Core policy; which package may append these types, `internal/core/workspace` only, a review rule like B18 item 5's; the capture-inputs document's format, which is Core-internal; the privacy-class wording, which stays register text; and every later part of F2, which joins through its own additive freeze (D113).
- **Consequences:** these four types now change only by addition: new optional keys, new types, new type versions with upgraders, and new `reason` tokens, which need no new version. A breaking change needs an owner decision entry listing every step it affects (D40). Ticket 05's S2 reaches `main` with this entry (D41), and S3, the code that writes these records, may start (D113). U3's foundation may consume the types once C2 is Done and its other prerequisites hold, and U4 and U9 once theirs hold. No step's status changes, and `ledger-format` stays at version 1.
- **Changes:** `CONTRACTS.md` (status line; the `ledger-format` row's text and status; the F2-05 catalogue lead-in); the `contract/ledger` comments that called F2-05 proposed (the package documentation's Catalogue and F2-05 sections, `Defined`, the type constants and two test comments); `ROADMAP-CORE.md` (status line and its account of the Core code; Start here; C1's promotion-records line; C2's scratch lifecycle events and Status) and `ROADMAPS.md` (status line and its account of what exists; the Path A paragraph); the scratch rows of Undercurrent §15.1; `ROADMAP-UNDERCURRENT.md` (two scratch-lifecycle source cells and the scratch-after-acceptance row); the open item in this log, which this entry closes, and a "See also" line under D113; the OG5 packet's answer line; ticket 05's comments.

### B23. C2: the git program, private scratches and exact capture (ticket 05)

- **Date:** 2026-10-12. **Decided by:** adopted. In OG-A, the design approval that ticket 05 asked for before any code, the owner picked "Approve as ruled" from the assistant's options, approving the design brief as the build advisor ruled on it (both in `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/`). In OG-B he picked "In parts, records first", which D113 records. Items 2, 4 and 6 also record the changes that the build advisor's review of S1a required, and items 5, 7 and 9 his later rulings on questions that S1b raised; those details are the advisor's, originator `assistant`, within the authority the owner delegated, and settle nothing outside the approved design.
- **Owner's words (question tool, 2026-10-12):**
  - OG-A, approve the design before any code: "Approve as ruled"
  - OG-B, how C2's F2 event families are frozen: "In parts, records first"
- **Question:** Ticket 05 builds C2's first code. Its record-free slice, S1, which D113 lets reach `main` before the code that writes F2-05's records (D115), is the package `internal/core/workspace`: a Git runner, the pinned capture inputs, private scratch worktrees and their removal, the capture engine, reservations and a capture-cost harness. D16, D17, D48, D56, D60, D70 and D74–D78, with the specification the owner approved (D112: `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C2; Core §13.2), select private worktrees, host-built full-tree capture under pinned inputs and immediate busy. They leave open how Core runs Git, for which `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C2 proposes the `git` program; which Git it needs; how the inputs are held and materialized; how a scratch is created and removed safely on Windows; how a capture proves its tree exact; how sealed trees are retained, the "object retention" that D56 and D77 leave to the C2 specification; how reservations are held; and what a capture costs. How does S1 settle them?
- **Decision:**
  1. **Scope** (P1, P2, P5; D52, D113).
     - S1 writes no ledger record and exposes no promotion or capture-only command; nothing in it accepts a promotion or moves `main`. The workspace service, which writes F2-05's records, `RequestPromotion` and Open's reconciliation are ticket 05's S3 (item 12).
     - The package imports the standard library and `internal/core/ledgerstore` only, within D45's `internal/core/...` row; S3 may add `contract/ledger` and `contract/ledger/appender`, which D45 and D50 admit (P5).
     - The capture engine takes a physical scratch, a trusted baseline tree, a reservation and a mode, observe or seal. In ticket 05 the baseline is the scratch's anchor tree. Ticket 06 passes a local parent's sealed tree through the same engine and reservation, and adds request IDs, D58's identity, the `K=4`/`Q=4N` admission, local heads, successor baselines, admission records, `merge-tree`, generations and lineage.
     - Tests drive seal in disposable repositories; in ticket 05 the product uses capture only in closure's observe. There is no test-only validator and no capture-only command: the first would be the permissive validator D52 forbids, the second a snapshot outside admission.
  2. **The `git` program** (adopts `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C2's [PROPOSAL] under `ROADMAPS.md` rule 6; P6, P13, P32; the S1a review).
     - Core runs every Git operation through the `git` program, resolved to an absolute path. It requires Git 2.42 or later, the oldest version with every option the code and ticket 06 use (the design brief §2.4): `--path-format=absolute` came in 2.31, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` in 2.32, `core.fsync` and `worktree list --porcelain -z` in 2.36, ticket 06's `merge-tree --write-tree` in 2.38 and `git var GIT_ATTR_*` in 2.42.
     - It checks the leading major.minor.patch triple of `git version`'s output and pins the whole text after `git version `, vendor suffixes included, such as `2.55.0.windows.3`. A missing, older or malformed Git gives `ErrGitUnavailable` at inspection and at scratch creation, and makes captures unavailable as `git_unavailable`. A running Git other than the pinned one makes scratch creation and captures unavailable as `inputs_stale`, naming both versions, so after a Git upgrade captures resume only once the owner approves the workspace's inputs again (P13).
     - Each call runs under `exec.CommandContext` with a `WaitDelay` of 5 s, keeps at most 64 KiB of stderr and states the exit codes it expects. Any other exit is a `*GitError`, which matches `ErrGit` and, after a cancellation or a failed start, that cause too. Outcomes come from exit codes, NUL-delimited output and Core's own checks, never from Git's message text, and a malformed listing yields no partial list. `--end-of-options` precedes every positional revision and path, and revisions resolve with `rev-parse --verify`. Core never fetches.
     - The package's tests run real Git, and a missing or older Git fails them; C3's CI must supply Git 2.42 or later on Linux and macOS (P32).
  3. **Three environments** (P6; the design brief §2.4).
     - *Inspection* reads the owner's live setup. It keeps the host's environment, Git's configuration variables included, but removes `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_WORK_TREE`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE`, `GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY`, `GIT_ALTERNATE_OBJECT_DIRECTORIES`, `GIT_COMMON_DIR`, `GIT_NAMESPACE`, `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES` and `GIT_DISCOVERY_ACROSS_FILESYSTEM`, and sets `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` and `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0`.
     - *Repository*, for Core's changes in a workspace repository, removes every `GIT_*` variable, case-insensitively on Windows, `GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS` and `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`/`KEY_n`/`VALUE_n` included. It sets `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` to the colony's empty file `workspaces/empty`, `GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS=1`, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` and `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0`, and its arguments name the common Git directory and set `core.hooksPath` to `workspaces/nohooks`, `core.fsmonitor=false`, `core.fsync=committed` and `core.fsyncMethod=fsync`. The repository's own configuration is still read, but these calls convert no file content, and the marker's `hash-object` uses `--no-filters`.
     - *Capture*, for captures and scratch materialization, is the repository environment without the Git-directory argument, plus `GIT_DIR` set to the pinned inputs' private Git directory, `GIT_WORK_TREE` to the scratch, `GIT_INDEX_FILE` to a private index, `GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY` to the common object store, and `GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM=1`. The workspace's configuration, hooks, `info/exclude`, `info/attributes` and refs are not read; the pinned inputs replace them.
  4. **Inspection and pinned inputs** (P9, P12, P14, P15, P17; D48, D74–D76, D112; the S1a review).
     - `Inspect` reads and changes nothing. It refuses a folder that is not a Git repository, a repository without `main`, and `main` checked out in any worktree (D48); a bare repository is allowed. Scratch creation checks D48 again before it creates a branch; registration's check is S3's (P17).
     - Its proposal holds the common Git directory exactly as Git prints it, absolute and with forward slashes, as F2-05's `common_dir` requires (D115); the Git version; the object format; seven settings, `core.autocrlf`, `core.eol`, `core.filemode`, `core.ignorecase`, `core.longpaths`, `core.precomposeunicode` and `core.symlinks`, each with Git's default when unset; and five host files of at most 1 MiB each, an absent file distinct from an empty one: the common Git directory's `info/exclude` and `info/attributes`, the global excludes file, and the global and system attributes files that `git var` names.
     - The capture-input document is Core-internal compact JSON, format `dwarf-harness/capture-inputs`, version 1, rules `capture-rules/1`, in a fixed key order: the Git version, the object format, the seven settings and the SHA-256 of each present file. Its decoder refuses unknown, missing, repeated and null keys, and must keep accepting every past version, because old digests are replayed forever (P12). It is not a ledger contract (D115). The service publishes it and the files as pinned bodies, which, not the materialized files, are the authority (S3). Only the owner approves a revision (D112), and a revision changes future captures only.
     - Every use materializes the current revision under `workspaces/<wdir>/inputs/<revision>`, compares every file with the pinned bytes, and rewrites a missing or changed one through a temporary file and a rename. The private Git configuration holds the pinned object format and settings, with `core.safecrlf=false`; the attributes file holds the system bytes, then the global bytes, so global rules win.
     - Git's standard ignore precedence applies, with the pinned files in place of the live `info/exclude` and `core.excludesFile`, and the captured scratch's `.gitignore` files govern its new files (P14; D75, D76). The captured scratch's `.gitattributes` applies, subject to item 7's refusals (P15; D74). No filter driver is granted (P9).
  5. **Scratch worktrees and the colony marker** (P16, P18; the ruling on S1b's question 4).
     - A scratch is a linked worktree on the private branch `refs/heads/dwarf-harness/<dir>`, cut at `main`'s tip, where `<dir>` is eight lowercase hex digits from the random part of the scratch's UUIDv7, never reused, which the service mints (S3). The anchor ref `refs/dwarf-harness/anchors/<scratch ID>` keeps the base commit.
     - Creation checks the pinned Git version and D48, lists the anchor tree with `ls-tree -r -z --name-only` and refuses a case collision there as `case_collision`, naming both paths, so a scratch that cannot hold its own base is never made. It then materializes the inputs, creates the anchor ref, runs `worktree add --no-checkout -b` and fills the worktree with a private `read-tree -u --reset` under the pinned conversion, which writes the worktree's own index; no live configuration, filter or hook runs (G2-R8). A failed creation tries to remove what it made, and Open's reconciliation owns any orphan (S3).
     - The marker `refs/dwarf-harness/colony` points at the raw blob `dwarf-harness colony <ID>` and an LF, written with `hash-object -w --stdin --no-filters` and created with `update-ref` against the zero ID, so only where none exists. After a lost race the marker is read again; another colony's marker is refused. Marker, anchor and capture refs are created by compare-and-swap, never overwritten. The marker is not a security boundary (C10).
  6. **Removal** (the ruling's major finding; P11, P23; the S1a review).
     - Core removes a scratch itself and never asks `git worktree remove --force` to traverse a directory it has not verified. A walk by `DirEntry` and `Lstat` unlinks symlinks, junctions and other reparse points with `os.Remove` without descending into them, descends only real directories and unlinks files. A regular file whose unlink fails for permission gets one `chmod 0600` and one retry; links and reparse points are never chmodded. A read-only file inside a scratch is removed (`TestRemovalUnlinksReadOnlyRegularFile`). Only then does `os.RemoveAll` remove the remaining directories.
     - Then `git worktree prune --expire=now` runs, the scratch branch is deleted by compare-and-swap only while it still points at its base, so a moved branch keeps its history, and the anchor ref is deleted. Any failure returns `ErrRemovalPending`, and the worktree's registration stays until the directory is gone. Closure, which records the close before removing, and Open's sweep of orphans retry this same primitive and never assume success (S3; Core §3.5).
  7. **Exact capture** (P7–P11, P24; the rulings on S1b's questions 4 and 5; the ruling's findings on `\r\r\n` and on the refusal order).
     - A capture rematerializes the inputs and uses a fresh private index, `workspaces/<wdir>/tmp/<capture ID>.idx`, which it removes on every outcome. It is unavailable, with its reason, without a capacity profile (`no_capacity_profile`), without Git at the pinned version (`git_unavailable`, `inputs_stale`), without this colony's marker in the workspace (`workspace_unverified`) and without the scratch directory (`scratch_missing`).
     - It loads the baseline into the private index and refuses a baseline gitlink or symlink as `unsupported_kind`, and a baseline case collision as `case_collision`, on every platform. It lists eligible untracked paths with `ls-files -z -o --exclude-standard`, where a trailing slash marks a nested repository (`unsupported_kind`), and ignored entries with `-i --directory` added.
     - Before Core reads, or Git hashes, any candidate file, Core walks the scratch without following links (P11): `ModeSymlink` is `unsupported_kind`, and `ModeIrregular`, which is how Go reports junctions and mount points, pipes, sockets and devices are `special_file`. Only the root `.git` file and wholly ignored real directories are skipped.
     - Core then reconciles exact path bytes with Git's listings. A tracked path the walk lacks is a deletion, unless the pinned `core.ignorecase` is `true` and the walk holds it in another case (`name_mismatch`); an eligible path the walk lacks is `name_mismatch`; a walked file that Git lists nowhere is `unobservable_path`; and a candidate name that is not UTF-8 is `invalid_path`. Tracked paths stay candidates whatever the ignore rules say (D60).
     - The profile's caps refuse before any file is read (`file_cap`, `byte_cap`). `check-attr` refuses `filter` as `unsupported_filter`, and `working-tree-encoding` or `ident` as `unsupported_conversion`, unless the value is unspecified or unset; an empty or unknown filter is refused too (P9).
     - Core reads every candidate in path-byte order, hashing it with SHA-256 and counting its bytes: a running total over `MaxBytes` is `byte_cap`, an open or read error `unreadable`, and a changed size or modification time `scratch_changed`. Git then removes the deleted paths from the private index, adds every present candidate under the pinned conversion and writes tree T. Core checks T's exact path set, its modes, 100644 and 100755 only, and case collisions over every path and directory prefix.
     - **Round trip** (P8). `cat-file --batch` streams each distinct blob, never buffered whole, through the SHA-256 of the blob and of crlf(blob), where crlf inserts a CR before each LF not preceded by one. Each file's raw SHA-256 must equal one of the two; otherwise the capture refuses with `scratch_changed` if the file changed, else `lossy_normalization`. Under `core.autocrlf=true` a file with mixed line endings is therefore refused. A lone CR is not special-cased: whether Git converts such a file is Git's choice, the tests take the expected outcome from reference Git, and the build advisor read Git's code as storing it unchanged, as binary. Every file read must still `Lstat` as when it was read (`scratch_changed`).
     - The result holds the capture ID, a UUIDv7; T; the baseline; the inputs' revision and digest; the raw manifest and its SHA-256; the counts of files and raw bytes; and, for a seal, the retention ref. The manifest is JSON Lines: a header with `format` `dwarf-harness/capture-manifest`, `version` 1 and `object_format`, then one row per path in path-byte order with `path`, `mode`, `id`, `size` and `sha256`; raw size and SHA-256 stay distinct from the blob ID (D74). A manifest over `ledgerstore.MaxBodyBytes` is `manifest_cap`. The manifest is returned, not published: ticket 06 publishes and binds it (P24).
     - `IgnoredEntries` counts the entries of the ignored listing, a wholly ignored directory once, without recursing into it; no ignored name is returned.
     - A refusal is a `*RefusalError` matching `ErrCaptureRefused`, naming its reason and path; it retains nothing and returns no result. The tests assert only the precedence that safety needs, `special_file`, `unsupported_kind` and the caps before any file is read, and the reason of each single-defect scratch; the full refusal order is not promised.
     - **Case** (P10). Collision keys use `strings.ToUpper`, whose simple uppercase mappings are close to NTFS's upcase table [INFERENCE]; this was not tested against every character. Unicode normalization (NFC/NFD) is not compared, because that needs `golang.org/x/text`, a dependency decision under D29; exact-byte matching already refuses a normalization difference between the file system and Git.
  8. **Retention and durability** (P19, P20; D56, D77, B3).
     - A seal creates `refs/dwarf-harness/captures/<capture ID>` at T against the zero ID; an observe creates no ref. Every ref Core creates lives under `refs/dwarf-harness/`.
     - This settles, for ticket 05's sealed trees and scratch anchors, the "object retention" that D56 and D77 leave to the C2 specification: a ref to a tree keeps the tree and its blobs through `git gc --prune=now`, while the private index does not (G1-R7). Each ref's owner releases it: a capture ref, the capture's owner, which is ticket 06's admission under the terminal-state rules of tickets 06 and 08; an anchor, its scratch's closure, after the removal. Ticket 05's tests release their own.
     - A sealed tree stays as captured: later edits of the scratch, its index, `HEAD`, attributes, ignore files or the live configuration, and `git gc --prune=now`, leave it and its blobs intact (D56).
     - Git writes with `core.fsync=committed` and `core.fsyncMethod=fsync`, objects precede refs, and refs precede the service's record; Open sweeps orphan refs (S3). This is not a power-loss guarantee (B3) or a same-user security boundary (C10).
  9. **Reservations** (P22; the build advisor's rulings on reservations; the ruling's finding on busy tests).
     - Each physical scratch has an in-memory exclusion, held exclusively by a capture, a closure or a promotion request, or shared by any number of writers. Each colony has an in-memory capture-bytes pool of `BytesInFlight`, from which each exclusive holder that may capture reserves `MaxBytes`. `reserve` takes both under one mutex, or neither.
     - An exclusive acquisition never waits: a held scratch gives the `*BusyError` `scratch_busy` and a short pool `capture_budget`, at once, with no snapshot, queued retry or interrupted writer, even after the caller's context has ended (D70). A writer waits for an exclusive holder, bounded by its context, and never interrupts a capture. The busy tests check that objects, capture refs and `tmp/` stay unchanged.
     - Only `reserve` mints the unexported token. The engine refuses, with a typed error matching `ErrInvalid`, a token that is nil or forged, bound to another scratch, already used, released, or without its bytes, and it marks a token used under the pool's mutex, so one token permits one capture, concurrent attempts included.
     - The caller that reserved owns the token and releases it, normally by `defer`, when its whole operation ends: for a closure, after observe, durable record and removal; for ticket 06's admission, after seal and admission record. The engine returns only the bytes share, when the capture ends, whatever its outcome. `release` is idempotent and returns whatever is still held. Ticket 06 adds its K-slot, which lasts until the request's terminal state, to the same token.
  10. **Capture profile and the missing validator** (P2, P21; the ruling's findings on the profile's name and on the validator seam).
      - `CaptureProfile` is the capture part of Core §13.2's `CapacityProfile`, which ticket 06 and C4 extend rather than add a second. `MaxFiles` bounds one capture's files; `MaxBytes` the raw bytes one capture reads, and it is also each capture's reservation; `BytesInFlight` the colony's total. `MaxFiles` and `MaxBytes` must be positive and `BytesInFlight` at least `MaxBytes`, else `ErrInvalid`; without a profile, captures are unavailable (`no_capacity_profile`).
      - `ProvisionalCaptureProfile` gives 100,000 files, 1 GiB per capture and 20 GiB in flight: provisional Core policy and calibration inputs (B15), not measured capacity, a colony cap or a service level. 20 GiB in flight lets a 20-pair synchronized burst, one capture per scratch, pass without `capture_budget`.
      - C2 has no validator: `no_validator` is unconditional, with no seam a test could satisfy, until C4 wires the real one.
  11. **Capture costs** (P29, P30; D78; Core Appendix A.11).
      - `TestCaptureWorkload`, a harness in test files only, runs only when `DWARF_CAPTURE_WORKLOAD_OUT` names a results file outside the repository, and skips under the race detector. At 1,000 and 10,000 files of 800 bytes it times scratch creation, a fresh and a warm observe, a seal after ten edits and a closure's observe, with Defender on and no exclusion added. It checks every tree against the anchor's or reference Git's and sets no threshold. Burst concurrency stays C4's feasibility gate (`ROADMAP-CORE.md` C2).
      - It ran once, on 2026-10-12, at f2942bd, whose package e40ffcb carries unchanged, on the owner's machine; Core Appendix A.11 records the setup, the model and the tables, and the raw lines are `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/ticket-05-capture-workload.jsonl`. Measured: creating a scratch of 10,000 files took 3.78 s, its fresh observe 84.7 s, a warm observe 2.84 s and the seal after ten edits 3.01 s; at 1,000 files a fresh observe took 9.94 s and a warm one 0.519 s. These are single measurements on one machine: they set no threshold, pass nothing and claim no capacity.
      - A capture reads its files one at a time; bounded parallel reads would come with a later B entry, if measurements ask for them (P30).
  12. **Left to S3 and later tickets** (P3, P4, P12, P17, P23, P27, P28; the S1a review; the ruling's note on loose objects).
      - S3, the workspace service, adds: registration and the owner's input updates, with F2-05's records and pinned bodies (P12; D115), and D48 checked at registration (P17); `RequestPromotion`, which answers binding errors first, then a missing profile, then busy, then the unavailable reasons ending in `no_validator`, and records nothing for busy or unavailable (P3, P4); closure, which observes against the scratch's represented tree, the anchor's in ticket 05, refuses while a capture refuses or an unrepresented edit remains, records the close before removal, deletes ignored entries and reports their count (P23); Open's replay, validation, reconciliation and poisoning (P27); and actors that callers state and C3 authenticates (P28). The service holds one Git runner, not one per `Inspect` call.
      - Ticket 06 or later: publishing and binding the manifest; the K-slot; releasing capture refs; and a maintenance policy for the loose objects that observes and refused captures leave in a workspace's object store, since plumbing never triggers `gc --auto`.
  13. **Landing and status** (D41, D113; `ROADMAPS.md`'s status rule).
      - S1a and S1b reach `main` after their reviews (D113) and after the owner approves the merge, as his instruction for the staged build requires (ticket 05's comments). They touch no frozen contract, owner decision or dependency (D41).
      - C2 becomes In progress, on the status board and in its roadmap in one commit. C2's Status in `ROADMAP-CORE.md` lies inside the range D112 approved; editing a status line changes no approved mechanism, and D112 itself edited that line.
- **Reasons:**
  - Each item applies D16, D17, D29, D48, D52, D56, D60, D70, D74–D78, B3, B4 and B15 and the specification that D112 approved, or settles a detail they leave open. The design brief's choices P1–P33 and the build advisor's ruling give each choice's options and sources.
  - **The `git` program.** Core §13.2 already requires pinned Git. Git's own ignore, attribute and conversion code is the recorded library that D29 asks for in a format with many edge cases, and it adds no module; a Go Git module would need a D29 record and the owner's sign-off, and Core's own copy of those rules is what D29 says to avoid (P6). The capture environment is what keeps later configuration edits from altering captured bytes, as ticket 05 requires.
  - **Walk first, then verify.** Git reads through junctions and silently skips a long path (G1-R5), so its listings alone cannot show a tree complete. Core's walk, raw read and round trip make Git's result checkable, as `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C2 and Core §13.2 ask: a host-built full-tree manifest that reads and hashes every candidate, never the child-mutable index (P7, P11).
  - **The round-trip rule.** `safecrlf=true` refuses every LF-only text file under the owner's `autocrlf=true` (G1-R2), and accepting any conversion would let lossy normalization through. "Raw is the blob or crlf(blob)" admits exactly the uniform line-ending normalization that D74 accepts. With filters, encodings and `ident` refused, line endings are the only conversion left, so the rule is complete for v1 (P8, P9).
  - **Case collisions up front.** A colliding tree cannot be materialized on Windows, the first target (D27), whether it arrives as the baseline or as the candidate. Without the up-front check, a baseline defect would read as a scratch rename under `ignorecase=true`, or as a deletion that could seal silently under `false` (the ruling on S1b's question 4).
  - **Removal.** Git traverses junctions (G1-R5) and, with a file held open, drops the registration and leaves an orphan (G1-R6). Only a Core walk that never follows a link can remove a scratch without reaching files outside it (the ruling's major finding).
  - **Retention refs.** A ref keeps a tree and its blobs through gc, while the private index does not (G1-R7). Relying on branches fails once a branch moves, and wrapping each tree in a commit would add author and time metadata to an immutable result (P19).
  - **Reservations.** Only the caller spans an operation's steps, such as a closure's observe, record and removal. Returning the bytes share when the capture ends keeps a closure's record and removal from starving other scratches' captures, and a token that permits one capture is the proof that Core §13.2's reservation preceded this snapshot (the rulings on reservations).
  - **The ignored count.** A count of the listing's entries tells the caller what was lost without walking ignored trees, which may hold junctions, and without putting private file names into logs (the ruling on S1b's question 5; D75).
- **Evidence:**
  - Git facts that the design's runners probed on this machine, with git 2.55.0.windows.3 and go1.27.2 (the design brief, Appendix D):
     - G1-R1: the system configuration sets `core.autocrlf=true` and `core.symlinks=false`; `git var GIT_ATTR_SYSTEM` and `GIT_ATTR_GLOBAL` print the system and global attributes paths; no global ignore or attributes file exists, and `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` is unset.
     - G1-R2: under `autocrlf=true`, `core.safecrlf=true` makes `update-index --add` refuse an LF-only text file with exit 128, and the whole batch writes nothing. With `safecrlf=false`, the private recipe gave the tree of a normal `git add -A` less the pinned-excluded file and stored a CRLF file as LF; it needed no `objects/` in the private Git directory and left the scratch's index unchanged. The probe's record of its environment was truncated; `TestControlledEnvironmentIgnoresHostGit` now asserts the exact variable sets.
     - G1-R3: a mixed-ending file is refused under `safecrlf=true`.
     - G1-R4: a nested repository is listed as `sub/`; `update-index` with `sub` writes a gitlink (160000), and with `sub/` nothing, with a warning.
     - G1-R5: Git lists and indexes files through a junction; `ls-files -o`, with or without `-i`, skips a 350-character path with exit 0 and only a warning.
     - G1-R6: with a file held open, `worktree remove --force` exits 255, drops the registration and most files, and leaves an orphan directory; a second `remove` exits 128.
     - G1-R7: a ref to a tree keeps the tree and its blobs through `gc --prune=now`, and gc prunes them once the ref is deleted; a blob that only the private index references is pruned; `core.fsync=committed` with `core.fsyncMethod=fsync` is accepted without warning.
     - G2-R8: `worktree add --no-checkout` leaves only `.git` and no index; a private `read-tree -u --reset` then writes the files, with CRLF under the pinned `autocrlf`, and the worktree's index; `git status --porcelain` is clean and the branch is checked out. Both steps took about 1.0 s for 1,000 files, on a machine also running another ticket's checks.
     - G2-R9: on go1.27.2 on Windows, a junction is `ModeIrregular`, neither a directory nor a symlink, from both `Lstat` and `DirEntry.Type()`; file and directory symlinks are `ModeSymlink` and could be created without extra privilege; `Lstat` of a path through a junction resolves to the target.
  - Of the Windows hazards that `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C2 marks [INFERENCE], the held file is now observed: G1-R6 for `worktree remove --force`, and `TestRemovalBlockedByOpenHandle` for Core's removal, which stays pending, with the registration kept, until a retry after the handle closes. Case-insensitive paths are handled by item 7's rules and the Windows test rows, not otherwise measured. The roadmap's text keeps its marks (D112).
  - S1a was reviewed at 2d427a3, on evidence collected in a separate worktree of that commit. `go test -count=1 -v ./internal/core/workspace` ran 80 tests and subtests, fuzz seeds included: 79 passed, and one, `TestRemoveDoesNotFollowSymlink`, which runs on Linux and macOS, was skipped. Each of the seven fuzz targets then present ran 120 s with no failure and no new file under `testdata`. The package imported the standard library only; `go mod tidy` changed nothing; linux and darwin vet passed, gofmt listed no file and every file of the package was LF in the index; `go run ./tools/check` passed all nine steps.
  - S1b was reviewed at f2942bd, on evidence collected the same way. `go test -count=1 -v ./internal/core/workspace` ran 155 tests and subtests, fuzz seeds included: 153 passed, 2 skipped (`TestCaptureWorkload`, which runs only when asked, and `TestRemoveDoesNotFollowSymlink`, which runs on Linux and macOS), and none failed. Each of the eight fuzz targets, `FuzzCRLFStreaming` added, ran 120 s with no failure and no new file under `testdata`. The busy, waiting, pool and release tests passed 20 times under `-race`. The package imports the standard library and `internal/core/ledgerstore` only; `go mod tidy` changed nothing and `go mod verify` passed; linux and darwin vet passed, gofmt listed no file and every file of the package is LF in the index and the worktree. `go run ./tools/check` passed all nine steps.
  - Fifteen deliberate rule breaks, eight while S1a was built and seven while S1b was, each failed named tests, and the restored code passed: inheriting `GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS`; dropping `--end-of-options` before a ref; accepting Git 2.41.9; splitting NUL-delimited output at newlines; classifying by `os.Stat` during removal, which deleted a junction target's file; removing with `git worktree remove --force`; skipping D48 at inspection; trusting materialized input bytes; reading files during the walk, before classification and caps; disabling the round-trip comparison; accepting an unminted token; waiting before `scratch_busy`; leaking the pool share after a refusal, an error or a cancellation; classifying the walk by `os.Stat`; and skipping the seal's retention ref.
  - D78, labelled: *assumption*, B15's 12 or 20 code-working pairs, each offering one change per 600 s, with a synchronized four-checkpoint burst (`ROADMAP-CORE.md` C2); *calculation*, 20 GiB in flight lets a 20-pair burst, one capture per scratch, pass without `capture_budget`; *measurement*, item 11 and Core Appendix A.11; *plan*, the concurrent cost of a burst is measured at C4's feasibility gate, and with A.11 it calibrates the provisional profile.
  - The full check runs again on the commit that reaches `main` (D46).
- **Limits:**
  - Nothing is frozen or approved here, and no contract changes: S1 writes no record, and F2-05 stays as D115 froze it. Promotion stays unavailable until C4's real validator (D52).
  - Platform status: verified on Windows, on NTFS, on this machine. Linux and macOS are compiled and vetted only; their test rows (FIFOs, sockets, symlinks, executable modes, unreadable files, case-sensitive renames and collisions, non-UTF-8 names, and `TestRemoveDoesNotFollowSymlink`) first run in C3's CI, which needs Git 2.42 or later (P32).
  - This is not a power-loss guarantee (B3) or a same-user security boundary. A process that swaps a directory for a junction during a capture or a removal races Core's check; D70 excludes host-managed writes during capture, and hostile same-user processes are C10's.
  - The case rule rests on item 7's [INFERENCE]; Unicode normalization waits for a decision under D29.
  - Observes and refused captures leave unreferenced blobs in the workspace's object store until a maintenance policy, ticket 06's or later.
  - As OG-A's summary told the owner: v1 cannot capture a repository with symlinks, submodules, LFS or other filters, `working-tree-encoding` or `ident`; under his pinned `core.autocrlf=true` a file with mixed line endings is refused, so agents' tools must keep endings uniform; a Git upgrade makes captures unavailable until he approves the inputs again; the caps are provisional; closing a scratch deletes its ignored files, reported as a count; and a scratch holding a refusing entry, such as a junction, a symlink or a mixed-ending file, cannot be closed by anyone until the entry is removed by hand.
  - The measurements are single captures on one machine, on synthetic repositories: burst concurrency remains C4's gate, and no figure is a threshold, a pass mark or a capacity claim.
- **Changes:**
  - `internal/core/workspace`, new: `doc.go`, `errors.go`, `git.go`, `gitparse.go`, `inputs.go`, `worktree.go`, `eol.go`, `capture.go`, `reserve.go` and `profile.go`; the tests `helpers_test.go`, `git_test.go`, `gitparse_test.go`, `inputs_test.go`, `worktree_test.go`, `worktree_windows_test.go`, `eol_test.go`, `capture_test.go`, `capture_windows_test.go`, `capture_posix_test.go`, `capture_linux_test.go`, `reserve_test.go` and `fuzz_test.go`; and the measurement harness, `workload_test.go`.
  - Commits on `main`: S1a 9e8fd48 and S1b e40ffcb, with the documents commit that carries this entry, by fast-forward on 2026-10-12.
  - Not changed: `contract/`, `internal/core/ledgerstore`, `internal/core/durable`, `tools/check`, `cmd/`, `go.mod` and `go.sum`. No module is added (D29).
  - Documents: in `DECISIONS.md`, this entry and "See also" lines under D56 and D77; in Core, the C2 status sentences in Start here, §3.5 and §13's opening, §8.6's "Workspaces" row, Appendix A's heading and A.1, and the new A.11; `ROADMAP-CORE.md` (status line and its account of the Core code; Start here; the adoption note on C2's `git`-program proposal; C2's Status) and `ROADMAPS.md` (status line and its account of what exists; the D52 paragraph; C2's status-board row), in one commit; the system specification's status sentence; ticket 05's comments.
  - Evidence under `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/`: the design brief, the build advisor's ruling and the raw capture results, `ticket-05-capture-workload.jsonl`.
- **Amended by B24:** item 6's removal no longer chmods and retries a file, deletes the anchor by compare-and-swap against the ID it reads and unlinks the scratch's root `.git` file last, and Open, retrying it, prunes once per workspace; B24 also records the workspace service that item 12 left to S3.
- **Amended by B25:** Core needs Git 2.45 or later, in item 2 and in the Limits' sentence on C3's CI, for `merge-tree --merge-base` over trees with no commit; stored `capture-inputs/1` documents keep their 2.42 rule, as item 4 requires. B25 also records what item 12 left to ticket 06.
- **Amended by B26:** a scratch whose private branch has moved to any value other than its acknowledged tip or previous tip, an equal tree included, no longer closes: closure answers `scratch_tip_mismatch` until the owner restores the branch. Item 6's removal still deletes a branch only at its base, so a moved branch keeps its history.

### B24. C2: the workspace service and its records (ticket 05 S3)

- **Date:** 2026-10-12. **Decided by:** adopted. In OG-A, the design approval that ticket 05 asked for before any code, the owner picked "Approve as ruled" from the assistant's options, approving the design brief as the build advisor ruled on it (both in `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/`); S3 is that design's workspace service. Item 3 also records the build advisor's later ruling R1 on input revisions, item 6 his ruling R3 on workspace directories, and item 9 his ruling R2 on orphans and the changes his review of S3 required; item 10 records the changes to S1 that the project manager authorized, and other items the details the build settled where the brief left a choice. Those details are the assistant's, originator `assistant`, within the authority the owner delegated, and settle nothing outside the approved design.
- **Owner's words (question tool, 2026-10-12):**
  - OG-A, approve the design before any code: "Approve as ruled"
- **Question:** Ticket 05's S3 is the workspace service in `internal/core/workspace` that writes the four F2-05 record types the owner froze (D115), on top of S1's Git runner, scratch worktrees, capture engine and reservations (B23). The approved design specifies its calls, records and lifecycle (the design brief §2.6–§2.13, as the build advisor's ruling modified it; B23 item 12), and D115 leaves to Core policy where the record rules are enforced and how Core names branches and directories. They leave open what Open checks for input revisions whose bodies collection may have deleted; how a current revision that the store cannot supply differs from one that contradicts its record; how Open proves the base of a scratch that no record names; how workspaces' directories stay distinct; what an append of uncertain outcome leaves behind; and how S1's removal serves Open's retries. How does S3 settle them?
- **Decision:**
  1. **Scope and calls** (P1–P5, P23, P28; D45, D50, D52).
     - `Open(ctx, store, colonyDir, Config)` takes the host's `ledgerstore` store, opened with `ledger.Defined()`, checks with `os.SameFile` that its log directory is `<colonyDir>/log`, validates the capture profile (B23 item 10) and returns the service with a `Reconciliation` (item 9). The other calls are `Register`, `UpdateInputs`, `Scratch`, `BeginWrite`, `CloseScratch`, which returns a `ClosureResult` (P23 as the ruling modified it), and `RequestPromotion`. `Close` waits for calls in progress and for `BeginWrite` leases and is idempotent; later calls return `ErrClosed`, and the host closes the store. One service runs per colony and holds one Git runner.
     - The package imports the standard library, `contract/ledger`, `contract/ledger/appender` and `internal/core/ledgerstore` only (P5), and no module is added (D29). Its errors are the design brief's (§2.13), with `*InconsistentError{Seq, Detail}` matching `ErrInconsistent`.
     - Callers state the actor: the service records it and checks an agent's context, and C3 authenticates callers later, with no stand-in until then (P28). Nothing accepts a promotion or moves `main`, and there is neither a capture-only command nor a validator seam (D52; P2).
  2. **Records written** (D115; P4, P25).
     - `Register` appends one commit, actor `owner`: `workspace.registered`, `workspace.capture_inputs_pinned` with revision 1, and that revision's `ledger.body_pin`. `UpdateInputs` appends one commit, actor `owner`: `workspace.capture_inputs_pinned` with revision n+1, its `ledger.body_pin`, and the `ledger.body_unpin` of revision n's pin. A pair's first `Scratch` appends one `workspace.scratch_created`, actor `core`, with the context in the envelope. `CloseScratch` appends one `workspace.scratch_closed` with the stated actor, the scratch's context and the reason `abandoned`, the only reason the service writes.
     - Captures, refused closures, `BeginWrite`, busy and unavailable answers and removal retries write nothing.
     - The present host files and the capture-input document are published as bodies before the commit that pins them. The `body_refs` of an inputs record and of its pin are the document's digest and those of the present files, in byte order, each once.
  3. **One rule set, when appending and at Open** (P27; D115; the build advisor's ruling R1).
     - One function checks the frozen record rules (D115) and the service's own: on the service's drafts before the store sees them, and on every record when Open replays the log, reading every record of each commit so that the rules' index positions hold. A draft that breaks a rule is refused with `ErrInvalid` and nothing is appended; a replayed break fails Open with `ErrInconsistent` naming the record's sequence (B21 item 10). The service holds its lock from the check to the store's acknowledgement and undoes its projection if the append fails, so no caller sees an unacknowledged record. Replay reads no clock and executes nothing.
     - The service's own rules, which D115 leaves to Core: a scratch's `branch` is `refs/heads/dwarf-harness/` followed by its `dir`; both of its Git IDs have the length of its workspace's object format; the service appends only the reason `abandoned`, while replay accepts any well-formed reason as a closure; and no two workspaces share the last eight hex digits of their IDs (item 6).
     - **Input revisions (R1).** Replay enforces on every `workspace.capture_inputs_pinned` record the rules the log alone decides: actor `owner`; a registered workspace; `body_refs` present and holding the document's digest; the same commit's `ledger.body_pin` with this `pin_id` and the same `body_refs`; revision 1 only at index 1 of a registration commit; and each later revision the current one plus 1, at index 0, with its pin at index 1 and the previous pin's `ledger.body_unpin` at index 2. The exact-set rule, that `body_refs` holds the document and every body it names and nothing else, is checked when the record is appended and, at Open, for each workspace's current revision only. Open never reads a superseded revision's bodies, which the update's unpin lets collection delete, so its verdict on a log does not depend on what collection has done.
     - **The current revision (R1).** After replay, Open reads and decodes each workspace's current document and files. A body the store cannot supply, or a document whose format, version or rules this service does not know, makes the workspace unusable as `inputs_unavailable`, with the digest and the cause in the detail: Open succeeds and lists it in `Pending`, and the workspace's scratch creation, closures and requests answer `inputs_unavailable` until a new revision or a repaired store. A malformed document of a known version, a named set that differs from `body_refs`, an object format other than the workspace's, or a file over 1 MiB fails Open with `ErrInconsistent` at the record's sequence.
     - Open does all this before it creates the colony's `workspaces/` layout or `scratch/`, or repairs anything; a missing Git does not fail Open (B23 item 2).
  4. **Registration and input updates** (P12, P13, P17, P18; D48, D75, D112).
     - `Register` is the owner's approval of an inspected proposal (D112). It inspects the proposal's repository again: the common directory, Git version, object format and seven settings must equal the proposal's, else `ErrStaleProposal`, and Inspect's refusals, D48's among them, apply (P17). The five host files are recorded exactly as the owner approved them (D75). A common directory already registered, by its text or by `os.SameFile`, is `ErrExists`. The capture-input document is validated before the colony marker is created or any body is published; another colony's marker is `ErrOtherColony`, and this colony's marker without a registration lets an interrupted registration complete (P18).
     - `UpdateInputs` is the owner's alone (D112), the caller authenticating him. An unknown workspace is `ErrUnknownWorkspace`; a fresh inspection must match the proposal's observed part and the workspace's common directory and object format, else `ErrStaleProposal`; and the current revision must be the expected one, else `ErrRevision`, which also answers once the largest revision is reached. Captures that begin after the commit use the new revision; a running capture keeps its inputs, and a sealed tree never changes (D112; P13).
     - `Register` and `UpdateInputs` run one at a time, each waiting within its context.
  5. **Scratches** (P16, P17; the design brief §2.7).
     - `Scratch` returns the pair's open scratch or creates it. Concurrent first uses of one pair create one scratch: later callers wait within their context and get it, and other pairs proceed.
     - Creation needs Git at the pinned version and a usable workspace. It mints the scratch ID again while the `dir` it gives matches a recorded name, a creation in progress, an existing directory or an existing branch, at most three times, then fails with `ErrInvalid`; it then runs S1's creation (B23 item 5) and appends the record.
     - If that append fails, the worktree, branch and anchor are removed, unless the service is or becomes poisoned because the outcome is uncertain (item 8). Then they are kept, because the record may exist: the next Open's replay decides, keeping the scratch if its record is there and removing the orphan if it is not.
     - `BeginWrite` holds the scratch shared, waiting within its context while a capture, closure or request holds it; an unknown scratch answers `ErrNoScratch` and a closed one `ErrClosed`. Its release is idempotent, and `Close` waits for it.
  6. **Workspace directories** (the build advisor's ruling R3).
     - `<wdir>`, the directory of a workspace's materialized inputs and capture indexes, is the last eight hex digits of its ID, derived and never recorded, as `dir` is for a scratch.
     - `Register` mints the ID again while its suffix matches a registered workspace's or names an existing `workspaces/<wdir>`, at most three times, then fails with `ErrInvalid`. Replay refuses a registration whose suffix is taken with `ErrInconsistent`. No other directory name is ever used.
  7. **Closure** (P23 as the ruling modified it; the ruling on S1b's question 5; the design brief §2.11).
     - The owner, or an agent whose context is the scratch's, may close it; anyone else gets `ErrNotBound`. A reason other than `abandoned` is `ErrInvalid`.
     - Closure holds the scratch exclusively (`scratch_busy`). If the directory exists, closure needs a capture profile (`no_capacity_profile`) and the capture preconditions, which answer as for a request (item 8), reserves `MaxBytes` (`capture_budget`) and observes against the anchor tree. A refusal is returned as is, and a tree other than the anchor's is `ErrUnrepresentedEdits`; neither changes anything. If the directory is missing, closure records without observing.
     - Closure appends its record, then removes the scratch (item 10). A failed removal returns `ErrRemovalPending`: the closure stands, and a later `CloseScratch` or Open retries the removal without a second record.
     - `ClosureResult.IgnoredEntries` counts the entries of Git's ignored listing that the observe found, an ignored directory once, and names no path; a call that only retries the removal reports 0.
  8. **Requests, busy and poisoning** (P2–P4, P22, P27; D52, D70).
