     - `RequestPromotion` answers, in this order: a closed or poisoned service; invalid IDs (`ErrInvalid`); an unknown workspace (`ErrUnknownWorkspace`); a pair without an open scratch (`ErrNoScratch`); the reservation, which answers `no_capacity_profile`, then `scratch_busy`, then `capture_budget`; then the unavailable reasons `git_unavailable`, `inputs_unavailable`, `inputs_stale`, `workspace_unverified`, `scratch_missing` and `anchor_missing`; and last `no_validator`, which C2 always answers. It releases the reservation, never captures and writes nothing.
     - A closure's or a request's anchor ref must point at the recorded base when it is checked, else `anchor_missing`.
     - `appender.ErrUncertain` or `appender.ErrPoisoned` from the store poisons the service: every later call except `Close` returns `ErrPoisoned` until the host recovers the store and opens a new service, which rebuilds from the log (B18 item 6).
  9. **Open's reconciliation** (P20, P27; the build advisor's ruling R2 and his review of S3).
     - It is best effort and never fails Open; it writes no record, reads no clock, and reports `Reconciliation{OrphansRemoved, RemovalsCompleted, Pending, Missing}`. It empties every `workspaces/*/tmp/` without following links. A workspace whose Git is missing, or whose common directory or colony marker fails the check, is listed in `Pending` and left alone.
     - In each usable workspace it lists the anchors and the branches under `refs/heads/dwarf-harness/` once. A closed scratch's removal is retried only while its directory, branch or anchor remains, and `git worktree prune --expire=now` runs once per workspace after the removals, so Open's Git calls do not grow with closures whose cleanup is complete. `RemovalsCompleted` counts the directories that existed.
     - An open scratch whose anchor is missing gets it back from its record by compare-and-swap, if the commit exists; otherwise, or if the anchor points elsewhere, which is never overwritten, the scratch is `anchor_missing` and listed in `Missing`. An open scratch without its directory is listed in `Missing`, answers `scratch_missing` to requests and may still be closed.
     - **Orphans (R2).** A directory under `scratch/` that no record names was never handed to a context. Its root `.git` file names `<common directory>/worktrees/<name>`, and its workspace is the usable one with that common directory, compared by `os.SameFile`. Only the single anchor whose scratch ID ends in the directory's name proves its base: with that proof the directory is removed, the branch deleted by compare-and-swap at the anchor's commit and the anchor deleted, while a branch that has moved is kept and listed in `Pending`. Without it, because the `.git` file is missing or unreadable, the workspace is unknown or unusable, or no anchor or several match, only the directory is removed; the branch and the matching anchors are kept, and the branch is listed in `Pending`. Core never deletes a branch without an old value and never infers a base from `HEAD` or `main`.
     - An anchor whose scratch is neither open nor present as a directory under `scratch/` is deleted by compare-and-swap, so an orphan whose removal is pending keeps its proof for the retry. A branch with neither a directory nor a record is never swept.
     - Open touches no capture ref: each is released by its owner (B23 item 8). Ticket 05's product never seals, and ticket 06, whose admission records own sealed captures, adds the sweep of capture refs that no record names. The build advisor's review removed an earlier sweep of every capture ref, which would have stripped ticket 06's admitted trees of their retention at each restart.
  10. **Changes to S1** (amends B23 item 6; the S1b review's notes; the project manager's authorization).
      - A file whose unlink fails is no longer chmodded and retried. Go's `os.Remove` already clears the read-only attribute on Windows, and on Linux and macOS the directory's permission governs an unlink [INFERENCE, the S1b review's reading of Go's runtime]; `TestRemovalUnlinksReadOnlyRegularFile` still passes.
      - The walk keeps the scratch's root `.git` file, when it is a regular file, until every other entry is gone, removing each emptied real subdirectory with `os.RemoveAll` as it leaves it; it then unlinks `.git` and removes the empty root. A scratch whose removal is pending thus keeps the link to its workspace that item 9's proof needs. A `.git` that is not a regular file is handled like any other entry, never followed.
      - The anchor is read and then deleted by compare-and-swap against the ID read. An absent anchor counts as deleted, and a lost race returns `ErrRemovalPending`, to be retried. With the branch's compare-and-swap, the primitive deletes no ref without an old value.
      - `CloseScratch` and a failed creation's cleanup use the primitive as before: the directory, then `worktree prune`, then the refs. Open uses its walk and its ref deletions, now a separate step, with one prune per workspace (item 9).
      - The package documentation states that cancellation surfaces either as the bare context error or inside `*GitError`.
      - The test seams are unexported: `environ`, `newID`, `beforeAppend` and `removeAll` on the service, `afterRawRead` and `manifestCap` on the capture engine, and `beforeAnchorDelete` on the Git runner; they serve deterministic regressions only (B18 item 8).
  11. **Left to later tickets** (B23 item 12; the design brief §0).
      - Ticket 06: request IDs and D58's identity; the `K=4`/`Q=4N` admission, with its K-slot on the same reservation token; captures against a local parent's sealed tree through the same engine; publishing and binding the manifest; admission records; releasing capture refs by terminal state, and the sweep at Open of capture refs that no admission record names; and the loose objects that observes and refused captures leave. Busy and unavailable answers stay unrecorded until then (P4).
      - Temporary scratches (ticket 07), finalization and recovery (ticket 08) and policy activation (ticket 09); accepted promotion waits for C4 (D52).
  12. **Landing, the ticket and status** (D41, D46; the design brief §6).
      - S3 and its fix-up reach `main` after the build advisor's review and after the owner approves the merge, as his instruction for the staged build requires (ticket 05's comments). They touch no frozen contract, owner decision or dependency (D41): the records are D115's, unchanged.
      - Ticket 05 is resolved: its slices S1–S4 are on `main` (the design brief §6). C2 stays In progress, with tickets 06–09 to come. `ROADMAP-CORE.md` and `ROADMAPS.md` record the closing in their status lines, and C2's row on the status board is unchanged.
- **Reasons:**
  - Each item applies D115's frozen rules and the design the owner approved at OG-A, or settles a detail they leave open; the design brief's choices P1–P33, the build advisor's ruling and his rulings R1–R3 give each choice's options and sources.
  - **One rule set.** D115 has the service check the record rules both when appending and at Open. One function serves both, so the two cannot drift, and a draft that replay would refuse never reaches the log.
  - **Superseded revisions unread (R1).** An update's unpin makes the old revision's bodies collectable. Reading them at Open would make its verdict depend on collection timing and would conclude consistency from absence, which D55 and B18 item 6 forbid. The structural rules still catch a foreign writer, and a superseded revision governs no capture.
  - **Two outcomes for the current revision (R1).** A body the store cannot supply is a store condition, repaired without touching the log, and one workspace's newer document must not close the whole service, just as P13 keeps unavailability per workspace. A readable body is content-addressed, so a record that contradicts its own document is a producer's rule break, which fails Open (B21 item 10).
  - **Orphan proof (R2).** Creation makes the anchor before the branch and the directory and appends the record last (B23 item 5), so every directory without a record has, or had, an anchor at its base named by its scratch ID, the only proof in the repository. Keeping an unprovable branch costs clutter in a reserved namespace; deleting it could lose history Core never examined.
  - **Distinct workspace directories (R3).** Two workspaces sharing `<wdir>` would rewrite each other's materialized excludes and attributes, so a capture could run under another workspace's pinned exclusions, against D75. A second naming rule for a state that never occurs is not worth having, and the F2-05 golden's two workspaces have distinct suffixes.
  - **Uncertain appends keep the scratch.** Only replay can tell whether the record reached the log; removing the worktree first could destroy a scratch that was handed out (P27).
  - **No capture-ref sweep.** Only an admission record can say whether a capture ref has an owner (B23 item 8); without one, Open would strip retention from every admitted tree once ticket 06 seals.
  - **Bounded retries.** P27 budgets Open at a second pass over the log; three Git processes for every closed scratch in history would grow without bound.
  - **S1's removal.** Keeping `.git` until last keeps a pending orphan's only link to its workspace. Deleting the anchor against the ID read extends R2's rule that no ref is deleted without an old value to the primitive itself. The chmod retry could not run.
- **Evidence:**
  - **The rulings' tests.** R1: `TestUpdateInputsCompareAndSwap` collects revision 1's bodies after the update, and the reopened service reports nothing and captures under revision 2; `TestReplayNeverReadsSupersededInputs`; `TestCurrentInputsUnavailable`, in which a deleted current document and one of a newer version each make the workspace `inputs_unavailable` without failing Open; and `TestOpenFailsClosedOnRuleBreak`, whose 41 rows each fail Open with `ErrInconsistent` at the breaking record's sequence, among them a pin whose `body_refs` differ, a document missing from `body_refs`, a current document naming another set and a malformed current document. R2: `TestOpenReconciles`, in which a creation killed in a child process between its worktree and its record leaves an orphan that the next Open removes with its branch and anchor; `TestOpenReconcilesUnprovenOrMovedOrphanBranch`; and, on Windows, `TestOpenReconcilesPendingOrphan`. R3: `TestRegisterWorkspaceSuffixCollisions` and the row `workspace_suffix_collision`. The review's changes: `TestOpenGitCallsIndependentOfCompletedScratches`, which counts real Git invocations, and `TestOpenReconciles`' subtests `closed_refs_without_directory` and `capture_owner_retains_ref`.
  - **Held files on Windows.** `TestCloseRemovalPending` keeps the closure durable while a held file blocks removal, answers `ErrClosed` to a writer and completes the removal when the call or Open retries; `TestOpenReconcilesPendingOrphan` keeps an orphan's directory, `.git` file, branch and anchor while a file is held and clears them at the next Open. With B23's `TestRemovalBlockedByOpenHandle`, the held-file hazard that `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C2 marks [INFERENCE] is observed on every removal path; the roadmap's text keeps its marks (D112).
  - The build advisor reviewed S3 at 760c24d, and the diff of its fix-up before it was committed as 047432d; he approved both on evidence collected in a separate worktree, on code identical to 047432d's. `go test -count=1 -v ./internal/core/workspace` ran 263 tests and subtests, fuzz seeds included: 260 passed, 3 skipped (`TestCaptureWorkload`, which runs only when asked; `TestHelperScratchCrash`, which runs only as `TestOpenReconciles`' child process; and `TestRemoveDoesNotFollowSymlink`, which runs on Linux and macOS), and none failed. The busy, waiting, pool, release, pair and poisoning tests passed 20 times under `-race`. The package imports the standard library, `contract/ledger`, `contract/ledger/appender` and `internal/core/ledgerstore` only; `go mod tidy` changed nothing and `go mod verify` passed; linux and darwin vet passed for the package and `./contract/...`, gofmt listed no file, every file of the package is LF in the index and the worktree, and no test directory was left under `%TEMP%`; `go run ./tools/check` passed all nine steps. S3 adds no fuzz target.
  - Eleven deliberate rule breaks while S3 was built each failed named tests, and the restored code passed: closing without comparing the observed tree with the anchor; sealing before answering `no_validator`; replaying a second open scratch for one pair; accepting a 64-digit base commit in a `sha1` workspace; working on after an uncertain append; creating two scratches for concurrent first uses of one pair; updating inputs without the previous pin's unpin; letting another context's agent close a scratch; deleting a moved branch at closure; checking the unavailable reasons before the reservation; and skipping orphan reconciliation.
  - D78, labelled: *assumption*, the service writes records per workspace, at registration and at each of the owner's input updates, and per scratch, at its creation and its closure (the design brief §2.16); *calculation*, two records per scratch lifetime are negligible beside B21's model of 960 records per context-hour; *measurement*, none new: A.11's single captures (B23 item 11) exclude closure's record and the removal, and `TestOpenGitCallsIndependentOfCompletedScratches` shows only that completed closures add no Git call to Open; *plan*, the concurrent cost of a burst stays C4's feasibility gate.
  - The full check runs again on the commit that reaches `main` (D46).
- **Limits:**
  - Nothing is frozen or approved here, and no contract changes: the service writes F2-05 as D115 froze it. Promotion stays unavailable until C4's real validator, and harness work stays testing-only (D52).
  - Platform status as in B23: verified on Windows, on NTFS, on this machine. Linux and macOS are compiled and vetted only, and their rows first run in C3's CI, which needs Git 2.42 or later (P32).
  - This is not a power-loss guarantee (B3) or a same-user security boundary (C10): a same-user process that moves or deletes Core's refs, edits a scratch's `.git` file or swaps a directory for a junction races Core's checks.
  - Actors are what callers state until C3 authenticates them (P28).
  - The projections keep every workspace, scratch and name ever used, so memory grows with history (B3).
  - Busy and unavailable answers are not recorded until ticket 06 adds request IDs (P4).
- **Changes:**
  - `internal/core/workspace`: new `service.go`, `register.go`, `scratch.go` and `promote.go`, and the tests `service_test.go`, `register_test.go`, `scratch_test.go`, `scratch_windows_test.go`, `promote_test.go` and `replay_test.go`; changed `doc.go`, `errors.go`, `git.go`, `worktree.go` and `helpers_test.go`.
  - Commits on `main`: S3 760c24d and its review fix-up 047432d, with the documents commit that carries this entry, by fast-forward on 2026-10-12. The fix-up removed Open's sweep of capture refs, which contradicted B23 item 8 and would have stripped ticket 06's admitted trees, and bounded Open's retries of closed scratches, whose Git calls had grown with history. Between S1's documents commit and S3 lies the documentation fix 21da058, which states the owner's freezes in the comments of `contract/ledger`, `contract/ledger/appender` and `contract/law`, with his sign-off "Correct them" (the comments of tickets 03 and 04).
  - Not changed by S3 or the fix-up: `contract/`, `internal/core/ledgerstore`, `internal/core/durable`, `tools/check`, `cmd/`, `go.mod` and `go.sum`. No module is added (D29).
  - Documents: in `DECISIONS.md`, this entry and an "Amended by" line under B23; `ROADMAP-CORE.md` (status line and its account of the Core code; C2's Status) and `ROADMAPS.md` (status line and its account of what exists), in one commit; ticket 05's status, criteria, Answer and comments.
- **Amended by B25:** the Limits' Git floor for C3's CI is 2.45, Core's runtime floor (B25 item 2).
- **Amended by B26:** items 1 and 5's `BeginWrite(ctx, scratchID)` is replaced by `BeginWrite(ctx, WriteBinding)` and its typed `Writer`, and a closed task scratch answers `ErrNoScratch`; item 7's closure and item 8's requests answer `scratch_tip_mismatch` while the private branch is at any value other than the scratch's acknowledged tip or previous tip, until the owner restores it.

### D116. Build process: reader and drafter roles, documents once per ticket, routine slice merges

- **Date:** 2026-10-12. **Decided by:** adopted. After the project manager's [process audit](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/process-audit-2026-10-12.md) of the build's agent transcripts, the owner chose in chat which of its recommended changes to make, and asked for the reader and drafter roles it proposed.
- **Question:** The audit found that the build spent much of its time and money on documents spread over many files, on repeated full checks and fuzzing, on quiet windows for a load-sensitive test and on owner stops before routine merges. Which of its recommended changes does the build adopt?
- **Owner's words (chat):**
  > Add a Sonnet reader role, an Opus 5.5 xhigh drafter role. Remove the time limit of the test. One document commit per ticket. Approval for routine slice merges. Make the following changes only of the recommended changes that save time and effort without sacrificing quality: One full check per slice fuzz only changed targets; Documents once per ticket; Collect evidence first so that the one advisor review evidence and documents together; freeze packets of 1-2 pages. Having documents spread all over the place is non ideal.
- **Decision:**
  1. **Roles.** The reader (Claude Sonnet 5.5, xhigh) maps requirements, digests APIs and quotes sources verbatim. The drafter (Claude Opus 5.5, xhigh) drafts a ticket's documents: edit files, B entries, freeze packets and status changes. The designer drafts design briefs only, and runners (Claude Haiku 5.5) run commands. The role files are in `.omp/agents/` (commit 5334b26).
  2. **The race test.** `TestBodiesConcurrentUse` has no wall-clock bound; a stall shows as `go test`'s timeout (commit 6b67490). Full checks need no quiet window for it.
  3. **Documents once per ticket.** A ticket's documents land in one commit when it closes: the ticket file with its claim, status and comments; its design brief and ruling; the owner's answers; one short B entry, which points to the ruling, the package documentation and the evidence instead of restating them; measurements; and status and contract-register changes. A slice that changes a contract or needs the owner's decision does not wait for the ticket's close: the documents that record that decision (its entry in `DECISIONS.md` and the register change) reach `main` with the slice (D41, D113); the ticket's other documents still land once, when it closes.
  4. **Status in one place.** A step's status is stated only on the status board of `ROADMAPS.md` and in the step's `**Status.**` line where its roadmap has one, which change in the same commit; a contract's version and status only in the table of `CONTRACTS.md`. Other documents point there.
  5. **Routine slice merges approved in advance.** A slice reaches `main` without a further question to the owner when the build advisor approved it, the full check passed on the exact commit, and it touches no frozen contract, owner decision or dependency (D41). Designs, freezes and his other decisions still wait for him. For routine slices this replaces his instruction of 2026-10-12, "stopping for human approval before moving forward", recorded in the comments of tickets 04 and 05.
  6. **Verification.** Each slice gets one full check, on the exact commit that reaches `main` (D46); coders run their package's checks. Only the fuzz targets a slice adds, or whose fuzzed code it changes, are fuzzed, for 120 s as before; every target's seed corpus still runs in the check. Evidence is collected before the build advisor's review, so that one review covers the code and its evidence and, at a ticket's close, its documents.
  7. **Freeze packets.** A freeze request (D40) reaches the owner as a packet of one or two pages: what is frozen, what freezing means for him, the evidence in one short table, his choices and the question. Supporting material stays in the evidence files the packet cites.
- **Limits:** The owner's gates are unchanged: D52 approvals, contract freezes (D40), his sign-off for changes that touch a frozen contract, an owner decision or a new dependency (D41), and pushing (D31, D46). Entries already recorded stay as written.
- **Changes:** `ROADMAPS.md` (the status rule under the board; rule 10 of "Working across roadmaps" and the list of decisions those rules implement); `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` (the Claim line); the status statements outside the allowed places, removed or replaced by pointers, in `ROADMAPS.md`, `CONTRACTS.md`, the four roadmaps, the Core, routing and Undercurrent designs, two research documents, `LEDGER-USER-NEEDS.md` and the system and fortress specifications; the contract-status clauses of the C1, C2 and U1 Status lines; ticket 03's comments; the process audit, added beside the specification; the drafter's date rule. The role files and the test changed in the earlier commits 5334b26 and 6b67490.
- **Amended by D117:** a ruled design no longer waits for the owner; item 5's other conditions and the Limits line stand.
- **Amended by D125:** contract freezes of completed slices no longer wait for the owner; item 7's freeze packet is no longer written, and the freeze record with its evidence file goes to the build advisor with the slice's review.
- **Amended by D135:** item 1's role files are no longer tracked; they stay in the owner's checkout, excluded through its `.git/info/exclude`.
- **Amended by D145:** item 3's documents land in two commits: the ticket file and the status and register changes in the project repository, and the brief, ruling, evidence and B entry in the private records repository.
- **Amended by D152:** coders run focused tests and only the rule breaks D152 names (item 6's "coders run their package's checks"); tickets are built in two to four slices; reviews block only on D152's list; the instruction of 2026-10-12 no longer applies to starting a ticket either (item 5); item 6's check on the exact commit moves to GitHub's runners once D152's item 6 takes effect.
- **Amended by D153:** item 6's exact-commit merge gate is the work branch's hosted `Check result`, with D128's document check retained and no additional routine local full check.

### D117. Ruled designs go to the build without the owner's approval

- **Date:** 2026-10-12. **Decided by:** owner.
- **Owner's words (chat):**
  > User approval is no longer necessary for ticket brief designs. Please update the workflow but keep the output of the briefs the same. User will read through and make adjustments should anything pop up. The system is working as intended with multiple 'eyes' on each step it is displaying reliability
- **Decision:** A ticket's design brief goes to the build once the build advisor has ruled on it, without a question to the owner. The briefs keep their form, with the owner summary first. The owner reads briefs and rulings as he chooses and asks for changes when anything comes up.
- **Limits:** Decisions the documents reserve to the owner still wait for him: contract freezes (D40), his sign-off for changes that touch a frozen contract, an owner decision or a new dependency (D41), D52 approvals, and pushing (D31, D46). Of D116 item 5, this changes only the sentence "Designs, freezes and his other decisions still wait for him."
- **Changes:** this entry and the "Amended by" line under D116.
- **Amended by D125:** contract freezes of completed slices no longer wait for the owner.

### D118. F2-06 promotion admission, generation and failure records frozen

- **Date:** 2026-10-12. **Decided by:** adopted. In OG-F6, ticket 06's F2-06 freeze request, sent with the [freeze packet](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/f2-06-freeze-packet.md), the owner picked "Freeze as presented" from the assistant's options.
- **Owner's words (question tool):**
  > Freeze as presented
- **Decision:** F2-06, ticket 06's part of C2's F2 family, is frozen by addition to `ledger-format` version 1 (D40, D111, D113), at commit 188f5c5, whose `contract/ledger` reached `main` unchanged in bde7c68:
  - three record types, each at version 1: `workspace.promotion_admitted`, `workspace.generation_derived` and `workspace.promotion_failed`, with their payloads, decoder rules and record rules as the F2-06 section of the `contract/ledger` package documentation states them; `Defined()` lists the eleven type versions of F0, F2-05 and F2-06;
  - the four points the packet put to the owner, as built: these names; conflict reports pinned in their generation's commit and released by a superseding generation or at the scratch's closure, never by an ending; the closure rule, stated in F2-06, under which a scratch with a live request has no `workspace.scratch_closed`, so that every log without F2-06 records stays valid; and `promotion_failed`'s `outcome`, an open token whose every value fails the lineage, with version 1 defining `conflict` and `dependency_failed`;
  - the golden sample `contract/ledger/testdata/f2/06/log/00000000000000000001.jsonl` (SHA-256 014cc612076390934fa8a668117c353c5cca2630a3b3fd90364a29542b96f40d); the F0 and F2-05 golden samples keep D111's and D115's digests.
- **Not frozen:** where the record rules are enforced, now Core's workspace service when it appends and at Open; the checkpoint bound per context and workspace and the generation window, both service policy; the rules that `integration-rules/1` names, Core policy named in the record, which change through a new value; which package may append these types, `internal/core/workspace` only, a review rule; the privacy-class wording, which stays register text; and every later part of F2, which joins through its own additive freeze (D113).
- **Consequences:** these three types now change only by addition: new optional keys, new types, new type versions with upgraders, and new `promotion_failed` outcomes, which need no new version. A breaking change needs an owner decision entry listing every step it affects (D40). Ticket 06's S2 reaches `main` with this entry (D41), and S3, the code that writes these records, may start (D113). `ledger-format` stays at version 1, and no step's status changes.
- **Changes:** `CONTRACTS.md` (the `ledger-format` row's text and status; the F2-06 catalogue); the `contract/ledger` comments that called F2-06 proposed (the package documentation's Catalogue and F2-06 sections, `Defined`, the type constants and six test comments); the "Promotion attempted" and "Promotion refused" rows of Undercurrent §15.1; the OG-F6 packet, with its answer line, and its evidence file, both added under `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/`; a "See also" line under D113.

### B25. C2: bounded checkpoints, the merge train and lineage outcomes (ticket 06)

- **Date:** 2026-10-13. **Decided by:** adopted. In OG-A6, the design approval that ticket 06 asked for before any code, the owner picked "Approve as ruled" from the assistant's options, approving the [design brief](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/ticket-06-design-brief.md) as the build advisor [ruled](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/ticket-06-design-ruling.md) on it; the question listed item 2's Git floor and item 5's absence of Git maintenance among the consequences. Items 3 and 4 record the project manager's rulings while S3 was built, with the build advisor's acceptance and figures for the identity sets, and item 6 the project manager's decision on the tests' cost as the advisor ruled on it; those details are the assistant's, originator `assistant`, within the authority the owner delegated, and settle nothing outside the approved design.
- **Owner's words (question tool, 2026-10-12):**
  - OG-A6, approve the design before any code: "Approve as ruled"
- **Question:** Ticket 06 builds C2's checkpoint admission, merge train and lineage outcomes in `internal/core/workspace`, on ticket 05's service (B23, B24), and writes the F2-06 records the owner froze (D118). The design brief and the build advisor's ruling specify it. What did the build settle, and what does it change in earlier entries?
- **Decision:**
  1. **The design as ruled** (P1–P25 and the ruling's findings). The package documentation, `internal/core/workspace/doc.go`, states the service as built; the design brief and the ruling give each choice's options and sources. The choices later tickets build on:
     - C2's product admits nothing (P1; D52). `RequestPromotion` runs the identity, head and reservation checks, then answers `no_validator` and keeps no slot. `admit` and `integrate` are unexported and reached only by package tests in disposable repositories, until C4 calls `admit` with its validator reserved; a seam a test could satisfy would be the permissive validator D52 forbids.
     - Refusals stay unrecorded (P4). Busy, unavailable, stale-head, mismatch and capture refusals write nothing and consume no request ID, which settles B24's "until ticket 06 adds request IDs": a recorded busy answer would make D58's replay answer busy forever, against D70's explicit retry.
     - Checks repeated under the exclusion (P3, P17; the ruling's minor findings). The head rule and closure's live-request check run again once the scratch is held exclusively, so a competing admission neither lets a stale request capture nor makes a closure fail at append.
     - Policy, not record rules (P5, P9). The checkpoint bound is checked at reservation and at append, and the generation window in `integrate`; replay checks neither, so a valid log never fails Open after a bound is lowered, as B21 does for the pin bound.
     - Merge attributes (P11). Integration ignores a repository's own merge attributes in both directions; a repository-wide merge rule belongs in the pinned attributes the owner approves (D112).
     - Restarts (P18). Unfinished requests keep their slots, refs and generations across a restart, because D55's `interrupted` needs ticket 08's intents to prove that nothing was promoted.
     - Test-only moves of `main` (P23; D48). `advanceMain`, which moves `main` in disposable repositories, lives in test files only.
  2. **Amends B23 item 2: Git 2.45 or later** (P10; the design probes, G6-M3).
     - Core's runtime floor is Git 2.45: `merge-tree --merge-base` accepts trees with no commit only from 2.45, and the delta base and the sealed submission are such trees. B23 chose 2.42 counting only `--write-tree`, and wrapping the trees in commits would add metadata around immutable trees and a second code path. B23's sentences that require 2.42, in item 2 and its Limits, and B24's Limits now read 2.45, for C3's CI too.
     - Stored `capture-inputs/1` documents keep their 2.42 rule, because old digests are replayed forever (B23 item 4); the project manager authorized this change to S1's inputs code, and `TestCaptureInputsKeepHistoricalGitFloor` holds it.
     - `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C2's adoption note changes to 2.45 with this entry; it is B23's note inside C2's text, not D112's approved mechanism text.
  3. **The project manager's rulings during S3.**
     - **Identity sets.** The projections keep UUID-only sets of every generation, capture and lineage ID ever recorded and, per workspace, a map from each inputs revision to its `capture_inputs_sha256`, beside the current revision (`register.go`'s `workspaceState`). After the build advisor's review, the fix-up 02edd7b added the lineage set, each scratch's count of live requests, the live requests' manifest pins, and each active report pin's owning scratch, held until its supersession or the scratch's closure, so that neither replay nor an integration scans every request ever recorded. D118's rules that a `generation_id` is new, that a `request_id` and a `capture_id` were never admitted before, and that an admission names a recorded revision with that revision's digest can be checked at replay only against every ID and revision ever seen. Neither structure reads a superseded revision's bodies (B24 R1), and nothing else is kept for ended requests.
     - **The window test.** `TestWindowBoundsGenerations` was corrected: C2's only ending is a failure, so the window is shown on a rebuilt train, with four generations, the survivors' rebuild after the leading request ends by conflict, the fifth request's first generation and no sixth. The design brief's "no other generation changes" needs ticket 08's acceptance.
     - **Canonical `main` as the first predecessor,** checked in two halves: `integrate` binds the `main` commit and tree it observed when it appends; Open checks the half the log decides, that no live request stands ahead of such a generation and that it carries no predecessor IDs, because F2-06 records no historical `main` tip.
  4. **Memory** (the build advisor's figures; P20). The identity sets cost about 16 bytes per generation, capture and lineage ever recorded, and the revision map about 40 bytes per inputs revision, beside about 200 bytes per request kept for life for D58's replay; a generation's payload is dropped when its request ends. This is the growth with history that the owner saw at OG-A6; a cold lookup waits for a measured trigger (B3).
  5. **No Git maintenance** (P19). Core runs no `gc`, `prune` or `repack`: repacking or pruning the owner's object store is a repository-wide effect no source authorizes (B23 item 8), and retention refs keep every tree Core still needs. That Git's own automatic maintenance reclaims released objects is an [INFERENCE] that bears no weight; V11's loose-object counts after each harness phase are the evidence (Core Appendix A.12). The policy is revisited at C4's capacity gate, so B23 item 12's "ticket 06 or later" is later.
  6. **The tests' cost** (the build advisor's ruling on it, 2026-10-13).
     - S3's tests made the package take 532 s, and 581 s under the race detector, run alone, against Go's default timeout of 10 minutes per package, which the full check does not override. A test-only commit, fcf23e4, runs the independent tests in parallel without removing a row, assertion or seam: 253 s and 280 s alone. Raising the timeout would hide the growth that tickets 07–09 add, and `tools/check` stays unchanged, since a different budget is a D42 change for the owner (D41).
     - **Baseline.** In the full check on 02edd7b, the package took 215 s in the tests step and 274 s in the race step. Tickets 07–09's assignments keep the package under that baseline plus their own tests' cost, and share fixtures before they add rows.
- **Evidence:**
  - Ticket 06's closing comment lists each criterion's tests, from the design brief's Appendix C.
  - The build advisor approved S1 at e207472 with three nits, none required, and S2 at 188f5c5, with D118's freeze documents once two changes were applied. S3's review: approved by the build advisor on its evidence, after the fix-up 02edd7b that bounded the projection's history scans and closed the ending-commit window, reviewed on its diff with V1, V5 and the full check rerun on 02edd7b. All nine steps of the full check passed on e207472, on c2c09de and on 02edd7b, each the commit `main` moved to (D46).
  - Twelve deliberate rule breaks while S1 was built, thirteen while S3 was and five while its fix-up was each failed named tests, and the restored code passed; the [ticket's evidence](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/ticket-06-evidence.md) names each break and its failing tests, with every slice's checks.
  - D78, labelled: *assumption*, B15's 12 or 20 code-working pairs, each offering one change per 600 s, with a synchronized burst of four checkpoints per pair (`ROADMAP-CORE.md` C2; the design brief §2.17); *calculation*, K × N admission slots, 48 and 80 at 12 and 20 pairs; about five records per checkpoint, 30–36 per pair-hour, under 4% of B21's model of 960 records per context-hour; manifest pins at most K per pair, about 176 MB at 20 pairs with 10,000-file scratches; memory as item 4; *measurement*, Core Appendix A.12, single admissions and integrations at 1,000 and 10,000 files with Git's loose-object counts after each phase; *plan*, burst concurrency, disk under load and a maintenance policy are C4's feasibility gate, where A.11 and A.12 calibrate the provisional profile.
- **Limits:**
  - Nothing is frozen or approved here: F2-06 was frozen by D118, and the service writes it as frozen.
  - Promotion stays unavailable until C4's real validator, and harness work stays testing-only (D52).
  - One machine and one run: verified on Windows, on NTFS, on this machine. Linux and macOS are compiled and vetted only, and their rows first run in C3's CI, which needs Git 2.45 or later. A.12's figures are single measurements, not thresholds or capacity claims.
- **Changes:**
  - The code is `internal/core/workspace` as S1 and S3 built it; ticket 06's comments name the commits.
  - Documents: in `DECISIONS.md`, this entry and "Amended by" lines under B23 and B24; in Core, Appendix A's heading and A.1, the new A.12 and §8.6's "Workspaces" row; in `ROADMAP-CORE.md`, the adoption note on C2's `git`-program proposal and C2's Status; ticket 06's status, criteria, Answer and comments; and under `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/`, the design brief, the ruling, the Git probes `ticket-06-git-probes.md` both cite, the evidence `ticket-06-evidence.md` and the raw results `ticket-06-capture-workload.jsonl`.
- **Amended by B26:** item 6's baseline for tickets 08 and 09 is B26 item 8's, 442 s and 424 s in the full check on 1ebb83b; D122 set the check's 20-minute limit that item 6 left to the owner.
- **Amended by B27:** item 1's restarts: unfinished requests keep their slots, refs and generations across a restart only until the bookkeeping phase ends them `interrupted`; until then Open lists their workspace `promotion_recovery_pending`, which refuses admission, integration, finalization and no-change.

### D119. F2-07 scratch observation and merge-back records frozen

- **Date:** 2026-10-13. **Decided by:** adopted. In OG-F7, ticket 07's F2-07 freeze request, sent with the [freeze packet](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/f2-07-freeze-packet.md), the owner picked "Freeze as presented" from the assistant's options.
- **Owner's words (question tool):**
  > Freeze as presented
- **Decision:** F2-07, ticket 07's part of C2's F2 family, is frozen by addition to `ledger-format` version 1 (D40, D111, D113), at commit b23d8f7, which reached `main` unchanged:
  - two record types, each at version 1: `workspace.scratch_changed`, Core's observation of a scratch, which advances its private tip and has no `authoring_context_id` because its `context_id` authors the observation; and `workspace.scratch_merge_back`, a private merge-back's plan or result. `Defined()` lists the thirteen type versions of F0, F2-05, F2-06 and F2-07;
  - three additions to frozen types: the optional `parent_scratch_id` of `workspace.scratch_created`, which makes the scratch a temporary scratch (D23); the optional `scratch_observation_id` and `merge_back_ids` of `workspace.promotion_admitted`, under the admission-window rule; and the reason `temporary_merged_back` of `workspace.scratch_closed`;
  - their payloads, decoder rules and record rules as the F2-07 section of the `contract/ledger` package documentation states them, including the pins, unpins and retention of observation manifests, prepared plans and conflict reports; each new record rule is conditional on a new key, type or reason, so that every log without F2-07 records stays valid;
  - the points the packet put to the owner, as built: these names; a temporary scratch beside its author's dedicated scratch; a private merge-back that does not represent the task's edits; one Core commit per observation, even when nothing changed; explicit retry of a merge-back whose write failed, with the owner repairing interference; a temporary's `abandoned` closure, whose branch keeps its commits; the refusal of an ignored file that obstructs a merge-back; `changed_paths`, present and possibly empty exactly for an applied merge-back; and full path sets, never truncated or chunked, bounded only by the envelope's `MaxRecordBytes` and `MaxCommitBytes`;
  - the golden sample `contract/ledger/testdata/f2/07/log/00000000000000000001.jsonl` (SHA-256 96de91f92432040683157279432512810c2dd27d36012695be46dd1eccac88c5); the F0, F2-05 and F2-06 golden samples keep D111's, D115's and D118's digests;
  - with them, the *Scratch changed paths* and *Temporary scratch merged back* events, proposed with `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C2's scratch lifecycle events, are adopted; *Scratch base advanced* stays a proposal.
- **Not frozen:** where the record rules are enforced, Core's workspace service when it appends and at Open, which ticket 07's S3 builds; the service's rules around these records, such as the `pathset_cap` refusal, the resumption of an interrupted temporary, and a merge-back's retry and interference check; the rules that `scratch-rules/1` and `scratch-merge-rules/1` name, Core policy named in the record, which change through a new value; which package may append these types, `internal/core/workspace` only, a review rule; the privacy-class wording, which stays register text; and every later part of F2, which joins through its own additive freeze (D113).
- **Consequences:** these types and additions now change only by addition: new optional keys, new types, new type versions with upgraders, and new `reason` and merge-back `failure` tokens, which need no new version; the merge-back `outcome` is closed, so a new outcome needs a new type version. A breaking change needs an owner decision entry listing every step it affects (D40). Ticket 07's S2 reaches `main` with this entry (D41), and S3, the code that writes these records, may start (D113). `ledger-format` stays at version 1, and no step's status changes.
- **Changes:** `CONTRACTS.md` (the `ledger-format` row's text, owning step and status; the F2-07 catalogue); the `contract/ledger` comments that called F2-07 proposed (the package documentation's Catalogue and F2-07 sections, `Defined`, the type constants, the closure reason and eight test comments); the "Scratch created", "Scratch changed paths", "Temporary scratch merged back", "Scratch closed" and "Promotion attempted" rows of Undercurrent §15.1; the OG-F7 packet, with its answer line, added under `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/`, and its evidence, `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/f2-07-freeze-evidence.md`; a "See also" line under D113.

### D120. Answers to the art questions deferred to tickets 108 and 137

- **Date:** 2026-10-13. **Decided by:** adopted, except item 3, owner. The owner asked for the questions deferred to tickets 108 and 137 to be repeated, because they did not appear in the tickets: B19 and B20 had recorded them as open, and the tickets only mentioned them in passing. He answered all eight in the question tool, picking one of the assistant's options for each except the stowed pick, where he typed his own answer.
- **Owner's words (question tool):**
  1. Do the Grand roles share their base role's action loop? "Own action for each Grand role"
  2. Do Grand officers carry a Corps marker? "A colony banner instead"
  3. How does a stowed pick read on an outfit without a pack? "I want to remove the pick during action frames in this case. This will avoid the conflict. Picks disappearing when the action frames start is fine for this pre-alpha"
  4. The vest hides the legwear: "Keep it as it is"
  5. The pennant cannot wave: "Keep it rigid"
  6. The 16-pixel map pick reads as a club: "Keep it"
  7. Large 64-pixel surfaces use three-step ramps: "Add a fourth step"
  8. The dog's fur is close to the floor in brightness: "Lighten the fur"
- **Decision:**
  1. The Grand Orchestrator, Grand Griot and Grand Diplomat each have their own role action loop on the three-quarter view, drawn for every part like the other roles' (D108 item 2), so `animations.txt` keeps one `act_<role>` loop per role.
  2. Grand officers carry a colony banner instead of a Corps marker in both 64-pixel views. Its design is ticket 108's, approved through the character sheet (D104 item 3), and ticket 107's manifest declares it as a marker kind beside the Corps marker. The option was the assistant's suggestion, described as a distinct Grand pennant for colony-level officers.
  3. On an outfit that carries the pick but not the pack, the pick is not drawn in the action frames, where it would lie across a pack that is not there; the idle and still frames show it as before. No dependency between parts is declared, so ticket 117 may let a user choose a pick without a pack, and ticket 107's manifest declares the rule.
  4. Ticket 137's next round adds a fourth step to the ramps of large 64-pixel surfaces and lightens the dog's fur ramp, together with the horse's mane, which shares it. The fourth step needs generator code, because the generator rejects a four-entry ramp, and it changes every colour choice; both sheets go to the owner's review again (D104 item 3). The vest, the pennant and the map pick stay as approved.
- **Amends:** D109's "they show only the Corps emblem and colour", for Grand officers, who show the colony banner instead.
- **Changes:** this entry; an "Amended by" line under D109 and "See also" lines under B19 and B20; dated comments on tickets 107, 108, 117 and 137.

### D121. The project manager owns workflow optimization

- **Date:** 2026-10-13. **Decided by:** owner.
- **Owner's words (chat):** "As the PM you are approved to make changes to the process to reduce redundancies. I see you've been doing this but the approval is explicit now. Optimizing the workflow is within your perview and is your responsibility", and in his next message: "By perview I of course meant **purview**"
- **Decision:** the project manager removes redundancy from the build workflow without asking: the order and kind of steps, the assignments the roles receive, which evidence is collected and by whom, and its own tooling. The decisions that bind the work still bind it: the owner's questions (D40 freezes, D41 sign-offs, D52 approvals, D29 dependencies and pushing, D31 and D46, as D117 lists them), one full check on the exact commit before `main` moves (D42, D46), and `AGENTS.md`'s record rules. A workflow change that alters a rule written in this repository is recorded as a decision with `Decided by: assistant`; changes to the project manager's own procedure are kept in its build procedure and named to the owner in the next status report.
- **Changes:** this entry.

### D122. The full check's tests and race steps run with a 20-minute limit

- **Date:** 2026-10-13. **Decided by:** adopted.
- **Owner's words (question tool):** "Set 20 minutes, report at 15"
- **Context:** `tools/check` set no limit of its own, so Go's default of 10 minutes per test binary applied. During ticket 07's S3b, `internal/core/workspace` took 516 s under the race detector run alone, after its slowest matrices were made cheaper without removing coverage, and tickets 08 and 09 add tests to the same package. The build advisor recommended 20 minutes. It ruled out splitting the package's tests into another package, which would need test-only exports, and keeping 10 minutes, which would mean cutting coverage.
- **Decision:** `go run ./tools/check` passes `-timeout 20m` to its tests and race steps (`testTimeout` in `tools/check/main.go`). When a slice's evidence shows a package's race step above 15 minutes, the project manager names it in that slice's evidence and its ticket's B entry and asks the owner again: raise the limit, or split the production package under D45.
- **Amends:** D42: the check's test steps now carry an explicit limit.
- **Changes:** this entry and the "Amended by" line under D42; `tools/check/main.go` and `tools/check/race.go`.
- **See also:** D126, the owner's answer when the workspace package's race step first passed 15 minutes.
- **Amended by D127:** the limit is 30 minutes (`-timeout 30m`), and the owner is asked again when a package's race step passes 25 minutes.

### B26. C2: temporary scratches, private observations and merge-back (ticket 07)

- **Date:** 2026-10-15. **Decided by:** assistant. Under D117, the build advisor's [ruling](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/ticket-07-design-ruling.md) on the [design brief](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/ticket-07-design-brief.md) ended ticket 07's design step without a question to the owner. The build's choices, the project manager's answers while S3 was built and the build advisor's rulings during S3b are the assistant's, within the authority the owner delegated, and settle nothing outside the ruled design. The owner's answers in this ticket are recorded where they decide: OG-F7, the freeze of F2-07, in D119, and the time limit of the check's test steps in D122.
- **Question:** Ticket 07 builds C2's temporary scratches, private observations and merge-back in `internal/core/workspace`, on the service of tickets 05 and 06 (B23–B25), and writes the F2-07 records the owner froze (D119). What did the build settle, and what does it change in earlier entries?
- **Decision:**
  1. **The design as ruled** (P1–P21 and the ruling's thirteen findings). The package documentation, `internal/core/workspace/doc.go`, states the service as built; the design brief and the ruling give each choice's options and sources. The choices later tickets build on:
     - Typed writers (P1–P3; the ruling's findings 2 and 3). A writer binds a context to a task's scratch. The task's own context writes its dedicated scratch; another context of the same agent always gets a fresh temporary forked from the task's acknowledged tip, never from `main` or uncommitted bytes, and resumes an interrupted temporary only by naming it. One live writer is allowed per context and task. Callers state the binding until C3 authenticates it, and no record carries an agent ID.
     - Observations are Core commits (P7, P8). Each observation, even of an unchanged tree, is a deterministic single-parent commit on the private branch, retained by a ref and recorded before the branch moves; it records the paths written since the previous tip and the full set changed since the anchor.
     - Merge-back (P10–P13, P15). Only a task that has not moved since the fork fast-forwards; otherwise Git merges with the fork as the explicit base, and Core makes one target commit on the task's tip. A conflict pins a report, changes neither scratch and leaves the temporary resumable or abandonable. A merge-back waits for the task's turn and for a capture's protected window, not for a live request. A dedicated scratch stays represented by its anchor, so a task holding observed or merged edits answers `ErrUnrepresentedEdits` at closure until ticket 08's acceptance.
     - Failures and notices (P16–P18). A prepared record precedes every change to the task's files, index or branch. A failed write is resumed only by an explicit `RetryMergeBack`, which refuses interference rather than overwrite it, and Open reports an unresolved merge-back and never retries it. `ScratchNotices` is a passive, replayable record of a task's merge-back outcomes, which C3 delivers into the task's next turn (D63).
     - Admission provenance (P14). An admission names the task's latest observation and, in order, the merge-backs applied since the previous admission; replay checks them from records alone.
  2. **Amends B23 item 6 and B24 item 7: a moved branch blocks closure and admission** (P7; the S3a review's finding 2). Before use and at Open, Core repairs a private branch only from the scratch's previous acknowledged tip or from absence, by compare-and-swap. At any other value, a moved branch with an equal tree included, routing, observation, admission and closure answer `scratch_tip_mismatch` and Open lists the scratch, until the owner restores the branch; Core never overwrites it. Removal still deletes a branch only at its base, so a moved branch keeps its history. Ticket 05's `moved_branch_equal_tree` row, which used to close, now answers `scratch_tip_mismatch`.
  3. **Amends B24 items 1 and 5: the typed `Writer`** (P1; the ruling's minor finding on B24). `Service.BeginWrite(ctx, scratchID)` is replaced, with no alias, by `BeginWrite(ctx, WriteBinding) (*Writer, error)`: `Writer.Lease` takes a physical lease for one tool edit, `CommitObserved` records an observation, and `Close` drains without revoking a lease. The live handle keeps the caller's binding in memory for its lifetime only. A closed task scratch answers `ErrNoScratch`, where B24 item 5 answered `ErrClosed`. The service's calls gain `MergeBack`, `RetryMergeBack` and `ScratchNotices`.
  4. **The build advisor's rulings during S3b** (2026-10-13).
     - **Retention: direct.** An admission retains only the observation its `scratch_observation_id` names and the merge-back commits its `merge_back_ids` name. A merge-back's source history is reached through its target commit and the source's moved branch, never through a restored observation ref.
     - **Lifetime: live.** A request retains that provenance exactly as long as its capture: both are added together and released by the same compare-and-swap. An unresolved preparation retains its prepared commit, an open scratch its own observations and applied merge-backs until it closes, and an ended request nothing. Ticket 08's retention of accepted work extends which requests retain, not how.
     - **The closure gap: A.** A merge-back's applied record and its source's closure are separate commits, as F2-07's frozen closure rule and golden have them. When the closure is missing, Open lists `merge_back_closure_pending` and writes nothing, and the next `MergeBack` or `RetryMergeBack` appends only the missing closure, never a second applied record; `abandoned` on an applied source answers `ErrInvalid`.
  5. **The project manager's answers during S3**, each reviewed with its slice. Each inputs revision keeps its digest and body references in memory, so that an explicit retry can pin a superseded revision's bodies. A live request on the task does not refuse a merge-back, and a capture's protected window only makes it wait (P11). Tip repair on a task with an unresolved preparation checks the branch without moving it. Replay does not tighten the frozen contract: a log with a new merge-back attempt on an applied, still-open source opens, although `MergeBack` never writes one.
  6. **The lock invariant** (the S3b review's finding 5; the fix-up). Every wait on the reservation pool comes before the task's routing lock, and under that lock Core takes only reservations that succeed or answer busy at once; `BeginWrite` releases the routing lock across its pool wait and checks again afterwards; and `MergeBack` takes the inputs lock only once it holds the task, so an owner's `UpdateInputs` never waits behind a writer. No holder of the routing lock waits on the pool, and no service mutex is held across Git or a context wait (P21). Ticket 08 builds on it.
  7. **The cost of a lease** (the S3a and S3b reviews' first findings). At b6d2cf4 each `Writer.Lease` checked the private branch with two to three Git processes: 107.3 ms per lease on the 10,000-file scratch, above the 25 ms at which the S3a review asked for the change. The fix-up 1ebb83b keeps a flag in the projection that a failed tip repair sets and a successful one clears; a lease reads the flag and runs no Git, and repair stays in the calls that read or move the tip and in Open. A lease then read 0 ms: its 32 leases finished within one step of this machine's monotonic clock, about 0.5 ms (A.10), so each took under about 16 µs (Core Appendix A.13).
  8. **The tests' cost** (the build advisor's test-cost ruling and budget recommendation; D122).
     - After S3a the package took 295 s and 363 s in the check's tests and race steps. After S3b's matrices each shared one Git history and one closed store snapshot, the package took 516 s under the race detector run alone, above the 420 s the advisor had set, and the owner chose a 20-minute limit with a report at 15 minutes (D122).
     - **Baseline.** In the full check on 1ebb83b, the package took 442 s in the tests step and 424 s in the race step; this replaces B25 item 6's baseline for tickets 08 and 09, and the [ticket's evidence](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/ticket-07-evidence.md) lists the fifteen slowest tests under the race detector. Under D122, a race step above 15 minutes is named in its slice's evidence and its ticket's B entry, and the owner is asked again.
     - **Per-row cost rules,** from S3b: one Git history and one closed real-store snapshot per matrix, copied per row, with `--no-hardlinks` clones; the service reopened only where Open or recovery is the subject; a crash matrix of one row per crash point and recovery outcome; `t.Parallel()` first, no sleeps and no duration assertions; and no `-short` skips, per-test deadlines, package splits or test-only exports as budget workarounds. Each slice's evidence reports both step times and the ten slowest tests under the race detector, and names any test above 60 s with the row count that explains it.
- **Evidence:**
  - Ticket 07's closing comment lists each criterion's tests, from the ruling's acceptance coverage.
  - The build advisor approved S1 at 2c53572 with two minor findings, carried to S3; S2 at b23d8f7, with D119's freeze documents once UC-5 was added and two nits applied; S3a at c1c1a25 with four minor findings and nits, carried to S3b; and S3b at b6d2cf4 with five, which the fix-up 1ebb83b addresses. Its review: the build advisor approved 1ebb83b on 2026-10-15, on its diff, the coder's V1, race and V5 runs and the full check on its tree, with one minor finding carried to ticket 08 (a failed tip repair's cause is not kept for the lease's refusal). All nine steps of the full check passed on 2c53572, 2fa3b79, c1c1a25 and b6d2cf4, each the commit `main` moved to (D46), and on 1ebb83b's tree before this entry's commit.
  - Ten deliberate rule breaks while S1 was built, eight while S3a was, twelve while S3b was and five while the fix-up was each failed named tests, and the restored code passed; each of S2's 67 mutations of `contract/ledger` was caught. The ticket's evidence names each break and its failing tests, with every slice's checks.
  - D78, labelled: *assumption*, B15's 12 or 20 code-working pairs, each offering one change per 600 s; provisionally three active turns per agent, the task's writer and up to two writers from its other contexts; and an unmeasured share of changes written through a temporary, anything from none to all (the design brief §5.3); *calculation*, each temporary is a full checkout of its task, O(files) on disk; each observation and each merge-back's verification reads O(files + raw bytes), while the merge writes only changed paths; a merge-back holds only its task; and a busy answer moves backlog to the caller, not throughput; *measurement*, Core Appendix A.13, single observations, a temporary's creation, a lease and a fast-forward and an explicit-base merge-back at 1,000 and 10,000 files, with the scratch and body bytes after each; *plan*, concurrent temporaries and merge-backs under load are C4's feasibility gate and turn scheduling is C3's, where A.11–A.13 calibrate the provisional profile.
- **Limits:**
  - Nothing is frozen or approved here: F2-07 was frozen by D119, the service writes it as frozen, and `CONTRACTS.md` is unchanged.
  - Promotion stays unavailable until C4's real validator, and harness work stays testing-only (D52). Callers state writer bindings and actors until C3 authenticates them, C3 delivers notices into turns, and C10 is the isolation boundary.
  - One machine and one run: verified on Windows, on NTFS, on this machine. Linux and macOS are compiled and vetted only, and their rows first run in C3's CI. A.13's figures are single measurements, not thresholds or capacity claims.
- **Changes:**
  - The code is `internal/core/workspace` as S1, S3a, S3b and the fix-up built it, and `contract/ledger` as S2 built it and D119 froze it; ticket 07's comments name the commits.
  - Documents: in `DECISIONS.md`, this entry and "Amended by" lines under B23, B24 and B25; in Core, Appendix A's dates, the new A.13 and §8.6's "Workspaces" row; in `ROADMAP-CORE.md`, the note on C2's scratch lifecycle events and C2's Status; ticket 07's status, criteria, Answer and comments; and under `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/`, the design brief, the ruling, the Git probes `ticket-07-git-probes.md`, `ticket-07-git-retry-probe.md` and `ticket-07-git-classify-probe.md`, the evidence `ticket-07-evidence.md` and the raw results `ticket-07-capture-workload.jsonl`.
- **Amended by B27:** item 4's "an ended request nothing": an open scratch's unfailed head, accepted or no-change included, and its represented request's capture stay retained while the scratch is open (B27 item 1); item 7's flag holds the failed repair's error, which a lease returns, and a repair that reaches no verdict because its caller's context ended leaves it unchanged; item 8's baseline for ticket 09 is B27 item 7's, 508 s and 621 s in the full check on 7a6624b.
- **Amended by B28:** item 8's per-row rule: a row's Git history is a private copy of an unregistered history, or a `--no-hardlinks` clone of a registered one; the F2-07 rule-break matrices apply their mutations serially on one shared projection with undo, as F2-08's do, and keep a real Open for each distinct obligation (B28 item 6).

### D123. F2-08 finalization, acceptance and no-change records frozen

- **Date:** 2026-10-15. **Decided by:** adopted. In OG-F8, ticket 08's F2-08 freeze request, sent with the [freeze packet](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/f2-08-freeze-packet.md), the owner picked "Freeze as presented" from the assistant's options.
- **Owner's words (question tool):**
  > Freeze as presented
- **Decision:** F2-08, ticket 08's part of C2's F2 family, is frozen by addition to `ledger-format` version 1 (D40, D111, D113), at commit c44484d, whose `contract/ledger` reached `main` unchanged in 0df6a0a:
  - three record types, each at version 1: `workspace.finalization_intended`, Core's intent, written before its one guarded update of `main`, binding a request and its current clean generation to the predecessor and target commits and trees and to `finalization_rules`; `workspace.promotion_accepted`, the durable update with the full changed path set, which becomes the workspace's witness; and `workspace.promotion_no_change`, a successful ending whose generation's tree equals its predecessor's. `Defined()` lists the sixteen type versions of F0, F2-05, F2-06, F2-07 and F2-08;
  - additions to frozen types: the optional `predecessor_witness_seq` of `workspace.generation_derived`, also on the three new types, which names the workspace's latest acceptance and is present exactly when one exists, on a generation only when it is built over canonical main with no train predecessor; and the outcomes `cancelled` and `interrupted` of `workspace.promotion_failed`, which D118's consequences admit without a new version;
  - their payloads, decoder rules and record rules as the F2-08 section of the `contract/ledger` package documentation states them, including one unresolved intent per workspace, the witness chain and the represented tree; each new record rule is conditional on a new type, key or outcome, so that every log without F2-08 records stays valid;
  - the points the packet put to the owner, as built: these names, success being its own types because every `promotion_failed` outcome fails the lineage; an intent that binds request, generation, predecessor, target and `finalization_rules` only, validation evidence and the policy, check, environment and law revisions joining by addition when F4, ticket 09 and U1.L freeze them; the first acceptance witnessing `main` as observed, after which a `main` moved outside the harness blocks the workspace's promotions until a later part defines reanchoring; a restart ending every unfinished request `interrupted` after the host's bookkeeping phase; cancellation too late once the intent is written; `changed_paths` on the acceptance only and full, a change set too large for the record being refused before the intent; `finalization-rules/1`, which needs the `files` ref backend and flushes the object and ref directories at a measured cost; and a scratch whose work was accepted still closing as `abandoned`, `promoted` being a later part's reason;
  - the golden sample `contract/ledger/testdata/f2/08/log/00000000000000000001.jsonl` (SHA-256 985e073a342d9f991fdd2afd407dc495c3542020c98e35d3079018e29db14b8c); the F0, F2-05, F2-06 and F2-07 golden samples keep D111's, D115's, D118's and D119's digests.
- **Not frozen:** where the record rules are enforced, Core's workspace service when it appends and at Open, which ticket 08's S3 builds; the service's rules around these records, such as the `pathset_cap` preflight, cancellation's `too_late` answer, the refusals while a workspace is recovery-pending and the recovery mapping of an unresolved intent; the rules that `finalization-rules/1` names, Core policy named in the record, which change through a new value; which package may append these types, `internal/core/workspace` only, a review rule; the privacy-class wording, which stays register text; and every later part of F2, which joins through its own additive freeze (D113).
- **Consequences:** these types and additions now change only by addition: new optional keys, new types, new type versions with upgraders, and new `promotion_failed` outcomes, which need no new version. A breaking change needs an owner decision entry listing every step it affects (D40). Ticket 08's S2 reaches `main` with this entry (D41), and S3, the code that writes these records, may start (D113). `ledger-format` stays at version 1, and no step's status changes. Nothing promotes in C2: the product still answers `no_validator` (D52).
- **Changes:** `CONTRACTS.md` (the `ledger-format` row's text, owning step and status; the F2-08 catalogue); the `contract/ledger` comments that called F2-08 proposed (the package documentation's Catalogue and F2-08 sections, `Defined`, the type constants, the outcome constants and seven test comments); the "Promotion attempted", "Promotion accepted" and "Promotion refused" rows of Undercurrent §15.1; the OG-F8 packet, with its answer line, added under `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/`, and its evidence, `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/f2-08-freeze-evidence.md`; a "See also" line under D113.

### D124. F2-09 policy activation records frozen

- **Date:** 2026-10-16. **Decided by:** adopted. In OG-F9, ticket 09's F2-09 freeze request, sent with the [freeze packet](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/f2-09-freeze-packet.md), the owner picked "Freeze as presented" from the assistant's options.
- **Owner's words (question tool):**
  > Freeze as presented
- **Decision:** F2-09, ticket 09's part of C2's F2 family, is frozen by addition to `ledger-format` version 1 (D40, D111, D113), at commit 93b76a4, whose `contract/ledger` reached `main` unchanged in 0a21963:
  - four record types, each at version 1: `workspace.policy_activation_requested`, the owner's exact approval of a retained field law, validation policy and local policy map, with its schedule, its derived `affected_workspace_ids` and the active manifest it expects, followed in its commit by a permanent pin of the approved bytes; `workspace.policy_activation_hold_changed`, the pending request's `due`, `clock_untrusted` or `none` hold; `workspace.policy_activation_ended`, a request's ending under its revision; and `workspace.policy_activated`, the colony's one switch to the approved manifest, whose effective sequence and identity are its envelope's. `Defined()` lists the twenty type versions of F0, F2-05, F2-06, F2-07, F2-08 and F2-09;
  - an addition to a frozen type: the optional `activation_record_hash`, the last key of `workspace.finalization_intended`, which names the `workspace.policy_activated` record current when the intent was appended; D123's consequences admit it without a new version, and through it the intent binds the policy, check, environment and law revisions that D123 left to join by addition, validation evidence remaining F4's;
  - their payloads, decoder rules and record rules as the F2-09 section of the `contract/ledger` package documentation states them, including one pending request per colony, the request's revision, the durable clock floor and the epoch; each new record rule is conditional on an F2-09 type or the new key, so that every log without F2-09 records stays valid and an intent without the key keeps its F2-08 bytes;
  - the points the packet put to the owner, as built: these names; `owner_approval_id`, the request being the owner's exact approval, ended only by cancellation or replacement under its revision, reapproval being a new request and C3's revocation joining later as an `ended` outcome; the approved bytes retained for the life of the log and never unpinned, check and environment bytes getting identity, not meaning; one pending request per colony, a stale expected manifest being refused at admission and never recorded; the derived `affected_workspace_ids`; signed UTC nanosecond fields and `observed_utc_ns` present exactly when the clock is trusted; durable holds owned by the request, an untrusted clock holding rather than bypassing; one colony-wide switch at an actual trusted time, never backdated; after the first activation, every new intent binding the active manifest and an unconfigured workspace unable to intend; the open `outcome` token, version 1 writing `cancelled` and `replaced`; and replay that never executes;
  - the golden sample `contract/ledger/testdata/f2/09/log/00000000000000000001.jsonl` (SHA-256 f1a0c473ee410c189c743547f37c86ebd638ce9c92c68cd44d1b2816f9f3ad88); the F0, F2-05, F2-06, F2-07 and F2-08 golden samples keep D111's, D115's, D118's, D119's and D123's digests.
- **Not frozen:** where the record rules are enforced, Core's workspace service when it appends and at Open, which ticket 09's S3a builds; the service's rules around these records, such as the timestamp parser, the single timer and its wake sources, the admission refusals of a stale or unknown expected manifest, `target_unavailable`, `policy_unavailable` and the `activation_recovery_pending` fence; which package may append these types, `internal/core/workspace` only, a review rule; the privacy-class wording, which stays register text; C3's revocation of an approval, which F3 defines; and every later part of F2, which joins through its own additive freeze (D113).
- **Consequences:** these types and the added key now change only by addition: new optional keys, new types, new type versions with upgraders, and new `workspace.policy_activation_ended` outcomes, which need no new version. A breaking change needs an owner decision entry listing every step it affects (D40). Ticket 09's S2 reaches `main` with this entry (D41), and S3a, the code that writes these records, no longer waits for the freeze (D113). `ledger-format` stays at version 1, and no step's status changes. Nothing activates or promotes through C2's product, which still answers `no_validator` (D52).
- **Changes:** `CONTRACTS.md` (the `ledger-format` row's text, owning step and status; the F2-09 catalogue); the `contract/ledger` comments that called F2-09 proposed (the package documentation's Catalogue and F2-09 sections, `Defined`, the type, schedule, hold and outcome constants and seven test comments, and the replay-essential sentence); the `PolicyActivationManifest` sketch of Undercurrent §13.2 and its §15.1 "Promotion attempted" and law-activation rows; the OG-F9 packet, with its answer line, added under `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/`, and its evidence, `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/f2-09-freeze-evidence.md`; a "See also" line under D113.

### D125. Completed contract slices are frozen without the owner's approval

- **Date:** 2026-10-16. **Decided by:** owner.
- **Owner's words (chat):**
  > User approval is no longer necessary to freeze completed slices or tickets. Freeze and proceed in the future
- **Decision:** When a contract slice is complete, the project manager freezes it and lands it without asking the owner. A slice is complete once it is built and evidenced, and the build advisor has approved it together with its freeze record. The freeze is recorded as a decision entry with the originator `assistant` (`AGENTS.md`: decided within leeway the owner delegated), citing this entry; the register change in `CONTRACTS.md`, the "frozen" wording in the package and the evidence file follow D118's and D123's form. No freeze packet is written. For that slice, the freeze is also the D41 sign-off for reaching `main`. Tickets: a ticket's close commit already lands on the build advisor's approval of its documents (D116 items 3 and 6) without a question to the owner; this entry adds no ticket-level gate and removes none.
- **Limits:** These still wait for the owner: a breaking change to a frozen contract (D40), D52 approvals, new dependencies (D29), other changes that touch an owner decision (D41), and pushing (D31, D46). He reads freeze records as he chooses; a change he asks for after a freeze follows D40's rules.
- **Changes:** this entry; "Amended by D125" lines under D40, D116 and D117; `CONTRACTS.md` (its opening sentence and the lifecycle table's *Frozen* row).
- **See also:** D130 records the project manager's freeze of F-U3-61, ticket 61's part and U3's first producer family, under this entry.
- **See also:** D139 records the project manager's freeze of F3-10, ticket 10's private-state record family, under this entry.
- **See also:** D140 records the project manager's freeze of F3-11, ticket 11's scoped authority, task and non-paid work record family, under this entry.
- **See also:** D144 records the project manager's freeze of F3-12, ticket 12's review and failure decision record family, under this entry.
- **See also:** D148 records the project manager's freeze of F3-13, ticket 13's paid route, credential, spend, attempt and settlement record family, under this entry.
- **See also:** D154 records the project manager's freeze of F3-139, ticket 139's external-resource registration, work-domain registration, declaration and receipt family, under this entry.
- **See also:** D157 records the completed additive ledger-reader checkpoint capability under this entry and D156's Ticket140-only sequencing exception.

### B27. C2: finalization, cancellation and recovery (ticket 08)

- **Date:** 2026-10-16. **Decided by:** assistant. Under D117, the build advisor's [ruling](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/ticket-08-design-ruling.md) on the [design brief](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/ticket-08-design-brief.md) ended ticket 08's design step without a question to the owner. The build's choices and the project manager's answers while S1 and S3 were built are the assistant's, within the authority the owner delegated, and settle nothing outside the ruled design. The owner's one answer in this ticket, OG-F8, the freeze of F2-08, is recorded in D123.
- **Question:** Ticket 08 builds C2's canonical finalization, request cancellation and crash reconciliation in `internal/core/workspace`, on the service of tickets 05–07 (B23–B26), and writes the F2-08 records the owner froze (D123). What did the build settle, and what does it change in earlier entries?
- **Decision:**
  1. **The design as ruled** (P1–P22 and the ruling's twelve findings). The package documentation, `internal/core/workspace/doc.go`, states the service as built; the design brief and the ruling give each choice's options and sources. The choices later tickets build on:
     - One sequence per workspace (P2, P5, P7). Integration, finalization, cancellation and reconciliation share one train lock per workspace; finalization takes no task lock, reservation or writer wait, so B26 item 6's lock invariant holds. Every Git read and the `pathset_cap` size check come before the intent; after it, one `update-ref --no-deref --end-of-options refs/heads/main <target> <old>`, and a re-read of `main`, never the exit code, decides: the old tip ends the request `interrupted` with its local suffix in one commit, the target goes on to durability and the acceptance, and any other tip holds the intent. There is never a second update, a rollback or a retry. The slot, the workspace's blocker and the lock are released only after the terminal record is acknowledged; until then a moved `main` is not accepted work and the workspace answers `finalization_pending`.
     - The witness (P3). A workspace's latest `promotion_accepted` is its witness. The first acceptance witnesses `main` as observed; afterwards a `main` moved outside the harness blocks the workspace's promotions until a later part defines reanchoring.
     - Endings (P6, P8, P11). Only `promotion_accepted` and `promotion_no_change` succeed; every `promotion_failed` fails the lineage, whatever its outcome token. No-change compares trees, never commits, with the witness, and a child built over a no-change parent's commit is rebuilt.
     - Cancellation (P9, P10; the ruling's nit on in-flight IDs). `CancelPromotion` accepts the owner, or an agent with the admission's context, and checks again under the train lock; it ends the request `cancelled` with its local suffix `dependency_failed` in one commit and leaves foreign requests alone. After the intent it answers `too_late` and writes nothing.
     - Representation and retention (P12, P13). An open scratch is represented by the sealed tree of its latest request that ended accepted or no-change, else by its anchor; closure and a new root's baseline use it, and the scratch's files, tip and index never change. An open scratch's unfailed head and its represented request's capture stay retained after those requests end, as B26 item 4 foresaw.
     - Durability (P14; the ruling's finding on fsync). Finalization needs Git's `files` ref backend, else it answers `ref_backend_unavailable` before the intent. After the update, `hardenCanonicalStorage` flushes the object and ref directories and the common directory, child before parent, each first checked to be a real directory, and re-reads `main`. There is one fsync recipe: the pinned `core.fsync=committed` is `objects|reference` in Git's code at 2.45 and 2.55, although Git's documentation describes it as objects; the design's probes verified the reference flush on the pinned Git, and changing the pinned Git re-verifies it.
     - Recovery (P15, P16; the ruling's finding 4). Open writes nothing; it lists each workspace with unfinished requests `promotion_recovery_pending`, and admission, integration, finalization and no-change refuse until the bookkeeping phase, `reconcilePromotions`, has run, while cancellation and status remain. Only an unresolved intent needs proof that the earlier writers stopped: the closed previous service in the same process, or completed waits on the previous Core and its Git children; a store lock, an absent PID, a timeout or a dead Core prove nothing.
