With the proof, `main` at the old tip ends the request `interrupted`; at the target, the phase flushes, then records the acceptance with the changed paths recomputed from the intent's two trees, without a second update; anything else leaves the intent blocked. Every other unfinished request then ends `interrupted` from the log alone, and its author resubmits under a new ID (D55).
     - The product admits nothing (P1; D52). `finalize`, `noChange` and `reconcilePromotions` are unexported and reached only by package tests in disposable repositories until C4, and `RequestPromotion` still answers `no_validator`.
  2. **Amends B25 item 1: restarts** (P16). B25 kept unfinished requests live in the train across a restart, with their slots, refs and generations, because D55's `interrupted` needed ticket 08's intents to prove that nothing was promoted. With them, a reopened workspace with unfinished requests refuses work until the bookkeeping phase ends those requests; they keep their slots, refs and generations until then. `TestOpenRebuildsAdmissionState` and `TestRequestPoisonReplay` were adapted, not deleted: after a reopen, status and slots are as before, integration refuses, and the phase ends the requests once.
  3. **Amends B26 item 7: a cancelled tip repair** (the finding ticket 07 carried; the S3a review's nit). A failed tip repair records its error, and a lease returns that error instead of a bare `scratch_tip_mismatch`; a repair that reaches no verdict because its caller's context ended leaves the recorded result unchanged, so a foreign context's cancelled call no longer refuses the task writer's leases (`TestWriterRepairCancellationKeepsLease`).
  4. **The project manager's answers during the build**, each reviewed with its slice.
     - `hardenCanonicalStorage` takes the target commit and tree, and its re-read after the flushes must equal them (S1).
     - Tests may set a Git child's `Cancel` to a no-op through the test-only `afterStart` hook before cancelling its context, so that the wait-delay kill and an ambiguous `update-ref` exit are reached with real processes; the hook writes only `Cancel`, before the cancellation, and never stops, replaces or fakes a process (S1; confirmed by its review).
     - `RequestState.State` gains `RequestFinalizing`, `RequestAccepted`, `RequestNoChange`, `RequestCancelled`, `RequestInterrupted` and `RequestUnknownFailure`, exported beside `RequestPending`, and a `FailureOutcome` field carries an unknown token (S3a; confirmed by its review).
     - The new exported calls and types are `CancelPromotion`, `Cancellation` and `CancellationResult`, and `ErrUnknownRequest`: `CancelPromotion` answers it for an unknown ID and `request_in_flight`, busy, for one whose admission is still in flight (S3b).
     - The recovery boundary is Open (S3b). It marks a workspace recovery-pending while it builds the projection, so admission refuses from then, and the mark clears once the phase has run. A blocked intent keeps its own request and slot, and the workspace answers `finalization_pending` until the intent resolves. `reconcilePromotions` acts when the workspace is recovery-pending or holds an unresolved intent, so a later call with proof resolves a blocked intent; with neither, it does nothing, so a repeat writes nothing. It makes no check for an admission in flight.
     - Windows rows (S3b): `TestWindowsPackedRefDurability` covers SHA-1 and SHA-256, and one native crash row opens the canonical Git child's process handle through `afterStart`, kills the helper Core and shows that reconciliation refuses until completed waits on both handles, with no sleep and a context deadline on every wait.
     - Open's report (S3b): `Pending` lists `promotion_recovery_pending` with the workspace's ID for a workspace with unfinished requests; the 25 assertions on `Pending` after a reopen, in eight test files, were checked and only those that failed on the new entry changed, and a test that reuses a request across a reopen first runs the phase. Every other assertion was kept.
  5. **The long-path test was dropped** (S1; confirmed by its review). Git 2.55.0.windows.3 refuses any `GIT_DIR` over 220 characters, MAX_PATH less 40, with or without `core.longpaths=true` or the `\\?\` form, and every Core call passes `--git-dir` or sets `GIT_DIR`. No working canonical common directory is longer, so the longest path `hardenCanonicalStorage` flushes stays under 260 characters, and the ruling's row for a common directory beyond 260 cannot be built with real Git. `TestWindowsFinalizationSharing` and `TestWindowsFinalizationSharingRecovery` carry no long-path row and say why; `durable.SyncDir`'s long-path support stays covered by `internal/core/durable/durable_test.go:41-44` and by the design's probe on a 341-character directory ([`ticket-08-git-probes.md`](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/ticket-08-git-probes.md), with the length probe appended).
  6. **A new import, `internal/core/durable`** (P14). The package calls Core's own OS durability adapter for `SyncDir`: a new edge inside D45's one-way layering and no new module (D29), where the alternative was a copied Win32 adapter. S1's evidence lists it, and every full check since has passed with it.
  7. **The tests' cost** (D122).
     - In the full checks, the package took 459 s and 569 s in the tests and race steps after S1 (ba5d5c8), and 494 s and 608 s after S3a (75fb821).
     - **Baseline.** In the full check on 7a6624b, the package took 508 s in the tests step and 621 s in the race step; this replaces B26 item 8's baseline for ticket 09, and the [ticket's evidence](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/ticket-08-evidence.md) lists the fifteen slowest tests under the race detector. No race step reached D122's 15 minutes, at which the owner is asked again.
     - **Per-row cost.** B26 item 8's rules stand. Each finalization that moves `main` costs about 0.2 s of flushes, measured at 188–213 ms per call in S1, and each crash row one helper process, run one after another. `TestScratchWaitsOnlyForItsPair`'s unrelated pair now has a two-minute deadline instead of 10 s, after it expired inside `git worktree add` while focused tests overlapped a full check; a hang still fails (S3b).
- **Evidence:**
  - Ticket 08's closing comment lists each criterion's tests, from the ruling's acceptance coverage.
  - The build advisor approved S2 at c44484d, with D123's freeze documents once two minor wording fixes and one nit were applied; S1 at ba5d5c8 with one minor finding, carried to S3a; S3a at 75fb821 with three nits, carried to S3b; and S3b: the build advisor approved 7a6624b on 2026-10-16, with one minor finding (the sequential crash matrix) and one nit carried to ticket 09, and one minor wording fix and two nits applied to these documents. All nine steps of the full check passed on e071168, ba5d5c8, 75fb821 and 7a6624b, each the commit `main` moved to (D46).
  - Sixteen deliberate breaks of S2's decoders and encoders, eight rule breaks while S1 was built, fourteen while S3a was and twenty while S3b was each failed named tests, and the restored code passed. The ticket's evidence names each break and its failing tests, with every slice's checks.
  - D78, labelled: *assumption*, B15's 12 or 20 code-working pairs, each offering one change per 600 s, with a synchronized burst of four checkpoints per pair (`ROADMAP-CORE.md` C2); *calculation*, finalization is one serialized sequence per workspace, so a workspace finalizes at most 3,600/F changes an hour, F being the whole sequence, and the provisional target of 1.25 times steady demand, 150 an hour at 20 pairs, needs F ≤ 24 s if all share one workspace, before validation or contention; a finalization that moves `main` appends three records in two commits and a no-change two in one (the design brief §5, as the ruling's removals leave it); *measurement*, Core Appendix A.14, a no-change ending, each step of one finalization and two reconciliations after a restart, at 1,000 and 10,000 files, with the log's records and bytes, live pins, retained refs and loose objects after each; *plan*, accepted throughput under contention, with real validation, is C4's feasibility gate (`ROADMAP-CORE.md` C4), where A.11–A.14 calibrate the provisional profile.
- **Limits:**
  - Nothing is frozen or approved here: F2-08 was frozen by D123, the service writes it as frozen, and `CONTRACTS.md` is unchanged.
  - Promotion stays unavailable until C4's real validator, and harness work stays testing-only (D52); validation evidence and the policy, check, environment and law revisions join the intent by addition when F4, ticket 09 and U1.L freeze them (D123). Callers state actors and contexts until C3 authenticates them. The proof that writers stopped covers only the writers the host owned; unknown same-user processes and Windows grandchildren are C10's, which is the isolation boundary (the ruling, P15).
  - One machine and one run: verified on Windows, on NTFS, on this machine. Linux and macOS are compiled and vetted only, and their rows first run in C3's CI. A.14's figures are single measurements, not thresholds or capacity claims.
- **Changes:**
  - The code is `internal/core/workspace` as S1, S3a and S3b built it, and `contract/ledger` as S2 built it and D123 froze it; ticket 08's comments name the commits.
  - Documents: in `DECISIONS.md`, this entry and "Amended by" lines under B25 and B26; in Core, Appendix A's dates, the new A.14 and §8.6's "Workspaces" row; in `ROADMAP-CORE.md`, C2's Status; ticket 08's status, criteria, Answer and comments; and under `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/`, the design brief, the ruling, the Git probes `ticket-08-git-probes.md` with the length probe appended, the evidence `ticket-08-evidence.md` and the raw results `ticket-08-capture-workload.jsonl`.
- **Amended by D126:** item 7's "No race step reached D122's 15 minutes" held for the evidence check on 7a6624b; the check on e7f1a5c, the same code and the commit `main` moved to, ran the package's race step in 918.992 s, and the owner kept the 20-minute limit.
- **Amended by B28:** item 7's crash rows no longer run one after another: `TestFinalizationCrashMatrix`'s eleven rows run in parallel, each with its own repository, colony and store, and `TestWindowsOwnedGitCrashBeforeRecovery` beside the package's other tests, as D126 planned; the matrix took 22.0 s instead of 48.2 s run alone (B28 item 7). Item 7's baseline for later tickets is B28 item 8's, 315 s and 407 s in the full check on 245a032.

### D126. The 20-minute limit stays after a race step above 15 minutes

- **Date:** 2026-10-16. **Decided by:** adopted.
- **Owner's words (question tool):** "Keep 20 min for now"
- **Context:** D122 asks the owner again when a package's race step passes 15 minutes. The full check on e7f1a5c, ticket 08's close and the commit `main` moved to, ran `internal/core/workspace` in 814.575 s in the tests step and 918.992 s in the race step. The same code (7a6624b) took 507.693 s and 621.305 s in its evidence check three hours earlier; whole checks that night varied between 1,601 and 2,965 s with no other load found. The options were to keep 20 minutes, raise the limit to 30, or split the production package under D45.
- **Decision:** The 20-minute limit stays. The project manager asks the owner again if a check's race step passes 15 minutes once more. Separately, the project manager takes the carried finding of ticket 08's S3b review and runs `TestFinalizationCrashMatrix` and `TestWindowsOwnedGitCrashBeforeRecovery` in parallel in ticket 09.
- **Changes:** this entry, and lines under D122 and B27.
- **Amended by D127:** the limit is 30 minutes and the owner is asked again when a race step passes 25 minutes.

### D127. The full check's tests and race steps run with a 30-minute limit

- **Date:** 2026-10-16. **Decided by:** adopted.
- **Owner's words (question tool):** "Raise to 30 min, report at 25"
- **Context:** Under D126 the project manager asked again after the check on 8691b65, the commit `main` moved to with ticket 09's S1, ran `internal/core/workspace`'s race step in 963.624 s; the same code took 673.671 s on d85ed78 three hours earlier. Whole checks of unchanged code had varied between 1,601 and 3,207 s on this four-core laptop on the Balanced power plan, which the project manager attributes to heat throttling during the long checks; it was not measured. The options were 30 minutes reported at 25, 20 minutes reported at 15, or splitting the production package under D45.
- **Decision:** `go run ./tools/check` passes `-timeout 30m` to its tests and race steps (`testTimeout` in `tools/check/main.go`). When a package's race step in a check passes 25 minutes, the project manager names it in that slice's evidence and its ticket's B entry and asks the owner again.
- **Amends:** D122's limit and report line, and D126.
- **Changes:** this entry and "Amended by D127" lines under D122 and D126; `tools/check/main.go`.

### D128. Markdown-only commits need no full check

- **Date:** 2026-10-16. **Decided by:** owner.
- **Owner's words (question tool):** "Markdown only changes don't need a full check. We need to reduce the lengh of these checks. This is Ticket 8/100+ so this isn't a sustainable practice in the current format"
- **Context:** D46 runs the full check before `main` moves, and the project manager ran it on every commit `main` moved to, documents-only ones included: ticket closes, freeze records and decision records. Their Go code had already passed, and no Go code or test reads the Markdown files, so those runs could only repeat a result. Between 2026-10-15 and 2026-10-16, five of twelve checks were such repeats, about 3.7 hours of machine time.
- **Decision:** A commit whose changes since a commit that passed the full check are only Markdown files (`.md`) moves `main` without a new full check. The project manager's document check runs instead: every relative link resolves and every file is LF (B1). Any other change, `.go`, `go.mod`, `go.sum`, `testdata` or tools, still runs the full check. The checks must also get shorter; the project manager reports how.
- **Amends:** D46.
- **Changes:** this entry and the "Amended by D128" line under D46; `AGENTS.md`'s Check line; `ROADMAPS.md`'s Integration rule.

### D129. The race step runs once per ticket

- **Date:** 2026-10-16. **Decided by:** adopted.
- **Owner's words (question tool):** "Race step once per ticket", selected with "Measure Defender exclusions" from the project manager's options for shortening the checks.
- **Context:** The check's race step reruns every package's tests under the race detector; `internal/core/workspace` alone took 621–964 s in it during tickets 08 and 09. Coders already run their changed package's whole suite under the race detector, and its concurrency tests twenty times (V5), on the tree the project manager commits and verifies by hash (D121).
- **Decision:** A slice merge runs `go run ./tools/check -race=false`, every step except the race step. Each ticket's last code slice runs the full check, race step included. Coders keep their race runs.
- **Amends:** D42's gate and D46.
- **Changes:** this entry and "Amended by D129" lines under D42 and D46; `AGENTS.md`'s Check line; `ROADMAPS.md`'s Integration rule; `tools/check` gains `-race=false`.
- **Amended by D153:** every hosted full check includes race on all three systems; local race/×20 applies only under D152 item 1, replacing the once-per-ticket race cadence and routine slice `-race=false` gate.

### B28. C2: one-off policy activation (ticket 09)

- **Date:** 2026-10-17. **Decided by:** assistant. Under D117, the build advisor's [ruling](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/ticket-09-design-ruling.md) on the [design brief](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/ticket-09-design-brief.md) ended ticket 09's design step without a question to the owner, and the build advisor's [test-cost ruling](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/ticket-09-test-cost-ruling.md) on the project manager's [diagnosis](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/ticket-09-test-cost-diagnosis.md) set the work the owner asked for on the tests' cost. The build's choices and the project manager's answers during the build are the assistant's, within the authority the owner delegated, and settle nothing outside the ruled designs. The owner's answers in this ticket are recorded in D124 (OG-F9, the freeze of F2-09) and D126–D129 (the check's limits and form); his pause and his choices for his machine, quoted below, are recorded in item 5.
- **Owner's words:**
  - chat, 2026-10-16: "Pause before commencing Ticket 09 S2. These tests will be diagnosed and optimized before moving on"
  - question tool, 2026-10-16, on the Program Compatibility Assistant service: "Restart it now"; on Defender's exclusion of the build's temporary directory: "Remove it; try a git.exe exclusion"; after a measurement with and without that exclusion: "Keep it (one UAC click)"
- **Question:** Ticket 09 builds C2's one-off policy activation in `internal/core/workspace`, on the service of tickets 05–08 (B23–B27), and writes the F2-09 records the owner froze (D124); during the build the owner asked for the package's tests to be diagnosed and made faster. What did the build settle, and what does it change in earlier entries?
- **Decision:**
  1. **The design as ruled** (P1–P18 and the ruling's twelve findings). The package documentation, `internal/core/workspace/doc.go`, states the service as built in its section on policy activation; the design brief and the ruling give each choice's options and sources. The choices later tickets build on:
     - The owner's approval (P1, P3). `RequestActivation`, `ReplaceActivation` and `CancelActivation` are owner commands, and `ActivationStatus` and `ActivePolicy` inert owner views. In C2 the owner's authorization is the pending request itself, with a caller-supplied `owner_approval_id`; it ends only by cancellation or replacement under its revision, and reapproval is a new request with new IDs. Nothing withdraws an approval: C3's revocation, when F3 defines it, joins as a new `ended` outcome.
     - Exact retention (P2). Admission takes the approved bytes by digest, checks each SHA-256, loads the law and the policy through `contract/law`'s `StandardV1` loaders, and publishes every body and pins it permanently in the request's commit; nothing unpins it. Check and environment bodies are identities without meaning in C2. The switch checks only their presence and digests again; missing or corrupt bytes answer `target_unavailable` and keep the hold.
     - One pending request per colony (P5, P18). A same-ID call with the same binding returns the request's state before any other check, and a changed binding is `ErrActivationMismatch`. Replacement and cancellation name the pending request's ID and revision, the actual sequence of its latest record, checked again under the service's lock; a replacement is one commit. A stale or wrongly present expected manifest is refused at admission, wrapping `ErrRevision`, and an unknown one is `ErrInvalid`; a refusal writes nothing and consumes no ID.
     - Scope (P4). One colony-wide epoch. The affected workspaces are derived against the expected manifest: the target's at the first activation, the union of the old and new when the law or the policy changes, otherwise the changed entries. Until the first activation, intents stay as F2-08 defines them; afterwards a workspace's new intent carries `activation_record_hash` and needs an entry in the active manifest, else `policy_unavailable`.
     - Time (P6, P7). Times are strict RFC3339 with an explicit offset, normalized with checked arithmetic to signed 64-bit UTC nanoseconds. Core samples an injected clock, and a sample is trusted only at or above the floor, the greatest recorded or in-process trusted instant, never an envelope's or a UUID's. An untrusted clock holds and never bypasses. One timer, armed only from a trusted sample before the request is due; a regressed sample arms nothing, and trust returns only through a wake: resume, an owner control, the finalizer's guard or an affected intent's ending.
     - Holds and the switch (P8–P10, P13). The finalizer runs its Git preflight under its train lock, then the activation guard, the intent's encoding and its append together under the service's lock, so an intent is either durable before a hold, and drains, or refused after it. A `due` hold is final. The switch waits only for the affected workspaces' unresolved intents, then appends one `policy_activated` at the live trusted time, never backdated, with the epoch one above the last; its sequence and identity are read back from the acknowledged envelope, and a failure to read them poisons the service, never undoes the commit.
     - Replay (P11). Open rebuilds requests, holds, the active manifest and the floor from the log; it reads no clock, starts no timer and appends nothing. A pending request without a durable `due` or `clock_untrusted` hold fences its affected workspaces `activation_recovery_pending` until `resumeActivationControls`, which runs independently of ticket 08's `reconcilePromotions`. A cancellation or replacement removes only its own hold.
     - The product admits nothing (D52). Activation takes no train lock and runs no Git, and `RequestPromotion` still answers `no_validator` after an applied activation.
  2. **A new import, `contract/law`** (P2, P17). The package loads the approved law and policy with U1.L's frozen format (D114), an edge the import table already allows from `internal/core/...` to `contract/...`; no new module (D29) and no change to `tools/check`. S1's evidence lists it, and every check since has passed with it.
  3. **The exported calls and types** (S3a; the S1 review's finding). New: `ActivationSchedule`, `ActivationRequest`, `ActivationReplacement`, `ActivationCancellation`, `ActivePolicy`, `ActivationState`, `ActivationControlResult`, `Service.RequestActivation`, `Service.ReplaceActivation`, `Service.CancelActivation`, `Service.ActivationStatus`, `Service.ActivePolicy`, `ErrUnknownActivation` and `ErrActivationMismatch`, with S1's `ActivationTarget`. S1's `ActivationTarget.WorkspacePolicies` became `[]ledger.WorkspacePolicy`, the frozen contract's type, and S1's `ActivationWorkspacePolicy` was removed. `resumeActivationControls` and the loop stay unexported, reached by the package's tests until C3 and C4 (P1).
  4. **The project manager's answers during the build, and the carried findings that set behavior**, each reviewed with its slice.
     - The encoder of `workspace.policy_activation_hold_changed` refuses a nonzero `observed_utc_ns` with `clock_untrusted`, zero being its absence (S2).
     - Under a `clock_untrusted` hold the finalizer's guard samples the clock again: trusted and before `not_before`, it records `none`, wakes the loop and permits the intent; trusted and due, it records `due`, wakes the loop and refuses `activation_pending`; untrusted, it records nothing and refuses `clock_untrusted` (S3a; confirmed by its review).
     - An `ended` outcome this version does not know is terminal and not applied, under only the checks the frozen text states (D124), which supersedes the ruling's P12 sentence on other tokens (S3a). The request's state keeps that token, so `CancelActivation` answers `already_terminal` for it (S3b; the S3a review's finding).
     - Replay no longer refuses a request whose `admission_utc_ns` is below the floor: the frozen text makes a trusted admission sample a producer rule and states no such record rule. The floor still rises with that value (S3b; the S3a review's finding).
     - The native sharing row meets `appender.ErrUncertain`, which poisons the service by design: the row shows the log's bytes unchanged, the request absent after release and reopen, and the same-ID retry succeeding without rewriting the published bodies. This replaces the S1 review's "clean error" (S3b).
     - `TestOpenRefusesNewerConsumedVersions` lists the 18 consumed types from the frozen F2-05 to F2-09 sections, checks that the list equals the consumption map's keys and expects each row's exact `UnsupportedVersionError`, so deleting a type from the map cannot delete its row; this refines the test-cost review's "iterate the map's keys" (S3b).
     - The break "crash rows sharing one store" is shown by construction, each row's colony and store being its own, which the review checks, as the test-cost ruling did for the finalization crash rows; no shared store is built on purpose (S3b).
  5. **The test-cost work** (the owner's pause; the test-cost ruling).
     - **The machine.** The diagnosis found the Program Compatibility Assistant service using a whole processor thread even at idle; its restart hung until a second elevated script ended the service's process, once it was shown to host nothing else. Defender's exclusion of the build's temporary directory had shown no material gain and was removed; with process exclusions for Git for Windows' three `git.exe` files, three Git-heavy tests took a median 53.2 s against 63.1 s without, and Defender used 36–56 % of one thread against 94–105 %. The owner kept the exclusions. Before any test changed, the package's plain run alone fell from 775 s to 482 s; every later figure is on this machine state. The exclusion is his choice for the build's tests and selects none for the product.
     - **O1–O4**, test files only. O1: each fixture is a private copy (`os.CopyFS`) of a Git seed that real Git builds once per process and object format, lazily, so that crash-helper children build none; a history is copied while unregistered and cloned with `--no-hardlinks` once a service has registered it; and building a seed checks that inspecting a copy gives the seed's inspection. O2: a fixture takes the seed's inspection, its common directory rebound, only when its host files equal the seed's, and otherwise inspects for real; Register's own reinspection is unchanged, and no Git runner is shared. O3: the finalization crash rows run in parallel (item 7). O4: the two F2-07 rule-break matrices apply each mutation, producing and replaying, on one projection with undo, as F2-08's do, and keep a real Open for each distinct obligation; no row was dropped.
     - **Figures**, the whole package run alone, plain unless stated: 482.3 s after the machine changes; 479.3 s with O1; 440.3 s with O2; 361.3 s with O3 and O4, and 411.5 s under the race detector, against 892.3 s before the machine changes. Focused, the crash matrix took 22.0 s instead of 48.2 s, and the F2-07 matrices 17.3 s and 10.0 s instead of 24.9 s and 15.3 s; twenty runs of both crash tests under the race detector took 448 s. In the check without its race step the package took 385 s on 8fb6ef1, against 620 s on f8e2dbc. O1 and O2 saved 42 s, short of the ruling's estimates, which priced the slower launches before the machine changes; the combined saving, 121 s, met the ruling's 120 s floor. OPT-1's own race figure was not measured, by the project manager's run plan (D121), the combined figure standing for it. One test passes 60 s, `TestOpenFailsClosedOnF2_07MergeBackRuleBreak` at 63.6 s under the race detector: its history and the fifteen real Opens the ruling keeps.
     - **Not changed** (the ruling, O5, O7 and O8; O6, Defender, went to the owner as above): the check's `-parallel`, which is D42's and the owner's, the default eight having beaten four and sixteen by 53.4 s and 40.8 s in the diagnosis's plain sweep; any row; and no `-short`, per-test deadline, package split or test-only export. Fewer Git launches in the product stays deferred: its trigger, the package above the targets after OPT-2, did not fire. If raised, it is a product slice with a design note keeping each Git call's semantics and every inspection value byte-identical, since `capture-inputs/1` digests replay forever (B23 item 4).
     - **Kept and corrected.** `mergeback_test.go`'s materialized history keeps its clone and real inspection, since its rows set `core.autocrlf` on the clone's configuration. Two of the ruling's expected kills were wrong. Recovery deciding without re-reading `main` is caught by the `cas` row only, since the `intent` and `torn_intent` rows hold no durable intent. Deleting a type from the consumption map survived before and after OPT-2: Open reads with `AllTypes: true`, so only that map refuses a newer version, which the package documentation promises and no test covered; S3b's `TestOpenRefusesNewerConsumedVersions` now covers all 18 consumed types (item 4).
     - The project manager now runs each check in one fixed worktree without clearing Go's test cache, whose keys include the package's directory, so unchanged packages are served from it; each ticket's last code slice runs uncached (D121).
  6. **Amends B26 item 8: the per-row fixtures** (O1, O4). B26 item 8 copied each matrix's history per row with `--no-hardlinks` clones and ran every row in parallel. A row's history is now a private copy of an unregistered history, or a `--no-hardlinks` clone of a registered one, and the F2-07 rule-break matrices apply their mutations serially on one shared projection, as F2-08's do, beside the real Opens they keep in parallel.
  7. **Amends B27 item 7: parallel crash rows** (O3; D126). B27 item 7 ran each crash row one after another. `TestFinalizationCrashMatrix`'s eleven rows now run in parallel, each with its own repository, colony and store and the same two-minute handshake deadline, and `TestWindowsOwnedGitCrashBeforeRecovery` runs beside the package's other tests, as D126 planned. The matrix took 22.0 s instead of 48.2 s run alone.
  8. **The tests' cost** (D127).
     - In the checks, the package took 863 s and 964 s in the tests and race steps after S1 (8691b65), the race step above the 15-minute line that D126 kept, which led to D127; and, without the race step (D129), 620 s in the tests step after S3a (f8e2dbc) and 385 s after the test-cost work (8fb6ef1).
     - **Baseline.** In the full check on 245a032, uncached and with its race step, the package took 315 s in the tests step and 407 s in the race step, under D127's 25-minute report line; this replaces B27 item 7's baseline for later tickets, and the [ticket's evidence](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/ticket-09-evidence.md) lists the ten slowest tests under the race detector and every test over 60 s.
     - **Per-row cost.** B26 item 8's and B27 item 7's rules stand as items 6 and 7 amend them. Activation rows use the store only, with Git only where an intent is their subject, and spawn no helper process.
  9. **C2's gates** (the ruling's owner gates: C2's status changes at the close only once its roadmap prerequisites are met). With ticket 09, every C2 ticket, 05 to 09, is resolved. C2's section states no exit criterion of its own. Its gates are C4's condition that C2 be Done only after D52's approval, C1's F0 freeze, full-tree capture and bounded admission and finalization proof (`ROADMAP-CORE.md` C4), all met by D111, D112 and tickets 05–09 (B23–B28), and C2's mechanics and boundary scenarios, which those tickets build. What C2's section and its row in §3 also name belongs to other steps: every-change capacity under steady and burst load is C4's feasibility gate (ticket 23), accepted promotion with real validation is C4's (D52), containment and the stop proof are C10's, and the Linux and macOS runs wait for C3's CI, as C0's and C1's did. C2's status therefore changes in this commit, in `ROADMAP-CORE.md` and on the status board, and so does U3's, whose foundation waited only for C2 and the frozen `ledger-appender` (D111); C3 still waits for the owner's private remote with CI (D42), and C10 for C3.
- **Evidence:**
  - Ticket 09's closing comment lists each criterion's tests, from the ruling's acceptance coverage.
  - The build advisor approved S2 at 93b76a4 with two nits that needed no change, with D124's freeze documents once two minor additions were applied; S1 at d85ed78 with one minor finding and one nit, carried to S3a, rejecting the D126 commit beneath it until 8691b65 moved its amendment line; S3a at a76866d with D127–D129 and the race flag, with two minor findings and two nits carried to S3b and one nit on `tools/check` that 50026d5 settles; OPT-1 at 36d0c51 and OPT-2 at 8fb6ef1 with one minor finding and one nit, carried to S3b; and S3b: approved by the build advisor at 245a032 with 50026d5 on 2026-10-17, with no findings on the code and one nit on B28 item 3, applied in this commit. All nine steps of the full check passed on 7a6624b, which carried S2, D124 and D125 with ticket 08's S3b, on 8691b65 and on 245a032, and the check without its race step (D129) on f8e2dbc and 8fb6ef1, each the commit `main` moved to (D46).
  - Forty-nine deliberate breaks of S2's decoder rules, twelve rule breaks while S1 was built, fifteen while S3a was, six while OPT-1 was and ten while S3b was each failed named tests, and the restored code passed; of OPT-2's seven, six failed named tests and one, the consumption gap of item 5, survived on the trees before and after it. `FuzzDecodeF2_09` and `FuzzDecodeF2_08` ran 2,660,267 and 4,678,101 executions without a failure. The ticket's evidence names each break and its failing tests, with every slice's checks.
  - D78, labelled: *assumption*, activation is the owner's occasional approval of one exact version, immediate or one-off, with no recurring scheduler (D65–D67; `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C2), not work per change; *calculation*, a request that applies appends four records in three commits, its request and pin, one `due` hold and the switch, a replacement adds one ending to its request's commit, and holds are written only when they change; admission reads and hashes each approved body once, each at most `MaxBodyBytes`, and derives an affected set linear in the configured workspaces; the switch adds no Git command or validation to finalization and waits only for the intents already recorded in the affected workspaces, each one finalization sequence (B27's D78 calculation); reading back an identity reads one commit, scanning from the start of its segment, bounded by the segment's size (the design brief §5; the ruling, P13); *measurement*, none, as ticket 09 adds no workload phase; *plan*, the switch's wait under contention, with real validation, is part of C4's feasibility gate (`ROADMAP-CORE.md` C4; ticket 23).
- **Limits:**
  - Nothing is frozen or approved here: F2-09 was frozen by D124, the service writes it as frozen, and `CONTRACTS.md` is unchanged.
  - Promotion stays unavailable until C4's real validator, and harness work stays testing-only (D52): an applied activation admits no promotion, its check and environment bodies have identity, not meaning, and their use is C4's. Callers state their actor until C3 authenticates them, and revoking an approval is C3's, through F3.
  - One machine: verified on Windows, on NTFS, on this machine. Linux and macOS are compiled and vetted only, and their rows first run in C3's CI. The test-cost figures are single runs of the build's tests in the owner's machine state, not product capacity; Core's rule that capacity is measured with Defender on and no exclusion (Core §3.5) is unchanged.
- **Changes:**
  - The code is `internal/core/workspace` as S1, S3a and S3b built it, with the tests OPT-1 and OPT-2 changed; `contract/ledger` as S2 built it and D124 froze it; and `tools/check`'s `-race=false` flag (D129) and per-step durations (50026d5). Ticket 09's comments name the commits.
  - Documents: in `DECISIONS.md`, this entry and "Amended by B28" lines under B26 and B27; in Core, one line in A.14's Limits; in `ROADMAP-CORE.md`, C2's Status and its row in §3; on the status board in `ROADMAPS.md`, the C2 and U3 rows; in `ROADMAP-UNDERCURRENT.md`, U3's Status; ticket 09's status, criteria, Answer and comments; and under `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c2-evidence/`, the design brief, the ruling, the test-cost diagnosis and ruling, and the evidence `ticket-09-evidence.md`.
- **Amended by D131:** item 5's fixed check worktree stays, for Go's build cache, but the check no longer uses Go's test cache: the tests and race steps run every test with `-count=1`, since validating and storing cached results cost more than they saved.
- **Amended by B30:** item 9's "C10 for C3" is C10's wait for its integration and A2 qualification, not for the start of its executors: ticket 45 began after C2 under its own gate (B30 item 1).

### D130. F-U3-61 graph analysis records frozen

- **Date:** 2026-10-17. **Decided by:** assistant. Under D125, the project manager froze F-U3-61 without a question to the owner, once the build advisor had approved ticket 61's S2 together with this record and its evidence file. The build advisor's design ruling for ticket 61 (its P5), which lands under `.scratch/dwarf-harness/u3-evidence/` with the ticket's close, named the family and its namespace and left this freeze to the project manager. No freeze packet was written.
- **Decision:** F-U3-61, ticket 61's part and U3's first producer family, is frozen by addition to `ledger-format` version 1 (D40, D111, D125), at commit ba8aa33, whose `contract/ledger` reached `main` unchanged in ba8aa33:
  - four record types, each at version 1, in the namespace `graph.`, now reserved to U3's record families: `graph.analysis_started`, an analysis attempt over a workspace's accepted tree against the current graph version; `graph.analysis_progress`, the attempt's work reservations, its checkpoints over durably committed chunks and its ending `unknown` or `superseded`; `graph.delta_chunk`, an immutable chunk of the attempt's net node, edge and coverage mutations; and `graph.version_committed`, which makes the attempt's chunks one graph version or confirms the current one. `Defined()` lists the twenty-four type versions of F0, F2-05 to F2-09 and F-U3-61;
  - their value shapes, decoder rules and record rules as the F-U3-61 section of the `contract/ledger` package documentation states them: every key required, integers as plain decimals, arrays in strict orders that exclude duplicates, the closed `phase` and coverage `state` tokens, the cross-field rules, such as a zero `base_version` exactly when `base_digest` is `EmptyGraphDigest`, and a zero `chunk_count` exactly when `chunks_sha256` is `EmptyChunksDigest` and `graph_digest` equals `base_digest`, and the record rules: actor `core`, `operation_id` equal to `analysis_id`, `measurement_path` `graph.analysis`, no body references, and the commit shapes;
  - the open tokens, node kinds, namespaces, adapters, edge types, geometries, analysis and coverage reasons and scopes, whose unknown values readers accept, with their version-1 writer values, and the two profile identifiers `engine-limits/1` and `graph-helper-limits/1`;
  - `EdgeID`, the SHA-256 of the compact JSON array of an edge's from key, to key and type, so that new evidence keeps an edge's ID; `EmptyGraphDigest`, the SHA-256 of `{"scopes":[],"nodes":[],"edges":[],"coverage":[]}`; and `EmptyChunksDigest`, the SHA-256 of no bytes;
  - the effective-version rule, under which an eligible `graph.version_committed` with chunks makes its own envelope sequence the effective graph version, and the zero-net confirmation rule, under which one without chunks keeps `base_version` current, records the rebuild and resets the counter;
  - the canonical state that `graph_digest` hashes: the scopes, nodes, edges and coverage in the documented orders, with nothing operational in it;
  - the golden sample `contract/ledger/testdata/u3/61/log/00000000000000000001.jsonl` (SHA-256 6c7169aa9cfe77bb8976348f515ef85ec8da82b277520f82fd2bf169e6c45e0f, 41 commits, 62 records); the F0 and F2-05 to F2-09 golden samples keep D111's, D115's, D118's, D119's, D123's and D124's digests.
- **Not frozen:** where the record rules are enforced: by `internal/undercurrent/graph` when it appends and at Open, which ticket 61's S3 builds, never by `Check`, `Reader`, `EncodeCommit` or the codecs; the exhaustion key; the `analysis_key` tuple, a service computation; the replay rules that derive `structural_since_rebuild` from the prefix and supersede a stale final record, which ticket 63 may narrow (the design ruling's P9); the 512 KiB chunk calibration; the node-key tuple structures per kind; the law vocabulary a field kind traverses; and every later part of this family: new node kinds, adapters, edge types, geometries, reasons and scopes join by addition in their own freeze records.
- **Consequences:** these types now change only by addition (D40): new values of their open tokens, which need no new version, and new types or type versions, each in its own freeze record. A breaking change needs an owner decision entry listing every step it affects (D40, D125). Ticket 61's S2 reaches `main` with this entry, the freeze being its D41 sign-off (D125), and S3 may now write these records. `ledger-format` stays at version 1, and no step's status changes. Nothing here is accepted promotion, engine capability or U5 readiness.
- **Evidence:** the [evidence file](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/u3-evidence/f-u3-61-freeze-evidence.md) gives each check's command and result.
  - The golden sample's SHA-256 above is the committed blob's. `EmptyGraphDigest` is 50615e395eebec3556dbfd7bb8684d2c65e3aee8810b9d17cbfb157c30471f91 and `EmptyChunksDigest` e3b0c44298fc1c149afbf4c8996fb92427ae41e4649b934ca495991b7852b855, as the tests print them.
  - V2, the package tests: PASS: `contract/ledger` in 8.5 s and `internal/core/ledgerstore` in 24.7 s, and `contract/ledger` in 35.8 s under the race detector; every seed corpus passed. V3, `FuzzDecodeU3_61` for 120 s: PASS: 4,336,133 executions of `FuzzDecodeU3_61` in 120 s; no new corpus file. V8, the earlier goldens regenerated: PASS: each earlier golden regenerates byte for byte, matching the digests of D111, D115, D118, D119, D123 and D124. V4, the check: `check PASSED: 8 of 9 steps passed, race skipped (D129)` in 752.5 s, on this commit before this result was written in, a Markdown-only change (D128).
  - The build advisor's review: The build advisor approved S2 at ba8aa33 on 2026-10-17 together with this record and its evidence file, with one minor comment edit on the code (the `go` adapter named among the version-1 writer values) and two minor findings on the record applied: the frozen record rules and the unfrozen replay rules named in this entry, and U3 added to the F2-06 consumers cell of `workspace.promotion_admitted`.
- **Changes:** `CONTRACTS.md` (the `ledger-format` row's text, owning step and status; the B2 table's U3 row; the F-U3-61 catalogue; the F2-06 table's `workspace.promotion_admitted` consumers); the `contract/ledger` comments that called F-U3-61 proposed (the package documentation's Catalogue paragraph and F-U3-61 section, `Defined`, the type, token and profile constants, and five test comments); two wording edits in that section, naming `go` among the version-1 adapter values and the encoder's string escaping in `EdgeID`'s construction; the graph-delta row of Undercurrent §15.1; the evidence file `.scratch/dwarf-harness/u3-evidence/f-u3-61-freeze-evidence.md`; a "See also" line under D125.

### B29. Art round 2 of ticket 137: a highlight step on large 64-px surfaces and lighter fur

- **Date:** 2026-10-17. **Decided by:** adopted. In the round's sheet review (D104 item 3), the owner picked "Approve" from the assistant's two options; its description read "Merge round 2 once its check passes (running now), and record it in a B entry". The question pointed him to both 4x sheets, the page previews and close-ups, and summarised the round's two changes, which carry out his D120 answers on F8 and F4c. The pick covers the sheets, the pages and the merge, and with them the round's generator code. The ramp design, rule texts, palette values and pixels below are the art agent's proposal as he approved it.
- **Owner's words (question tool, 2026-10-17):** "Approve"
- **Question:** D120 item 4 gives this round two findings of round 1: F8, a fourth step for the three-step colour ramps of large 64-px surfaces, and F4c, the dog's fur, which is close to the floor in brightness. It says what to change, but not which ramps gain the step, whether the step is lighter or darker than theirs, where it goes in a palette with 3 of its 40 entries free, how the generator admits it, where a form carries it, or what the fur's new values are. How does round 2 settle them?
- **Decision:**
  1. **The highlight** (F8). Skin, hair and clothing, the three-step colour ramps of the large 64-px surfaces, gain a fourth, lightest step, the highlight. Role and Corps ramps keep two steps; wood, metal, stone, floor, fur and coat keep three. In `colors.txt` each skin, hair and clothing choice keeps its three colours and gains a fourth, 12–40 luma above its light step and leaning toward yellow: skin fair fadcb0, tan ecbc88 and deep c48c5c; hair auburn e08c48, black 767080 and grey e4e2e0; clothing blue 98b4d8, brown bc9864, red dc7462 and grey b6b4b4.
  2. **Palette** (amends B19 item 7's 37 entries). `palette.txt` appends `!` `skin.highlight` c48c5c, `@` `hair.highlight` e08c48 and `=` `clothing.highlight` 98b4d8 at indices 37–39, and holds 40 entries, STYLE.md's cap. Because they are appended, each mask's entries still run from shadow to light in index order (skin 4, 5, 6, 37; hair 7, 8, 9, 38; clothing 10, 11, 12, 39), and no approved world pixel changes index, as when B19 item 7 added the Corps entries.
  3. **Generator** (D104 item 3). A mask's ramp has two to four entries instead of two or three, a bound tied to the constant `highlightStep` in `input.go`. The fourth entry may appear only in the 64-px views: an overworld map that uses it fails at its row, so the approved 16-px sprites keep three steps. `doc.go` says the same. `sample.json` stays `dwarf-harness.art-sample.v2` (B19 item 10): its skin, hair and clothing masks list four indices and their choices four colours, with no key or schema change.
  4. **Rules.** `tools/artgen/STYLE.md` changes in three passages:
     - In the lighting rule, top and left surfaces take "the light step of their ramp", no longer the lightest, and: "On the 64 px views the highlight marks where a large form's top and left surfaces meet, the part that faces the light most squarely."
     - The ramp rule adds: "Skin, hair and clothing have a fourth, lightest step, the highlight, for the large surfaces of the 64 px views; the 16 px sprites keep three steps, and the generator rejects the highlight there." A ramp's digit is now "the light step", and "the highlights are ! (skin), @ (hair) and = (clothing)."
     - Outline and shading gains: "At 64 px, large forms of skin, hair and clothing shade in four steps from the light: highlight, light, base, shadow. The highlight sits where a form's top and left surfaces meet, inside its upper-left outline: on the left part of a lit top row wider than two pixels, tapering to the outer column of its lit strip on the upper rows. On the face it marks only the top of the nose. Each highlight touches the light step; it never runs a whole edge or round the outline, and never marks a small form such as fingers, cuffs, braids, the ear or a lit strip one pixel wide."
  5. **Drawing.** 902 hand-placed highlight pixels lie in 72 frames of 18 maps: the front still and the seven three-quarter frames of `back_accessory.pack`, `body.stout`, `hair_beard.braided`, `hair_beard.topknot`, `headwear.hat`, `legwear.trousers`, `shoes.boots`, `torso_clothing.tunic` and `torso_clothing.vest`. Each turns a light-step pixel into its highlight (index 6 to 37, 9 to 38, 12 to 39) at a large form's upper-left crest: the head's dome, the top of the nose, the shoulder and arm, the chest's edge, the hair cap, the bun, the moustache's top, the beard's edge, the hat's crown and brim, the pack's top, the boot's shaft and heel, and the vest's and trousers' edges. Placement follows each form, not the outfit: the highlights on the trousers' hip and the boot shaft are covered in all four sample outfits and show only in their part cells, while the boots' heel (rows 58–59) shows in every outfit. Boots and trousers stay identical in all seven frames (B20 item 7, planted rows).
  6. **Fur** (F4c). The fur ramp `D`, `d`, `8` goes from 4a3020, 7a5232, a87a4e to 7c5236, ac7c48, d4a868, and its luma from 54, 90, 131 to 91, 132, 174, against the floor's 67, 93, 123. The dog's base now sits 39 luma above the floor's. The horse's mane, drawn in `D`, lightens with it. No rule text changes.
  7. **Interpretations**, for later rounds and ticket 108:
     - **"Every colour choice."** D120 item 4 said the fourth step changes every colour choice. It changes the skin, hair and clothing choices only: role and Corps choices keep two colours, and B19 item 9's Corps guards and thresholds are unchanged.
     - **A full palette.** A new entry, in a later round or in ticket 108, needs the owner to raise STYLE.md's 40-entry cap, or an entry to be freed.
     - **Authoring aids** (B20 item 7). `apply_highlights.py`, a throwaway script outside the repository, wrote the highlight pixels from `highlights.spec`, a hand-written list of every pixel, and repeated a frame's listed pixels on frames whose rows are shifted copies, after checking that each target held the same light pixel. `analyze_frames.py` only reported those shifts, and `sheetcheck.go` and `runlog.py` made the evidence and its logs. Every committed frame is explicit text, the art agent reviewed every changed row, nothing in the repository runs the scripts, and all of them except the spec are deleted. The review question did not mention them: the owner was told in the project manager's report of 2026-10-17, after his review.
- **Reasons:**
  - **Up, not down.** The ramps' darkest steps already sit near the outline (hair.black's shadow is only 13 luma above it), so the new step goes above the light.
  - **Three ramps.** F8 named the large 64-px surfaces, which skin, hair and clothing cover. Wood and metal cover small 64-px parts, and steps for them would exceed the cap.
  - **Appended entries.** Appending keeps every approved index, so index pixels change only in the 18 highlighted strips, and the dog and the horse change colour only.
  - **16 px.** Rejecting the highlight in the overworld keeps the approved 16-px sprites at three steps; F8 concerned the 64-px surfaces.
  - **Sparing placement.** Under STYLE.md's one light from the upper left, a form faces it most squarely where its top and left surfaces meet; along an edge or round the outline the highlight would be the pillow shading STYLE.md forbids. At 902 pixels it reads clearly at 4x and subtly at 1x, and how far it spreads was a point of the owner's sheet review.
  - **Fur.** The old fur base sat 3 luma below the floor's base; the new one sits 39 above it.
- **Limits:**
  - No module (D29) and no format change (item 3). Against 88ca88f, the round's baseline, `index.tsv` is byte-identical and no pixel outside a cell changed. On the map sheet the 7 animal cells and the room changed; its 16 tiles, 36 parts and 14 cells each of roles, roles-varied and roles-gray are unchanged, so every role keeps its trim and badge (ticket 137 criterion 2; D109). On the character sheet 9 of 13 front cells, 63 of 94 three-quarter cells and every roster, roster-gray and page cell changed; the 8 corps cells, the pick, the pennant, the emblems and the tool are unchanged.
  - The approved round is commit be1da70, drawn from 88ca88f and rebased onto 01cbada, with `contentSha256` 1297923a3527535b4a87d6a6ddefbe2db232a5b8117457e28ea2786d2c773267. The generator's `sourceSha256` is now f36c553a0a8fa7910e87cf9d187dcf9d72e8b947d0660ae1266812cecb275912, because its code changed; the toolchain is go1.27.2. The owner's answer and the sheet digests are under ticket 137's Comments.
  - Evidence: the generator's tests pass, plainly and under the race detector, including `TestSampleIsReproducible`, which compares the committed sample with a fresh generation (criterion 3), and a new case in which a skin highlight in a world map fails at its row; gofmt, vet, staticcheck and the Linux and macOS vets report nothing. The check on be1da70: `check PASSED: 8 of 9 steps passed, race skipped (D129)` in 309.4 s. No build advisor reviewed the round; the owner's sheet review gated it (D104).
  - Nothing is frozen. `sample.json` v2 stays a draft for ticket 107, and ticket 108 still owns the full pack.
  - F5, F6 and F7 are closed, kept as approved (D120 item 4). With F4c and F8 done, none of the findings that B20 left to later rounds remains open.
  - No contract freeze, readiness advancement or passed runtime gate is claimed.
- **Changes:** `tools/artgen`:
  - `input.go`, `input_test.go` and `doc.go`;
  - `STYLE.md`, in three passages;
  - `art/palette.txt` (the highlight entries, the fur ramp and the legend comment) and `art/colors.txt` (a fourth colour for ten choices);
  - in `art/rpg/front/` and `art/rpg/three_quarter/`, the nine parts of item 5;
  - the regenerated `sample/`: every strip, both sheets at 1x and 4x, the eight previews, `sample.json` and `provenance.json`.

  In all, 18 maps and 72 frames.

### D131. The check runs every test, without Go's test cache

- **Date:** 2026-10-17. **Decided by:** assistant. Under D121, the project manager changed the check after measuring where its tests step spent its time.
- **Context:** In the slice checks of D130, ticket 61's S1 and ticket 45's S1, the tests step took 705–1,056 s while its slowest package, `internal/core/workspace`, ran for 265–394 s. On the same tree, `go test -json -count=1 ./...` took 418 s, the package's own time. With caching on, a test binary logs every file, directory and environment variable it touches, and `go test` re-checks each entry, to validate a cached result and again to store a new one. `internal/core/workspace` logs 863,700 entries (49.7 MB). On this Windows machine, `go test ./internal/core/workspace` took 293 s to report `(cached)`, re-checking them. Packages whose tests open `%TEMP%`, whose listing changes between runs, never hit the cache and pay twice.
- **Decision:** `tools/check` runs its tests and race steps with `-count=1`: no test log, no cached results, every test every time. B28 item 5's fixed check worktree stays, for Go's build cache.
- **Evidence:** the full check on 3eb4754, which made the change, passed all nine steps in 974.1 s. The tests step took 403.9 s, with `internal/core/workspace` at 401.1 s; the race step took 546.0 s, with that package at 514.8 s and `tools/artgen` at 426.4 s. The full check on 245a032 (B28 item 8) took 1,087.5 s, its tests step 497.4 s and its race step 573.6 s. The measurements are the project manager's, on 2026-10-17.
- **Limits:** No step is removed or weakened. Every check now runs every package's tests, so a check's tests step lasts about as long as its slowest package, currently `internal/core/workspace`, even when a change touches one other package. D129's uncached run for each ticket's last code slice now holds for every check.
- **Amends:** B28 item 5.
- **Changes:** this entry and the "Amended by D131" line under B28; `tools/check/main.go` and `tools/check/race.go`.

### B30. C10: the Windows containment executor (ticket 45)

- **Date:** 2026-10-18. **Decided by:** assistant. Under D117, the build advisor's [ruling](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c10-evidence/ticket-45-design-ruling.md) on the [design brief](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c10-evidence/ticket-45-design-brief.md) ended ticket 45's design step without a question to the owner. The gate ruling it carries as §0 settled the ticket's start gate, and its §1–§5 the design. Its §6–§8, written while S1 was built, are the revisions that the [checkpoint probes](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c10-evidence/ticket-45-checkpoint-probes.md) and their [second round](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c10-evidence/ticket-45-checkpoint-probes-2.md) required. The reconciliation in item 1, the build's choices and the project manager's answers during the build are the assistant's, within the authority the owner delegated, and settle nothing outside the ruled design. The owner was asked no question in this ticket.
- **Question:** Ticket 45 builds C10's Windows containment executor in `internal/core/winexec`. The executor runs real test processes in a restricted process tree and proves the tree closed before it reuses anything, before C3's runtime or C4's adapters exist to call it. `ROADMAP-CORE.md` and B28 item 9 say that C10 waits for C3; the ticket says that it can begin after C2. Which gate applies, what did the build settle, and what does it change in earlier entries?
- **Decision:**
  1. **The start gate** (the gate ruling, P1–P5; amends B28 item 9). Ticket 45 began after C2 under its own gate, "Can begin with real test processes after C2; claims neither C3 integration nor A2 until later qualification". C10's wait for C3 in `ROADMAP-CORE.md` is the step's integration and qualification wait, carried by tickets 16, 46 and 91, not a start gate for its executors. C10's in-layer interfaces, its request, receipt, output channel and quarantine fence, are Core-internal code, not contracts (D40, D45; the gate ruling, P1 and P2). Ticket 45 writes no ledger record. Whichever ticket first needs a durable executor fact, C4's ticket 46 or C3's ticket 91, specifies it under `CONTRACTS.md`'s rules. This commit clarifies C10's Waits-for line and its row in §3 of `ROADMAP-CORE.md` to say so; the status board's "Waits for" stays C2. D27, D87 and D112 are unchanged, and no owner decision is amended.
  2. **The design as ruled and qualified** (P6–P14, the ruling's findings F1–F8 and revisions §6–§8). The package documentation, `internal/core/winexec/doc.go`, states the executor as built; the brief and the ruling give each choice's options and sources. The choices as qualified on this machine:
     - One Core-internal package with a synchronous `Run` and one slot per root; other systems get `ErrUnsupported` (P6).
     - The profile is a regular AppContainer with zero capabilities and a fresh SID per run (P14 as amended; §7). LPAC, the brief's stricter profile, was qualified first and rejected on Windows 10 19045: with zero capabilities `WSAStartup` fails with 10107, and Go's file I/O fails with it.
     - The launch is suspended, with three attributes: the security capabilities, the explicit handle list and `JOB_LIST`. The process is therefore born in the per-run non-breakaway Job, with `DETACHED_PROCESS` and no breakaway flag (P7). No ancestor ACEs are added, since the user holds `SeChangeNotifyPrivilege` (§6).
     - The environment block is `SystemRoot`, `WINDIR`, `LOCALAPPDATA`, `TEMP`, `TMP` and `DWARF_WORK`, and the working directory is `input/` (§6.1). An AppContainer launch needs the host's real `LOCALAPPDATA`, which Windows rewrites, with `TEMP` and `TMP`, into the run's profile folder.
     - Before resume the executor reads back token classes 29, 30 and 31, `IsProcessInJob` and the Job's limits; class 46 cannot be queried on this build (§6.2).
     - Positive closure: the root is signalled, its exit captured and owned references closed, then the retained Job must report zero active processes within `StopGrace`. A kill request, root exit or pipe EOF is never closure (P11). A run that exited naturally keeps its stop grace, whatever arrives later (the S1 review's finding).
     - A private durable fence under the run root is written before any native effect and closed only after that proof. Open quarantines anything that is not a valid closed fence and never reruns a request (P10).
     - The caller supplies a complete finite profile. Each bound maps to one Win32 control and one refusal, and none defaults to unlimited (P8, P9).
  3. **The project manager's answers and the findings that set behavior**, each reviewed with its slice; ticket 45's comments give their order.
     - S1's checkpoint: `CreateProcess` failed with 203 under LPAC, and probes traced it to the child's environment, not to LPAC or `JOB_LIST` (§6). Go's file I/O then failed under LPAC; probes showed the Winsock failure, and §7 applied P14's switch.
     - An observer test's `ERROR_INVALID_HANDLE` was the test's own handle lifetime, not a native boundary. The observer duplicates the Job, and the count of active processes is recorded per path without a universal assertion: 0 after termination, and 1 after a natural exit while the observer's handle is open.
     - x/sys's race wrapper for `windows.ReadFile` dereferences its count pointer unconditionally, so the executor passes a kept non-nil count cell.
     - Under the race detector a Go grandchild died at ThreadSanitizer start (error 487). After §8's two probes, the rows that need only a held descendant use a System32 `cmd.exe` grandchild in both builds. The race build proves the host executor; the plain run proves the payload rows (§8).
     - In S2 the Job's limit readback was once refused before resume. The native structures that crossed x/sys's `uintptr` wrappers were on the stack; they are now pinned with `runtime.Pinner` and moved to the heap, as `-gcflags=-m` shows. `verifyJob`'s error now names the actual and expected fields. The refusal is recorded as fixed by the pinning, but it was not conclusively attributed to it.
     - S2 refuses duplicate and ASCII-case-colliding input paths before any effect, and drops the run SID's grant on the pipes (the S1 review's findings).
     - Each fix during S2's final runs was approved: a test's `unsafe.Pointer` misuse became `WriteProcessMemory` and `ReadProcessMemory`, staticcheck's ST1008 was fixed, and a supervisor row's startup race now waits for both readiness acknowledgements. 6e168e6 routes a test observer's three Job queries through the pinned `queryJob` (the S2 review's finding).
     - The tests' cleanup removed only test-owned objects, by their exact names: seven profiles and eight temporary roots after S1's crashed race run, and 45 profiles in S2's fixture teardown. The product deletes only its own bound profile, after proof.
  4. **The second importer of `golang.org/x/sys`** (the gate ruling, P4). `internal/core/winexec` imports `golang.org/x/sys/windows`. D45's table admits that import throughout `internal/core/...`, and B11 records it for Core OS adapters. `internal/core/durable/durable.go`'s comment no longer calls that package the only importer. `tools/check` is unchanged, no module is added (D29), and there is no cgo (D28).
- **Evidence:**
  - Ticket 45's closing comment lists each criterion's tests, from the S1 and S2 reports.
  - The build advisor approved S1 at 1ed46a5 on 2026-10-17 with one major finding, the relabel of an already-exited run as timed out, canceled or shut down when the wall, the caller's deadline or Close arrives during its drain or cleanup, carried to S2 with its regression test, two minor findings and two nits. A fresh reviewer had been stopped by Anthropic's cyber-content filter. He approved S2 at 77b9968 on 2026-10-18 with no product findings, one minor finding on a test observer's unpinned Job queries, fixed in 6e168e6, and three nits. The S1 stop-grace defect is fixed with its three-row regression. Every §6.2 row observes real native state: handle snapshots, Job PID lists, real Git and ledger targets, supervisor kills, retained-Job ambiguity, sharing-blocked renames and fifteen corrupt fences. The enforcement-readback refusal is fixed by pinning the buffers that cross x/sys uintptr wrappers.
  - The check without its race step (D129) passed on 01cbada in 1,070.5 s; its tests step was slowed by Go's test cache, which D131 then removed. The full check, with its race step, passed on 6e168e6, the commit `main` moved to by fast-forward (`check PASSED: all 9 steps passed in 749.2s`).
  - Seven deliberate rule breaks were made, three while S1 was built and four while S2 was, and the restored code passed. Six failed named tests. The seventh, the removal of a pin, shows in the compiler's escape analysis, not as a runtime failure. `FuzzFenceDecode` ran 4,129,250 executions in 120 s on 6e168e6 without a failure. The [ticket's evidence](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c10-evidence/ticket-45-evidence.md) names each break, its tests and its logs, and gives the measurements and the machine state verbatim.
  - D78, labelled:
    - *Assumption:* one explicit local test call per observed run, made with one copied test executable and one held `cmd.exe` descendant. Each run has a finite exchange and one private scratch file, with no provider or model work.
    - *Calculation:* retained bytes are bounded by the declared input and output budgets, with at most three host pumps holding 98,304 bytes of buffers, and one Job and one profile per admitted slot.
    - *Measurement:* run alone, setup took 0.73–0.80 s plain and 0.93–0.95 s under the race detector, dominated by profile creation and by staging the 6.3 MB or 8.9 MB executable. An ordinary execution took about 50 ms, and a stop 14–24 ms.
    - *Plan:* declared-workload throughput, admission economics, other C4 environments and qualification are ticket 46's.

    No figure is a capacity or a shipping default.
- **Limits:**
  - Nothing is frozen, approved or claimed here. There is no contract or ledger record, and `CONTRACTS.md` is unchanged. The ticket claims no A2 label and no capacity, no C3 integration (tickets 16 and 91) and no C4 receipt or V6 matrix (ticket 46).
  - The boundary is the regular AppContainer's: the token holds ALL APPLICATION PACKAGES (`S-1-15-2-1`), so any object that grants it is reachable. Core's protected objects must never grant it.
  - The child's environment shows the host's LocalAppData path, and with it the user name.
  - A handle held outside the Job to an exited descendant keeps the Job's count of active processes above zero until that handle is closed. A persistent third-party hold therefore quarantines the run. The product holds none, and `StopGrace` should be sized for scanners.
  - Scratch and profile storage are measured, not quota-enforced. The receipt's peak memory figures are native Job and process counters, not RAM.
  - The fence survives a process crash, not a power loss.
  - One machine: Windows 10 Pro 19045 on NTFS. Linux and macOS compile, vet and refuse with `ErrUnsupported`. The figures are build-test evidence under Defender with the owner's `git.exe` exclusions (B28 item 5), not product capacity.
- **Changes:**
  - The code is `internal/core/winexec` as S1 (01cbada) and S2 (77b9968) built it, with 6e168e6's test change, and the comment in `internal/core/durable/durable.go`.
  - Documents:
    - in `DECISIONS.md`, this entry and the "Amended by B30" line under B28;
    - in `ROADMAP-CORE.md`, C10's Waits-for and Status lines and its row in §3;
    - in `ROADMAPS.md`, C10's row on the status board;
    - ticket 45's status, criteria, Answer and comments, and a comment on ticket 46;
    - under `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c10-evidence/`, C10's evidence home: the design brief, the ruling, the checkpoint probes `ticket-45-checkpoint-probes.md` and `ticket-45-checkpoint-probes-2.md`, and the evidence `ticket-45-evidence.md`.

### D132. Go dependency resolution: `golang.org/x/tools/go/packages` in a separate analyzer helper

- **Date:** 2026-10-19. **Decided by:** adopted. In OG-GO, the question that ticket 61 sent once its S4 had landed, the owner picked "Approve the go/packages helper" from the assistant's three options; the packet had recommended another, the toolchain's own `go list` decoded in the graph. The build advisor's design ruling for ticket 61 (§4, P8) named the question, and his review of the packet added that alternative. The ruling and the packet, `og-go-packet.md`, land under `.scratch/dwarf-harness/u3-evidence/` with the ticket's close.
- **Owner's words (question tool):**
  > Approve the go/packages helper
- **Question:** Ticket 61's graph reports Go files as unknown, with reason `dependency_unapproved`. The Undercurrent design, its roadmap and the ticket said that Go resolution would use `golang.org/x/tools/go/packages` only with the owner's D29 approval. D29 requires a decision entry for a `golang.org/x/*` module and keeps Undercurrent packages to the standard library, and D45's import check rejects a package tree without a row. Does the owner approve that package in a separate Go analyzer helper program, with that program's row, or should the graph run the toolchain's own `go list` and decode its output itself?
- **Decision:**
  1. **The dependency (D29).** Module `golang.org/x/tools` v0.50.0, which `go.mod` already requires for the check's tools (B17), is admitted for one package, `golang.org/x/tools/go/packages`, in one program, the Go analyzer helper. That package runs the `go` command's `go list` and reads its output; the resolution itself is the `go` command's. The helper's build compiles packages of three modules only: `golang.org/x/tools` v0.50.0, `golang.org/x/sync` v0.23.0 and `golang.org/x/mod` v0.41.0, all already in `go.sum`. Core §8.2 holds the record. No other package of the module and no other module is admitted, and Undercurrent and Core keep their import rules.
  2. **The program and its row (D45).** The helper is the main package `helpers/go-analyzer`, a separate executable, in a new top-level tree `helpers/` for D29's separate helper programs. Its row: `helpers/go-analyzer` may import, besides the standard library, `golang.org/x/tools/go/packages` only. Each further helper program needs its own row. The helper is built with the pinned toolchain under `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`, `CGO_ENABLED=0`, an empty `GOFLAGS` and `-trimpath`. The graph runs it only by absolute path after verifying its SHA-256, as it runs its Python and Node helpers. It is a third program that the harness builds and ships, beside D39's two; the Go resolver slice adds its build to the harness's.
  3. **Inputs and bounds.** The helper speaks the graph's private `graph-helper/v1` protocol under the Python and TypeScript helpers' limits: one direct child at a time, 30 s, 8 MiB each way, 1 MiB per file, 10,000 files and 64 KiB of error text, with work charged before launch (the ruling's P6 and P7). Its input is the immutable files of one accepted tree, which the graph writes into its private temporary folder. Nothing located outside that tree enters the graph: a package that `go list` places outside the tree, or an import that needs one, is reported unknown. The verified toolchain's own standard packages are the exception: `go list` marks them `Standard` inside the toolchain's root, which is pinned configuration, so an import of one resolves, creates no node or edge, and does not by itself make the file unknown. The environment does not ensure this alone, since a `go.mod` `replace` naming a folder makes `go list` read files there; the Go resolver slice enforces the rule and proves it with such a `replace`. Nothing is downloaded.
  4. **Bound configuration.** The helper gives `go/packages` a complete environment. It names one verified `go` toolchain by absolute path, SHA-256 and version, whose `bin` folder is the helper's only `PATH` entry. It sets `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`, `GOENV=off`, `GOWORK=off`, `GOPROXY=off`, `GOPACKAGESDRIVER=off`, an empty `GOFLAGS`, `CGO_ENABLED=0` and a fixed `GOOS` and `GOARCH`, and puts `GOPATH`, `GOMODCACHE` and `GOCACHE` in empty folders inside the private temporary folder. Its load mode asks only for package names, files, imports, dependencies and modules: no syntax trees, type checking, export data or tests. All of it, with the helper's and the toolchain's digests, enters the graph's configuration digest, so any change is a new analyzer epoch.
  5. **Descendants.** The helper's only children are `go` commands of that toolchain, which `go/packages` starts, waits for and stops at its deadline: with an interrupt and, after 5 s, a kill where the operating system allows interrupts, and with a kill at once on Windows. The helper's deadline falls inside the graph's, so it normally ends its `go` commands and exits by itself. If the graph has to kill the helper, the process tree counts as unconfirmed: the graph starts no further analysis and keeps the temporary folder until the host restarts, and Go reports unknown, as for any child whose exit is unconfirmed. The Go resolver slice must show, by observing the process tree, that `go list` starts no process of its own in this configuration; until it does, Go stays unknown.
- **Not decided here:** the helper's code and tests, the graph's Go adapter and the row's encoding in `tools/check`, which ticket 61's Go resolver slice builds under a ruled design (D117). No contract changes, since `go` is already a version-1 adapter value of F-U3-61 (D130). The approval adds no operating-system CPU or memory limit and no isolation claim (the ruling's P7), no `go/parser` substitute, no building or running of analyzed code, no cgo and no network access.
- **Consequences:** Go dependency coverage may now be built. Until the Go resolver slice lands, Go files stay unknown with reason `dependency_unapproved`, and no other ticket waits for it.
- **Evidence:** the packet compares the two routes and cites the sources: the module's release, licence and requirements from the Go module proxy, the Go wiki on the x repositories' maintenance, and the v0.50.0 source of `go/packages`' launch of `go`. On d790462 with go1.27.2, a runner listed the packages the helper's build compiles (`go list -deps golang.org/x/tools/go/packages`). He also built two unstripped probe programs for windows/amd64 with `CGO_ENABLED=0` and `-trimpath`: one calling `packages.Load`, 8,254,464 bytes, and one using only the standard library, 4,170,240 bytes.
- **Changes:** this entry; a "See also" line under D29 and an "Amended by" line under D45; core §8.2's layout paragraph, a row of its dependency table and the `golang.org/x/tools` record; U3's row of remaining gates in `ROADMAP-UNDERCURRENT.md` and the list of other unrun gates in `DESIGN-UNDERCURRENT.md`.
- **Amended by D134:** item 4's environment also sets `GODEBUG=goindex=0`, which turns off the `go` command's module index.

### D133. Three-OS CI for C3

- **Date:** 2026-10-19. **Decided by:** adopted. In OG-DEPS, OG-BUDGET and OG-START, the three questions of the build advisor's [ruling](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/ci-design-ruling.md) on the CI [design brief](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/ci-design-brief.md), the owner picked one of the assistant's options each; items 1, 3 and 4 say which. Items 2 and 5 are the build advisor's ruling, originator `assistant`, which under D117 went to the build without a question. The brief and the ruling land under `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/` with this entry.
- **Owner's words (question tool):**
  - OG-DEPS, the CI's pinned downloads: "Approve all three as recorded"
  - OG-BUDGET, the Actions budget: "$0 paid, included minutes only"
  - OG-START, when C3 may start: "When the workflow exists and Windows is green"
- **Question:** D42 requires a private GitHub remote with CI on Windows, Linux and macOS no later than C3, and C3 waits for it. The owner created the remote, `https://github.com/Darius-PJ/dwarf-harness`, on 2026-10-19. The workflow the ruling approved downloads code that D29 and D41 leave to the owner, costs paid minutes, and may first run red on Linux and macOS, where C1 and C2 code has never run natively. Which downloads may it use, what caps its spending, and when may C3 start?
- **Decision:**
  1. **Downloads, for CI only (D29, D41)** (the recommended option). The CI may use three pinned downloads and redistributes none: `actions/checkout` v7.0.1 at commit `3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1` (MIT), its only action; Go 1.27.2's official archives from go.dev, D28's toolchain, each checked against item 2's size and SHA-256 before use; and, for the Windows job, D28's race compiler, llvm-mingw 20260922's `llvm-mingw-20260922-ucrt-x86_64.zip` from its GitHub release (190,725,905 bytes, SHA-256 `e3ad77d117a4bea19a7a3b333341824d79a5a371004a10e25b8504e7b3047666`), whose mixed licences are not fully audited. Core §8.2 records the action and the compiler download. `actions/setup-go` was considered and not admitted: it compares no checksum and may install `actions/go-versions` builds instead of go.dev's archives.
  2. **The workflow (D42).** `.github/workflows/check.yml`, on `main` since fe5f758, runs the full `go run ./tools/check`, race step included, on every push to `main` that changes more than Markdown (`paths-ignore: '**.md'`) and on manual dispatch. Its native jobs are `windows-2022` (amd64), `ubuntu-24.04` (amd64) and `macos-15` (arm64), never a `-latest` label; Linux arm64 and Intel macOS stay cross-built only. Each job downloads Go 1.27.2 from go.dev and checks its size and SHA-256 before extracting it: Windows `1314008898bd40df77af4b014f777f08873dbdfbcd3d92308728ee03304fe04f` (79,008,081 bytes), Linux `ecbadb99091a3f46e31f5f934b068b1864eafa7995211b39eaddf76996045fe5` (70,590,635) and macOS `76812b213b1b2302c978d28fa52fa92d541704b9e7d9d5db8002c50e4018c4c5` (68,195,916). With `GOROOT` empty, the job fails unless `go version` reports go1.27.2 on the native target, `go env GOROOT` is the extracted root and Git is 2.45 or newer. The token is `contents: read` and is not kept; there is no cache, artifact, retry or `continue-on-error`; `fail-fast` is off; and the job timeout is 120 minutes, while the check keeps D127's 30-minute package limit and 25-minute report line. The graph helper controls stay empty, so the native Python, Node and TypeScript helper rows skip, as each job logs.
  3. **Budget** (not the recommended option). No paid minutes: before the first push the owner sets an Actions budget of $0 with "Stop usage when the limit is reached", so runs use only his plan's included minutes and stop when those run out, and no coverage is waived to stay at zero. A run is modelled at up to $2.41 before included minutes (Windows $0.010, Linux $0.006 and macOS $0.062 a minute, each job rounded up to a minute); how GitHub charges included minutes for each system is not stated in its documents. A run the budget stops is missing evidence, not a pass, and the project manager asks the owner whether to wait for the next cycle or change the cap.
  4. **C3's start** (not the recommended option). C3's CI prerequisite clears when the workflow runs on the remote and its Windows job passes; Linux and macOS failures become repair tickets, worked alongside C3. C3 still cannot exit until the check is green in CI on all three (its exit criterion), and its other gates stand.
  5. **Hosted evidence (the ruling's P5).** The winexec resource test no longer reports the laptop's Defender settings on every host: fe5f758 removes its constant and two evidence keys, and no assertion changed. The Windows job logs its own host's OS build, administrator role and Defender state instead, or that they were not observed.
- **Not decided here:** the first run's results, which ticket 10's comments record, with a B entry only if a decision results. The CI qualifies no isolation: a hosted Windows Server 2022 job under an administrator account is not ticket 45's Windows 10 profile, and it gives no C10 profile or A2 label. Runner images roll, so the jobs check the Git floor and the native target rather than pinning an image.
- **Consequences:** the first push is the owner's (D31, D46). A red product row becomes a repair ticket under `.scratch/dwarf-harness/issues/`, linked from ticket 10 and the producing ticket, and is fixed with a deterministic regression, never a CI-only skip or retry; a setup or network failure is not a pass.
