Actions logs expire, so each recorded run's commit, URL, per-OS durations and inventory lines are copied into ticket 10.
- **Evidence:** before the workflow landed, the coder downloaded the four archives and recomputed their sizes and SHA-256: all matched the pins. The Windows setup steps ran on the owner's machine under Windows PowerShell 5.1: the extracted `bin/clang.exe` reported `clang version 23.1.2` and `Target: x86_64-w64-windows-gnu`, and `tar.exe` extracted Go in 13.5 s and LLVM in 9.6 s, against 104.6 s and 56.4 s for `Expand-Archive`, one run each. winexec passed in 24.1 s, and under the race detector (37.7 s) with `CC` set to the extracted `bin\clang.exe`, byte-identical to the installed copy (SHA-256 `20ba1856942744291a6aaaf9f2c94f6d0cd7cac5aa9557fce96471dc7ef25d6f`). The full check, race step included, passed on fe5f758, the commit `main` moved to by fast-forward (`check PASSED: all 9 steps passed in 1146.1s`). No hosted run exists yet.
- **Changes:** this entry; the CI design brief and its ruling under `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/`; "See also" lines under D30 and D42; core §8.2's dependency table and CI records; `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C0's repository row and C3's Waits-for, Stop point and Status; C3's row on the status board; a comment on ticket 10.
- **See also:** D141 pushes `main` once per ticket, so CI runs once per ticket within OG-BUDGET's included minutes.
- **Amended by D143:** the workflow loses its `push` trigger while CI is paused, so only a manual dispatch starts a run.
- **Amended by D146:** the `push` trigger returns, and the repository is public, so its standard runners cost nothing and item 3's budget no longer limits runs.
- **Amended by D147:** items 1 and 2 name go1.27.3's official archives, with the SHA-256s and sizes D147 lists, in place of go1.27.2's.

### D134. The Go helper turns off `go list`'s module index

- **Date:** 2026-10-19. **Decided by:** adopted. The owner picked the assistant's recommended option in the question that followed the full check's failure on ticket 61's S5 fix-up, dece7c5.
- **Owner's words (question tool):**
  > Add GODEBUG=goindex=0
- **Question:** Under the full check's load, each `go list` that the Go helper ran took about 20 s and reached the helper's 20 s deadline, so Go came out unknown and every real-toolchain Go test failed. The time went into `go list` indexing the standard library into the empty cache that D132 item 4 requires, on every call. `GODEBUG=goindex=0` turns that index off, but it changes D132's approved environment. Add it?
- **Decision:** D132 item 4's environment also sets `GODEBUG=goindex=0`, which turns off the `go` command's module index. The setting enters the graph's configuration digest with the rest of the environment, so it begins a new analyzer epoch. Nothing else in D132 changes.
- **Consequences:**
  - A cold `go list` no longer writes the standard library's index into its empty cache. The call takes a fifth of the time or less, and the helper's temporary folder holds far fewer files.
  - The switch is an undocumented debugging flag, `#goindex`, read in go1.27.2's `cmd/go/internal/modindex/read.go`, so a later Go release may drop or change it. A toolchain upgrade already requalifies the helper (D132 item 5), and that requalification covers the switch.
- **Evidence:** on 2026-10-19 the project manager ran `go list -e -deps -json` on a small module importing `net/http` (201 packages), with go1.27.2, a fresh `GOCACHE` each time and D132's other settings, three times each way on an idle machine.
  - With the index: 1.3–4.8 s and about 529 cache files (0.9 MB).
  - With `GODEBUG=goindex=0`: 0.27–0.30 s and no files.
  - With `-test` added, the two outputs were identical apart from one path naming each run's own cache folder.
  - In the full check on dece7c5, each cold call took 19.5–20.1 s and hit the deadline.
- **Changes:** this entry and an "Amended by D134" line under D132. Ticket 61 S5's fix-up adds the setting to the helper's environment and configuration.

### D135. The agents' role files are not tracked

- **Date:** 2026-10-19. **Decided by:** owner.
- **Owner's words (chat):**
  > .omp/agents should not be tracked. Please exclude it with local exclusion, not .gitignore
- **Decision:** The role files in `.omp/agents/`, which configure the build's agents (D116 item 1), leave the repository. They stay in the owner's checkout, where the harness reads them, and that checkout's `.git/info/exclude` lists `/.omp/agents/`, so Git ignores them without a `.gitignore`. A role-file change is made there and is not committed.
- **Consequences:**
  - Earlier commits still hold the files, including the ones pushed to GitHub on 2026-10-19. This decision does not rewrite history, which would change every commit hash these records cite.
  - A new worktree or clone has no `.omp/agents/`. Agents are still started from the owner's checkout.
  - A clone that should ignore the files needs the same line in its own `.git/info/exclude`.
- **Changes:** this entry and an "Amended by D135" line under D116. The same commit removes the eight role files from the index and keeps them on disk.
- **Amended by D145:** the role files are committed to the private records repository, and the rewritten history holds no `.omp/` at all.

### D136. The agent instruction files are not tracked

- **Date:** 2026-10-19. **Decided by:** owner.
- **Owner's words (chat):**
  > Agents.md and Claude.md should likewise be added to .git/info/exclude
- **Decision:** `AGENTS.md`, the agents' canonical instructions, and `CLAUDE.md`, which imports it, leave the repository as the role files did under D135. They stay in the owner's checkout, which `.git/info/exclude` excludes with `/AGENTS.md` and `/CLAUDE.md`. Changes to them are made there and not committed.
- **Consequences:** earlier commits, including those on GitHub, still hold both files. A new worktree or clone has neither, so agents read them from the owner's checkout. Documents that name `AGENTS.md` keep naming it as the owner's local file.
- **Changes:** this entry and an "Amended by D136" line under D135. The same commit removes both files from the index and keeps them on disk.
- **Amended by D136:** `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` are untracked the same way.
- **Amended by D145:** both files are committed to the private records repository, and the rewritten history holds neither.

### D137. The check analyzes every supported target

- **Date:** 2026-10-20. **Decided by:** adopted. After the first three-OS CI run failed staticcheck on Linux and macOS, the project manager proposed that the local check run staticcheck for those systems too, and the owner accepted in chat. Extending the same change to vet and govulncheck, which had the same gap, is the project manager's, originator `assistant`, under D121.
- **Owner's words (chat):**
  > That's an excellent catch, a very apt and adroit Project Manager would check that the staticcheck for linux and mac OS happen. This was the root of one of our issues. See that the local check covers this please
- **Question:** D42's `go run ./tools/check` ran vet, staticcheck and govulncheck only for the host, windows/amd64. Four functions used only by Windows code therefore passed it, then failed staticcheck's U1000 check on the Linux and macOS runners ([ticket 138](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/issues/138-repair-the-first-three-os-ci-run.md)). Should the check analyze the other targets too?
- **Decision:**
  - The check's vet, staticcheck and govulncheck steps each run once for every supported target, as the import-boundary and cross-build steps already do: windows/amd64, linux/amd64, linux/arm64, darwin/amd64 and darwin/arm64.
  - A step fails naming every target that failed.
  - staticcheck and govulncheck are built once per check for the host and run with each target's `GOOS` and `GOARCH`, since a tool built for another system cannot run here.
  - The steps keep their numbers and titles.
  - The tests and race steps still run only on the host; CI runs them on each system (D133).
- **Consequences:**
  - A platform-specific static finding now fails the local check before it can reach a push.
  - The first full check with the change, on e6ac925, spent 86.3 s on the three steps, against 4.7 s for the host alone on cd21243.
  - Measured warm on the owner's machine on 2026-10-20, each extra target costs under 1 s of vet and of staticcheck, and 2–3 s of govulncheck.
  - From an empty build cache, the three steps take about five minutes for all five targets, against about two for the host alone.
  - The project manager's separate Linux and macOS vet in the evidence script is no longer needed.
- **Evidence:**
  - On c6bfc5e, staticcheck built for the host and run with `GOOS=linux` and with `GOOS=darwin` reproduced CI's four U1000 findings; with `GOOS=windows` it found none.
  - `TestStaticChecksCoverTargets` gives the shared runner a vet error in a Linux-only file. The runner must fail naming exactly linux/amd64 and linux/arm64, and the test fails if the runner is reduced to the host.
  - The full check on e6ac925, `check PASSED: all 9 steps passed in 1091.5s` (vet 15.1 s, staticcheck 55.0 s and govulncheck 16.2 s for all five targets; tests 421.7 s, race 568.3 s), ran each of the three steps for all five targets.
- **Changes:** this entry and an "Amended by D137" line under D42; `tools/check` (ebc24b8); the description of `tools/check` under C0 in `ROADMAP-CORE.md`.

### D138. A Windows first release with portable code

- **Date:** 2026-10-20. **Decided by:** owner. He decided in chat, after the project manager reported that tickets 47 and 49 waited for Linux and macOS hosts. The documents it changes, item 2's ticket mapping and item 3's portability rules are the project manager's, originator `assistant`.
- **Owner's words (chat):**
  - "The plan for ticket 47 & 49 need a live OS? That must have slipped through during the design phase. This machine has WSL but I either need a linux solution or to skip the live OS with tests."
  - "Linux and MacOS aren't even necessary for a first release but this should be coded in a portable fashion"
- **Question:** C10's Linux and macOS executors and their qualifications (tickets 47–51) and U2's A2 measurements on those hosts (tickets 68–70) each need a real host: a Linux kernel with namespaces, Landlock and cgroups, or an Apple-silicon Mac with a signing identity and Apple's licence. Tickets 47 and 49 have named these hosts among their gates since their publication on 2026-10-06, and C10's exit criterion names Windows, Linux and Apple-silicon macOS drills. On 2026-10-17 the project manager recorded the hosts as an owner dependency, but never asked how they would be provided. Which platforms must the first release support, and how is the rest of the code treated?
- **Decision:**
  1. **The first release needs Windows only.** Acceptance that needs a real host, such as containment, guests and VMs, signing and packaging, or desktop and accessibility checks, is required on Windows only.
  2. **Linux and macOS follow the first release:**
     - **Deferred:** tickets 47–51 and 68–70. Their numbers, blockers and criteria are unchanged.
     - **Windows parts only:** tickets 58, 123 and 129 need only their Windows parts for the first release.
     - **Not triggered:** rows in tickets 52 and 78 that apply only when a Linux or macOS profile is enabled.
  3. **The code stays portable:**
     - Platform-neutral code builds and passes its tests on every supported target. D133's CI runs the full check on Windows, Linux and macOS, and D137's static steps cover all five targets. C3 still cannot exit until all three CI jobs pass.
     - Code for one platform's mechanism sits behind build constraints and refuses explicitly on other platforms, as `winexec` does. It never substitutes a weaker mechanism.
     - Interfaces that later platforms will implement stay platform-neutral, so the deferred executors can be added without changing their callers. C3's execution interface to the executor is one of them.
     - Rows that CI's hosted Linux and macOS runners can exercise stay in scope, such as ticket 30's Unix-socket IPC and ticket 16's three-OS CI.
- **Not decided here:**
  - what else the first release contains;
  - when the Linux and macOS work starts. D87's single macOS slot and B11's signed helper stay the design for it.
- **Consequences:**
  - C10's first-release exit needs only the Windows path: ticket 45, resolved, and ticket 46's qualification.
  - No Linux or macOS machine is needed now. This decision replaces the owner dependency recorded on 2026-10-17.
- **Changes:** this entry; a "See also" line under D87; C10's exit criterion and Status in `ROADMAP-CORE.md`; GUI-E's packaging paragraph in `ROADMAP-GUI.md`; C10's board row and a consolidation comment in `ROADMAPS.md`; a comment in the spec; comments on tickets 47–51, 58, 68–70, 123 and 129.

### D139. F3-10 private-state records frozen

- **Date:** 2026-10-21. **Decided by:** assistant. Under D125, the project manager froze F3-10 without a question to the owner, once a temporary GPT-6 Astra reviewer, standing in for the build advisor while Fable was paused, had approved ticket 10's S2 together with this record and its evidence file. The owner: "Spawn temporary openai/gpt-6-astra agents on effort:max to handle the pending reviews. Fable remains the main advisor and should remain so in all skills and workflows". The build advisor's design ruling for ticket 10 (P11 and P13), which lands as `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/ticket-10-design-ruling.md` with the ticket's close, specifies this four-type family and names schema 1's canonical submission form as a producer rule to record in this freeze. No freeze packet was written.
- **Decision:** F3-10, ticket 10's part of F3, is frozen by addition to `ledger-format` version 1 (D40, D111, D125), at commit 24d38d0, which reached `main` unchanged:
  - four record types, each at version 1: `runtime.agent_registered`, binding the agent's directory, sole session and general context; `runtime.context_created`, creating a general or work context; `private.revision`, selecting an atomic revision of one agent or context scope's private objects; and `runtime.command_receipt`, recording the command's canonical submission and applied result. The namespaces `runtime.` and `private.` are reserved for C3's Core runtime. `Defined()` lists twenty-eight type versions: F0, F2-05 to F2-09, F-U3-61 and F3-10;
  - their value shapes, encoder order, decoder rules and record rules as the F3-10 section of the `contract/ledger` package documentation states them: strict JSON and required/non-null known members, duplicate-key refusal at every depth, canonical identities and digests, plain unsigned-decimal integers, additive unknown optional members, safe directory names, sorted distinct slots, revision and initialization constraints, permitted slot/mode combinations and transcript predecessor rules. The complete payload, including whitespace and extensions, is capped at 16 KiB on both decode and encode;
  - the open tokens `kind`, `operation` and `outcome`, matching `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$` with at most 64 ASCII bytes, whose unknown well-formed values decoders preserve. Version-1 writers use `general` and `work`; `agent_register`, `context_create` and `private_write`; and `applied`, respectively. An unfamiliar outcome is not proof of an applied effect. The closed values are scope `agent`/`context`, slots `instructions`/`memory` for agent scope and `continuation`/`handoff`/`memory`/`transcript` for context scope, and modes `replace`/`append`/`clear` in the documented combinations;
  - the record rules: one commit per complete registration, creation or write group, with six, four or three records respectively, ending in its immutable receipt; the same required agent/session/context/operation binding on every record including the F0 command; `operation_id` equal to the command ID; actor `owner` on command, registration, creation and receipt, and `core` on revision. An agent-scope write names its originating registered context. `turn_id`, `measurement_path` and `body_refs` are absent; the optional receipt `request_id` is absent in version 1. For `private_write`, the receipt's target and expectation match the revision; every receipt's command ID and submission digest match the F0 command. Private bytes are durably published before commit and never enter the ledger or body store; materialization and projection complete before acknowledgement. Refusals before Submit consume no command ID, and there is no private start/end, pending/interrupted outcome or recovery append. Open replays without dispatch or append, completes and reports stale materializations from verified objects, and blocks foreign bytes or missing/corrupt objects. The service enforces these record rules before submission and at Open; the codecs, Check, Reader and EncodeCommit do not enforce cross-record or filesystem rules;
  - schema 1's canonical submission form as a producer rule: SHA-256 of compact JSON with keys `schema` (1), `actor` ("owner"), `agent_id`, `session_id`, `context_id`, `operation`, `target`, `expected_version`, `arguments`, in that order. `target` has `scope` then sorted `slots`; registration arguments have `directory`, `agent_sha256`, `agent_bytes`; creation has `kind`; private-write arguments have `changes` in the documented change-key order, excluding the derived transcript predecessor. Every supplied byte enters through its exact digest and size, with all supplied binding IDs and expectations. The command ID, ephemeral handles, paths/view spellings, ledger metadata and derived predecessor are excluded. Same ID/digest replays the receipt; mismatch refuses before revision/CAS checks. Schema-1 bytes remain stable for later producers, and a new meaning cannot reuse an old command ID. A later change to this producer rule must be a visible decision, not an unrecorded service edit;
  - the golden sample `contract/ledger/testdata/f3/10/log/00000000000000000001.jsonl` (SHA-256 9622b72877ed546a18fcbfd28a19807e0bab237909e23ffe76e7c3da1c260ecd, 10 commits, 34 records). Every earlier golden stays byte-identical, keeping D111's, D115's, D118's, D119's, D123's, D124's and D130's digests.
- **Not frozen:** the provisional runtime limits, apart from this family's 16 KiB payload cap and its fixed payload constraints; the `internal/core/agents` service's implementation and API, as distinct from the frozen producer and record rules it must obey; later F3 families in tickets 11–16; C7's `launcher-protocol`; and R1's `context-module`.
- **Consequences:** these types change only by addition (D40), including later well-formed values of the open tokens without a new type version, and new types or versions through their own freeze records. A breaking change needs the owner's decision entry listing every affected step (D40, D125). This freeze is S2's D41 sign-off for reaching `main`; after S1 and this freeze, ticket 10's S3a may build the producer. `ledger-format` stays at version 1. No step's status changes, C3 is not Done, and nothing here supplies tasks, grants, provider turns, accepted promotion or real colony-work readiness.
- **Evidence:** the [evidence file](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/f3-10-freeze-evidence.md) gives the commands and results. The golden's SHA-256 above identifies the committed blob.
  - V2, the package and consumer tests: passed on the tree whose nine file hashes match 24d38d0: `contract/ledger` in 7.3 s plain and 33.3 s under race, and `internal/core/ledgerstore` in 25.6 s (CoderT10S2's final runs); the PM's `internal/core/ledgerstore` run on 24d38d0 passed in 29.0 s. V3, `FuzzDecodeF3_10` for 120 s: 1,637,734 executions in 120 s on 24d38d0, with no failure and no corpus file left. V8, golden regeneration and earlier digests: on 24d38d0 the regeneration exited 0 and left `contract/ledger/testdata` unchanged, and every earlier golden's SHA-256 equals its freeze entry's: D111 319c8053…, c72c4a73… and 3ee9f566…; D115 843663a1…; D118 014cc612…; D119 96de91f9…; D123 985e073a…; D124 f1a0c473…; D130 6c7169aa…. V4, the record-commit check: `go run ./tools/check -race=false` on 931e6e2, the record commit before this Markdown-only amendment, exited 0: `check PASSED: 8 of 9 steps passed, race skipped (D129) in 552.3s` (tests 477.2 s).
  - The review: On 2026-10-21 the temporary GPT-6 Astra reviewer returned approve-with-changes for S2 at 24d38d0 together with its F3-10 freeze record and evidence, requiring the receipt-target clarification and fixture-inventory instruction correction, both applied before the D125 freeze, with the record-commit check still required before landing.
- **Changes:** `CONTRACTS.md` (the `ledger-format` row's text, owning step and status, the B2 table's C3 row and the F3-10 catalogue); Core §13.1 (the F3 freeze clause and D125's delegated freeze authority); nine comment-only replacements in `contract/ledger` (`doc.go` twice, `catalogue.go` once, `private.go` four times, `fuzz_test.go` once and `private_golden_test.go` once); the evidence file `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/f3-10-freeze-evidence.md`; and a See also line under D125. `ROADMAP-CORE.md`'s C3 section contains no proposed/unfrozen F3-family wording to replace and remains unchanged.
- **Verified by the build advisor on 2026-10-22:** confirmed-with-changes (B31).
- **See also:** D140 records the shared private head: `private.scoped_revision` advances the same scope revision and transcript predecessor chain as `private.revision`, so a consumer of F3-10 alone cannot reconstruct the private heads of a log that holds F3-11 and must not be used on one.

### B31. C3: the private-state service (ticket 10)

- **Date:** 2026-10-22. **Decided by:** assistant. Under D117, the build advisor's [ruling](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/ticket-10-design-ruling.md) on the [design brief](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/ticket-10-design-brief.md) ended ticket 10's design step without a question to the owner and reserved nothing to him. The reading in item 2 is that ruling's. The build's choices and the project manager's answers during the build are the assistant's, within the authority the owner delegated, and settle nothing outside the ruled design. The project manager froze F3-10 under D125 (D139). No design, freeze or approval question was put to the owner in this ticket. While Fable was paused, every review after the ruling was done, at the owner's word quoted below, by a temporary GPT-6 Astra reviewer standing in for the build advisor; on 2026-10-22 the build advisor verified them all (Evidence).
- **Owner's words (2026-10-21):**
  - on the pending reviews, at 14:30: "Spawn temporary openai/gpt-6-astra agents on effort:max to handle the pending reviews. Fable remains the main advisor and should remain so in all skills and workflows"
  - after the project manager described the fix for S1's durability finding and its cost, at 14:50: "The coder should be informed about this optimization"
  - on S3a's review, at 16:25: "Have a stand-in reviewer run these and note that it was done by the stand-in. Fable can double check and verify things after the review"
  - asked whether a stand-in should review the CI fix now or Fable after its reset, at 18:08: "Stand-in now"
  - asked whether a stand-in should review S3b that night or Fable after its reset, at 23:02: "Stand-in should review. This will also be checked by the Fable advisor"
- **Question:** Ticket 10 builds C3's first part, Core's private-state service in `internal/core/agents`: each agent's one execution session, its general and work contexts, their private memory, handoff, transcript and continuation, revision-checked writes and durable command receipts, all rebuilt at restart from the ledger alone. It writes F3-10, which the project manager froze (D139). What did the build settle, and how does it read C3's rule that a restart mismatch "blocks/reports rather than guessing"?
- **Decision:**
  1. **The design as ruled** (P1–P14 and the ruling's nine findings). The package documentation, `internal/core/agents/doc.go`, states the service as built; the brief and the ruling give each choice's options and sources. The choices later tickets build on:
     - One Core package, `internal/core/agents`, owns identities, private objects, Markdown materializations, receipts and replay. It is not named `runtime`, which would shadow the standard library's package. It uses the real store through `Submit`, `ledger.OpenReader` and `durable`, and its production imports are the ruling's dependency set (P1). `ledgerstore`, `workspace`, `durable` and `tools/check` are unchanged.
     - The exported surface is the owner's: `Open`, `Close`, `RegisterAgent`, `CreateContext`, `WritePrivate`, `Snapshot`, `ReadPrivate`, `CommandStatus` and `ScratchBinding`. Core derives the actor. Every call names its agent and context, and is refused with `ErrNotBound` when that pair is unregistered or mismatched and with `ErrClosed` after `Close`. There is no agent entry point; ticket 11 adds it with its grants (P2).
     - One ledger group per transition, as `workspace`'s `UpdateInputs` does it. Content-addressed private bytes are published durably first. Then one `Submit` group commits, six records for a registration, four for a creation and three for a write, ending in its immutable receipt. The Markdown files and the in-memory projection are updated before the acknowledgement. A refusal writes nothing and consumes no command ID, and there is no pending or interrupted private operation (P7, rejecting the brief's admission/completion split).
     - A retry under the same command ID with the same canonical submission digest returns its receipt, and another digest is `ErrCommandMismatch`; both are decided before the revision and capacity checks (P8). The expected revision is checked once, under the lock, just before `Submit` (P4).
     - `core/state.json` is not written: the projection is held in memory and rebuilt at Open, and a stray file is ignored (P5).
     - Scratch bindings are a read-only view of C2's frozen scratch records; the service never creates a scratch (P10).
  2. **The reading of "blocks/reports rather than guessing"** (`ROADMAP-CORE.md`'s C3 ownership rule; the ruling's finding 3, P5 and P9). Each Markdown materialization is `current`, equal to its committed head; `stale`, absent or equal to an earlier committed object of its slot; or `foreign`, anything else or not a regular file. Open completes a `stale` one from the verified head object and reports the completion. Foreign bytes or a missing or corrupt object block the scope, and the report gives the committed and observed hashes, never content. Completing Core's own unfinished step from verified committed bytes is not guessing, and without it an editor holding `Memory.md` on Windows would wedge a context. After a live materialization failure the write is committed but unacknowledged: the typed error names the committed revision, the slot stays `stale`, and the scope's next write supersedes it. Open appends nothing and dispatches nothing.
  3. **The project manager's answers and the findings that set behavior,** each reviewed with its slice; ticket 10's comments give their order.
     - The version-1 operation values are `agent_register`, `context_create` and `private_write`: the ruling's token grammar wins over the brief's dotted names.
     - S1's review found directory durability remembered only within one call, so a publication after a failed directory flush could succeed without it. The fix, made after the owner's words above, remembers per service instance the owned directories whose flush succeeded, marked child before parent and never after a failure, and flushes a replaced directory again.
     - S3a's review: an owner's `workspace.scratch_closed` without an envelope context is valid F2 and no longer poisons replay; `MaterializationError` keeps its cause; and the ruled names `Receipt` and `MaxConcurrentWrites` replace `CommandResult` and `MaxPendingWrites`, without aliases.
     - CI runs 5 and 6 failed on Windows only, because `TestPrivateScopePaths` built a path relative from the runner's `D:` checkout to its TEMP on `C:`. 73462eb moves that check into the sequential `TestPrivateScopeRelativeRoot`, inside the temporary folder.
     - S3b changed no production logic. Its torn-commit row injects a torn tail and asserts that the reopened store truncated it. Its `LockFileEx` row takes the lock just before `Submit`, since an earlier lock blocked a history read rather than the append. A held `Memory.md` refuses replacement with Win32 error 5, not 32, on this machine; the row accepts either and logs the cause. S3b's review made the four crash rows run in parallel (d99cb60).
- **Evidence:**
  - Ticket 10's closing comment lists each criterion's tests, from the ruling's acceptance coverage checked against the tests on `main`. The [ticket's evidence](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/ticket-10-evidence.md) gives each slice's runs, rule breaks, checks, fuzzing and P14 measurement, and every CI run's link, commit and times.
  - The reviews, each by a temporary GPT-6 Astra reviewer (`openai-codex/gpt-6-astra` at thinking `max`) standing in for the build advisor: ReviewT10Astra rejected S1 at 2401b53 on the durability finding and approved S2 at 24d38d0 with its F3-10 record, with two record changes (D139); ReReviewT10S1Astra approved S1's fix at 1053df9; ReviewT10S3aAstra rejected S3a at d1946f5 with one major and two minor findings; ReReviewT10S3aAstra approved its fix at 71a9042; ReviewT10CIFixAstra approved the CI fix at 73462eb; ReviewT10S3bAstra approved S3b at a6428d7 with one minor finding; and ReReviewT10S3bAstra approved its fix at d99cb60.
  - The verification: on 2026-10-22, at the owner's "The time has come, send the reviews to the Advisor", a fresh build advisor (Fable) verified every review above and D139, and returned confirmed-with-changes. It confirmed each verdict, S1's and S3a's major findings included, and D139 as an assistant freeze under D125 that names its reviewer truthfully; no owner gate was triggered. Its four findings, all answered in 32f3af8:
    - minor: no test replayed the frozen F3-10 golden through the service's own rule set. The golden's submission digests come from a test-side encoder, and the service's `encodeSubmission` is pinned only to its own vectors, so a drift between the two would make Open refuse the golden with no test failing. 32f3af8 adds `TestPrivateFrozenGoldenReplay`;
    - nit: `doc.go` named only old lookups as history scans. 32f3af8 names the per-write and per-scope scans (Limits);
    - nit: `paths.go`'s `os.SameFile(info, info)` reads as a tautology but loads the Windows file ID, so that a replaced directory cannot later compare equal. 32f3af8 says so in a comment;
    - nit: two non-blocking selects in `TestPrivateIndependentScopesProgress` could not fail on the bugs they named. 32f3af8 removes them.
  - The check without its race step (D129) passed on every slice: S1 on 2401b53 in 429.5 s and its fix 1053df9 in 614.7 s; S2's record commit 931e6e2 in 552.3 s (D139); S3a on d1946f5 in 617.5 s, after a first run failed only on winexec's two load-sensitive rows, which S3a does not touch, and its fix 71a9042 in 476.0 s; and the CI fix 73462eb in 459.8 s. The full check, uncached and with its race step, passed on a6428d7 (`check PASSED: all 9 steps passed in 1007.5s`) and on d99cb60 (`check PASSED: all 9 steps passed in 1039.4s`). As the last code commit, 32f3af8 carries the ticket's full check (D129), which passed (`check PASSED: all 9 steps passed in 1129.5s`) on its second run. The first run failed only in its race step, on `internal/core/winexec`'s `TestMappedInputCleanup/held-through-grace`: a package 32f3af8 does not touch, unchanged since d99cb60's passing full check, whose row's own 1 s `StopGrace` ran out under the load. The check was rerun once, as the project manager's rule for such a failure requires, and ticket 45's follow-up 4 takes the row. Every test in S3b's final runs stayed within the ruling's 60 s bound under race (P12).
  - `FuzzDecodeF3_10` ran 1,637,734 executions in 120 s on 24d38d0, and `FuzzPrivateGroupReplay` 96,336 executions in 120 s on a6428d7, both without a failure.
  - 38 deliberate rule breaks were made: S1's A–J, S2's A–G, S3a's A–K, the CI fix's L, S3b's A–H and the verification fix-up's M. Each failed its named test, and the restored code passed; S3b's fix-up repeated A and B.
  - CI (D133): run 2, on ticket 138's repair branch, failed only Windows' `TestCaptureUnreadable`; run 3 there and run 4 on 976e19b passed on all three systems; runs 5 and 6, on 1053df9 and 71a9042, failed on Windows only, in `TestPrivateScopePaths`; runs 7 and 8, on 73462eb and d99cb60, passed all nine steps on Windows, Linux and macOS.
  - D78, labelled (the ruling, P14):
    - *Assumption:* a storage-only fixture of 12 or 20 contexts and 1 or 3 callers, at normal and worst-case payloads, plus a synchronized 16-write burst, with no provider, model or Git work.
    - *Calculation:* a write publishes its objects, commits one three-record group and completes its Markdown. Ledger transitions serialize, so throughput does not multiply with callers. A successful write scans the ledger from the start twice, Open scans it twice per clean scope, and old-receipt lookups scan history too, so latency grows with the ledger. At the default 16 preparations and the 4-MiB operation cap, copied supplied bytes are bounded by 64 MiB: a model bound, not a measured footprint.
    - *Measurement:* one run on this Windows machine. 144 writes were applied, none refused or busy, and 288 replays checked; 15.0–25.8 operations per second at normal sizes and 7.5–12.4 at the worst; the burst in 0.61 s; recovery in 0.19–0.94 s. Three old lookups took about 1–10 ms, and six recorded zero elapsed time, which does not show zero latency.
    - *Plan:* the ruling asked for this one smoke and no capacity claim; declared-workload capacity is not measured here.

    No figure is a capacity, an SLA or a shipping default.
- **Limits:**
  - Nothing is frozen, approved or claimed here beyond D139's freeze, and `CONTRACTS.md` is unchanged. The service stores private state for test agents only: nothing admits colony work (D52).
  - No grants, tasks, turns, providers, relationships, agent entry point or repair command (the ruling's scope; tickets 11–16).
  - A foreign materialization or a missing or corrupt object stays blocked: ticket 10 has no repair path, and ticket 16 or a later entry supplies the authorized repair.
  - A successful private write scans the whole ledger twice, and Open scans it twice per clean scope, so their cost grows with the ledger. The projection already holds what the scans recompute: a later entry can keep each slot's committed digest history at install instead.
  - A crash between a temporary file's write and its rename leaves an orphaned `.private-*` file in the owning directory, which nothing in ticket 10 sweeps: a publication removes only its own temporary file.
  - The crash rows are process-death evidence at each boundary, not power-loss proof, and the torn tail and the directory-sync failures are injected. The native Windows rows show a held `Memory.md` refusing replacement with error 5 and completed without an append after the handle's release, and a `LockFileEx` lock making the append fail with `ERROR_LOCK_VIOLATION` (33) and poisoning both the service and the store.
  - The P14 figures are Windows storage-only evidence, not product capacity; their memory figures include the callers' inputs and the verification's allocations.
  - The rows ran on one Windows machine. Five-target static checks are not native execution; the native Linux and macOS evidence is CI runs 7 and 8, and the two native Windows rows run only on Windows.
- **Changes:**
  - The code is `internal/core/agents` as S1 (45ddafe, with its fix 1053df9), S3a (d1946f5, with its fix 71a9042), the CI fix (73462eb), S3b (a6428d7, with its fix d99cb60) and the verification fix-up 32f3af8 built it, and F3-10 in `contract/ledger` as S2 (24d38d0) built it, with its freeze record d2e3cd2 (D139).
  - Documents:
    - in `DECISIONS.md`, this entry and the verification line under D139;
    - in `ROADMAP-CORE.md`, C3's Status line;
    - in `ROADMAPS.md`, C3's row on the status board;
    - ticket 10's status, criteria, Answer and comments, including runs 2's and 3's links, and a comment on ticket 11;
    - under `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/`, the design brief `ticket-10-design-brief.md`, the ruling `ticket-10-design-ruling.md` and the evidence `ticket-10-evidence.md`.

### D140. F3-11 scoped authority, task and non-paid work records frozen

- **Date:** 2026-10-23. **Decided by:** assistant. Under D125, the project manager froze F3-11 without a question to the owner, once the build advisor had approved ticket 11's S2 together with this record and its evidence file. The build advisor's design ruling for ticket 11 (P13, P16 and findings 2, 10 and 11), which lands as `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/ticket-11-design-ruling.md` with the ticket's close, specifies this ten-type family as an addition that changes no F3-10 byte, rule or golden and meets no owner gate. It moves the family's count and size ceilings out of the decoders into producer `Limits`, gives the reading of the shared private head recorded below, and asks for every group shape in the golden. No freeze packet was written.
- **Decision:** F3-11, ticket 11's part of F3, is frozen by addition to `ledger-format` version 1 (D40, D111, D125), at commit 8f5a63f, which reached `main` unchanged:
  - ten record types, each at version 1, in C3's `runtime.` and `private.` namespaces: `runtime.grant_issued`, issuing an immutable owner root grant or delegated child grant; `runtime.grant_revoked`, revoking one irreversibly; `runtime.task_changed`, a task's complete versioned snapshot after its creation or a transition; `runtime.capacity_configured`, the owner's non-paid limits for one colony, agent or context scope; `runtime.work_admitted`, `runtime.work_dispatched` and `runtime.work_ended`, admitting a turn or local operation, dispatching local work, and recording the ending that matches a start; `runtime.local_used`, charging one atomic agent operation; `private.scoped_revision`, an agent's authorized non-initial private revision; and `runtime.scoped_receipt`, recording the command's schema-2 canonical submission and result. `Defined()` lists thirty-eight type versions: F0, F2-05 to F2-09, F-U3-61, F3-10 and F3-11;
  - their value shapes, encoder order, decoder rules and record rules as the F3-11 section of the `contract/ledger` package documentation states them: strict complete JSON, duplicate-key refusal at every depth, required and never-null known members, canonical UUIDv7s and nonzero digests, plain unsigned-decimal integers in 0..MaxInt64 with checked increments, additive unknown optional members, the nested objects, kind-conditional `Resource` and `SubjectRef` members, the documented non-empty and strictly sorted lists, scope sets of at least four entries, `units` 1 and versions that advance by one. The record rules are the documented group shapes, one `Submit` commit per transition ending in its immutable receipt, with the same originating agent, session and context on every record and `operation_id` equal to the command ID; the derived owner, agent or core principal on command, receipt, grant, task and configuration records, and `core` on work, scoped revision, usage and body pin and unpin records; no `measurement_path`; and `body_refs` only on a creation's body pin and on every nonterminal task snapshot, for its description. The service enforces the record and group rules before submission and at Open; the codecs, Check, Reader and EncodeCommit do not;
  - the open tokens, matching `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$` with at most 64 ASCII bytes, whose unknown well-formed values decoders preserve: `SubjectRef.kind`, where version 1 writes `artifact` and the service refuses to apply an unfamiliar kind; `lifetime`, where version 1 writes `until_revoked` and any other value grants nothing; `outcome`, where version-1 work endings write `released`, `cancelled`, `interrupted`, `released_unsent`, `succeeded`, `failed` and `unknown`, and receipts write `applied`, `admitted`, `dispatched` and `recorded`; and `failure_cause`, for which the package documentation names no version-1 writer value. `unknown` is an ending record that releases nothing, every unfamiliar outcome is held the same way, and neither is read as success. The closed sets are the resource kinds and data classes, each class's operations, the context scopes and their slots, the `ScopeKey` kinds, task actions, states and wait reasons, creator and assigner actors, work kinds, capacity scopes, `tool_schema` 1, the local file operations, and the submission actors and operations, as documented;
  - the 64 KiB cap on the complete payload, including whitespace and extensions, on both decode and encode, which `TestF3_11Payloads/producer_maxima` shows `runtime.grant_issued` and its receipt meet at the producer maxima with unescaped ASCII path bytes; paths that JSON escapes expand and the encoder refuses over the cap; and the resource-path grammar: 0 to 1,024 UTF-8 bytes of slash-separated relative components, with no empty internal component, `.` or `..`, backslash, colon, control character, absolute, drive or UNC form, or `.git` component in any ASCII case, checked lexically on every host and never cleaned into acceptance. External resource keys are opaque, non-secret strings of 1 to 256 UTF-8 bytes without control characters;
  - the shared private head, the ruling's reading (finding 10), originator `assistant`: `private.scoped_revision` advances the same scope revision and transcript predecessor chain as `private.revision`, and a consumer of private heads consumes both types in ledger order. A consumer of F3-10 alone cannot reconstruct the heads of a log that holds F3-11 and must not be used on one: it could keep an old head or refuse a later owner revision as stale. F3-10's text, bytes and golden are unchanged, and F3-11 groups never embed F3-10 records;
  - schema 2's canonical submission form as a producer rule (P13): SHA-256 of compact JSON with keys `schema` (2), `actor`, `origin`, `operation`, `target`, `grant_id`, `expected` and `arguments`, in that order. `grant_id` is present for agent operations and for every work operation, whatever the actor; `expected` has `version`, then `assignment_id` and `generation` on task mutations and `authority_version` on grant issue and revoke; each closed operation's arguments follow the documented order. Every supplied target, binding, expectation, argument or reference hash, byte size and limit is bound. The command ID, receipt sequence, observed authority, recomputed scope set, transcript predecessor, prepared handles and private input bytes are excluded. The receipt embeds its submission, whose digest is checked by re-encoding it. Schema-1 bytes stay unchanged, and, as for schema 1 (D139), a later change to this producer rule must be a visible decision;
  - the golden sample `contract/ledger/testdata/f3/11/log/00000000000000000001.jsonl` (SHA-256 145bb11c2b1c58e9e8530a98e6972b35329e4adb9dfb945a9f15381ae221bba6, 39 commits, 130 records), with its object and body fixtures under `contract/ledger/testdata/f3/11/`. It holds every ordered group shape the package documentation tables (finding 11). Every earlier golden stays byte-identical, keeping D111's, D115's, D118's, D119's, D123's, D124's, D130's and D139's digests.
- **Not frozen:** the `Limits` ceilings and their defaults, which are producer guards checked before `Submit`, while replay accepts larger historical values within the payload cap (finding 2, P16): among them the agent's three turn slots, delegation depth 8, the counts of operations, resources, recipients, audience agents, dependencies, scopes and selected objects, 4 MiB for `bytes` and `max_bytes`, and 1 MiB for a description; the `internal/core/agents` service's implementation and API, as distinct from the frozen producer and record rules it must obey; later F3 families in tickets 12–16; C7's `launcher-protocol`; and R1's `context-module`.
- **Consequences:** these types change only by addition (D40): later well-formed values of the open tokens need no new type version, as when ticket 12 adds `SubjectRef` kinds, and new types or versions come through their own freeze records. A breaking change needs the owner's decision entry listing every affected step (D40, D125). This freeze is S2's D41 sign-off for reaching `main`; after it, ticket 11's S3a and S3b may build the producers. `ledger-format` stays at version 1. No step's status changes, and nothing here supplies reviews, paid holds, provider turns, an executor, accepted promotion or real colony-work readiness.
- **Evidence:** the [evidence file](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/f3-11-freeze-evidence.md) gives the commands and results. The golden's SHA-256 above identifies the committed blob.
  - V3, `FuzzDecodeF3_11` for 120 s: 1,246,122 executions in 120 s on 8f5a63f, 55 new interesting inputs, no failure, and no corpus file left in `testdata`. V8, golden regeneration and earlier digests: the regeneration exited 0 and left every golden byte unchanged (`git status` on `testdata` empty), and every earlier golden's SHA-256 equals the digest recorded by D111, D115, D118, D119, D123, D124, D130 and D139. V4, the record-commit check: `go run ./tools/check -race=false` on 28f59c1, the record commit before this Markdown-only amendment, exited 0: `check PASSED: 8 of 9 steps passed, race skipped (D129) in 833.3s`.
  - The review: On 2026-10-23 the build advisor (Fable) approved ticket 11's S2 at 8f5a63f together with this record and its evidence, with no blocker or major finding; its minors (the B2 C3 row's stale later-family list and two decoder rules added to doc.go) and nits (the V9 wording for the two terminator-less fixtures, the maxima clause, and an unfamiliar-kind encoder canonicalization carried to ticket 12) were applied to the record before the freeze or carried as stated.
- **Changes:** `CONTRACTS.md` (the `ledger-format` row's text, owning step and status, the B2 table's C3 row and the F3-11 catalogue); Core §13.1 (the F3 freeze clause); seven comment-only replacements in `contract/ledger` (three in `doc.go`: the status sentence and the two decoder rules the review added; one each in `catalogue.go`, `scoped_authority.go`, `scoped_submission.go` and `scoped_values.go`); the evidence file `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/f3-11-freeze-evidence.md`; and See also lines under D125 and D139. `ROADMAP-CORE.md`'s C3 section contains no proposed or unfrozen F3-family wording and remains unchanged.
- **See also:** D144 makes `private_revision` a known `SubjectRef` kind wherever one decodes, F3-11's records included, corrects `SubjectRef.MarshalJSON` for unfamiliar members without changing a stored byte, and records the shared task reading: an approve or fail `runtime.review_result` is a task transition without a `runtime.task_changed` record, so a consumer of F3-11 alone cannot reconstruct the tasks of a log that holds F3-12 and must not be used on one.
- **See also:** D148 records the shared turn-child and unit reading: a `runtime.paid_reserved` whose `parent_turn_id` names a turn is that turn's child, counted by F3-11's rule for a turn's ending, and a `runtime.paid_result` of any kind is its ending; a `runtime.paid_receipt` carrying `usage` charges one local unit on existing F3-11 scopes; so a consumer of F3-11 alone cannot reconstruct the turns, child endings or non-paid book of a log that holds F3-13 and must not be used on one. F3-13's admission is the first consumer of an external grant's `model.call`, and F3-11's text, bytes, receipts, lookups, group shapes and golden are unchanged.

### D141. Pushes go once per ticket

- **Date:** 2026-10-23. **Decided by:** adopted. The owner picked one of the project manager's options for staying within D133's included Actions minutes, and deferred another. The options were: hold pushes until the allowance resets; push once per ticket; time limits per job; macOS, or the race step, only on manual dispatch; a small paid budget. The project manager had recommended the first three.
- **Owner's words (chat):**
  > Let's push once per ticket instead of per slice, running the tests per ticket. We can save installing time limits for if these still take up too much of the budget
- **Context:** Under D133's OG-BUDGET, "$0 paid, included minutes only", GitHub blocks Actions once the month's included minutes are used up. Every push to `main` that changes more than Markdown starts the three-OS check (D133), and three pushes on 2026-10-23 started runs 11–13. The owner then reported the Actions budget at 90% "as of the last run".
- **Decision:** The project manager asks the owner to push `main` once per ticket, after the ticket's close, instead of after each slice merge, so CI runs the full check once per ticket. Pushing still waits for his word (D31). Slice merges and their local checks are unchanged (D116, D129); they use no Actions minutes.
- **Not decided here:** time limits per job in `check.yml` wait until pushes once per ticket still use too much of the allowance. The workflow, its triggers and its budget stay as D133 set them.
- **Amends:** D31.
- **Changes:** this entry, an "Amended by D141" line under D31 and a "See also" line under D133.
- **See also:** D143 pauses CI, so a push starts no run until the owner decides.
- **See also:** D146 resumes CI, which runs once per ticket because pushes go once per ticket.
- **Amended by D153:** guarded slice branches run CI before exact-SHA promotion, and routine branch/passing-main pushes no longer wait for the owner or a ticket's close; other owner gates remain.

### B32. C3: scoped grants and atomic task ownership (ticket 11)

- **Date:** 2026-10-24. **Decided by:** assistant. Under D117, the build advisor's [ruling](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/ticket-11-design-ruling.md) on the [design brief](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/ticket-11-design-brief.md) ended ticket 11's design step without a question to the owner and reserved nothing to him. None of its conditional owner gates was triggered: OG-D52, OG-BREAK and OG-DEP stayed untriggered through the build. The build's choices and the project manager's answers during the build are the assistant's, within the authority the owner delegated, and settle nothing outside the ruled design. Item 4 is the one exception: ticket 61's design ruling left the assignment of resource and work-domain registration to a B entry at ticket 11's design brief, which missed it, and no D entry mentions that registration, so it meets no owner gate. The project manager froze F3-11 under D125 (D140). Every review was a fresh build advisor (Fable), and no stand-in ran.
- **Owner's words (2026-10-23):**
  - asked whether to start ticket 11's design, at 12:17: "Start now"
- **Question:** Ticket 11 builds C3's scoped authority in `internal/core/agents`: owner and delegated grants checked at admission, dispatch and disclosure, atomic task claims and reassignment, Core's non-paid budget book, bound turn and local-work admission, and an agent's own private and artifact access, all rebuilt at restart from the ledger alone. It writes F3-11, which the project manager froze (D140). What did the build settle?
- **Decision:**
  1. **The design as ruled** (P1–P17 and the ruling's thirteen findings). The package documentation, `internal/core/agents/doc.go`, states the service as built; the brief and the ruling give each choice's options and sources. The choices later tickets build on:
     - `BindAgent` binds one agent's origin for a trusted host; it is not authentication. Every method re-derives authority from the ledger under the lock (P2).
     - The owner issues root grants, and a grant holding `grant.issue` delegates attenuated, depth-bounded children. One leaf and its complete current chain authorize a whole operation, never a union of grants (P3). A `task.create` grant names no task: the audience, the assignee and the grant's units bound each creation (finding 1).
     - There is no timer, lease or cached permit. The chain and the task generation are checked at entry, after unlocked preparation, before commit and before disclosure; revocation is irreversible, and only the `until_revoked` lifetime is current (P4, finding 5).
     - A reassignment issues a new assignment ID and generation on a work context no task has bound, and every later mutation names both, so an old generation's start, submission or completion cannot touch the replacement (P8).
     - One budget book, rebuilt by the same reducer, holds the owner's finite non-paid limits, consumed units and holds over the colony, agent, context and grant scopes, with checked arithmetic and no money. Authority is checked before capacity, so denied and busy stay distinct, and context limits are optional (P9, finding 6). Tickets 13 and 14 add their paid holds to the same book.
     - `StartTurn` is the agent's alone and is admitted or busy at once, executing nothing. Local work is admitted by the agent, and dispatched and ended only through host-only methods (P10, P11, finding 7). Calibration bounds, among them the agent's three turn slots, are `Limits` defaults, not contract rules (P16, finding 2).
     - Open reads no clock and appends nothing. Admissions it finds pending stay `recovery_pending` until the host's explicit `ReconcileAdmissions` ends each: `released_unsent` if undispatched, `unknown` if dispatched without proof, with its holds kept, and a turn `interrupted` once its children have ended (P15, finding 8).
  2. **The project manager's answers that changed reviewed behaviour,** each confirmed by its slice's review; the [evidence](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/ticket-11-evidence.md) lists every answer:
     - S3a's answer 4, as answer 9 corrected it: an agent may name an artifact as a task's subject exactly when it could read it now, through an exact current `artifact.read` chain or through the task description's audience with a current `task.inspect`. Either route suffices, and both are checked again at commit and at Open.
     - S3b's answer 4: an agent's `turn.cancel` may use a separate current grant of its own, relaxing S3a's reviewed rule that every control uses the admission's grant. The review found it within F3-11's frozen text.
     - S3b's answer 7: a retry of an in-flight private write waits on the same-scope gate and returns the receipt only after the materializations. This changes ticket 10's owner `WritePrivate`, which returned a matched receipt at once; no ticket 10 test pinned the old behaviour.
  3. **The reviews' findings that set behaviour,** each carried to a later slice: S1's five minors (zero `Limits` refused, no `null` lists, no zero digests, `grant_id` on every work operation, busy distinct from invalid in the tests) and its note leaving one path grammar, `ledger.ValidResourcePath`; S3a's three minors (`FuzzScopedGroupReplay` run against one long-lived service, `MaxAgentTurnSlots` ≤ 0 refused, and an agent creating a task under an existing ID refused without learning its version); and S3b's minor, the owner's `TurnStatus` refusing a supplied grant, which S4 made. S2's nit on `SubjectRef.MarshalJSON` is carried to ticket 12.
  4. **Resource and work-domain registration is assigned to C3.** Registered shared resources and owner-registered work domains (the node kinds of Undercurrent §8.3 and U3's definition table) join C3's row in `CONTRACTS.md` as a later C3 family, added only when specified. Ticket 61's design ruling left this assignment to a B entry at ticket 11's design brief, as ticket 59's ruling handled the C4/C5 emitter (its P4), and sent it to the owner only if it contradicted a D entry. The brief missed it, so this close makes it; no D entry mentions resource or work-domain registration. Ticket 61's resource and work-domain nodes (its first criterion) and its positive resource→consumer usage (its third, with ticket 15's action facts) wait for this family.
- **Limits:**
  - Nothing is frozen, approved or claimed here beyond D140's freeze. `CONTRACTS.md` gains only item 4's later family in C3's row, which freezes nothing. Grants and tasks serve test agents only: nothing admits colony work (D52).
  - No review results, paid holds, provider turns, executor, scheduler, relationship contexts, service loss or concurrent restart proof (the ruling's scope; tickets 12–16).
  - Local work that was dispatched and left `unknown` has no resolution path in ticket 11: its holds stay charged, and ticket 15's executor supplies the proven ending.
  - Agents cannot create their own work contexts, because F3-10 freezes creation as an owner action. A claim therefore needs a work context the owner created and no task has bound, until a later producer adds agent-originated creation by a new type.
  - `BeginLocal`, `EndLocal` and `EndTurn` have no production caller until ticket 15; the tests prove state and checks, never an effect (P11).
  - S4's labelled profile measured the cost of the ledger scans the S3a and S3b reviews located. For ten times the commits, 87 to 874, Open went from 1.42 s to 75.8 s, against the store's own 19 ms and 166 ms, and each producer operation took 4.5 to 9.4 times as long, `StartTurn` 250 ms to 2.36 s. The review found no correctness defect. It sends the cost to a new ticket, which the project manager asks the owner to publish, with these figures as B3's measured trigger, before ticket 15 makes `StartTurn`, `AdmitLocal` and `BeginLocal` production paths.
  - The rows ran on one Windows machine. CI run 13 is native Linux and macOS evidence for S1 and S2 only.
  - The crash rows are injected boundaries in child processes over copied real stores, not power-loss or isolation proof, and S4's smoke and profile are labelled admission-and-storage-only measurements, not capacity.
  - No producer ticket exists yet for resource and work-domain registration (item 4); the project manager asks the owner whether to publish one.
- **Evidence:**
  - Ticket 11's closing comment lists each criterion's tests, from the ruling's acceptance coverage checked against the tests on `main`. The [ticket's evidence](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/ticket-11-evidence.md) gives each slice's runs, rule breaks, checks, fuzzing and the project manager's answers, and CI runs 9–13.
  - The reviews, each by a fresh build advisor (Fable, `claude-fable-5-1`): AdvisorT11Ruling ruled the design, approve-with-changes; AdvisorT11S1 approved S1 at 51eb580; AdvisorT11S2 approved S2 at 8f5a63f together with the F3-11 record, S2's D41 sign-off (D140); AdvisorT11S3a approved S3a at 0061b12; AdvisorT11S3b approved S3b at 444fd28; AdvisorT11S4 approved S4 at ee9efa7 with two nits, no change required; and the close: On 2026-10-24 the build advisor (Fable) approved ticket 11's close documents onto ee9efa7 under D116 items 3–5 and D128 (document check only; the ticket's one push follows, D141): B32 with the design as ruled, the three answers that changed reviewed behaviour, the carried findings, the assignment of resource and work-domain registration to C3's row (item 4, within ticket 61's ruling, no D entry contradicted, the producer ticket an owner question) and S4's measured cost referred to a new ticket as B3's trigger with the figures' own 4.5 to 9.4 times range, ticket 11 resolved with every criterion mapped to tests present on main, the evidence of every slice and of CI runs 9–13 with no run 14 result claimed, and C3 still In progress; approve-with-changes, one major (ticket 61's comment had listed the resolved tickets 07 and 09 among its remaining blockers; the applied text names 15 and 17) and four nits, none required.
  - S4's review confirmed the project manager's three answers, among them breaks A and G as layer-accurate mutations, and found no correctness defect in the labelled smoke and profile; its ruling on their cost is under Limits.

As the last code commit, S4 carries the full check, uncached and with its race step: `check PASSED: all 9 steps passed in 1294.8s` (tests 454.1 s, race 779.4 s).

S4 touches no fuzzed function, so nothing was fuzzed beyond the seed corpora.

Run 9 is the first CI run of ticket 10's 32f3af8, and run 13 the first of ticket 11's S1, S2 and D140. Run 14 starts with ticket 11's push after this close (D141), and no result is claimed for it.
