### D142. Tickets 139 and 140 published, with three blocker changes

- **Date:** 2026-10-24. **Decided by:** adopted. In the overnight log's questions 5 and 6, the project manager recommended publishing a producer ticket for resource and work-domain registration (B32 item 4) and a ticket for the private-state service's measured replay cost (B32's Limits). The owner answered in chat, naming both numbers, and asked for the tickets' dependencies and order to be audited; he then approved the project manager's summary of the two tickets and the audit's changes.
- **Owner's words (chat):**
  - answering questions 5, 6 and 3, at 11:35: "5. The family ticket should be its own ticket: 139, 6. Publish this as ticket 140, then audit and update ticket dependancies and order" and "3. The next ticket shall be Ticket 12 then 13"
  - on the summary: "Approve and publish"
- **Decision:**
  1. **Ticket 139,** "Register shared resources and work domains through Core" (C3, blocked by 11), produces the family B32 item 4 assigned to C3, with the scoped declarations that assign a registered resource or work domain to a task or context (Undercurrent §8.2's declared relationships), which no entry had assigned to a producer. Ticket 61's resource and work-domain nodes (its first criterion) and positive resource→consumer usage (its third) wait for it, so ticket 61's blockers become 07, 09, 11, 15, 17, 139.
  2. **Ticket 140,** "Bound the private-state service's replay and lookup cost" (C3, blocked by 11), takes the cost that ticket 11's S4 review sent to a new ticket, with S4's profile as B3's measured trigger. The review asked for the fix before ticket 15 makes turn start and local work production paths, so ticket 15's blockers become 14, 140.
  3. **Order.** Ticket 12, then 13, as the owner answered; the tracker's frontier, the lowest-numbered open and unblocked ticket, already selects them first. Ticket 140 lands before 15 through 15's blocker. U3's Status line and board row now name the producer freezes that ticket 61's remaining criteria and ticket 62 wait for as tickets 15, 17 and 139; ticket 11 is resolved and F3-11 frozen (D140).
  4. **The audit** of the open tickets that name ticket 10 or 11, the registration family or the service's replay, lookup or startup cost changes one more blocker, item 5's. Tickets 16 and 62 gain cross-link comments: 140 reaches 16 through 15, and 139 reaches 62 through 61. Ticket 11, whose comments sent both items to the owner, gains a cross-link naming the two tickets. Two blockers are now numbered higher than the tickets they block, 140 on 15 and 139 on 61; the tracker requires only that every listed ticket be resolved (`docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`), and the graph stays acyclic.
  5. **Ticket 59 no longer waits for ticket 61,** so its blockers become 04, 07, 08, 09, 17. Its comment of 2026-10-13 listed 61 only for the graph-delta family, one of the five producer freezes its gate ruling requires; that family is F-U3-61, frozen by D130, and later node and edge kinds join it by addition. Keeping 61 would make 59 wait for 61's remaining criteria and, through them, for 139. It still waits for 17, the validation-evidence freeze. U1's Status line now says that U1.E is blocked, through ticket 59, on ticket 17's freeze alone.
- **Limits:** Publication grants no implementation, contract-freeze, release or study approval and changes no step's status. Ticket 139's family is frozen under D125 once built, unless it reaches an owner gate (D29, D40, D52). Every ticket that waits for 15 now also waits for 140, among them 16, 17 and 59, and every ticket that waits for 61 also waits for 139, among them 62.
- **Changes:** this entry and a See also line under B32; tickets 139 and 140; the blockers and rework comments of tickets 15, 59 and 61; cross-link comments on tickets 11, 16 and 62; in `ROADMAP-UNDERCURRENT.md`, U1's and U3's Status lines, and U3's row on the status board of `ROADMAPS.md`.

### D143. CI paused: a push starts no run

- **Date:** 2026-10-24. **Decided by:** owner. His Actions budget, which D133's OG-BUDGET set at "$0 paid, included minutes only", had run out.
- **Owner's words (chat, 14:37):** "Github Action budget has been reached, adjust the repo so that no CI tests run for the time being. A final decision/resolution will be forthcoming"
- **Decision:** `.github/workflows/check.yml` loses its `push` trigger, so a push to `main` starts no run. Its jobs and `workflow_dispatch` stay, so a run starts only when the owner starts one by hand. The pause holds until his final decision.
- **Unchanged:** the local check, `go run ./tools/check`, still gates `main` (D42, D46, D128, D129), with its static steps and cross-builds for all five targets (D137). Pushes still go once per ticket on the owner's word (D141); they start no run.
- **Limits:** C3 cannot exit while CI is paused, because its exit criterion needs the check green in CI on Windows, Linux and macOS (D133). No step's status changes. Native Linux and macOS test runs stop until CI resumes.
- **Amends:** D133 item 2, the workflow's triggers.
- **Changes:** this entry; `.github/workflows/check.yml`; an "Amended by D143" line under D133 and a "See also" line under D141.
- **Amended by D146:** the pause ends: a push to `main` that changes more than Markdown starts a run again.

### D144. F3-12 review and failure decision records frozen

- **Date:** 2026-10-24. **Decided by:** assistant. Under D125, the project manager froze F3-12 without a question to the owner, once the build advisor had approved ticket 12's S2 together with this record and its evidence file. The build advisor's design ruling for ticket 12 (P3, P11, P14 and findings 1, 3 and 6), which lands as `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/ticket-12-design-ruling.md` with the ticket's close, specifies this three-type family as a separate family that changes no F3-11 byte, rule, operation, group shape or golden, beyond the two `SubjectRef` additions that D140's Consequences provide for, and meets no owner gate. It makes the check and configuration hashes bound digests instead of artifact references, replaces two result types with one that carries a closed verdict, and gives the shared task reading recorded below. No freeze packet was written.
- **Decision:** F3-12, ticket 12's part of F3, is frozen by addition to `ledger-format` version 1 (D40, D111, D125), at commit bfc54c7, which reached `main` unchanged:
  - three record types, each at version 1, in C3's `runtime.` namespace, named as `Defined()` names them: `runtime.review_requested` (`TypeRuntimeReviewRequested`), which establishes a task's review or failure request or explicitly replaces the current one; `runtime.review_result` (`TypeRuntimeReviewResult`), the reviewer's decision on that request; and `runtime.review_receipt` (`TypeRuntimeReviewReceipt`), recording the command's schema-3 canonical submission and outcome. `Defined()` lists forty-one type versions: F0, F2-05 to F2-09, F-U3-61, F3-10, F3-11 and F3-12;
  - their value shapes, decoder rules and record rules as the F3-12 section of the `contract/ledger` package documentation states them: the compact encoder order of every payload and of the nested `ReviewPrincipal`, `ReviewBinding`, `PrivateRevisionRef` and `ReviewUsage`; strict complete UTF-8 JSON, duplicate-key refusal at every depth, even inside ignored extensions, required and never-null known members, canonical UUIDv7s and nonzero digests, plain unsigned-decimal integers in 0..MaxInt64, and ignored unknown optional members, except unfamiliar `SubjectRef` members, which are preserved; a binding naming the task, its version, assignment and generation, the assignee as a `Binding` without a session, the reviewer, the subject, the acceptance, check and configuration digests, the required evidence, the authorization revision and the deadline; required evidence that is non-empty and strictly ascending by each reference's compact encoding, which codecs never sort or deduplicate; `failure_cause` and `failing_evidence` exactly on a failure request, and `cause` and `failing_evidence` exactly with the verdict `fail`; and a result's `task_version` one above the binding's for approve and fail and equal to it for reject. The record rules are the three ordered group shapes, `C,Q,R` for a request or replacement, `C,V,R` for a reject and `C,V,U,R` for an approve or fail, whose `U` releases the task description's existing pin exactly once; one `operation_id`, agent, session and context on every record of a group; the actual owner or agent actor on the command, request, result and receipt, and `core` on the unpin; no `turn_id`, `measurement_path` or `body_refs`; a result that repeats its request's exact binding, names its committed sequence and matches its kind; `task.complete` authority for a review and `task.fail` for a failure, with both participants' current read rights on the subject and every evidence occurrence, rechecked at the callback without requiring the colony `authority_version` unchanged; one current request per nonterminal task version and assignment, which advances no task version and, while pending, rejected or expired, refuses a direct complete or fail, the owner's included; a request observed before its deadline and a result at or before it, never below the prior committed floor; and one local unit charged for each agent decision, reject included. The service enforces these record and group rules before publication and at Open;
  - the closed sets and open tokens: request `kind` is closed to `review` and `failure`; `verdict` to `approve`, `reject` and `fail`; the reviewer's, requester's and submission's actor to `owner` and `agent`, with `grant_id` present exactly for `agent`; and schema 3's operations to `review.request`, `failure.request`, `review.resolve` and `failure.resolve`. The open tokens match `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$` with at most 64 ASCII bytes, and decoders preserve their unknown well-formed values: the failure cause, for which the package documentation names no version-1 writer value (the golden's failures write `semantic_failure`); the receipt `outcome`, where version-1 producers write `requested`, `approved`, `rejected` and `failed`, and an unknown outcome never implies applied state; and `SubjectRef.kind`, where version 1 writes `artifact` and `private_revision` and the service refuses every other kind;
  - `private_revision` as a known `SubjectRef` kind, encoded as `kind` and a `private_revision` member holding `binding`, `scope`, `slot`, `revision`, `source_sequence`, `sha256` and `bytes`: one changed object or transcript chunk of a validated `private.revision` or `private.scoped_revision`, never a clear, an absent object, a mutable head or a concatenated transcript. Its scope is closed to `agent`, with the slots `instructions` and `memory`, and `context`, with `continuation`, `handoff`, `memory` and `transcript`. It is known wherever a `SubjectRef` decodes, F3-11's records included, as D140's Consequences provide for; `artifact`'s historical encoding and ignored-extension behavior are unchanged;
  - the `SubjectRef.MarshalJSON` correction (P3): a direct encoding of unfamiliar members now canonicalizes them as the outer encoding does, so the two agree on escaping `<`, `>`, `&`, U+2028 and U+2029. It changes no stored byte or hash;
  - `check_sha256` and `config_sha256`, required nonzero digests in the binding that the result repeats (finding 1): they identify the checklist and configuration the reviewer judged against; the service verifies neither, and no record here asserts that a check ran. `acceptance_sha256` equals the task's immutable acceptance digest;
  - the 64 KiB cap on the complete payload (`MaxReviewPayloadBytes`, 65,536 bytes), including whitespace and ignored extensions, checked on input before parsing and on compact output; required evidence has no decoder count ceiling below it (P14);
  - schema 3's canonical submission form as a producer rule (P11): SHA-256 of compact JSON with keys `schema` (3), `actor`, `origin`, `operation`, `grant_id`, which the owner omits, and `arguments`, in that order. The arguments are `request_id`, `binding`, `replaces_request_id`, `failure_cause` and `failing_evidence` for both requests; `request_id`, `request_sequence`, `binding` and `verdict` for `review.resolve`; and `request_id`, `request_sequence`, `binding`, `cause` and `failing_evidence` for `failure.resolve`, each in that order with its conditional members as documented. The command ID, host observation, derived request, result and task versions, usage scopes and charges, and envelope sequences are excluded; the already-observed `request_sequence` is included, and producers sort caller evidence sets before fingerprinting. The receipt embeds its submission, whose digest is checked by re-encoding it. Schema 2's closed operation table and the bytes of schemas 1 and 2 are unchanged, and, as for schemas 1 and 2 (D139, D140), a later change to this producer rule must be a visible decision;
  - the shared task reading, the ruling's reading (finding 6), originator `assistant`: an approve or fail `runtime.review_result` is a task transition. It advances the task's version and state exactly as a `runtime.task_changed` complete or fail would, without a `runtime.task_changed` record. A consumer of tasks consumes both families in ledger order. A consumer of F3-11 alone cannot reconstruct the tasks of a log that holds F3-12 and must not be used on one: it would keep the task submitted and live, and could accept a direct transition that the result already closed. F3-11's text, bytes, rules and golden are unchanged, apart from the two `SubjectRef` additions above, and F3-12 groups embed no F3-11 record.
- **Not frozen:** the `Limits` ceilings and their defaults, which are producer guards checked before `Submit`, while replay accepts larger historical values within the payload cap (P14): among them `MaxReviewEvidence`, default 16, and the existing object-byte and preparation ceilings; the `internal/core/agents` service's implementation and API, as distinct from the frozen producer and record rules it must obey; later F3 families in tickets 13–16 and 139; C7's `launcher-protocol`; and R1's `context-module`.
- **Consequences:** these types change only by addition (D40): later well-formed values of the open tokens need no new type version, as when C4's evidence arrives as a later `SubjectRef` kind (P2), and new types or versions come through their own freeze records. A breaking change needs the owner's decision entry listing every affected step (D40, D125). This freeze is S2's D41 sign-off for reaching `main`; after it, ticket 12's S3a and S3b may build the reducer and the producers. `ledger-format` stays at version 1. No step's status changes, and nothing here supplies gate state, executed checks, paid holds, provider turns, an executor, accepted promotion or real colony-work readiness.
- **Evidence:** the [evidence file](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/f3-12-freeze-evidence.md) gives the commands and results.
  - The golden sample `contract/ledger/testdata/f3/12/log/00000000000000000001.jsonl`, SHA-256 6586720b28a19f997dd0890a6dbc71d27ffdf395fd000e68b6aedbbbe0ce79f8 (the committed blob), 25 commits and 90 records, with its object and body fixtures under `contract/ledger/testdata/f3/12/`. It holds the three group shapes, all three verdicts, both request kinds, a replacement, and artifact and `private_revision` subjects, and no checklist or configuration surrogate task (finding 1). Every earlier golden stays byte-identical, F3-11's included, keeping D111's, D115's, D118's, D119's, D123's, D124's, D130's, D139's and D140's digests.
  - V3, `FuzzDecodeF3_12` for 120 s and the `FuzzDecodeF3_11` rerun: 1,016,491 and 3,386,095 executions in 120 s each on bfc54c7, with 39 and 25 new interesting inputs, no failure, and no corpus file left in `testdata`. V8, golden regeneration and earlier digests: the regeneration on bfc54c7 exited 0 and left every golden byte unchanged (`git status` on `testdata` empty), and every earlier golden's SHA-256 equals the digest recorded by D111, D115, D118, D119, D123, D124, D130, D139 and D140. V4, the record-commit check: `go run ./tools/check -race=false` on df72ff6, the record commit before this Markdown-only amendment, exited 0: `check PASSED: 8 of 9 steps passed, race skipped (D129) in 657.6s` (tests 580.3 s).
  - The review: On 2026-10-24 the build advisor (Fable) approved ticket 12's S2 at bfc54c7 together with this record, its evidence and the D143 commit b332e47, with no blocker or major finding; its minor (the package documentation's Catalogue section, stale since D140, now naming F3-11 and F3-12 and forty-one types) and nit (the Date line's F3-11 clause qualified by the two `SubjectRef` additions) were applied to the record commit before the freeze.
- **Changes:** `CONTRACTS.md` (the `ledger-format` row's text, owning step and status, the B2 table's C3 row and the F3-12 catalogue); Core §13.1 (the F3 freeze clause); eight comment-only replacements in `contract/ledger` (three in `doc.go`, including its Catalogue section, two in `review_values.go`, and one each in `catalogue.go`, `review.go` and `review_submission.go`); the evidence file `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/f3-12-freeze-evidence.md`; and See also lines under D125 and D140. The C3 section of `ROADMAP-CORE.md` states review and failure requirements but no freeze status, and is unchanged.
- **See also:** D148's shared turn-child and unit reading records that a `runtime.paid_receipt` carrying `usage` charges one local unit on the named F3-11 scopes, as `runtime.review_receipt`'s usage does, and that F3-12's text, bytes, receipts, lookups, group shapes and golden are unchanged and F3-13 groups embed no F3-12 record.

### B33. C3: exact-subject review and failure results (ticket 12)

- **Date:** 2026-10-24. **Decided by:** assistant. Under D117, the build advisor's [ruling](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/ticket-12-design-ruling.md) on the [design brief](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/ticket-12-design-brief.md) ended ticket 12's design step without a question to the owner and reserved nothing to him. None of its conditional owner gates was triggered: OG-D52, OG-BREAK and OG-DEP stayed untriggered through the build. The S4 review added a fifth slice, S5, under D117 and D116 (item 4); it changed no ticket order, so no question went to the owner. The build's choices and the project manager's answers during the build are the assistant's, within the authority the owner delegated, and settle nothing outside the ruled design. The project manager froze F3-12 under D125 (D144). Every review was a fresh build advisor (Fable), and no stand-in reviewer ran.
- **Owner's words (2026-10-24, chat):**
  - answering the overnight log's question 3, at 11:35: "3. The next ticket shall be Ticket 12 then 13"
  - answering the project manager's report, which named ticket 12's design and build as next, at about 12:12: "PM-Alpha is closed you are approved to proceed"
- **Question:** Ticket 12 builds C3's review and failure decisions in `internal/core/agents`: typed requests and results bound to a task's exact version, assignment, subject, acceptance, check and configuration digests, evidence, authorization revision and deadline, with duplicates idempotent, stale or cross-context callbacks deciding nothing, and task state that no chat, board or narrative replaces. It writes F3-12, which the project manager froze (D144). What did the build settle?
- **Decision:**
  1. **The design as ruled** (P1–P15 and the ruling's ten findings), with S5's correction (item 4). The package documentation, `internal/core/agents/doc.go`, states the service as built, its cost paragraph as S5 rewrote it; the brief and the ruling give each choice's options and sources. The choices later tickets build on:
     - Review is semantic. A named, currently authorized reviewer judges an artifact or an exact `private_revision`; the service refuses every other subject kind, a promotion generation, a gate receipt or a passed check among them, so an approve completes a task and promotes nothing (P2, P3).
     - `check_sha256` and `config_sha256` are bound digests that the result repeats and nothing verifies; no record asserts that a check ran (P4, finding 1).
     - A nonterminal task holds at most one current request, which advances no task version and which a new request replaces only by naming it. The live request states are pending and rejected: a reject keeps the request, and a pending request past its deadline stays pending, because nothing expires. Either keeps guarding until it is replaced or the task is cancelled, reassigned or re-versioned. There is no timer, retry or lease (P5).
     - The authority to request or decide is the decision's own: `task.complete` for a review and `task.fail` for a failure, with the reviewer among the chain's recipients and both participants' read rights on the subject and every evidence occurrence. A callback rechecks the current chains, never the colony's `authority_version` (P6, P7, finding 4).
     - An approve or fail result is the task transition, through the same kernel, in one group with the task description's unpin and without a `runtime.task_changed` record; a reject records the judgment and keeps the guard. A consumer of tasks reads both families in ledger order (P8, finding 6; D144).
     - A direct complete or fail refuses `ErrReviewRequired` while a pending or rejected request holds the task's version and assignment, the owner's included; cancel and reassign stay open (P9, finding 5).
     - Time comes from the clock seam `serviceDeps.reviewClock`, sampled after the duplicate check and again under the lock before `Submit`, and the second sample is the stored observation. A request needs its sample before the deadline, and a result at or before it. A sample below the floor, which replayed envelopes, prior observations and every sample this incarnation has read raise, refuses `ErrReviewClock`. Open, queries and retries read no clock (P10).
     - With S5 on `main`, the ordinary path adds no whole-history scan: a fresh request proves its ID new by a `maxRequest` watermark, source witnesses are read from the segment that holds them, and the artifact-read authority check loads only the artifact's own task (P13, finding 2; item 4).
  2. **The project manager's answers that changed a reviewed rule or the ruling's text,** each confirmed by its slice's review; the [evidence](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/ticket-12-evidence.md) lists every answer:
     - S3a's answer 2: a group whose receipt outcome is unknown is refused at preflight and at Open as inconsistent. The S2 review's note 16, which had asked that such groups replay as unapplied, was withdrawn by its author.
     - S3a's answer 3: the frozen reader refuses a cursor inside a commit, so the ruling's literal `After: source_sequence-1, Through: source_sequence` gave way to a segment-aligned cursor, whose `After` is the first sequence of the segment holding the smallest witness, minus 1. `sourceRecord` took the same cursor.
     - S3b's answer 1: a direct F3-11 fail carrying `private_revision` evidence stays `ErrDenied`, ticket 11's class for every non-artifact task subject, not the `ErrInvalid` that the S3a review's nit asked for.
     - S3b's answer 2: the service ignores a stray `core/state.json`, so finding 7's forged-file row, whose single-file premise was wrong, forges `core/state.json` and `Memory.md` together.
     - S3b's answer 3: the guards run in two passes, and within the brief's step 4 trusted time comes before subject rights, so a late caller without them gets `ErrReviewExpired`, which discloses nothing.
  3. **The reviews' findings that set behaviour,** each carried to a later slice:
     - S1's minor, deleting `reviewUnixNano`, done in S3b, and its nit, dropping `guardReviewTask`'s unreachable terminal-state clause, done in S3a.
     - S2's minor, the package documentation's Catalogue section naming forty-one types, applied in the freeze record.
     - S3a's minor, `loadTask`'s cold scan restored to the per-record pattern, and its three test nits, closed in S3b.
     - S3b's minor, `ReviewReceipt.ThroughSequence` meaning two things: S4 deleted the exported field, which had no caller before ticket 15. `internal/core/agents` is not a contract (D45), and `Sequence` and `EffectSequence` locate the receipt. S3b's two nits, closed in S4: every successfully read clock sample raises the incarnation floor, and the prose test's inert check also covers the direct guard and the book.
     - S4's two `doc.go` nits, folded into S5: the cost paragraph names no unexported identifier and states the retry's cost as built.
  4. **A fifth slice, S5, ruled by the S4 review.** S4's labelled measurement found a cost regression in ticket 11's artifact-read authority check. Every new review decision and every replayed F3-12 group calls that check for each participant and each reference. It loaded every task the participant's grants named before it knew which task the artifact belonged to, and for a finished task each load is a whole-history scan. A decision was therefore O(G·H) and Open O(N·G·H), against finding 2, a major, which required the ordinary path to add no whole-history scan. The review ruled one bounded fix slice before the close rather than a referral to ticket 140, whose criteria are written on ticket 11's driver and operations. Its ruling is S5's design (D117), with no designer brief. The check resolves the artifact's source once, compares the task that source names with each task grant before loading any task, and loads only the matching one. It adds no cache, index or retained state and changes no accept or deny outcome; only corrupt or unreadable data behaves differently. The ruling expected a decision to cost O(G + witness batch) and Open O(N·(G + segment)), P13's bound, which S5's rerun bears out (Limits).
- **Limits:**
  - Nothing is frozen, approved or claimed here beyond D144's freeze. Review and failure decisions serve test agents only: nothing admits colony work, creates gate state or promotes (D52).
  - No paid holds, provider turns, executor, concurrent restart proof, task-fact listing, resource registration or cost index (the ruling's scope; tickets 13–16, 89, 139 and 140).
  - The ruling's five limits, recorded at its request:
    - after a reject, F3-11's frozen task table has no way from submitted back to active, so the ways on are a replacement and then an approve or fail, a cancel or a reassignment; revise and resubmit needs a later type version;
    - a pending failure request on an active task goes stale when the assignee submits or waits, because the task's version moves, and the requester requests again;
    - a requester must hold the decision authority it delegates (P6), so an assignee without `task.complete` cannot request its own review; the completing authority or the owner does;
    - the guard binds the owner, whose escapes are P9's: replace the request naming himself as reviewer and resolve it, cancel, or reassign;
    - a backwards system clock refuses new review decisions until it passes the floor (P10).
  - S4's [labelled measurement](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/ticket-12-evidence.md#s4s-labelled-measurement) ran to 2.07 times the baseline history, not the brief's planned 10 times. The project manager's answer during S4, which the S4 review confirmed, set about twice for a run under 45 minutes, so the 10× figures are projections, not measurements. Before S5, on a quiet machine and under the CPU profiler:
    - `agents.Open` took 43.9 s at 1,047 records and 330.6 s at 2,163, against the store's 75 ms and 134 ms;
    - one decision took 1.16 s and 6.0 s;
    - the read-rights path took 56–75 % of the profiled CPU;
    - the coder's run under load measured 119.8 s, 800.5 s, 1.91 s and 16.06 s.

    After S5, quiet and under the profiler, the driver rerun on d37ca15 put the read-rights path at 0.000 of every profile: at 2× (the project manager's rerun) `agents.Open` took 14.7 s at 1,047 records and 50.7 s at 2,163 (exponent 1.70; the coder's run 13.2 s → 47.8 s, 1.78) and one approve 84 ms → 140 ms (the coder's 85 → 160 ms); the coder's run on the tree whose hashes equal d37ca15's blobs reached a measured 10.04× (H 10,515, N 868, G 328): `agents.Open` 15.6 s → 1,191.1 s (exponent 1.88) and one approve 94 → 671 ms (0.85). What remains of Open's growth is ticket 10's and 11's per-scope history validation (about 45 % of its CPU) and scoped replay (23–29 %), with the review path's witness batch at 9–11 %, the ruling's O(N·(G + segment)) in a one-segment log; the driver's 10× label was changed by the project manager's answer, hashes recorded, and the 10× log's unmeasured-10× line is false for that run ([evidence](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/ticket-12-evidence.md#s5-the-artifact-read-check-order-2026-10-24)).
  - What ticket 140 still inherits (P13, as the S4 review restated it):
    - the F3-12 groups in its index, since task state needs both families;
    - the cold request and receipt lookups as index sites;
    - the witness batch, which in a one-segment log reads up to each witness's position.

    `sourceRecord`'s from-0 scan is no longer among them, because S3a moved it to the segment-aligned cursor.
  - CI is paused (D143), so no hosted run covers any of ticket 12's commits, and the brief's §6.4 counts a missing run as missing evidence. The rows ran on one Windows machine; portability rests on each check's `go vet`, staticcheck and cross-builds for the five targets.
  - As the brief's §6.4 bounds it, the evidence is semantic task and review bookkeeping, injected crashes, metadata-only references and Windows measurements, with no runtime executor, C4, power-loss, A2 or whole-C3 claim.
- **Evidence:**
  - Ticket 12's closing comment lists each criterion's tests, from the ruling's acceptance coverage checked against the tests on `main`. The [ticket's evidence](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/ticket-12-evidence.md) gives each slice's coder and runs, rule breaks, checks, fuzzing and the project manager's answers, S4's measurement and the state of CI.
  - The coders, a fresh one for every slice at the owner's word of 14:12, which ticket 12's comments quote: CoderT12S1, CoderT12S2, CoderT12S3a and CoderT12S3b ran on Codex. CoderT12S3b paused when Codex reached 98 % of its window, and CoderT12S3bOpus (Claude Opus 5.5, effort high) finished S3b at the owner's "Fresh Claude coder now". CoderT12S4 and CoderT12S5 (role `coder-claude`, Claude Opus 5.5 at effort xhigh) built S4 and S5.
