### D145. A rewritten history for the public repository, and private records

- **Date:** 2026-10-25. **Decided by:** owner. He decided to make the repository public, what its history must no longer hold, the three new documents and the full rewrite. His question-tool picks among the project manager's options (the commit identity, the agent files' removal from history and a private records repository) and the cutover the project manager described, which he started, are adopted.
- **Question:** The owner asked what making the repository public would change. Before it goes public, what must leave its history, how is the history rewritten, and where do the records it no longer holds live?
- **Owner's words (chat, 12:06):**
  > Alright, there are several documents that I would like untracked and removed from history in that case. The windows username, laptop details, games mentioned, verbatim quotes need to go. I would like a README.md, a SECURITY.md and an ARCHITECTURE.md. I'd like to rewrite the entire history.
  > The evidence files don't need to be tracked either. That seems to be the source of most of the local-only information. I will verify the cleaned history before making the repo public
- **Owner's words (chat, 12:12):**
  > Decisions.md should be untracked as well
- **Owner's words (chat, 12:14):**
  > References to decisions.md need not be changed since they are vital to the workflow but no one should be able to access the DECISIONS file
- **Owner's answers (question tool, 12:22):** the name on every rewritten commit: "Darius-PJ"; the agent files in old commits: "Remove them"; how to keep the untracked records: "Private records repository".
- **Owner's words (chat, 13:54):**
  > I've updated email and name settings. This is intended to be a professional github profile. So my name stays, hints 'Darius-PJ' as the username. The new repos have been created and the old one has been renamed. The current state looks clean. You can initiate the switchover. https://github.com/Darius-PJ/dwarf-harness-records & https://github.com/Darius-PJ/dwarf-harness are the new repos
- **Decision:**
  1. **Repositories.**
     - `Darius-PJ/dwarf-harness` is a new repository holding the rewritten history. The owner makes it public once he has verified the history.
     - The original repository, renamed `Darius-PJ/dwarf-harness-archive`, stays private with the original history, `main` at 3bc46c4. Its local copy is `Documents/PROJECTS/DwarfHarness/dwarf-harness-archive.git`.
     - `Darius-PJ/dwarf-harness-records`, also private, tracks the records of item 4.
  2. **The rewritten history.** Each commit of 3bc46c4's history was rebuilt in order, one for one: 207 commits, ending at fee7a16, the counterpart of 3bc46c4. 3c9db5c adds `README.md`, `SECURITY.md` and `ARCHITECTURE.md`. In that history:
     - **Identity:** every author and committer is `Darius-PJ <173397901+Darius-PJ@users.noreply.github.com>`, GitHub's ID-based private address for his account. Dates and time zones are unchanged.
     - **Removed paths:** no commit holds `docs/DECISIONS.md`, `.scratch/dwarf-harness/*-evidence/`, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md` or `.omp/`. The 23 commits that changed only those paths stay, empty.
     - **Personal details:** reviewed line replacements were applied to every version of every other Markdown and Go file, and to commit messages: 251 lines in 151 files, and 27 message lines. They removed:
       - his quoted words and the times of his messages;
       - his Windows user folder;
       - the development machine's processor, memory, Windows build, antivirus settings and exclusions, administrator rights, power plan and heat throttling;
       - the programs he ran;
       - remarks about his budgets and spending.
     - **Code and test data:** at the tip, only two Go comments differ, in `internal/core/workspace/doc.go` and `internal/core/ledgerstore/workload_test.go`. Test data is byte-identical.
     - **Citations and links:** the 642 commit hashes cited in Markdown and in messages name the new commits. Links and references to `docs/DECISIONS.md` and the evidence folders are unchanged, so they do not resolve in a clone, as the README says.
  3. **The project checkout** is on the published history.
     - Its `.git/info/exclude` lists `/docs/DECISIONS.md` and `/.scratch/dwarf-harness/*-evidence/`, beside D135's and D136's lines.
     - Its repository-level Git identity is item 2's, for every worktree.
  4. **Private records.**
     - `docs/DECISIONS.md`, the evidence folders, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and `.omp/agents/` stay at their paths in the project checkout.
     - The records repository tracks them and nothing else. Its Git directory, `Documents/PROJECTS/DwarfHarness/dwarf-harness-records.git`, takes that checkout as its work tree.
     - Its first commit imports the records unchanged. Its second translates the commit hashes their Markdown cites, 715 in 49 files, with the map it adds as `.scratch/dwarf-harness/rewrite-evidence/commit-map.txt`.
  5. **Quoting** (`AGENTS.md`, and the drafter's role file).
     - Only `docs/DECISIONS.md` and the evidence folders quote the owner.
     - Every file the project repository tracks, and every commit message, states what he decided and cites the entry.
     - None of them quotes him, names his Windows user folder, or describes his machine, its settings or the programs he runs.
- **Limits:**
  - These GitHub steps are the owner's:
    - making the repository public;
    - private vulnerability reporting;
    - protecting `main`;
    - Issues, Wiki and Discussions.
  - CI stays paused until his final decision (D143). Resuming it restores `check.yml`'s push trigger, which is a code change.
  - Pushes to either repository wait for his word (D31, D46, D141).
  - No step's status changes.
- **Consequences:**
  - Old commit hashes resolve only in the archive, and the map translates them. CI runs 1–14 belong to the archive's Actions history.
  - A ticket's close becomes two commits. The ticket file and the status and register changes go to the project repository. Its brief, ruling, evidence and B entry go to the records repository. A D entry lands in the records repository alone.
  - Every push to the project repository publishes. Before each push, the outgoing commits are scanned for credentials and for the owner's name, addresses, Windows user folder and machine details.
- **Amends:** D42 (the remote's visibility); D116 item 3 (one documents commit per ticket); D135 and D136 (the agent files are committed, to the records repository).
- **Evidence:** the [evidence file](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/rewrite-evidence/public-history-evidence.md) and the tools, maps and reports beside it.
- **Changes:**
  - this entry, with "Amended by D145" lines under D42, D116, D135 and D136, and this log's status line;
  - `AGENTS.md`'s decision-record rule, and the drafter's role file;
  - the records repository's three commits;
  - the project checkout's `.git/info/exclude`.
- **Amended by D146:** the repository is public, CI runs once per ticket, and a ruleset protects `main`. Issues, Wiki and Discussions await the owner's decision.

### D146. CI resumes once per ticket, and a ruleset protects `main`

- **Date:** 2026-10-25. **Decided by:** owner. He decided that CI resumes once the repository is public and runs once per ticket, never per slice, and asked for the rule that blocks force pushes to `main` and its deletion. He made the repository public, turned on private vulnerability reporting and created the ruleset himself. His question-tool picks among the project manager's options (creating the ruleset himself, with the settings the project manager listed, and pushing the resumed workflow at once) are adopted.
- **Owner's words (chat, 14:57):**
  > I want Issues, Wiki and Discussions to be tracked in the dwarf-harness-records repo. Once dwarf-harness is public we can Resume CI tests but they should only run per ticket and not per slice. Please add the rule that blocks force-pushes to main and blocks deleting it. I have turned the main repo public and turned on private vulnerability reporting
- **Owner's words (chat, 14:58):**
  > I need guidance on Issues, Wiki and Discussions for public repos, disregard the statements concerning them earlier
- **Owner's answers (question tool, 15:09):** how the ruleset is added: "I've added it myself"; when the resumed workflow is pushed: "Push now".
- **Question:** With the repository public, does CI resume, how often does it run, and how is `main` protected on GitHub?
- **Decision:**
  1. **The repository is public.** `Darius-PJ/dwarf-harness` is public, with private vulnerability reporting on, as `SECURITY.md` asks. The owner made both changes on 2026-10-25, as D145 left them to him.
  2. **CI resumes, once per ticket.**
     - 8ad40fa restores the `push` trigger that D133 set and D143 removed: a push to `main` that changes more than Markdown starts the three-OS check. Manual dispatch stays.
     - A push starts one run, and pushes go once per ticket, after its close (D141). So CI runs once per ticket and never per slice.
     - Slice merges stay local, gated by the local check (D116, D129).
  3. **The minutes cost nothing.** GitHub Actions is free for public repositories that use standard GitHub-hosted runners ([GitHub's Actions billing](https://docs.github.com/en/billing/concepts/product-billing/github-actions)), and all three jobs use them. D133's budget therefore no longer limits runs.
  4. **A ruleset protects `main`.**
     - The owner created the branch ruleset "Protect Main" (id 25833473): enforcement active, targeting the default branch.
     - It holds two rules, "Restrict deletions" and "Block force pushes". GitHub's public API lists exactly `deletion` and `non_fast_forward` for `main`.
     - Its bypass list is visible only to admins; the steps he followed left it empty.
     - Pushes that fast-forward `main` are unaffected. A history rewrite like D145's needs the ruleset disabled first.
  5. **The first run.** The push of 8ad40fa at 15:13:18 started run 1 of the repository's Actions, id 40224996258 (https://github.com/Darius-PJ/dwarf-harness/actions/runs/40224996258), at 15:13:26. It is ticket 12's run, which the pause had left without one. Its result is recorded like every run (D133).
- **Not decided here:** Issues, Wiki and Discussions.
  - The owner withdrew his first sentence about them and asked for guidance first, so their settings are unchanged.
  - At 15:01 GitHub's public API reported Issues on, Wiki and Discussions off, and pull requests open to everyone.
- **Unchanged:** pushes wait for the owner's word (D31, D46, D141), and the local check still gates `main` (D42, D46, D128, D129).
- **Amends:**
  - D143: the pause ends;
  - D133 items 2 and 3: the push trigger returns, and the budget no longer limits a public repository's standard runners;
  - D145's Limits: CI, and the protection of `main`.
- **Evidence:**
  - The local check on 8ad40fa, in the gate worktree, exited 0: `check PASSED: 8 of 9 steps passed, race skipped (D129) in 466.7s`. The full check had passed on its parent, 3c9db5c (D145's evidence).
  - The push printed `3c9db5c..8ad40fa  main -> main` and `PUSH_EXIT=0`, and `ls-remote` showed 8ad40fa62c0b8f247546b17412b6377916516a77.
- **Changes:**
  - this entry;
  - in the project repository: `.github/workflows/check.yml`, `README.md`'s CI sentence, C3's Status line in `ROADMAP-CORE.md` and its row on the status board of `ROADMAPS.md`;
  - "Amended by D146" lines under D133, D143 and D145, and a "See also" line under D141.
- **Amended by D153:** item 2 and the Unchanged local-gate/push clauses give way to work-branch CI, exact-SHA `Check result` before fast-forward `main`, and routine guarded pushes without owner approval; a `main` push starts no run.

### D147. Toolchain: go1.27.3

- **Date:** 2026-10-25. **Decided by:** adopted. Ticket 13's first slice adds Core's TLS client for paid provider calls, and the check's vulnerability scan then failed on it: GO-2026-6809 (CVE-2026-111943, "Distrust compromised intermediate certificates in crypto/x509"), found in go1.27.2's `crypto/x509` through `x509.Certificate.Verify` and fixed in go1.27.3, released 2026-10-19, whose only changes are security fixes to `crypto/x509`. `main` was unaffected, because nothing in it verified certificates. The project manager proposed moving the pin before the slice could merge, and the owner approved.
- **Owner's words (question tool, 19:31):**
  > Move to Go 1.27.3 now
- **Decision:** go1.27.3, installed permanently with the official installer, replaces go1.27.2. `go.mod` keeps `go 1.27` and declares `toolchain go1.27.3`; the check reads that pin and runs with `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`. CI downloads go1.27.3's official archives, each checked against its published SHA-256 and size: `go1.27.3.windows-amd64.zip` f26081f438319757f8a1b46d7d0b2c5ec7583d4038e485e0aa725cfde7885fff (79,010,309 bytes), `go1.27.3.linux-amd64.tar.gz` 65efbc80fdc1fa2cd2631cad5b481777a68e8725f34e89f50fc6ac714e379b40 (70,588,678 bytes) and `go1.27.3.darwin-arm64.tar.gz` dd3908cf20959d798c1e06ebe5b72213f0505ce01373b40ce3110975a6cd99c2 (68,196,737 bytes). The rest of D28, D106 and D133 stands.
- **Installation:** the project manager downloaded `go1.27.3.windows-amd64.msi` from go.dev, checked its SHA-256 (89ec8325f667c674fe1b94a5a88e26932aa12323589881927ff54da99d4b7faa) and installed it elevated, after the owner accepted the Windows prompt; `go version` reports `go1.27.3 windows/amd64`, and `go.exe`'s SHA-256 is 83b7ba66215e696414fa49462d0db2da4f4cf15c7bebd42f0b189fa04866999d.
- **The Go analyzer's qualified tuple** (ticket 61; D132 item 5; D134) becomes go1.27.3 windows/amd64 with that `go.exe`. The check's descendant proof, `TestGoListHasNoDescendants` under `DWARF_GRAPH_GO`, requalified it on 64c4afb, in both the tests and the race steps.
- **Changes:**
  - in the project repository, commit a2cf109: `go.mod`; `.github/workflows/check.yml`'s three Go archives with their SHA-256s and sizes, and its two version checks; the two tests that verify the installed toolchain (`internal/undercurrent/graph`, `internal/core/graphfoundation`) and a derived fixture's `go.mod`; the art sample's `provenance.json`, whose toolchain name is the only change when the sample is regenerated; core §8.1; `ROADMAP-CORE.md` C0; `README.md`; system specification implementation decision 5 and a dated comment; fortress terminal research step 1;
  - in the project repository, commit 5cabee1: core §8.2's CI records (the archives the workflow installs, and the toolchain llvm-mingw serves);
  - in the project repository, commit ffcca82: the Go analyzer's native test request names go1.27.3, beside the two tests above; and the graph's wrong-version row uses go1.26.0, below the module's `go 1.27`, so that it never names the running toolchain;
  - "Amended by D147" lines under D28, D45, D106 and D133; D133's says that items 1 and 2 now name go1.27.3's archives, digests and sizes.
- **Unchanged:** measurements taken with go1.27.2 (core Appendix A, core §8.2's binary-size records, ticket records) stay as recorded. Ticket 61's S5 record of the go1.27.2 tuple stays as that slice's history; ticket 61's next documents note the new tuple. Three test fixtures pin the installed toolchain, and a toolchain move changes exactly these: `installedGoTools` (`internal/undercurrent/graph/go_test.go`), `foundationGoConfig` (`internal/core/graphfoundation/helpers_test.go`) and `nativeRequest` (`helpers/go-analyzer/main_test.go`). Every other version string in the tests is a fixture that runs no toolchain and stays as it is.
- **Evidence:** ticket 13's notes record the failing scan (S1's gate on 00c97ad, `check FAILED: 2 of 9 steps failed`, with govulncheck at step 4 on all five targets) and the full check with the race step on 64c4afb under go1.27.3: every step passed but the tests and the race steps, which failed only on two version pins, both fixed in ffcca82: the Go analyzer's native test request still named go1.27.2 (three tests), and the graph's wrong-version row named go1.27.3, now the running toolchain. The vulnerability scan passed on all five targets, and regenerating the art sample left the tree clean. On ffcca82, `go run ./tools/check -race=false` (D129; the race step had run on 64c4afb): `check PASSED: 8 of 9 steps passed, race skipped (D129) in 521.4s`; and the two fixed packages' tests, run again under the race detector, passed. They land with ticket 13's close, beside the fix-up's probe evidence.

### D148. F3-13 paid attempt and accounting records frozen

- **Date:** 2026-10-25. **Decided by:** assistant. Under D125, the project manager froze F3-13 without a question to the owner, once the build advisor had approved ticket 13's S2 together with this record and its evidence file. The build advisor's design ruling for ticket 13 (P8, findings 2 and 3, and nits 11, 12 and 14), which lands as `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/ticket-13-design-ruling.md` with the ticket's close, specifies this eight-type family as a separate family that changes no F3-11 or F3-12 byte, rule, receipt, lookup, group shape or golden, and leaves its freeze to the project manager, with no owner gate triggered. It makes the zero-settlement rule a fact of the dated, hashed, expiring profile instead of a constant in code, binds the profile's digest in the route submission and carries the profile once, in the route record, and gives the shared turn-child and unit reading recorded below. No freeze packet was written.
- **Decision:** F3-13, ticket 13's part of F3, is frozen by addition to `ledger-format` version 1 (D40, D111, D125), at commit 687ab01, which reached `main` unchanged:
  - eight record types, each at version 1, in C3's `runtime.` namespace, named as `Defined()` names them: `runtime.paid_route_configured` (`TypeRuntimePaidRouteConfigured`), the owner's configuration of a paid route with its complete provider profile; `runtime.credential_changed` (`TypeRuntimeCredentialChanged`), which binds or revokes a credential version and carries only its non-secret source locator; `runtime.spend_configured` (`TypeRuntimeSpendConfigured`), a scope's spend terms, set by the owner or delegated by an agent to a child grant; `runtime.paid_reserved` (`TypeRuntimePaidReserved`), an admitted attempt, which installs the same full finite hold in every scope; `runtime.paid_dispatch_intent` (`TypeRuntimePaidDispatchIntent`), the intent to send, which means SENT, not provider acceptance or success; `runtime.paid_result` (`TypeRuntimePaidResult`), an attempt's ending or financial evidence; `runtime.paid_overrun_acknowledged` (`TypeRuntimePaidOverrunAcknowledged`), the owner's acknowledgement of a bound violation, which retires a profile without refunding its charge; and `runtime.paid_receipt` (`TypeRuntimePaidReceipt`), recording the command's schema-4 canonical submission and outcome. `Defined()` lists forty-nine type versions: F0, F2-05 to F2-09, F-U3-61 and F3-10 to F3-13;
  - their value shapes, decoder rules and record and group rules as the F3-13 section of the `contract/ledger` package documentation states them: the compact key order of every payload, of the schema-4 submission and of the nested `PaidScope`, `SpendTerms`, `CredentialRef`, `SemanticHeader`, `SealedDescriptor`, `PricedDimension`, `CostBound`, `PaidUsage`, `NoChargeEvidence` and `PaidLocalUsage`, with private objects as `ObjectRef`s; strict complete UTF-8 JSON, duplicate-key refusal at every depth, even inside ignored extensions, required and never-null known members, ignored unknown optional members, canonical UUIDv7s and nonzero lowercase digests; money and counts as plain nonnegative JSON integers through MaxInt64, never floats, exponents or clamped values, exact rates as `0|[1-9][0-9]*` strings over positive denominators, and `version` equal to `expected_version` plus one; ordered sets nonnull, sorted bytewise and distinct, and object lists kept in order, nonempty when present and never repeating a digest; and each payload's conditional members, among them a route's observation exactly when it is enabled, `ceiling_micros` required in `finite` mode and it and `warning_ppm` forbidden in `unlimited` mode, a colony scope without an ID and every other scope with one, at least four scopes on a reservation, `usage` and `no_charge` never together, both predecessor fields exactly on a correction, an `unknown` result with an `error_class` but no usage, no-charge evidence, cost, messages or predecessor, and a reservation's `input_object` equal to its descriptor's `body_sha256` and `body_bytes`, so that the input object is the exact sealed request body. The record and group rules are one `[F0 command, exactly one effect, runtime.paid_receipt]` group per operation, every record with the operation ID equal to the command ID and one origin and turn: the owner or the delegating agent on a control, with no turn; the agent on an admission's command and receipt and `core` on its effect; `core` on every record of an intent, result, release or correction, at the admission's origin and parent turn; and the owner on an overrun acknowledgement, with no turn. Route configuration is owner CAS, a capability version permanently binding its full profile digest and a tariff version its tariff digest; a bind introduces a fresh credential-version ID, revocation is irreversible, and a restart never silently rebinds a mutable source. Admission recomputes the colony, agent, credential, optional task and leaf-and-ancestor grant scopes, requires enabled terms and current chain and task authority, and installs one full finite hold atomically in every scope; `unlimited` removes only the cap, and delegation changes no hold and cannot expand its parent's terms. Admission and intent check current authority, caps, credential, the exact seal, route and profile identity and trusted nonregressing time strictly before expiry, against one shared observation floor. A reservation ends in an intent (SENT) or `released_unsent`; SENT in `unknown` or a financial settlement; `unknown` in another evidence-bound `unknown` or a final settlement; and a settlement admits only a duplicate or an explicit correction, which replaces the cost by one delta in the original admission period and never releases twice. Exact usage and cost beyond the bound are recorded unclamped, with a violation that fences every intersecting scope until an evidence-backed correction or the owner's acknowledgement, which keeps the charge and retires the profile digest across routes. `Open` reduces ledger facts only and reads no paid artifact bytes; for a pending attempt it may at most stat the input object. The service enforces these record and group rules;
  - the closed sets and open tokens: result `kind` is closed to `released_unsent`, `unknown`, `usage`, `no_charge` and `correction`; the credential `action` to `bind` and `revoke`; spend `mode` to `finite` and `unlimited`; `period_kind` to `lifetime`, `utc_day` and `utc_month`; the scope kind to `colony`, `agent`, `credential`, `task` and `grant`; the profile `schema` to 1; and schema 4's operations to `paid.route.configure`, `paid.credential.bind`, `paid.credential.revoke`, `paid.spend.configure`, `paid.spend.delegate`, `paid.admit`, `paid.dispatch`, `paid.release`, `paid.observe`, `paid.correct` and `paid.overrun.acknowledge`. The open tokens match `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$` with at most 64 bytes; usage classes are dot-separated such tokens of at most 64 bytes in all, and region, tiers, speed and source topics are open tokens of at most 64 bytes. Decoders preserve unfamiliar well-formed values, which confer no permission to dispatch or release money: the provider; the receipt `outcome`, where version-1 writers use `configured`, `bound`, `revoked`, `delegated`, `reserved`, `sent`, `released_unsent`, `unknown`, `settled`, `no_charge`, `corrected` and `acknowledged`; `error_class`, for which the package documentation names no version-1 writer value; `source_kind`, where version-1 writers use `environment` and `windows_credential_manager`; the usage classes, where supported Anthropic usage carries exactly the five exclusive classes `input.cache_read`, `input.cache_write_1h`, `input.cache_write_5m`, `input.uncached` and `output.generated`, with reasoning counted once inside output; and the members of `no_charge_rules` (finding 3), whose version-1 writer value is `anthropic_refusal_before_output_2026_09_24`, an unfamiliar rule being held as unknown, never released and never a refusal to `Open`;
  - the profile and its digests (finding 3; nit 11): the profile digest's preimage is the compact JSON of the profile's known fields, not its source JSON, in the exact order `schema` (1), `capability_version`, `tariff_version`, `provider`, `authority`, `method`, `path`, `api_version`, which is always present, `model_id`, `region`, `requested_tier`, `allowed_served_tiers`, `speed`, `context_max`, `input_max`, `output_max`, `usage_schema`, `cache_classes`, `tool_choices`, `no_charge_rules`, `tariff`, `verified_utc_ns`, `expires_utc_ns` and `sources`, with the tariff as `{currency, rates}`, each sorted rate as `{class, numerator, denominator}` and each source as `{topic, url, retrieved_utc_ns, quote}`, sorted by topic and URL; the tariff digest covers the compact `{currency, rates}` likewise. Both use Go `encoding/json`'s escaping, so `<`, `>`, `&`, U+2028 and U+2029 become `\u003c`, `\u003e`, `\u0026`, `\u2028` and `\u2029`, and a direct profile encoding agrees with its bytes inside the route record; S2's agreement test shows that `internal/core/providers` computes the same digest. The profile occurs once, in the route record: `paid.route.configure` binds `profile_sha256`, and later records carry digests and versions;
  - the request and quote digests as producer rules: with `frame(b) = u64be(len(b)) || b` over unnormalized UTF-8, the request hash frames `router-provider-request/v1`, the provider, HTTPS authority, model, region, `POST`, escaped path, requested tier, credential scope ID and credential version, then the header count as `u32be`, each sorted header name and value, and the exact body bytes; authentication is never an input, and version-1 writers use exactly the `accept`, `anthropic-version` and `content-type` semantic headers. The quote hash frames `router-cost-quote/v1`, the raw 32-byte request hash, the capability and tariff versions, the tariff hash as lowercase hex text (P7), `USD` and the canonical decimal maximum, then the component count and each component's encoding, sorted bytewise; overlapping classes refuse. The producer reserves the full `min(input_max, context_max)` at the largest eligible input rate, independently of the requested output, plus the requested output at the largest output rate, with one final upward rounding and no subtraction or double count;
  - the 64 KiB cap (`MaxPaidPayloadBytes`, 65,536 bytes) on every complete payload and schema-4 submission, including whitespace and extensions, and on encoder output; producer collection, stream and installation limits are not decoder limits;
  - schema 4's canonical submission form as a producer rule: SHA-256 of compact JSON with keys `schema` (4), `actor`, `origin`, `operation`, `grant_id` and `arguments`, in that order, `grant_id` occurring exactly for an agent's `paid.admit` or `paid.spend.delegate` and for Core's `paid.dispatch`, `paid.observe`, `paid.release` and `paid.correct`, every other operation being the owner's. Each operation's arguments follow the order the package documentation gives: those of `paid.route.configure` end in `profile_sha256` (nit 11), `paid.admit`'s argument grant matches the top-level grant, and `paid.observe`'s kind is `unknown`, `usage` or `no_charge`. Derived scopes, period, observations, authority, usage, cost and new versions, the command ID, secrets and handles are not caller inputs, and a completion command ID is supplied freshly after the network, never reserved during intent. The receipt embeds its submission, whose digest is checked by re-encoding it, and binds it to F0's command and the resulting entity version; `attempt_id` occurs on it exactly for an admission, dispatch, observation, release, correction or overrun acknowledgement, and `usage` exactly for an agent's delegation (P8). Schemas 1 to 3 are unchanged, and, as for them (D139, D140, D144), a later change to this producer rule must be a visible decision;
  - the shared turn-child and unit reading, the ruling's reading (finding 2), originator `assistant`, in the words the package documentation gives it: (a) a `runtime.paid_reserved` whose `parent_turn_id` names a turn is a child of that turn; F3-11's rule that a turn's released/cancelled/interrupted ending requires an ending record for every child counts paid children; a `runtime.paid_result` of any kind is that child's ending, `unknown` and `released_unsent` included, and `unknown` retains its money; (b) a `runtime.paid_receipt` carrying `usage` charges one local unit on the named existing F3-11 scopes, as `runtime.review_receipt`'s usage does; (c) a consumer of F3-11 alone cannot reconstruct the turns, child endings or non-paid book of a log that holds F3-13 and must not be used on one: it would accept a turn ending while a paid child is unresolved and undercount consumed local units; (d) F3-13's admission is the first consumer of an external grant's `model.call`; F3-11's `unavailable/producer_absent` refusal stays for the other external operations; (e) F3-11's and F3-12's text, bytes, receipts, lookups, group shapes and goldens are unchanged, and F3-13 groups embed no F3-11 or F3-12 record. An external grant names a paid route by the `resource_key` `paid-route/<route UUIDv7>`, and no F3-11 kind is changed;
  - the period note (nit 14): the first colony spend configuration fixes the colony's period kind, a disabled configuration included, and other scopes match it; a period ID is derivable from `admission_utc_ns` and the colony's period kind, so a later change of kind can be added by addition.
- **Not frozen:** `PaidLimits` and their defaults, the profile's 32 KiB installation limit and the other producer guards, while replay accepts larger historical values within the payload cap; which source topics a provider's profile must carry, the supported models, tiers and classes, and the other installation rules of `internal/core/providers` (`ParseProfile`), so that an unfamiliar provider's profile stays decodable; the byte encodings of the output and observation objects, which the agents service writes in ticket 13's S3; the records name them by digest and size only, the input object being the sealed body itself, and the golden's output and observation files are fixtures; the `internal/core/agents` and `internal/core/providers` services and their APIs, as distinct from the frozen producer and record rules they must obey; later F3 families in tickets 14–16 and 139; C7's `launcher-protocol`; and R1's `context-module`.
- **Consequences:** these types change only by addition (D40): later well-formed values of the open tokens, such as another provider, source kind, usage class or no-charge rule, need no new type version, and new types or versions come through their own freeze records. A breaking change needs the owner's decision entry listing every affected step (D40, D125). This freeze is S2's D41 sign-off for reaching `main`; after it, ticket 13's S3a, S3b and S3c may build the reducer, the owner controls and admission, and dispatch and settlement. `ledger-format` stays at version 1. No step's status changes, and nothing here supplies a live provider call, a verified live profile, production spend configuration, provider turns, an executor, accepted promotion or real colony-work readiness.
- **Evidence:** the [evidence file](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/f3-13-freeze-evidence.md) gives the commands and results.
  - The golden sample `contract/ledger/testdata/f3/13/log/00000000000000000001.jsonl`, SHA-256 cd406c4f0d3ef2d7a51a42a0d0b7706074b0f5dabf75dd986b3f1839b55e8eca (the committed blob), 48 commits and 150 records, with its object and body fixtures under `contract/ledger/testdata/f3/13/`. It holds all eight types and all eleven schema-4 operations, every result kind, owner and delegated spend terms, a credential rotation that revokes the previous version and a revocation, a usage settlement after a UTC day rollover, a correction, a documented no-charge refusal under the profile's `no_charge_rules`, a billed refusal, an unclamped overrun with its acknowledgement and a route revised after it, and an unlimited colony scope whose attempt still carries a finite quote. Its profiles are synthetic, with loopback authorities and `https://localhost/` sources, and their source quotes hold `&` and `<`, so the route records pin the profile bytes and `profile_sha256` together. Every earlier golden stays byte-identical, F3-11's and F3-12's included, keeping D111's, D115's, D118's, D119's, D123's, D124's, D130's, D139's, D140's and D144's digests.
  - V3, `FuzzDecodeF3_13` for 120 s and the `FuzzProviderProfile` rerun: on 687ab01, `FuzzDecodeF3_13` ran 120 s, 2,626,156 executions with 67 new interesting inputs, and passed; the `FuzzProviderProfile` rerun ran 120 s, 532,251 executions with 80 new, and passed; no corpus file was left in either package's `testdata`. V8, golden regeneration and earlier digests: on 687ab01, `-update` exited 0 and left `testdata` unchanged, and every earlier golden's SHA-256 equals the digest its D entry records: D111's three logs, D115, D118, D119, D123, D124, D130, D139, D140 and D144. V4, the record-commit check: `go run ./tools/check -race=false` on the published record commit 34330e1 exited 0: `check PASSED: 8 of 9 steps passed, race skipped (D129) in 549.6s`, with no cached package.
  - The review: On 2026-10-25 the build advisor (Fable) approved ticket 13's S2 at 687ab01 together with this record and its evidence, with no blocker or major finding; its minor (the input object is the exact sealed request body, so only the output and observation encodings stay unfrozen) and one nit (a `doc.go` sentence stating that an arguments object carrying another operation's member is refused) were applied to the record before the freeze, its nit on the golden's request-body key order was accepted without regeneration, and its notes for S3 were carried.
- **Changes:** `CONTRACTS.md` (the `ledger-format` row's text, owning step and status, the B2 table's C3 row and the F3-13 catalogue); Core §13.1 (the F3 freeze clause); nine comment-only edits in `contract/ledger` (four in `doc.go`: its F3-13 heading; two in its Catalogue section, which now names F3-13 and forty-nine types and corrects F3-12's reference to D144; and one sentence in its schema-4 section stating that an arguments object carrying a member of another operation's argument set is refused; and one each in `catalogue.go`, `paid.go`, `paid_profile.go`, `paid_submission.go` and `paid_values.go`); the evidence file `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/f3-13-freeze-evidence.md`; and See also lines under D125, D140 and D144. The C3 section of `ROADMAP-CORE.md` states reservation and settlement requirements but no freeze status, and Core §13.10 names no paid record; both are unchanged.
- **Amended by D149:** the golden sample is regenerated with the owner's `task.start` after the four grant issues, so that Core's `Open` replays it; its SHA-256 is 50b41cf739cbef89bec234cf64873e80af85188b256c66d6cb1e86ebea3555ff; the types, rules, type sequences and every earlier golden stand.

### D149. The F3-13 golden corrected so that Core replays it

- **Date:** 2026-10-26. **Decided by:** adopted. Ticket 13's S3a coder found that the F3-13 golden frozen with D148 does not replay through Core's real `agents.Open`: at sequence 27 the owner starts task 0x300 before its assignee holds `turn.start` on that context, whose grant group came at sequences 40–42, and the agents service requires it: "The owner's task-start entrance requires only the assignee's current turn-admission chain" (the `internal/core/agents` package documentation, section "Scoped authority, tasks and accounting"). Core refused it with `agents: inconsistent record at sequence 27: invalid scoped transition: agents: denied`. The contract's tests check only codecs and group rules, and on `main` no service consumed F3-13 yet, so neither S2's tests nor its review could see it. The project manager offered three fixes: correct the golden inside S3a, a separate fixture slice first, or keep the golden and prove S3a on another log. The owner chose the second.
- **Owner's words (question tool, 10:04):**
  > Separate fix first
- **Decision:** a fixture-only slice, S2b, moves the owner's `task.start` for task 0x300 from sequences 27–29 to 40–42, after the four grant issues (0x410–0x413, now 27–39), in the golden's generator, `contract/ledger/paid_golden_test.go`, and regenerates the golden, at commit cd3221f, which reached `main` unchanged. No record type, field, rule, codec or package-documentation text changes, and every earlier golden is byte-identical. The new golden is `contract/ledger/testdata/f3/13/log/00000000000000000001.jsonl`, SHA-256 50b41cf739cbef89bec234cf64873e80af85188b256c66d6cb1e86ebea3555ff (48 commits, 150 records), replacing D148's cd406c4f0d3ef2d7a51a42a0d0b7706074b0f5dabf75dd986b3f1839b55e8eca; its scenarios, types, group shapes and fixture identities are D148's, with the sequences, positional command and commit IDs, commit event times and chain hashes that follow from the new order; no submission digest, version or authority version changes, and every F3-13 record keeps its sequence (43–150). Core's `Open` replays the whole log; its F3-13 records pass unconsumed until S3a's reducer reads them.
- **Unchanged:** D148's freeze of the types, their shapes, decoder rules, record and group rules, closed sets and open tokens, digests, the 64 KiB cap and the schema-4 submission. If S3a's reducer finds a defect in the golden's paid groups, a further entry amends the golden again, as the option he chose described. `CONTRACTS.md` is unchanged: its F3-13 catalogue records no digest and only sequences 43–150, which stand.
- **Evidence:** a throwaway probe test opened the golden with Core's `Open`: before the fix it refused at sequence 27 with the error above, and after it replayed every record through sequence 150 with no reconciliation issue and the durable sequence unchanged; the coder's final runs on cd3221f's files: `contract/ledger` plain 8.063 s and race 37.398 s, `internal/core/providers` 2.823 s and 10.97 s, and the consumers `internal/core/agents` 57.262 s and `internal/core/ledgerstore` 29.381 s, all ok, with gofmt, vet and staticcheck clean for the five targets; the PM's rerun of the probe in the gate worktree reached sequence 150 with no issue; `-update` exited 0 and left `testdata` unchanged, and every earlier golden still equals its D entry's digest; on the commit that reached `main`, `go run ./tools/check -race=false` exited 0: `check PASSED: 8 of 9 steps passed, race skipped (D129) in 556.2s`, with no cached package; the review: On 2026-10-26 the build advisor (Fable) approved ticket 13's S2b at cd3221f together with this record, with no blocker or major finding: the generator moves only the owner's `task.start` after the four grant issues, the probe shows Core's `Open` refusing D148's golden at sequence 27 and replaying the corrected golden through sequence 150 with no issue, every F3-13 record keeps its sequence and submission digest, the fixture objects and the twelve earlier goldens are byte-identical, and its minor record corrections (the rule's citation, the scope of the reorder, what changes with the order, and the unchanged `CONTRACTS.md`) were applied before the entry landed.. The evidence file `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/f3-13-freeze-evidence.md` gains a section for this correction, with the probe's source and logs.
- **Changes:** this entry and an "Amended by D149" line under D148; in the project repository, only the generator and the regenerated golden log (cd3221f); the evidence file's new section.

### D150. The authorized live drill moves to ticket 14, against OpenAI

- **Date:** 2026-10-26. **Decided by:** modified. Ticket 13's design ruling, which lands as `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/ticket-13-design-ruling.md` with the ticket's close, made one optional owner ask: a keyed Models lookup for the exact token limits and one live Anthropic Haiku 4.5 attempt in S4, in a throwaway colony with USD 1.000000 in each scope (its gates OG-LIVE, OG-MODEL-FACTS and OG-SPEND). Once S3c had merged, the project manager asked for it, recommending the drill in S4. The owner authorized an OpenAI drill instead, with a key he provisions in an environment variable. Ticket 13 builds only Anthropic's Messages API, so the project manager asked where that drill belongs, recommending ticket 14, which adds OpenAI Responses, and the owner chose ticket 14. The change from the proposal: the drill calls OpenAI instead of Anthropic, and runs in ticket 14 instead of ticket 13's S4.
- **Owner's words (question tool):**
  - 16:38:
    > Authorized for an OpenAI drill in S4, use the environment variable 'Codex_API'
  - 16:43:
    > Ticket 14's drill
- **Decision:**
  1. Ticket 13 makes no live call: no keyed Models lookup and no Anthropic attempt, so no live profile is verified, and its close reports local TLS/SSE and injected-crash evidence only. OG-LIVE, OG-MODEL-FACTS and OG-SPEND are not run in ticket 13.
  2. Ticket 14's design plans the authorized OpenAI drill. The key is read only from the environment variable the owner named; it is never asked for in chat or written to a file, argument, log, fixture or repository, and no file of the project repository names the variable. The drill runs only after ticket 14's local evidence passes; its model facts, finite spend ceiling and dispatch gates are ticket 14's to settle under its own ruling.
- **Changes:** this entry. At ticket 13's close: its Answer and evidence, and a dated comment on ticket 14 that names no variable.
- **Evidence:** the project manager's notes for ticket 13 record both questions with their options, and both answers (2026-10-26, 16:23 to 16:43).

### D151. `tools/check` enforces the x/sys importer allowlist

- **Date:** 2026-10-27. **Decided by:** assistant, implementing B18 item 7. B18 item 7 left B3's narrow `golang.org/x/sys` rule to review, because D45's row for `internal/core/...` admits `golang.org/x/...`, and made "a check rule, an allowlist of x/sys importers with its own decision entry" due no later than the arrival of a second importer or C3's CI step. Both had passed: `internal/core/winexec` became the second importer with ticket 45 (B30 item 4), and CI has run C3's code since D146. Ticket 13 adds a third, `internal/core/credentials`, whose Windows source reads Credential Manager through `advapi32.dll`. Ticket 13's S1 review asked that its close either schedule the rule or revise B18 item 7 with a reason; the project manager built the rule in ticket 13, as slice S4b.
- **Decision:** the import step of `tools/check` admits `golang.org/x/sys` and every package below it only in:
  - exactly `internal/core/durable` (B3, B18), `internal/core/winexec` (B30 item 4) and `internal/core/credentials` (ticket 13, B34), with their own test variants, never a package below them;
  - the test variants of `internal/core/agents`, whose Windows native-credential test loads `advapi32.dll` through `windows.NewLazySystemDLL`; its production code may not import it.

  The rule covers every target the step already checks, and a violation names the importer, the import and this entry. D45's rows are unchanged, so other `golang.org/x/...` modules stay as D45 admits them. A new importer needs a decision entry and an allowlist change, not review alone. No module is added (D29).
- **Changes:** `tools/check/boundary.go` and `tools/check/boundary_test.go` (S4b, d8336c8); this entry; a "See also" line under B18.
- **Evidence:** S4b's tests (nine `TestImportBoundary` rows on fake-module fixtures: each listed importer, the agents test variants, an unlisted package importing the module root, a subpackage and a Linux-only file, and the message) and breaks (A, `credentials` dropped from the list; B, every test variant admitted; C, only the module root matched: each red, restored byte-identically, green), and the real repository's import step clean on all five targets; the full check with the race step on d8336c8: `check PASSED: all 9 steps passed in 1600.0s`, uncached, after a first run whose race step failed only in `internal/core/winexec` (`TestSeparateRunsCannotReadPeerInput`), a package this change does not touch; the review: a temporary GPT-6 Astra reviewer, standing in for the build advisor while Fable was held at the owner's instruction, approved S4 and S4b with record-only changes, all applied, and a second stand-in confirmed the merge; a fresh Fable advisor verifies the whole ticket after its weekly reset.

### D152. A faster build: focused tests, two to four slices, blocking-only reviews, no routine stops, design scaled to the ticket, checks on GitHub

- **Date:** 2026-10-27. **Decided by:** adopted. Ticket 13 took 36 hours from its start to its last merge. Asked whether process steps slow the coders, with the end of December as the target, the project manager measured the ticket's runs and proposed seven changes, among them running each slice's check on GitHub's hosted runners so that the laptop no longer limits how many tickets run at once. The owner approved five of them and GitHub's runners, and later a sixth, a fresh project-manager session per ticket. His first list of rule-break areas left out money (holds, charges and settlement), which the proposal named beside permissions, credentials, crash recovery and the sandbox; he then said that was unintentional, so the proposal stands as written. He left two proposals unanswered: the coders' reasoning level and how to reach the December target.
- **Owner's words (chat, 12:22):**
  > Coder tests can be more focused: permissions, credentials, crash recovery, and the sandbox. Two to four slices per ticket is approved. Review blocks on a lighter standard. Eliminate the routine approvals that caused overnight stalls. Scale effort of designers to match ticket complexity. If we can run checks on Github let's go with that. Are ther any rules or security measures that should be added to Github in that scenerio? I want the repo to be public for the benifit, not to open it up to tampering
- **Owner's words (chat, 12:50),** after applying the GitHub settings the project manager listed:
  > Settings are changed, Github is up to the new standard. money was left off of the list unintentionally. I will also clear the session after each ticket
- **Decision:**
  1. **Coder tests.** A coder runs the focused tests of the code it changes and one plain, verbose run of each package it changes, with `gofmt`, `go vet` and staticcheck for the host. It adds a whole-package race run, and the ×20 race repetition of the affected rows, only when the slice changes locking, goroutines, channels or other shared state, and the five-target vet and staticcheck only when it changes files with build constraints; the check on the exact commit runs everything else. Rule breaks, each red on a named test and green again after a byte-identical restore, cover only permissions (grants, authority and disclosure), credentials, crash recovery, the sandbox and money. A measurement runs only where the ticket or its ruling asks for a figure, sized to answer it (D121).
  2. **Slices.** A ticket is built in two to four slices.
  3. **Reviews.** A finding blocks a merge only when it shows behaviour contrary to an acceptance criterion, the ruling or a frozen contract; an exposed secret or credential; an authority, permission, disclosure or sandbox bypass; a wrong charge, hold or settlement; data loss, corruption or a broken crash recovery; or, for one of these, a missing test or one that cannot fail on the bug it claims to catch. Any other finding is a note for the next slice or the close: it starts no fix-up round and needs no confirmation pass. The fix of a blocking finding gets a short re-review limited to it.
  4. **No routine stops.** The project manager no longer asks the owner:
     - before starting the next ticket on the frontier: the instruction of 2026-10-12 that D116 item 5 lifted for slices no longer applies to tickets either;
     - when a provider's usage window reaches 0.95: he moves the roles on that provider to the other one and reports it, as the owner decided on 2026-10-24 and 2026-10-26, with stand-in reviews while Fable's window is held and a fresh Fable advisor verifying those tickets after its reset; only when both providers are at 0.95 does the build stop, at the next slice boundary;
     - before going on without an optional owner ask, such as a live drill: he asks once and builds on until it is answered.

     What still waits for the owner: D52 approvals, breaking changes to frozen contracts (D40, D125), toolchain changes (D28), new dependencies and owner-decision documents (D29, D41), anything that needs his keys, money, accounts or machine, and the art sign-offs (D104, D105). Pushing waits for him (D31, D46, D141) until item 6 takes effect.
  5. **Design effort.** A small ticket (one package, and no record, contract, provider, credential, money or sandbox change) gets a short brief from the project manager, ruled in its first slice's review. A medium ticket (a new record family, or a change across packages) gets a designer's brief from a source list, without readers, and a fresh advisor's ruling. A large ticket (providers, credentials, money, the sandbox or a contract freeze) is designed as ticket 13 was, with readers and a ruling.
  6. **Checks on GitHub.** Each slice's check runs on GitHub's three hosted runners on the exact commit, and `main` moves only to a commit whose check passed there; the local full check stops being the merge gate. This takes effect once the workflow change and the repository rules that protect it are in place, recorded in their own entry; until then D141, D146 and the push rules stand.
  7. **A project-manager session per ticket.** The owner clears the project manager's session after each ticket's close; the project manager leaves the handoff ready for the next ticket as the close's last step.
- **Unchanged:** a fresh coder for each slice; fresh reviewers; D145's rules for the public repository; the scan before every push.
- **Evidence:** the project manager's [analysis](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/process-speed-analysis-2026-10-27.md) of ticket 13's transcripts and the coders' command records: about 9.5 coder hours outside the measurement, 6.7 of them the model writing code and tests and 2.8 running commands; the ticket's 36 hours also held about 11 hours waiting overnight for an owner answer and about 8 hours of measurement, 4.7 of them a superseded first run.
- **Changes:** this entry; an "Amended by D152" line under D116; the evidence file; in the records repository, the verdict section of the advisor's role file. The project manager's procedures and the coders' standing context changed outside the repositories.
- **Amended by D160:** item 2's slice count allows two more slices after a failed preregistered bound; item 3's reviews are one per slice and one at the close; item 4's stand-in approvals close tickets, with Fable's audit after; item 7's handoff is written once per session, which runs up to three tickets.

### D153. Each slice is checked on GitHub before `main` moves

- **Date:** 2026-10-27. **Decided by:** adopted. Implements D152 item 6: the project manager proposed the work-branch workflow and repository safeguards, CoderCIPerSlice built the workflow, and temporary GPT-6 Astra stand-ins reviewed it under D152's blocking standard. The owner applied the settings and then confirmed the required checks' GitHub Actions source.
- **Owner's words (chat, 12:50):**
  > Settings are changed, Github is up to the new standard.
- **Owner's confirmation (chat, recorded 22:00):**
  > Adjusted, Github actions is the source of these checks for the rule
- **Activation:** public run 3, [40612562217](https://github.com/Darius-PJ/dwarf-harness/actions/runs/40612562217), passed the classifier, all three full-check jobs and `Check result` on exact commit 8aea12c771e06807f26ae2a605b94414ca916d8a. The owner's subsequent confirmation clears the required-check selection/source blocker. The PM completes this record, AGENTS.md and the procedure cutover before promoting that reviewed, passing SHA; no CI rerun or protection bypass is needed.
- **Decision:**
  1. **Work-branch CI.** `.github/workflows/check.yml` runs on pushes to `build/**`, `docs/**` and `fix/**`, and on manual dispatch. A push to `main` starts no run: `main` advances only to an already-checked branch commit. Per-ref concurrency cancels superseded runs.
     - `changes` compares paths since the branch's merge base with `main`. A non-Markdown path or manual dispatch runs the full Windows, Linux and macOS check, race included. On pushes, Markdown-only or empty diffs skip the matrix.
     - `Check result` succeeds only if classification succeeded and the matrix either succeeded or was skipped for that exemption. Failed, cancelled or missing prerequisite outcomes do not pass.
  2. **Exact-SHA integration.** Rebase onto current `main` as needed, then obtain fresh D152-standard review approval and successful hosted `Check result` on the exact final SHA before fast-forwarding and pushing `main`. A fix or rebase creates a new SHA and needs its own run. Strict up-to-date mode is off; the fast-forward procedure preserves the checked subject.
     - The local full check is no longer an additional routine merge gate. Coders use D152's focused local checks; local full checks remain available for diagnosis.
     - Markdown-only branches still require the PM's link/LF document check (D128), which the workflow does not run. The exemption assumes no tracked Markdown is an input to code, embeds, tools or tests; the current-tree inventory supported that premise. Revisit it if such an input is introduced.
  3. **Repository protection.** "Protect Main", id 25833473, retains deletion and force-push blocks and adds linear history and required `Check result` from GitHub Actions, with no bypass. Leave the up-to-date requirement and creation exemption off.
     - The owner reported the recommended Actions settings applied: GitHub-created actions only, full-length SHA pins, approval for all external contributors' fork workflows, read-only workflow permissions and no PR creation/approval by Actions. The workflow itself has no PR trigger or secret references.
     - The reported standard also includes repository secret push protection, Dependabot alerts without its PRs, collaborators-only or disabled Issues/PR participation, and Projects off. Projects off and the baseline rules were publicly observable; the remaining settings are owner-reported. The pre-pin API snapshot's empty check list is historical, not a contradiction of his subsequent adjustment.
     - The requested Vercel/collaborator/deploy-key/webhook inventory has no separate confirmation. Do not infer either retained access or removal. The unauthenticated API's `bypass_actors: null` does not independently establish the bypass list.
  4. **Routine pushes.** The PM pushes guarded slice branches so CI runs during review, then pushes approved, passing fast-forward `main`, without a routine owner question or a once-per-ticket wait. Private records push separately and start no project CI.
     - Changes under `.github/` still require actual owner sign-off recorded in a decision entry. `DWARF_ALLOW_WORKFLOW=D<entry>` is the local guard's mechanical override, not authorization by itself.
     - Every other D152 item 4 owner gate remains: D52, breaking frozen-contract changes, toolchain, dependencies and owner-decision documents, keys, money, accounts, machine and art sign-offs.
  5. **Outgoing-commit guard.** The untracked main checkout's `.git/hooks/pre-push`, shared by project worktrees, calls `pre_push_scan.py` beside it. It scans every outgoing commit's patch, message, identities and changed paths, not only the endpoint diff.
     - Both modes refuse merge histories, whose own changes or simplified side histories could escape the scan. Public mode also refuses credentials, private paths, personal details, the drill variable name, any identity other than D145's published one, and unsigned-off `.github/` changes. Credentials, identity and privacy have no override.
     - Machine-term hits require a contextual human read. The private records hook uses the same script with `--records`, checking credentials and merges without rejecting D145's intended private material.
  6. **Trust boundary.** These safeguards limit public tampering, not malicious authorized writers. A required check identifies a job name and reporting app, not the implementation it ran. A writer could replace a job or weaken `tools/check`; the hook is bypassable with `--no-verify`, another client or the API. GitHub's path-restricting push rules are unavailable to this public repository. `Check result` means full-check-or-document-exemption, not that every commit ran the suite.
- **Amends:** D116 item 6 (hosted exact-commit gate); D129 (race in each hosted full check, local race only under D152); D141 (push cadence and routine owner approval); D146 item 2 and its local-gate/push clauses (work-branch CI and hosted gate). D128's document check stands.
- **Evidence:** the [CI cutover evidence](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/ci-per-slice-evidence.md), the four workflow-branch commits ea2e6f4, edef6ef, d663698 and 8aea12c, and public run 3 above. StandInCIPerSliceConfirm approved guarded publication and conditional promotion after clearing the merge-history major in both hooks. Fable double-checks the complete change after its reset.
- **Changes:** this entry and four one-line amendments; AGENTS.md's Check/Commits lines; private evidence; the five managed process skills. The workflow branch updates README, Core §8.2 and tickets139/140. Prepared Markdown followthrough 93e8f0a updates README/dispatch wording, code-slice wording, ROADMAPS integration rule 3 and the paired C3 Status/board without closing ticket13; it follows workflow promotion and requires its own exact-SHA aggregate and document check.

### D154. F3-139 registration and declaration records frozen

- **Date:** 2026-10-28. **Decided by:** assistant. Under D125, the project manager freezes the completed contract without an owner question or packet. The amended Ticket139 ruling governs (the amended design ruling, retained with the whole-ticket packet and to be archived as `.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/ticket-139-design-ruling.md` at close); design approval is not completed-contract approval. **Joint S1/specification/fixtures/evidence/record review, by GPT-6 Astra as temporary stand-in for Fable:** GPT-6 Astra, acting as a temporary stand-in for Claude Fable 5.1, approves Ticket139 S1 at 86f952cfcae37bde06767c6c4eaa615a503dd96d, parent 82d8d48856c7191019625c9f6e896d10b9b8498c, jointly with its completed F3-139 specification, generated fixtures, observed evidence and proposed 14-edit D125 freeze record. No blocking findings remain. This is technical approval for PM freeze recording, not a claim that the freeze has landed or D153 integration gates have passed. Fable remains the main advisor; its whole-ticket double-check after reset is pending.
- **Decision:** add four version-1 types to `ledger-format` version 1: `runtime.resource_registered`, `runtime.work_domain_registered`, `runtime.declaration_changed` and `runtime.registration_receipt`. Freeze their documented strict codecs, shapes, ordered sets, versions and 65,536-byte complete payload/submission cap (`contract/ledger/doc.go:2560-2745`). C3 owns payloads; only Core's agent service through `ledgerstore.Submit` produces them. Consumers are Core owner metadata queries/replay and Ticket61's later independently authorized reader; all known fields and required envelope/submission/receipt bindings are replay-essential.
  - Freeze schema 5's three CAS operations, `resource.set`, `work_domain.set` and `declaration.set`, canonical root `schema, actor, origin, operation, arguments`, documented compact known-field SHA-256 encoding, and exactly `[F0 command, matching effect, registration receipt]` with common owner/origin/command bindings. Operation/concrete arguments bind the target without a redundant target field.
  - The registration UUID and explicit opaque F3-11 external address are separate. Exact immutable/unique address ownership is service enforcement, not codec history lookup. No URL/account/credential semantic inputs, resolver or derived-key fallback; the grammar is not a secret detector. Identities are kind-namespaced. Registered audiences scope evidence, never permission; declarations pin the source version current at commit, obey source/task/context audience constraints and completely replace consumers, including withdrawal. Source revisions do not retarget declarations; existing grants remain independent.
- **Not frozen:** implementation/APIs; `MaxRegistrations`/`MaxDeclarations` and their 256/1024 defaults; later families, graph/action facts, views, provider work, `launcher-protocol` and `context-module`. Population limits do not constrain replay or matching retries. Earlier schemas 1–4, types, rules and goldens are unchanged.
- **Evidence:** code `86f952cfcae37bde06767c6c4eaa615a503dd96d`, base `82d8d48856c7191019625c9f6e896d10b9b8498c`; the [evidence file](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/f3-139-freeze-evidence.md) separately records the final public freeze-record SHA/hosted gate. The generated 19-commit/65-record golden, SHA-256 `db1476e07b355d909dfb07cce79f142387469618709bc52a3f496395b94c5134`, places a real F3-11 root grant before registration and covers all registration/declaration shapes, revisions, mixed consumers, source repins and withdrawals. That file gives observed sequences/all fixture hashes, D152 focused checks/PM fuzz, unchanged earlier fixtures including D149, and actual Core positive/negative prerequisite-probe/restoration evidence; the probe does not validate the later registration reducer.
- **Consequences:** additive-only under D40; breaking changes still need the owner. This D125 freeze is S1's D41 sign-off, not D52 readiness or operational producer/graph/provider proof. D153 gates exact-final-public-SHA integration. Retain whole-ticket Fable verification after the temporary stand-in review.
- **Changes:** `CONTRACTS.md` definition/ownership/status, C3 B2 row and catalogue; Core §13.1; five comment-only status edits across `registration.go`, `registration_submission.go`, `catalogue.go` and `doc.go`; this entry, D125's pointer and the evidence file. No ticket-close or roadmap-status change.

### D157. Additive ledger-reader checkpoint capability frozen

- **Date:** 2026-10-29. **Decided by:** assistant. Under D125 and D156, the project manager freezes the completed C1 Reader increment within Ticket140's one fifth-and-final assignment, without a new owner question or packet. The original brief (SHA256 93bf8a13f847d03626672caae2a77f35e97ddfcc968b63568dd4ea12bc81cc55) and actual fresh Astra ruling (SHA256 ef2dfa6612bf2114dcb3229d474d841389df749d868fdb1dfb9bfb36ca702611), including its P7 correction, govern. D156's adopted sequencing choice was privately published at b63d618c76b864ec08855bd78eadcf3e27cf7588; it was not technical approval or an upfront freeze. **Joint completed-code/evidence/exact-record review:** Temporary GPT-6 Astra stand-in reviewer (openai-codex/gpt-6-astra) jointly approves completed C1 code 81ae94d3e92b5fd5574f46046c42fc9c5e30ce6d, completed M1 evidence SHA256 c6405bf018387cc66d72ead83156a3a6959828dd03f0b50be318706bc22f364c, and exact proposed R1–R4 record/fill-map SHA256 192487c0cf06973c15d2f5e8589b38bae638e86bd38acd9eb10e0d44a59e6383 as a nonbreaking addition to ledger-reader version 1 under D125/D156. Only the enumerated later factual fills may proceed without routine note-confirmation; material changes require affected proof/review. This is not Core release, numerical acceptance or whole-ticket verification. Fable double-checks the whole ticket after its reset.
- **Decision:** freeze the nonbreaking optional addition to existing `ledger-reader` version 1: `type ReadCheckpoint struct`, `func OpenReaderWithCheckpoint(logDir string, opts ReadOptions, checkpoint *ReadCheckpoint) (*Reader, error)` and `func (r *Reader) Checkpoint() *ReadCheckpoint`. C1 owns it; registered consumers remain those in `CONTRACTS.md`. The fixed-width private-field token has exactly the ruled thirteen fields, no pointer-bearing or retained-resource payload, and is returned as a fresh value copy. It is process-only, with no persistent format, constructor, marshaler, unmarshaler or raw-token import. Its checksum detects accidental damage, not hostile same-process forgery.
  - The Reading guarantee remains whole commits and verification of every read record, consumed or not: hash, chain, sequence, commit framing, colony and time. Previously completed Reader verification plus this call's fresh equality of every framed byte in the containing-segment prefix and all fields of the freshly read predecessor chain establish the same prefix invariants. Revalidate context, numeric advancing bounds/exact After, segment selection/continuity, version/checksum/shape and base chain before restoration; hash the full prefix with bounded 64-KiB scratch on the same descriptor used for suffix reading. Restore only the complete after-chain at the absolute logical offset; the suffix retains the original parser/verifier and this open's consumption/version behavior.
  - Only a fully processed numeric Through, terminal EOF, matching cursor/verifier sequence, complete final chain and successful actual Close can issue a checkpoint. ToEnd/equal bounds, early Close, partial commits and read/close failures issue none. Missing, damaged, mismatched, rotated, short or byte-changed proofs use ordinary scanning while retaining opt-in checked-close mode. Failure to derive optional Abs-then-Clean path identity disables proof capture/use, not ordinary reading. Genuine non-EOF I/O, seek and close failures remain errors.
  - Retain the first actual opt-in close failure across automatic EOF/error closure, transition, explicit and repeated Close, predecessor-helper and discarded-candidate closure; stop before opening a next segment after transition-close failure. Normal EOF cannot erase a close failure, and failed closure forbids issuance. Legacy `OpenReader` and `Check`, `ReadOptions`, `Commit`, signatures and existing consumers retain their behavior. See the completed package documentation at `81ae94d3e92b5fd5574f46046c42fc9c5e30ce6d:contract/ledger/doc.go:156-176` for the detailed mechanics.
- **Not changed or established:** ledger-format, record families/catalogue/Defined count, schema, fixtures or goldens; appender/ledgerstore, Submit/Receipt, Digest unmarshaling or privateStore; existing consumers or Core/Undercurrent step status. Prefix I/O and SHA-256 remain. The proof inherits the existing append-only writer protocol, not a filesystem snapshot, hostile-rewrite atomicity, OS isolation or power-loss guarantee. There is no C3 integration, scan-free/O(1), capacity/provider/executor/SLA or new numerical acceptance claim.
- **Evidence:** implementation `81ae94d3e92b5fd5574f46046c42fc9c5e30ce6d` on `84d0ced9446168777c7b7bfa3382974977bf27ed`; all five committed source hashes match. The [Reader evidence](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/ticket-140-s5-reader-freeze-evidence.md) binds actual continuation/options/prefix/predecessor/fallback and real-file-close observable proof; four PM-accepted one-predicate semantic reds, exact restores and same-command greens. Four serial120s-budget/parallel1 campaigns passed: ReaderCheckpoint27147, Truncation14993, TornTail285 (one new interesting input), Corruption14993; actual package/native durations and raw outputs are retained separately from the budget. The first wrong-path launcher failed before any Go; no campaign repeated. The pre-freeze prefix oracle is `TestReaderCheckpointChangedPrefix`, never a yet-unauthorized Core Service test. Focused run: 21 top-level/60 PASS rows. The single plain run: 84 top-level tests + 15 top-level fuzz seed runs, 9382 PASS rows, 0 FAIL/0 SKIP, package 7.301s.
