### D158. Ticket140-specific exception for one sixth and final slice

- **Date:** 2026-10-29. **Decided by:** adopted. The project manager presented the completed fifth-slice cohort and the choice between one Ticket140-only sixth slice and holding the ticket open. The owner selected the assistant-written option below. Like D155, this changes the slice-count limit for this ticket only, not D152's general rule; for Ticket140 alone it supersedes D155's and D156's statement that no sixth slice is implied.
- **Owner's selection (chat):**
  > Authorize one sixth slice
- **Option as presented:** "Fresh root-cause/design review, one bounded task.create fix, then all seven original bounds, final reviews and CI. No relaxed safety or performance requirements."
- **Decision:** permit one sixth and final Ticket140 slice, solely to bring the long-history `task.create` latency within its unchanged original bound.
  - It builds on the fifth slice's unpublished candidate `2bce72e610ec9380c08314645f0ecea7e3a44ea8`. One bounded root-cause diagnostic, a fresh design brief and a fresh technical ruling come before any code; the selection approves no mechanism.
  - Any Reader, ledgerstore or contract change must be named and separately ruled first. A change to a frozen contract, D157's included, or an owner-reserved or breaking change still needs the owner.
  - All seven numerical bounds, the original driver traffic and the fixed eligibility/noise rules stay unchanged, as does the single 256-entry/8 MiB charged-metadata limit. No larger cache, persistent snapshot, traffic reduction, threshold relaxation, unsafe command-absence assumption or suppression of authoritative errors is approved.
  - The final combined SHA needs its own focused local and fuzz proof, a fresh final review, its own full hosted check and the original measurement protocol before `main` moves. No seventh slice or further scope is implied if this one fails; Ticket140 stays open until its quantitative and whole-close gates pass.
- **Evidence:** the original-protocol cohort on exact `2bce72e610ec9380c08314645f0ecea7e3a44ea8` has three eligible instances per driver, all six exit 0, no exclusions and no noise extension. Both startup bounds and four of the five incremental bounds pass. `task.create` fails: median 101.9633 ms against its 70.1972 ms bound (twice its short-history median, 22.5986 ms, plus 25 ms); all three paired differences are positive. Analysis SHA256 `5c0a31d9d3dfb43486edd7f058176b90fac1aeb1c6a92ed8207b4957de7a3be9`. Focused, plain, race, affected ×20 and four 120 s fuzz runs pass on that SHA. PM-local sources: `t140-s5-campaign-summary.json`, `t140-s5-core-local-complete.json` and `t140-s6-owner-selection.json`.
- **Unchanged:** D152's other rules; D153's exact-SHA review, hosted-check and fast-forward publication gates; D157's frozen Reader capability; all semantic and frozen-contract requirements; the separate Fable whole-ticket verification. Settled C1 and fifth-slice proofs are not reopened.
- **Changes:** this entry. No implementation, interface, schema, fixture, dependency, toolchain, workflow, ticket status, B allocation or numerical acceptance is changed by recording it.

### D159. Ticket140 sixth slice: Open seeds the acknowledgement checkpoint

- **Date:** 2026-10-29. **Decided by:** assistant. Under D158, a fresh technical ruling by the Fable advisor (claude-fable-5-1) approves the sixth slice's design with conditions; the project manager records it before assigning the coder, as the ruling requires. Design brief SHA256 `2888377df3f439d55e1a8cdf5d893d3e38a1fd29de4ab6cda47acf054e26e090`; ruling SHA256 `08b099c6eaf1b868616afc6deea2f08e7433df0f99061c0856932fccbb72e1d8`. Both are retained PM-local as `t140-s6-design-brief.md` and `t140-s6-design-ruling.json`.
- **Cause it addresses:** the one D158 diagnostic on `2bce72e` puts 120 of the long-history `task.create` call's 125 ms sampled CPU in the acknowledgement read: the call is the first scoped command after the service reopens, Open's replay uses the legacy Reader and captures no checkpoint, so that acknowledgement re-reads and verifies the whole containing-segment prefix (`t140-s6-diagnostic.json`).
- **Decision:** `openService`'s initial replay opens with D157's frozen `OpenReaderWithCheckpoint(logDir, opts, nil)`, drains through the unchanged `scanReader` and reducer, and, only when Open succeeds, publishes the minted checkpoint through the existing acknowledgement cache entry at the saved replay-final frontier. The ruling's amended fifth-slice rule, verbatim:
  > Exactly two sites may publish the single historyAckRead entry: (a) a successful nonduplicate contiguous scoped acknowledgement, installing the checkpoint returned by its own readCommittedRange at the new frontier; and (b) a successful openService, installing the checkpoint returned by its own initial replay - opened with OpenReaderWithCheckpoint(logDir, opts, nil), drained by the unchanged scanReader and reduced by the unchanged acceptCommitLocked - at the saved replay-final openedThrough, immediately before the successful return and after inspection and the pending/unbound views. A reopened Service is cold until its own Open seeds it; a failed Open or OpenPaid seeds nothing.
  - Every other historical scan and every `refreshLocked` advance stay on the legacy Reader and publish nothing; the review path still discards its proof; every accepted advance still removes the entry; `Close` still clears and disables the index.
  - An actual descriptor-close failure during that replay now returns from `Open` and `OpenPaid` instead of being ignored. The ruling classifies this as nonbreaking: `internal/core/agents` is not a contract package, and no register row, owner-reserved item or ticket criterion covers ignoring OS close failures. No owner gate.
- **Owned paths:** `internal/core/agents/service.go` (`openService` only), `internal/core/agents/scoped_receipt_test.go` (`TestScopedAcknowledgementPrefixChanged` only, adding an `open` provenance row that first asserts the seed exists) and `internal/core/agents/doc.go` (comments only).
- **Proof required:** focused, plain, vet, staticcheck, module and import checks; the `wrong_session` mutant (discarding the replay acceptor's error) red, byte-identical restore and green; `FuzzScopedGroupReplay` for 120 s on the final SHA; then the unchanged original cohort for all seven bounds, a fresh final combined review, the final SHA's own hosted check, guarded publication and the separate whole-ticket verification. The ruling does not require a local race run or ×20 repetitions (no locking change; the hosted check runs race), nor the other three fuzz targets.
- **Unchanged:** D157's frozen Reader; the single 256-entry/8 MiB charged-metadata limit; all seven bounds, driver traffic and eligibility/noise rules; D153's gates.
- **Changes:** this entry. No code exists yet; no contract, schema, fixture, dependency, ticket status, B allocation or numerical acceptance changes.

### B35. C3: bounded private-state replay and lookup cost (ticket 140)

- **Date:** 2026-10-29. **Decided by:** assistant, within D117/D152 and the Ticket140-only D155/D156/D158 exceptions. Six slices follow the original [ruling](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/ticket-140-design-ruling.md), its endpoint/S4 amendments and the [S5](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/ticket-140-s5-design-ruling.json) and [S6](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/ticket-140-s6-design-ruling.json) rulings. D156 resolved S5's completion-unit prerequisite; D157 froze the completed additive Reader capability before Core consumed it. D159 records the final Open-only amendment.
- **Decision:** use discardable ledger-derived lookup hints, one bounded metadata cache, fresh current-head checks and fused startup inspection, then byte-revalidated Reader checkpoints for contiguous acknowledgements. Successful Open seeds the same checkpoint entry from its own replay, removing the measured first-after-reopen prefix-decoding cost without trusting a receipt or adding a second authority. The [package documentation](../internal/core/agents/doc.go) specifies the built boundary and remaining cold paths; the rulings retain alternatives and reasons.
- **Evidence and limits:** the [acceptance evidence](../.scratch/dwarf-harness/c3-evidence/ticket-140-acceptance-evidence.md) maps all four criteria across six slices. Final code `453ec63abfc3eb8b15a2322ef09d279dcce22d1e` passes all seven original numerical bounds with three eligible instances per driver and no noise extension; earlier failed cohorts remain evidence. D153 gates final reviews, exact-SHA hosted success and publication. These are admission-and-storage measurements, not capacity or power-loss qualification. The one 256-entry/8 MiB charged-metadata limit is not an RSS bound. Prefix I/O and hashing remain, and eviction, an accepted frontier advance or segment rotation can make the first scoped acknowledgement after Open cold again. Initial Open/OpenPaid replay now reports actual descriptor-close failures, as D159 rules; no record, golden, authority, provider or money semantics change. D52/C4 real-work admission and Ticket13's verification remain separate.
- **Changes:** Ticket140's close and paired C3 progress lines, with private B35, acceptance evidence and eight verbatim design-source copies. D157's completed register change is not repeated.

### D160. A twenty-step ticket loop: fewer owner stops, one review per slice, one project manager for several tickets

- **Date:** 2026-10-29. **Decided by:** modified. After the project manager's audit of tickets 03–140, the owner adopted ten of its eleven proposals. He changed the fifth (records), dropping its mailbox for a second project manager: one project manager runs several tickets. He did not adopt the fourth (the project manager's model when its provider runs out). The flow is provisional until he reviews it after the next ticket.
- **Owner's words (chat, 23:34):**
  > Implement recommendations 1, 2, 3, 5 (with no reference to a secondary PM, one PM will handle multiple tickets), 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, & 11. Reducing from 50 to 20 steps and we will revisit the flow after the next ticket. This does not need to be exhuastively documented in the DECISIONS file. The session history will suffice. That DECISIONS file is already bloated
- **Decision:**
  1. When a preregistered numerical bound fails, the project manager adds up to two slices without asking, each after one labelled diagnostic and a fresh ruling, and reports them. A third slice, and any change to the bounds, traffic or eligibility rules, is the owner's.
  2. A ticket closes on its stand-in reviewer's approval. A fresh Fable advisor audits it after Fable's window resets, and a finding opens a fix ticket.
  3. The project manager decides reversible matters outside D152 item 4's owner list and reports them. Each design ruling lists the owner-reserved items its ticket will meet, which the project manager asks together right after the ruling.
  4. Records: one notes file per ticket in plain sentences; a receipt only when the evidence file cites it; evidence links raw logs by path and SHA-256 instead of copying them; the handoff written once per session.
  5. One fresh review per slice, covering standards and specification together, and one final review of the close documents with any code not yet reviewed.
  6. One project manager session on this machine runs up to three tickets at once.
  7. A ticket with numerical bounds starts with one profiled run of its acceptance driver.
  8. Each measurement instance runs through a runner that waits for a quiet machine and writes its own admission record, after a smoke run on the smallest history.
  9. Designers get the language-server tool; coders run their own focused tests unless a measurement holds the machine.
  10. A watcher wakes the project manager after 15 minutes without activity while work is pending.
- **Amends:** D152 items 2, 3, 4 (the stand-in clause) and 7; the per-ticket loop of D116 and D121. D116's documents-once-per-ticket rule and D153's gates stand.
- **Changes:** this entry and an "Amended by D160" line under D152; in the records repository, the designer, coder, advisor and drafter role files. The project manager's procedure, its two scripts and the coders' standing context changed outside the repositories.

Uncertain outcomes retain holds without automatic retry; Open reduces the ledger without reading paid artifacts.
